"The Young Lady will have many chances to eat. She'll be coming out to the Central Plains often from now on, won't she?"

Hanseol understood. That Geom Muguk was encouraging her to come out often from now on.

Geom Muguk spoke to the two again.

"So please eat your fill before you go."

Sobing said with a smile.

"We shall."

"If you ever come out to the Central Plains, even just the two of you, please send word to me. Then I shall treat you to even more delicious food."

Even if it was mere pleasantry, they were grateful words. Besides, hadn't he taken good care of them at yesterday's drinking session as well?

Only after definitely scoring points with the two experts of the Northern Sea did Geom Muguk's gaze turn to Ian, who was standing a short distance away and looking at him.

"What secret conversation took so long?"

In the meantime, Ian had calmed down considerably.

"He earnestly asked me to take good care of his son."

At her jest, Geom Muguk laughed. Geom Muguk could tell. That his father had bestowed an elixir upon her. The fine fragrance of the ten-thousand-year snow ginseng still lingered on her. It was a scent he knew well from having eaten it before.

Congratulations, Ian.

Having congratulated her in his heart, he spoke to Hanseol.

"Now then, let us go."

Geom Muguk and Ian saw Hanseol and the Hanbing Twin Swords off to the main gate.

As she walked, Hanseol looked at the Demon statues erected everywhere. She had stayed too briefly to form any lasting impressions of the Heavenly Demon Cult, but she felt she would never forget those massive Demon statues for as long as she lived.

A carriage awaited them at the main gate.

Hanseol was sorry to part with Ian, but she did not express it in words.

"See you next time."

"Farewell."

Ian watched the carriage they rode in grow distant before looking at Geom Muguk.

Geom Muguk stood silently with a taciturn expression, making one wonder when he had been such a chatterbox. People did not know. That this face was closest to the Young Lord's true nature.

Geom Muguk smiled at Ian and said.

"Let's go, to Master."

* * *

The Fist Demon was conducting morning training with the Iron Fists.

Below the Fist Demon, who was throwing punches from a platform, Cheon Sohui was throwing punches, and before her, the Black Fists in black martial arts uniforms were throwing punches with the same movements.

Pah-aang!

As they were the top experts among the Iron Fists of the Eastern Fist Gate, every single one of their movements contained discipline and power that no one could easily imitate.

From the Fist Demon to the Black Fists, though they were basic movements of fist arts, they devoted themselves fully to each and every motion.

No matter how complex and dazzling a technique, it unfolded based on these fundamental movements. The Fist Demon placed extreme importance on these basics.

The reason he personally taught the Iron Fists was to make these fundamentals completely ingrained in their bodies.

When that round of training ended, Geom Muguk and Ian entered.

"Father!"

The gazes of the Iron Fists split in half. The fervent young gazes turned toward Ian, while mainly the older ones turned toward the Fist Demon.

It was because they knew this was a chance to see an expression on the Fist Demon's face that was normally never seen. He was not one to reveal his emotions, but this moment was an exception.

"You have come?"

The Fist Demon's expression softened. That pleasant and comfortable expression hidden within that fearsome face was surprising to see every time.

The Iron Fists who had been looking at the Fist Demon shared this astonishment as they looked at one another.

Geom Muguk, who had followed Ian, spoke.

"The unfilial daughter has arrived."

"Unfilial daughter?"

When Ian asked in surprise, Geom Muguk explained.

"After arriving at the main cult, you didn't greet Master but went straight to drinking with me."

Ian spoke as if she were wronged.

"It was already so late, wasn't it?"

"You knew full well Master doesn't sleep at night."

Ian glared at Geom Muguk. Are you really doing this?

Smiling, Geom Muguk respectfully cupped his hands in greeting to the Fist Demon.

"Master, have you been well?"

The Fist Demon nodded and welcomed Geom Muguk with a pleased face. It was a reunion with both daughter and disciple after a long time.

At that moment, a greeting like a shout rang out.

"We greet the Young Lord!"

The Iron Fists greeted in booming voices. They felt pride that the Young Lord was the Fist Demon's disciple.

"Have you all been well?"

Having trained together before, Geom Muguk also greeted the Iron Fists warmly.

Cheon Sohui sent a wordless greeting with a hungover face to Geom Muguk and Ian.

The Fist Demon entrusted the training to her and then walked together with Geom Muguk and Ian.

Once they were far from the training ground, Ian spoke with an apologetic face.

"I think I must give you some unfortunate news. Something has come up, so I must leave the cult with the Young Lord immediately."

Knowing that Ian's heart would grow heavy if he showed disappointment, the Fist Demon nodded silently without asking the reason.

"And there is something I must tell you."

Ian told the Fist Demon that she had received the ten-thousand-year snow ginseng from Geom Ujin. And she told him the reason exactly as it was.

"The Cult Leader said that?"

"Yes."

Because he knew the relationship between his father and the Fist Demon better than anyone, Geom Muguk could see it. The uncontainable emotion that flickered across that fearsome face. To the Fist Demon, those words from his father were surely far more precious than the ten-thousand-year snow ginseng.

At moments like these, Geom Muguk was always reminded of one image. The sight of those two men of steel roaming the Central Plains in their youth.

In the meantime, the three arrived at the entrance of the Eastern Fist Gate.

"Go and return safely."

"Yes, Father."

Ian, who had been about to turn around after the greeting, spoke to the Fist Demon.

"The excessive gift I received from the Cult Leader today is all thanks to Father."

The Fist Demon shook his head.

"It is thanks to you."

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon added.

"If you had not become my daughter, none of this would have happened."

The moment she heard those words, tears streamed down Ian's eyes.

She had held them back before Cheonma, and she had held them back before Geom Muguk. The tears she had held and held back finally burst forth before her father.

She was so grateful to them all. She had been nothing but a child abandoned by the Heavenly Demon Cult. Was it really okay for her to receive such excessive love from among these great people?

"I... I'll be back."

Flustered by the unstoppable tears, Ian hurriedly left first.

The Fist Demon looked at Geom Muguk. It was a blunt gaze, but Geom Muguk read the Fist Demon's heart within it.

'Take my daughter and return her safely.'

Don't worry, Master. Your daughter will take good care of the Young Lord and return. You know, don't you? How strong your daughter is.

Having bid farewell to the Fist Demon, Geom Muguk also came out.

Outside, Ian spoke as if nothing had happened.

"You said we were busy, so why are you dawdling?"

Geom Muguk looked at her and laughed.

You little rascal, the tear marks around your eyes haven't all faded yet.

He didn't tease her for crying. Because to her, there were no tears more precious than these.

"Ready?"

"Yes!"

Geom Muguk leaped forward first and said.

"Let's go, Ian!"

* * *

Seojin was looking up at the signboard.

Never in her life had she seen such a large signboard.

Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.

As if the size of the signboard displayed the might of this martial hall, the four characters "Yellow Dragon Martial Hall" were written in gold upon the massive signboard.

Not a single martial artist guarded the entrance, and the doors were wide open. It gave off an air of confidence, as if saying, 'Do you dare enter the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall with irreverent hearts?'

Inside, a massive training ground was visible. The training ground was so large that the end could not be seen. Countless people were coming and going there.

Then a passing martial artist saw her and walked out.

"What brings you here?"

Seojin looked at the martial artist.

"I've come to meet Instructor Lin."

"There are more than one or two Instructors Lin; which Instructor Lin do you mean?"

"Instructor Lin Xian."

"Wait a moment."

The martial artist went inside and then came back out shortly.

"Follow me."

Seojin followed the martial artist inside.

Walking after him, she observed the interior. It was so vast that she couldn't tell whether she had come to a Martial Alliance headquarters or a martial hall.

Everywhere in the training ground, members were training in martial arts. Dozens were gathered in one place, and a few were training together in another. She could feel their passion to grow stronger.

"This place is truly amazing."

The martial artist guiding her answered proudly.

"You've never heard of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, the greatest martial hall in the Central Plains? We don't accept people just because they pay money like ordinary martial halls. This is a place you can only enter by passing an exam."

That was also why it was more famous. And it was known that graduating was more difficult than entering. If one couldn't pass the graduation exam, they couldn't claim to be from this place anywhere.

The martial artist guided Seojin to an office in a large building.

"Welcome. I am Giseok, a Master of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall."

The hierarchy of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall was divided into Hall Master, Great Master, Master, and Instructor. There were over a hundred Instructors, but only seven Masters, who were called the Seven Masters.

He was one of the Seven Masters of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.

She had naturally expected to be guided to Lin Xian, so being led here instead, Seojin was inwardly puzzled.

"I am Seojin."

"You came looking for Instructor Lin?"

"That is correct."

"What is your relationship with Instructor Lin?"

For some reason, a foreboding thought came to Seojin.

"I am a younger sister from his hometown."

Lin Xian had been a childhood friend of her older brother, the Gwiryeongja, but had been closer to her. He had played with her more than her brother, who was always cooped up in his research lab.

Today, she had come to find him to receive an item that Lin Xian was to pass on to her brother.

"I see."

Giseok's expression darkened, and soon he delivered news like a bolt from the blue.

"Instructor Lin passed away recently."

Seojin cried out in shock, "Ack—". She was so flustered that she didn't know what to say.

"...My brother died?"

Her voice trembled.

Giseok brought cold tea and gave it to her.

"First, drink this and calm yourself."

Holding the teacup, Seojin asked him.

"How did it happen?"

"There was an accident during training."

"What kind of accident?"

Had he died in a martial contest with his disciples? Or what kind of accident had he met?

"I cannot give details to those who are not immediate family."

"I am no different from family."

"That doesn't make you family, does it?

His mother, who had been his only remaining family, had passed away over ten years ago, and now he had no family.

"It is this hall's principle, so please understand."

Seojin sighed. What good would it do to know the reason now? He was already dead. He had died alone and lonely in this distant foreign land.

"What about his body?"

She thought she should at least take care of Lin Xian's body. She had intended to bury him in their hometown.

"If you are not immediate family, we cannot release it. We have already buried him, so you need not worry."

They had buried him? But without contacting her or her older brother?

"Instructor Lin did not leave any emergency contacts."

Doubt sprouted in Seojin's heart.

'How could that be?'

Long ago, Lin Xian had told her that he had set things up so they would be contacted in case of an emergency. That they were his family now.

'They won't tell me the cause of death, won't release the body, and are even lying to me?'

She intuited that they were hiding something about Lin Xian's death.

Giseok asked her.

"But what business did you have with Instructor Lin?"

Seojin, who had been staring at Giseok, sighed and said.

"I was planning to enter the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall."

Chapter 566: It Wasn't Bad Being With You.
------------------------------------------
“You were thinking of entering this martial hall?”

Seojin’s answer had been an instinctive choice. She had made the spur-of-the-moment judgment that she would have to enter this place if she wanted to investigate Im Hyeon Oppa’s death.

“I was thinking of learning martial arts in the place where Oppa was.”

Seojin let out a sigh.

“But Oppa is dead now….”

She spoke as though she were troubled, unsure of what to do.

At present, she seemed more worried about herself than saddened by Im Hyeon’s death.

She tried to appear as though she was someone who was sad about his death, but whose own concerns took priority—no more and no less.

If she acted too grief-stricken or insisted on entering no matter what, it would look as though she had come to investigate Im Hyeon’s death.

‘I’m sorry, Oppa.’

At first, she had been too shocked to think clearly, but once doubts about Im Hyeon’s death arose, her mind began to turn coldly and methodically.

“Even if Instructor Im were still here, you would have had to take the entrance examination.”

“I’m confident I can pass.”

“Then take the examination and enter. Our martial hall offers everyone a chance, anytime.”

Giseok spoke as though he had no objection to her entering. Seen this way, he looked like someone completely unrelated to Im Hyeon’s death.

“I think I need to give it some thought. Whether I can attend a place where Oppa isn’t. I’ll be going now.”

As she left the room, Giseok offered her words of comfort.

“Don’t grieve too deeply. No martial artist can live while clinging to life and death.”

“Thank you for your kind words.”

Seojin bowed politely to Giseok before leaving.

A short while after she departed, a man entered the room. He was Pyosan, an instructor at the Huanglong Martial Hall and Giseok’s right-hand man.

When he entered, Giseok was looking down from the window at Seojin as she left the building. Pyosan walked over to his side and spoke.

“The name Instructor Im had written down as the person to contact in an emergency….”

His gaze also turned toward Seojin, who was walking below.

“It was Seojin.”

Giseok’s eyes sharpened as he looked at her.

“She didn’t seem ordinary. Which clan is she from?”

“The Guimun.”

Giseok turned to Pyosan.

“The Guimun?”

“Yes. That woman is the youngest daughter of the Guimun Lord.”

“This has become troublesome.”

The Guimun’s ghost arts were exceptionally difficult for ordinary martial artists to deal with.

He had intended to kill her and silence her if necessary, but things were different if she was a blood relative of the Guimun. If he killed her carelessly, the Guimun would rise to avenge their daughter.

“That little ghost seemed suspicious of Instructor Im’s death. She’ll probably enter the hall and investigate.”

“I’ll make arrangements to have her fail the examination.”

Giseok shook his head.

“If she has the ability but still fails, she’ll become even more suspicious. Rather than letting her make a fuss investigating from outside, it would be better to keep her close and watch her.”

Giseok looked back at Pyosan.

“If she enters, take charge of her personally.”

“Yes.”

Giseok’s gaze returned to Seojin, standing in the distance beside the training ground.

She had stopped for a moment and was watching the hall members training on the field.

A scene came to her mind. The countless people around her had all disappeared, leaving Im Hyeon lying alone on the vast training ground, covered in blood.

‘…Oppa.’

Only after leaving Giseok’s office and being alone did her grief finally come rushing in earnest.

Im Hyeon Oppa, who had spent every day since childhood following their father and researching the Great Method, had been boring. Whenever he refused to play after she asked him first, he would call for her next.

―Jin, let’s go catch cicadas!

―I have to study. Father will scold me!

―Study later. Let’s go catch cicadas!

It had been the same when they grew a little older.

―Jin, they say a performing troupe has come to the village. Let’s go watch!

―Oppa gave me something to do. If I just leave, he’ll scold me!

―I won’t regret it even if I get scolded! They say it’s a really entertaining show!

For her, who had found life conducting research into the Great Method unbearably stifling, Im Hyeon had been more than just the neighborhood oppa. He had been a breath of fresh air.

Once, during an argument with Gwiryongja over researching the Great Method, she had shouted at him.

―Let’s say the Regression Great Method really is possible, just as Oppa says. If you went back to the past, what would you do? Would you shut yourself away and research again? Since you’ve already achieved regression, would you research reincarnation next? If you thought of doing anything else… Oppa, then we’re living the wrong lives, aren’t we? We should be doing that thing and living that life now, shouldn’t we? We should live the life we want to return to, shouldn’t we?

Looking back now, Im Hyeon had also been the one who taught her that such a life existed. She had seen the world outside the laboratory while playing with him.

But over the past few years, she had hardly seen him properly. Once, she had refused when he asked her to come see him because she was too tired, and another time, she had been genuinely too busy to meet him. She had thought she could see him later. As always.

‘If I’d known this would happen, I should have gone to see him then.’

Guilt welled up inside her, and tears gathered in her eyes. But she forced them back. This was not the time to cry.

‘If someone murdered Oppa and covered it up, I will uncover the truth no matter what.’

This incident had not left only one victim.

She was a victim, and so was Gwiryongja Oppa, who would hear the news later. They would both spend the rest of their lives regretting it and suffering.

She was more worried about her oppa.

He had lost the one friend he had. Gwiryongja was the one who had rejected his advances most often because he was researching the Great Method.

* * *

The next day, Seojin took the entrance examination.

The result was exactly what she had expected.

“Pass!”

Seojin safely cleared the forms demonstration, the internal energy test, and even the final sparring match against an instructor. Although she had primarily learned ghost arts, her martial ability was more than sufficient to enter the Huanglong Martial Hall.

“Congratulations.”

The person who had sparred with her was Pyosan, Giseok’s right-hand man.

“Thank you.”

Pyosan briefly explained the Huanglong Martial Hall’s system.

“New members who have just entered the hall belong to the White Dragon Class. After passing the White Dragon Class, you move on to the Azure Dragon Class; after the Azure Dragon Class comes the Black Dragon Class, and the final class is the Yellow Dragon Class. Only after graduating from the Yellow Dragon Class can you truly call yourself a graduate of the Huanglong Martial Hall.”

“How long does it usually take to complete the Yellow Dragon Class?”

“Those with outstanding martial talent can do it in less than a year, while those without talent can fail to do it even after twenty years.”

Once she entered, she realized there were truly a great many members in the hall.

There were already hundreds of members in the White Dragon Class to which she would belong, and as many as thirty instructors teaching them.

The money they made must be enormous as well. How many people paid their monthly fees without ever managing to graduate because leaving the hall was so difficult?

In any case, her vague hope that she would somehow uncover the truth once she entered the martial hall was nothing more than a vague expectation. It would not be easy to find someone who knew Im Hyeon.

“Come. Follow me.”

The hall could have assigned a member to guide her, but Pyosan personally showed her around. This building was for this, that building was for that.

He pointed out the places every newcomer needed to know: where they could wash, where they could change clothes, and where they could eat.

Finally, he brought her to one corner of the training ground.

“This is where you’ll train starting tomorrow. Be sure to arrive on time!”

“Understood.”

Pyosan turned to leave, but Seojin suddenly asked him a question.

“Instructor, did you know Instructor Im?”

“I did.”

“Then do you also know what kind of accident caused his death?”

Pyosan turned back to her.

“What was your relationship with Instructor Im that you’re asking such questions?”

“He was an oppa I was close to in my hometown. I tried to enter this place because of him, too.”

Pyosan appeared to ponder for a moment before speaking.

“Instructor Im died while sparring with a disciple.”

Seojin was startled by the unexpected answer.

“In cases like this, the cause of death is kept secret out of consideration for the instructor’s honor.”

“Who was he fighting?”

Pyosan’s expression turned cold.

“If you knew, what would you do? Go and take revenge? He died in a fair spar.”

“No.”

“Then why do you want to know?”

Leaving Seojin, who had no answer, behind, Pyosan strode away.

As she watched his back, Seojin’s gaze slowly turned cold.

Because he had answered so directly, without hiding anything like Giseok, his words sounded entirely truthful. But Seojin did not believe him.

He had made one mistake.

When she had first arrived, the martial artist who guided her had said there were many instructors with the surname Im and asked which one she was looking for.

Yet when she had asked Pyosan if he knew Instructor Im, he had immediately answered that he did. Normally, he should naturally have asked, “Which Instructor Im?”

‘There’s only one reason he knew.’

He had already spoken with Giseok.

What were the odds that, in a place with so many instructors, Giseok had happened to discuss her with the person assigned to oversee her examination? In the end, it meant the two of them were in league with each other.

‘What are you people hiding that makes you go this far?’

* * *

The sounds of insects came from far away.

Geom Mugeuk and Ian were circulating their energy in the forest.

They were making their way toward the Huanglong Martial Hall using lightness techniques. With his increased internal energy, Ian could run for a long time without resting. He was also much faster than a galloping horse.

They ran and ran. When their internal energy ran low, they circulated their energy; when it was replenished, they ran again.

It was a journey consisting of nothing but running, with no time for a proper conversation or even a proper meal, but Ian was happy nonetheless.

It was the first time he had ever run to his heart’s content with Geom Mugeuk.

Ian opened his eyes after finishing his energy circulation.

Geom Mugeuk should have finished circulating his energy already, but he remained deep in thought with his eyes closed. His expression was so serious that Ian could not bring himself to speak to him.

Geom Mugeuk was remembering what had happened that day.

“Seojin!”

When he arrived after using his lightness technique to the utmost, Seojin was collapsed on the ground. In the distance, he could see the back of the murderer fleeing at full speed.

The only thing he had been able to see was long, flowing white hair.

He could not pursue him. Seojin’s condition was critical.

“Stay with me!”

He pressed her pressure points to stop the bleeding. But the wound was too deep. It was so deep that he could only consider it fortunate that she had not died instantly.

“Just hold on a little longer!”

He tried to carry Seojin to a physician, but she grabbed his arm and spoke.

“No.”

She had sensed that she was going to die.

Seeing the despair covering Geom Mugeuk’s face, she forced herself to smile.

“It’s all right, friend.”

“I’m not all right.”

Geom Mugeuk let out a deep sigh.

She had been his only friend. His friend, and his hope.

If he had not heard about the Regression Great Method from her, he would never have struggled so desperately to survive. He would have either killed himself or gone to find Hwa Mugi and died a pointless death.

“When you meet Oppa later, be sure to tell him this. I’m leaving after having lived a life without regrets.”

Her eyes grew clear in a final rally of strength, and she spoke distinctly, as though she were uninjured.

“Please tell Oppa not to spend his life researching only the Great Method. Tell him to live his own life.”

“All right. I’ll be sure to tell him.”

Instead, Seojin tightly clasped Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

“Living as a wanderer wasn’t so bad, since I was with you.”

Geom Mugeuk’s heart ached. He had lived as a wanderer without attaching any meaning to death. But now he was so furious that he felt he might explode.

“You’ll be able to return to the past, I’m sure of it.”

As her eyes slowly closed, Geom Mugeuk shouted. There was something she had to hear before she died.

“Who was that bastard? Who was the one who killed you?”

Seojin gave Geom Mugeuk one final smile.

“…Just think about your revenge.”

Seojin’s eyes slowly closed.

When her eyes had shut completely, Geom Mugeuk opened his own.

Seojin had told him not to seek revenge, but he had tracked the man down with dogged determination and taken revenge.

He had gone to find Gwiryongja afterward. He could not go to him without first avenging her. He could not visit him without taking revenge and then deliver her words.

Ian carefully asked him,

“Why do you look so serious? Did you have a bad dream?”

The seriousness slowly faded from Geom Mugeuk’s face, and he smiled at Ian.

“I dreamed I lent you money and never got it back.”

Ian gave a faint smile. Young Master, whom did you lose in your dream?

“How much did I borrow? If I was going to default, I should’ve defaulted on a huge amount. Ian in the dream, live well even without me!”

Geom Mugeuk laughed cheerfully at Ian’s banter.

“Shall I carry you for a while? It’ll be faster.”

Knowing that he meant they should travel faster using rapid steps, Ian swiftly climbed onto Geom Mugeuk’s back.

Ian whispered into Geom Mugeuk’s ear.

“I’d like to borrow some money toooooooo!”

* * *

From the next day onward, Seojin’s life at the Huanglong Martial Hall truly began.

Along with the others who had entered around the same time, she learned martial arts from Pyosan.

After receiving three hours of training a day, the rest of the time was free. They could return home or remain behind to practice martial arts.

After training ended that day, Seojin was looking around the martial hall.

Today was her opportunity. Since it was her first day of training, she could go anywhere. Even if she entered a restricted area, she could claim that she had not known better.

Thus, she toured every corner of the Huanglong Martial Hall, drawing a map in her mind.

As she gradually ventured deeper into the hall, the color of the martial uniforms changed. From white to blue, and farther in, to black. Naturally, the bearing of the members also became sharper and more powerful.

No one paid her any attention as far as the Black Dragon Class’s territory. But when she entered the area where people wearing yellow uniforms were gathered, someone finally stopped her.

“What is a member of the White Dragon Class doing all the way here?”

From the Yellow Dragon Class’s territory onward, guards stood at every entrance. Judging by their yellow uniforms, they appeared to be martial artists who belonged to the Yellow Dragon Class as well.

“It’s my first day since entering the hall. I was curious, so I was looking around.”

At the mention of her first day, the martial artist’s cold expression softened somewhat.

“You cannot enter here. Go back.”

Just as the martial artist was about to send her away, a voice came from behind.

“A newcomer?”

Seojin turned around.

A white-haired man with long, flowing hair was walking toward her.

Chapter 567: Even Ghosts Cannot Stop Human Greed
------------------------------------------------
At the sight of the man, the martial artist standing guard politely saluted him.

The martial artists’ expressions brightened when they saw him. From their reactions, Seojin could guess what kind of person this man was to his subordinates.

“He asked if you were a newcomer.”

Seojin answered politely.

“Yes. I arrived today.”

The man’s taut skin made him seem young, yet the maturity in his expression and eyes suggested that he was older. His flowing white hair made it even more impossible to guess his age.

“Curious?”

“Pardon?”

“I asked if you were curious about what’s inside.”

Before she could answer, the man strode inside and said,

“What are you waiting for? Aren’t you coming?”

“Yes.”

Seojin followed him inside.

She did not even know who he was, but the atmosphere was hardly one in which she could refuse.

The Yellow Dragon Class district was completely different from the outside.

Unlike the other districts, which consisted only of training grounds and buildings, this place was beautifully maintained. The buildings were larger and more elegant, and old ginkgo trees had been planted beside neatly arranged gravel paths. Training grounds for individual use had been built here and there amid gardens filled with beautiful flowers.

The intent was unmistakable: Hurry up and train hard so you can come here too.

The Yellow Dragon Class martial artists passing by politely bowed their heads to the man.

He clearly wasn’t a member of the Yellow Dragon Class, but he didn’t seem to be an instructor either. Was he one of the Seven Masters? Just who was this man?

The deeper they went, the more heavily guarded the area became. At the end of the line of guards stood a building as large as a fortress.

“What is that place?”

A strange emotion entered the man’s eyes as he gazed at the building.

“That is the Academy Master’s Hall of the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy. It is where the master of this place resides.”

At that, a single remark naturally slipped from her lips.

“The master of the academy is its members.”

The man turned to look at her.

“That is what was written at the very beginning of the admission form I filled out yesterday.”

A glint flashed through the man’s eyes. The emotion was clearly closer to curiosity than anger.

“So? Do you think you are the master?”

“I don’t know yet. As I said, today is my first day.”

Since meeting the man’s gaze was uncomfortable, she turned her eyes back toward the Academy Master’s Hall.

The man suddenly asked,

“Do you want to go inside and take a look?”

“Would I dare enter?”

“You don’t seem to acknowledge the authority of the master of that place.”

She politely refused.

“You misunderstand. I have seen more than enough for today. Thanks to you, I had a good look around.”

She did want to enter the Academy Master’s Hall once, but doing so on her very first day would draw far too much attention. No—she had already attracted enough attention as it was.

“Then I’ll be going now.”

Just as she politely bowed and turned to leave, the man spoke from behind her.

“Who said you could leave?”

She flinched in surprise and turned back toward him.

“I haven’t given you permission to go.”

Seojin stared directly at the man and asked,

“May I leave?”

Perhaps because she showed no fear, the man did not tell her she could go.

Just then, someone approached them.

“Great Hero Yeon.”

The person who appeared was Giseok, one of the Seven Masters. A short distance behind him stood Pyosan, his trusted aide.

At that moment, Seojin noticed that the man’s expression toward Giseok was far from pleasant. He made no effort to conceal his dislike of the other man.

“It has been a long time.”

Giseok greeted him politely, but the man merely gave a slight nod.

As if this was not the first or second time it had happened, Giseok showed no sign of taking offense.

His calm gaze turned toward Seojin.

“What are you doing here?”

“This gentleman was showing me around.”

Giseok said to the man,

“She entered the academy today. Is it really appropriate to teach her by having her break the rules first?”

Then he turned back to Seojin.

“Leave!”

“Yes, Master.”

At that moment, Seojin realized something.

Giseok had not stepped forward for her sake. If so, it meant he disliked seeing her together with this man.

‘At least he isn’t on this man’s side.’

Of course, she could not jump to conclusions. People in the martial world could pretend to be enemies while secretly working together, and those who fought like mortal foes yesterday could become friends today.

She was about to turn and leave when the white-haired man said,

“I still haven’t told her to go.”

She turned around again and looked back and forth between the two men.

The victor of this clash of wills had been decided from the beginning. Giseok immediately withdrew.

“Then I’ll be going.”

Giseok turned and walked away, with Pyosan following behind him.

After watching them for a moment, the man looked back at Seojin.

“Now, please let me go. It’s my first day, and I’m already quite nervous.”

She remained calm and composed, acting according to instinct. With the man before her, she believed it was better to show confidence than to shrink back in fear.

“At the very least, you should know who I am before you go.”

“Who are you?”

“I am Yeon Baekjin.”

The man added in an even tone,

“I am the man who was born too late to become the master of this place.”

He was Yeon Baekjin, the academy master’s younger brother. At the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy, he was the freest person, the most frightening person, and also the most popular. He was a man who showed a different side of himself depending on whom he faced.

Seojin politely saluted him, then turned away. After walking a few steps, she said,

“Don’t be too disappointed. It was clearly written that the master of this place is its members.”

Yeon Baekjin silently watched her leave.

Two people were watching the same scene from afar. They stood by a corridor window inside the building—Giseok and Pyosan.

“It seems Great Hero Yeon is interested in Seojin. Though there must be quite an age difference between them.”

At Pyosan’s words, Giseok shook his head.

“Everyone thinks Yeon Baekjin is an outspoken and free-spirited man. Someone who likes women and enjoys having fun.”

Many women admired him. He was the academy master’s younger brother and possessed formidable martial prowess, so rumors often linked him with various women.

But this was how Giseok judged Yeon Baekjin:

“Yeon Baekjin is more calculating and ruthless than anyone.”

“Then are you saying he approached Seojin deliberately?”

“Yes. He must have caught the scent of Master Im’s affair.”

Pyosan’s expression hardened. If that was true, it was a serious matter. One was the Ghost Sect Leader’s daughter, while the other was the academy master’s younger brother.

“The final excavation is not far away. What should we do?”

“We need to turn their attention elsewhere.”

Giseok had already reached his conclusion.

“Even so, we cannot kill the academy master’s own kin, can we?”

* * *

That evening, Seojin was drinking alone at an inn.

She was not someone who enjoyed alcohol, but today she had a sudden urge to drink. It was because of Yeon Baekjin, whom she had met earlier.

‘I’ll use him to investigate this incident.’

That had been her first thought. If, as she had seen earlier, he truly was on bad terms with Giseok, it was certainly worth trying.

But she felt reluctant.

She did not know whether it was her instinct for danger or a woman’s intuition, but something inside her warned her away.

Don’t get close to this man. Exchange as few words with him as possible.

Reality, however, was unfolding in precisely the opposite direction.

“Are you staying at this inn?”

She raised her head and saw Yeon Baekjin. Without asking permission, he sat down across from her.

Seojin kept her thoughts hidden and calmly said,

“I didn’t properly greet you earlier, my lord.”

“That goes for you too, doesn’t it?”

Yeon Baekjin already knew her identity.

“You were the Ghost Sect Leader’s daughter.”

He spoke as though he had only learned it after meeting her.

“Can you show me some of the Ghost Sect’s sorcery? I have never seen it before, and I’m curious.”

Of course, Seojin was not someone who would show it so easily.

“I’m still inexperienced. I might even kill you.”

Yeon Baekjin poured liquor into a fresh cup and said,

“How presumptuous.”

“I apologize if that is how it sounded.”

After emptying his cup, Yeon Baekjin stared at her and asked,

“Can the name of the Ghost Sect truly serve as a shield to protect you?”

The words could be taken as an insult to her family. But Yeon Baekjin continued to warn her to the end.

“Human greed cannot even be stopped by ghosts.”

This man knew something.

Seojin had that feeling. And he was not even trying to hide it. What was his purpose in approaching her?

Giseok, Pyosan, and Yeon Baekjin.

All of them were connected to Elder Brother Im Hyeon’s death, whether directly involved or indirectly linked to it.

This time, she emptied her cup before asking,

“Why do you assume the Ghost Sect is the only shield I have?”

“What else is there?”

She wanted to tell him this:

I am the second squad leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit of the Heavenly Demon Divine Sect.

If she said that, would he still be able to look at her with those arrogant eyes?

More than anything, she wanted to come here under her identity as a member of the Ghost Shadow Unit and uncover every detail of whatever these people were plotting.

But she had not yet joined the Ghost Shadow Unit. She had decided to finish this matter before leaving.

Her final task before becoming a demon.

‘Somehow, I will resolve this and return.’

That was when she set her empty cup down on the table.

“Are you people from the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy?”

A young man had approached them.

He seemed to have recognized her because she was wearing the White Dragon Class uniform.

“We came to take the admission examination too! What a relief to meet you!”

A woman walked up behind the young man.

“Since fate has brought us together, please tell us how we should take the examination!”

The two of them naturally joined Seojin and Yeon Baekjin at their table.

Seojin and Yeon Baekjin were both startled when they saw them, though for entirely different reasons. Neither stopped them from taking a seat.

Yeon Baekjin was startled because the young man and woman were exceptionally handsome and beautiful. The woman in particular was breathtakingly beautiful. The moment he saw her, he found himself unable to say something as crass as, “How dare you sit down without permission?”

Seojin, on the other hand, was startled for a different reason. The young man was Geom Muguk, and the woman was Ian. The young master and the commander of the Ghost Shadow Unit had come to find her.

Seojin was shocked, but she desperately tried to remain calm. Through Ian, she already knew that the Geom Muguk she had seen at Elder Brother’s wedding was the young master of the Heavenly Demon Divine Sect. But this was hardly a situation in which she could properly greet him.

“I only entered the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy today.”

“Then that’s even better. You must still have a vivid memory of the examination, since you took it so recently.”

Geom Muguk’s gaze shifted toward Yeon Baekjin.

“Then is this white-haired gentleman our senior as well?”

Seojin introduced him precisely.

“This is Great Hero Yeon Baekjin, the younger brother of the master of the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy.”

“Oh! So you are an esteemed personage! It is an honor to meet you.”

Perhaps because his words and eyes did not match, Yeon Baekjin asked coldly,

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“What kind of look was I giving you?”

Yeon Baekjin had felt uncomfortable when his eyes met Geom Muguk’s. That gaze had seemed to peer into the depths of his heart. It was the first time he had ever felt so shaken by someone’s eyes.

“Please don’t misunderstand. I naturally feel competitive whenever I see someone handsome.”

He would never know what Geom Muguk had felt when he saw Seojin sitting across from Yeon Baekjin at the inn.

So this was where their connection began.

Seojin would not die until much later, but the beginning of their ill-fated relationship had been forged here.

Geom Muguk had rushed here in order to sever that ill fate from the very beginning. He was worried that his regression might cause that fate to come sooner.

Seojin, now that I’m here, everything will be all right.

The moment he and Ian had taken their seats beside Seojin, her fate had already begun to travel down a different path.

Geom Muguk’s gaze turned toward Yeon Baekjin.

And you? Will you walk the path of fate that ends with me killing you once more?

“Your hair is incredible. I would like to go around with white hair like that too.”

“Shut up.”

While Geom Muguk was already drawing the words shut up from Yeon Baekjin’s mouth, Seojin and Ian were exchanging secret messages through telepathy.

―Commander, how did you know to come here?

―I told you I was coming here to take care of something, didn’t I?

Of course, she had said that. What she wanted to know was why Ian had come here.

―I came because I wanted to see you as soon as possible. If you resolve this matter even one day sooner, you’ll be able to join the Ghost Shadow Unit that much sooner too.

Had she really come for that reason? No. She had certainly come because she knew Seojin was in a difficult situation. But how had she found out?

―You came to help me, didn’t you?

―Do you need help?

The gazes of the two women intertwined in midair. Seojin answered honestly. To insist on her pride now would not be pride, but foolishness.

―Yes, I need it. Please help me.

Ian conveyed her sincerity to Seojin.

―Commander Seo’s affairs are my affairs too.

That was why she had not come to help Seojin; she had come to handle her own affairs. Ian had already made up her mind.

―This will be our Ghost Shadow Unit’s first mission.

Seojin was happy. When she had wanted to tell Yeon Baekjin that she was the second squad leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit, she had thought of Ian. And now, as if by a lie becoming truth, Ian had come to her.

Ian raised her cup and said,

“Now, let’s have a drink, future comrades.”

How could Seojin not know that the comrades Ian meant were not comrades of the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy, but comrades of the Ghost Shadow Unit?

Following Seojin as she heartily raised her cup, Geom Muguk clinked his cup against hers.

“Let’s take over the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy!”

Chapter 568: You’re Actually Bad at Swearing, Aren’t You?
---------------------------------------------------------
Yeon Baekjin laughed aloud.

“Why do you laugh?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, he made a scornful expression.

“Three newcomers say they’ll take over the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall—laughter is inevitable.”

“Then will you help us?”

“What?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him and asked.

“If the Great Hero helps, wouldn’t we be able to take it over?”

In that moment, Geom Mugeuk saw it. Yeon Baekjin’s pupils trembling slightly. It was a moment that should have been laughed off as nonsense.

This uncontrollable agitation clearly stemmed from accumulated, long-held regret.

Yeon Baekjin set down his wine cup with a sharp clack.

Geom Mugeuk quickly spoke to him.

“I’m sorry. I’m nervous about tomorrow’s exam, so I was spouting nonsense.”

Yeon Baekjin glared at him with an intensity that could bore holes through him—the one who had requested to sit together with a beauty who could be called the most beautiful woman under heaven.

“Who are you?”

The intense suspicion that had been momentarily suppressed by Ian’s appearance surged forth.

“I’m Geom Yeon. Not the yeon from inyeon, but the yeon meaning smoke. When the work is done, I’ll disappear like smoke.”

“I didn’t ask.”

“Yes. I’m sorry.”

Yeon Baekjin’s suspicion soon subsided. If someone had intended to approach him deliberately, they wouldn’t have sent such a strange fellow. And they certainly wouldn’t have sent such a beautiful woman along with him. These two stood out too much, even if one were trying to stand out.

Yeon Baekjin stood up from his seat and spoke to Seo Jin.

“Let’s have a drink next time when it’s quiet.”

Seo Jin silently lowered her head slightly. That he asked her for a drink even with Ian beside him meant he didn’t see her as a mere object of desire. If he had, he would have approached Ian.

Before leaving, Yeon Baekjin glanced at Ian. Truly, she was beautiful even upon a second look. It seemed things would get noisy for a while once this woman entered the martial hall.

When Yeon Baekjin left the inn, only three people remained there now.

Seo Jin saw it. Geom Mugeuk’s change. Before Yeon Baekjin, he had truly looked like a greenhorn facing tomorrow’s entrance exam. He had really seemed to misspeak and appeared genuinely flustered. But now, nowhere in Geom Mugeuk’s bearing could such clumsiness be felt.

“He regrets failing to become the Hall Lord of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.”

Seo Jin confirmed that Geom Mugeuk had seen correctly.

“You saw well. When he introduced himself to me, he said he was someone who couldn’t become the master of this place because he was born too late.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded. When he had carried out Seo Jin’s revenge in the past, he hadn’t been able to thoroughly investigate Yeon Baekjin or the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall. Because revenge was urgent then. Now, it was necessary to calmly assess him.

Seo Jin spoke to Geom Mugeuk and Ian.

“Shall we go up to my room for a moment?”

Seo Jin led the two to her room on the upper floor of the inn.

She greeted Geom Mugeuk with the utmost courtesy she could muster.

“I offer my formal greetings. I am Squad Leader Seo Jin of the Ghost Shadow Division, and I greet the Young Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk welcomed her with a smile.

“Welcome to the Ghost Shadow Division.”

She wouldn’t know. How moved he was right now. With what heart he was looking at her.

‘In this life, it seems I won’t be able to hear those grateful words you spoke to me as a friend.’

That was regrettable, but an incomparably better life awaited her. She would take Ian as the Great Leader, Cheongmyeon as a fellow squad leader, and meet other comrades.

‘Now, there will be no more dying as a nameless vagabond in some nameless field.’

This time, Seo Jin showed courtesy to Ian as well.

“I, Squad Leader Seo Jin, offer my greetings to the Great Leader.”

“Thank you for believing in me.”

“Please speak comfortably now, Great Leader.”

“I will, Squad Leader Seo.”

Ian spoke with an inscrutable smile.

“You know I’ve gone through a lot because of you?”

The two women looked at each other and laughed. It was something that let one know how much Ian had struggled to persuade her. Because Seo Jin was by no means an easygoing personality.

“Still, I wanted to make you my person even a day sooner.”

“Thank you.”

After exchanging greetings, Ian asked.

“Squad Leader Seo, tell me what the situation is.”

Seo Jin told everything that had happened since coming to the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.

“So you mean your master Gi Seok and Instructor Pyo San are hiding something, and that Yeon Baekjin we saw today also seems to know something.”

“Yes, that’s right.”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk. Asking with her eyes what to do now, Geom Mugeuk took a step back.

“Don’t look at me. This is the Ghost Shadow Division’s affair, so handle it yourselves. I’ll only play the role of shield and sword.”

That was also what Ian wanted. Because she wanted to resolve this matter on her own with Seo Jin as much as possible.

“With the sturdiest sword and shield in the martial world, there’s nothing to fear. Shall we go conquer the martial world then?”

Literally, Ian didn’t hesitate at all.

“First, let’s enter the hall tomorrow, then decide what to do.”

“Great!”

Geom Mugeuk was excited, saying it was his first time entering a martial hall since birth.

But he didn’t let his guard down. Geom Mugeuk went down and took the room next to Seo Jin’s from the innkeeper.

“It may be inconvenient, but you two use the same room. I’ll stay next door.”

If there were an ambush by any chance, it would be safer for them to be together. Besides, it would be a chance for the two to get close.

That night, Geom Mugeuk practiced martial arts by unleashing the Heaven-Soaring Secret Technique within the Space-Time Reversal Art.

There, Geom Mugeuk strove and strove again to break through the seventh star of the Nine Transformations Demonic Art and ascend to the eighth star. Because far more time was given to him than others, his effort was also multiplied several fold.

A space that belonged to him alone.

For Geom Mugeuk, this place was the true martial hall.

* * *

The next day, Seo Jin was attending the martial arts class of Pyo San of the White Dragon Class.

“With vigor! With precision!”

The number of hall members in the White Dragon Class led by Pyo San was about thirty.

He had taken charge of the new recruits’ class in order to monitor Seo Jin. Among these, those with sufficient skill would be sent up to the next class, and after going through several stages within the White Dragon Class, they would take the exam to ascend to the Azure Dragon Class.

The hall members feared the instructors and tried their hardest to impress. If you got on their bad side, you wouldn’t even be able to take the class promotion exam. Today as well, everyone was sweating hard, lest they incur Pyo San’s displeasure.

When class was almost over, an instructor brought two people walking there.

One member practicing forms stumbled and his posture collapsed.

But no one paid him any mind. Everyone was too busy looking at the two people coming there to notice their surroundings.

“New recruits for the White Dragon Class.”

The two who captured everyone’s gaze were none other than Geom Mugeuk and Ian. Having passed the entrance exam, they were joining the White Dragon Class.

Not only the White Dragon Class members, but even Pyo San, who was teaching them, had his gaze stolen by Ian. Before long, Pyo San spoke to the two.

“Come out and introduce yourselves.”

Geom Mugeuk went out first and greeted them.

“I’m Geom Yeon.”

“I’m Ian.”

Pyo San spoke to the two.

“Come here starting tomorrow.”

Geom Mugeuk and Ian said spiritedly:

“Yes, understood.”

Pyo San’s gaze turned to Seo Jin. Seo Jin was also looking at Ian. She, too, acted normally like the other members.

“Today’s training ends here.”

When Pyo San stepped back, attention focused on the two. There were those who stared openly and those who stole glances.

Among them, one stepped forward, brazenly taking the lead.

“I am Yu Gwang.”

He seemed somewhat belligerent, but he was quite large and loud.

“I’ll be blunt. I like you.”

It was an attitude on the border between bravery and rudeness, but to the present Ian, it was simply cute. Moreover, the voice of the fellow trying hard to put on bravado was trembling slightly.

“Is that so.”

“What?”

“I said I understand how you feel.”

Perhaps because it wasn’t the reaction he had expected, Yu Gwang was flustered.

Maybe because everyone’s attention was focused? He didn’t back down just like that.

“Let’s have a drink to welcome you.”

“All together?”

“No, just the two of us.”

“I don’t feel like drinking from the first day. Let’s do it next time.”

She had refused nicely, so it could have ended with “next time,” but Yu Gwang acted like a man with a plan.

“A junior who refuses a senior’s request from the first day. You have quite some nerve?”

They were of the same White Dragon Class, but he was trying to act like a senior just because he joined sooner.

Ian glanced at Geom Mugeuk. He was looking this way with an expression that said he was enjoying the interesting spectacle with Seo Jin.

“I like women with guts.”

It was precisely then.

“Enough.”

The one who stepped forward again was Gyo Seok. He was a hall member with a smaller frame than Yu Gwang who had come out earlier. But even so, he was brave.

“Why force someone who said no?”

Yu Gwang’s expression hardened at once. The anger of being rejected turned toward Gyo Seok.

“Anyway, this bastard always picks fights in my affairs?”

Seeing him curse right off the bat, they didn’t seem to get along normally either.

“It’s not picking a fight, it’s telling you not to harass people.”

“What do you mean harass, you crazy bastard? I just asked her to drink.”

“That is harassment.”

It seemed Gyo Seok had also fallen for Ian at first sight like Yu Gwang. He hadn’t seemed like the type to step forward like this.

“You might get in trouble for acting up relying on your family background.”

From those words, one could tell that Gyo Seok, who had stepped up later, was the child of a considerably famous prestigious family.

“That again!”

“What is there to see in you besides your family background? You moron! If you’re upset, come at me!”

Geom Mugeuk and Seo Jin were enjoying this situation from a short distance away.

“It’s a mess.”

“Isn’t that a face that causes brawls? If the youths’ blood didn’t boil seeing Ian, that would be strange.”

Seo Jin agreed as well. Because she possessed looks that even another woman couldn’t help but acknowledge.

“Squad Leader Seo likes pretty women too, right?”

“How did you know?”

How did he know? Because she had told him.

The Seo Jin from before the regression had truly liked pretty women. She had said that seeing pretty things made her feel good. She liked not only beautiful women but also flowers and pretty clothes.

Seo Jin felt a strange sensation from the deep gaze directed at her.

The Young Leader’s gaze was not one of frenzy and boiling blood. Yet, looking into it, she felt an inexplicable resonance.

Seo Jin looked at Ian and asked.

“You must have known this chaos would happen. Why didn’t you have her disguise herself to look ugly?”

Then Geom Mugeuk gave an unexpected answer.

“She was born pretty, so she should live enjoying that pretty face.”

Because she had spent a long time suffering the side effects of the Complete Body Petrification Art, he wished for Ian to enjoy such things too.

“She spent her life protecting me, so she’s never experienced things like that. Men dueling and lining up because they like her. It’s a memory worth bragging about to your children later, isn’t it?”

Geom Mugeuk wished for her to continue living with her own face going forward. Instead of time spent on disguises, he would train in martial arts, guiding her to live a life where living with that face wasn’t bothersome or difficult.

Seo Jin looked at Geom Mugeuk with a momentarily incomprehensible feeling.

‘Does he really care for his subordinate down to such things?’

She still didn’t know Geom Mugeuk well.

Then, a woman among the White Dragon Class martial artists walked toward Geom Mugeuk.

“There, she’s coming to see me. See, women too—aren’t they also boiling-blooded youths?”

The woman approached right in front of the two.

“You said Geom Yeon, right?”

“It’s Geom Yeon, the yeon meaning smoke. They say I disappear like smoke, so Geom Yeon. Want to have a drink together? I don’t mind even on the first day. Oh, what’s your name? What do you like?”

He poured out words without being asked, and a slight disappointment flashed across the woman’s face. He was handsome and had a good atmosphere, but to be such a chatterbox from the first word!

The woman quietly stepped back.

“I have something today.”

When she retreated, Seo Jin teased Geom Mugeuk.

“She really disappeared like smoke.”

Geom Mugeuk made a disappointed expression.

Of course, Seo Jin knew. That Geom Mugeuk had deliberately driven the woman away.

“You pathetic bastard! Get lost!”

“You back off from the new recruit!”

Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok, who had been quarreling, seemed about to come to blows at any moment, but Geom Mugeuk simply watched with a grin as if it were none of his business.

In the end, Seo Jin stepped in. She was, after all, a squad leader—she couldn’t just sit still for the Great Leader.

“If you assault someone over a private matter, you’ll be expelled immediately.”

At Seojin’s words, Yugwang and Gyoseok looked at her.

“That’s what it said on the admission documents. I read those kinds of things very carefully.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly. She had been the same even when they lived as wandering warriors. While most wanderers entered contracts carelessly, Seojin would read them meticulously and fix any strange clauses. Her personality hadn’t changed at all.

Even at the mention of expulsion, the two did not back down.

“If a senior can’t protect a newcomer, then he deserves to be expelled!”

Just then, Ian spoke in a bright tone.

“In honor of your passionate friendship, we should have a drink.”

At the word friendship, Yugwang and Gyoseok flinched.

“Friendship?”

When Yugwang asked as if protesting, Ian gave an unexpected answer.

“You two are close, aren’t you?”

“What are you talking about?”

Ian stepped up in front of Yugwang.

“You’re actually not very good at cursing, are you?”

Yugwang twitched. When Ian brought her face close, the man who had been barking insults couldn’t even meet her eyes.

“I grew up hearing a lot of curses. I’m good at telling real curses from fake ones.”

She moved her face toward wherever Yugwang turned his gaze.

“You’re actually the nicer one, aren’t you? That’s how it looks to me.”

Yugwang averted his eyes again. Ian faced him once more, and in the end, Yugwang squeezed his eyes shut.

Gyoseok, who had been listening, confessed.

“Sorry. I asked my friend to do it. Because I liked you.”

The plan had been for Yugwang to pick a fight, and then for Gyoseok to step in like a righteous hero and save her. In truth, the two were best friends.

“How did you know?”

At Gyoseok’s question, Ian smiled.

“Your eyes were innocent, and your acting was awkward.”

Yugwang looked apologetically at Gyoseok.

“Sorry.”

“No, I’m sorry. I ruined it for no reason.”

Ian slipped between them and linked arms with both.

“You two are so cute.”

The two were startled, and their faces turned bright red.

“Yes, this is what a martial academy should be like. It has to be this pure to be a martial academy. Come on, let’s go have a drink. I have some things to ask you too.”

With her arms linked through theirs, the two were dragged away by Ian.

Everyone watching was surprised by Ian’s unexpected side. The beauty who seemed too difficult to even speak to was talking in such a friendly way.

Seojin was surprised as well. She had already known Ian had such a bright personality, but what surprised her was that Ian had realized they had planned it. Seojin had not noticed. Of course, she had been farther away than Ian, but she didn’t think she would have noticed even if she had been close.

“I still have a long way to go compared to the Captain.”

If the Captain had known, would the Young Cult Leader have known too? Seojin wondered about that.

Ian, who had been walking ahead, turned her head toward Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk smiled broadly at her and mouthed silently,

You can descend the mountain now, Ian.

Chapter 569: The Shield is Recruiting.
--------------------------------------
Yugwang and Gyoseok looked at each other, then turned their eyes forward again.

A beautiful woman they would long to see even in their dreams was sitting before them, asking a question.

“I asked if there was anything you wanted to eat.”

Yugwang answered in a trembling voice.

“I eat anything!”

Just how flustered did he have to be to speak so politely?

“You said I was your junior, didn’t you?”

When Ian asked teasingly, Yugwang quickly shook his head.

“We’re in the same White Dragon Class, so we’re all peers.”

“Right? Since we’re peers, let’s speak comfortably.”

Yugwang nodded. He did not know it. The fact that he had just become peers with the captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

“Then I’ll order what I like?”

Though she said that, Ian ordered the dishes Geom Mugeuk liked.

Yugwang was bewildered. The beautiful women he knew—no, the beautiful women he had imagined—were mysterious beings.

They spoke wordlessly with their eyes, so noble that one would not dare even step on the hem of their clothing.

But the beauty before his eyes had linked arms with them and come all the way here like a friend. She truly treated them without the slightest reserve. Where in the world could there be such a bright-natured peerless beauty?

If he were to confess honestly, he too had fallen for Ian. When Gyoseok asked him to play the villain, he had readily agreed for his friend’s sake, but inwardly, he had felt regretful.

‘I should have spoken first.’

But no. Judging by appearance, anyone could see that he was far more suited to being the villain.

Even now, the touch of her hand lingered on his arm.

Would there ever again be a moment in his life when such a beauty linked arms with him? There would not.

While Yugwang, who had taken on the villain’s role today, was lost in such thoughts, Gyoseok, who had tried to become the chivalrous hero of justice, looked at Geom Mugeuk and Seojin sitting side by side a little distance from Ian.

‘What is their relationship? Why did they come all the way here with her?’

They had naturally followed them to this inn and sat down at the same table.

As if she had read his question, Ian introduced the two of them to Gyoseok.

“They’re friends I made after coming here. As you know, that’s Geomyeon, who took the entrance exam with me, and I happened to meet Seojin at an inn.”

Geom Mugeuk waved at them and said,

“You two had a truly wonderful friendship. It made me think of the friends I left behind.”

It was not an empty remark; he meant it sincerely. All the more so because he had left the cult in a hurry this time and had not been able to see many people.

Gyoseok felt jealousy toward Geom Mugeuk. He felt it even more because he thought the other man was pretending to be at ease.

‘Who are you to be mingling with these women?’

Was being handsome everything? As if he had read that very thought, Geom Mugeuk said to him,

“I decided to become that young lady’s sword and shield.”

The faces of the two hot-blooded youths underwent the same change in expression.

At first, their expressions became, “What is this bastard saying?” and then soon turned into, “Yes, I would have done the same.”

Yugwang and Gyoseok understood Geom Mugeuk. If it was a woman like this, they too felt they might have said they would become her sword and shield. That was why they felt regretful.

‘Ah! I should have entered the academy at this time. If I had, I’d be in that spot.’

Ian smiled happily. The more she heard those words about becoming her sword and shield, the better they made her feel. Especially the part about becoming a shield lingered in her heart. After all, she had lived her whole life as the Young Cult Leader’s shield.

—I am your shield, Young Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk answered Ian’s voice transmission.

—Then who protects my shield?

—A shield is…

Ian’s mischievous gaze turned toward Yugwang and Gyoseok.

“We’re still recruiting shields!”

Yugwang’s face flushed red again. He thought that if it was for this woman, he would not mind living his whole life as a bodyguard warrior. Gyoseok, who had actually tried to play the chivalrous hero, was not quite to that extent.

“Now, have a drink from me.”

When Ian poured him wine, Yugwang’s hand holding the cup trembled violently.

“Can you even hold a sword with that hand?”

Gyoseok’s hands did not tremble, but he could not take his eyes off Ian.

Geom Mugeuk watched the sight with a pleased heart.

‘Enjoy yourself to your heart’s content, Ian.’

To Geom Mugeuk, this moment was more valuable and precious than the moment of killing an enemy.

He hoped Ian would grow while taking the lead in handling this martial academy matter. That she would learn much and feel much.

But there was something he wished for more than anything else.

‘Enjoy yourself, Ian.’

He hoped she would enjoy her youth in this place full of young people. He hoped she would receive to her heart’s content the praise for her beauty that she had never received in her life.

At that moment, Seojin sent Geom Mugeuk a voice transmission and asked,

—There’s something I want to ask you.

—Anything.

—Did you know too, Young Cult Leader? That they had planned it and acted it out?

—I knew.

—How?

From where they had been standing earlier, it had been difficult to see the two men’s expressions clearly. She could not believe he had figured it out simply by hearing their voices.

But what Geom Mugeuk had paid attention to was not them.

—From the reactions of the students who were watching.

—Ah!

Even while conversing with her, Geom Mugeuk had been observing the expressions of the other students around them.

—If he were the sort of fellow who stepped forward like that, he would surely have bullied his classmates on a regular basis, but no one frowned. On the contrary, they looked intrigued.

Seojin let out a soft sigh. Once she heard it, it was a simple answer, but at the time, it had not occurred to her at all.

—I missed both sides, then.

Ian had figured it out from those two, while Geom Mugeuk had figured it out through the people around them.

Geom Mugeuk filled the disappointed woman’s cup.

If he wished for Ian to enjoy herself, what he wished for Seojin was different.

Until now, she had been raised preciously as a blood relative of the Ghost Gate. Now it was time for her to truly step outside the greenhouse.

Seojin, now you will take your first step toward that splendid self you once became. No, in this life, I hope you become someone far more splendid than that.

—If you’re a martial artist, you have to look out of habit. Wherever you go, thoroughly observing the space and the people in it should become second nature. If it’s a place that makes you feel at ease or a place with people who look easy to deal with, you need to be even more alert. That is when martial artists die.

Seojin acknowledged her carelessness. She had unconsciously thought of this as a place full of greenhorns from the White Dragon Class. In truth, the greenhorn had been herself.

—I’ll keep it in mind, Young Cult Leader.

Even as they did so, the drinking continued.

It was mainly Ian who dealt with Yugwang and Gyoseok.

Even as the two drank, it felt like a dream. Such a beautiful woman was calling their names and treating them like friends.

Moreover, even though they had tried to deceive her, she treated them so comfortably, and they were deeply moved by it. With a few cups of wine added on top, various stories flowed out easily.

“My dream is to open a martial school in my hometown and live there.”

Yugwang’s dream had always frustrated Gyoseok.

“It sounds nice to call it a martial school, but do you know how much money it takes to open even one?”

“I can open a small one.”

“Then no students will gather. You tried so hard to enter the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy yourself, didn’t you?”

That did not mean Gyoseok’s dream was realistic.

“My dream is to make a lot of money. Once I leave this place, I’m going to become a bodyguard warrior for rich people. If I stay beside rich people, I’ll be able to learn how to make money. I’ll make a lot of money and open the martial school for you.”

At those words, Ian smiled faintly. He did not know because he had yet to experience it. How difficult a job being a bodyguard warrior was. How realistically difficult it was to learn how to make money from the rich while guarding them. And how difficult it was to spend a large sum for someone else once one had actually earned that money.

Even so, Ian liked it. At least for now, she could feel that their hearts were sincere.

“Hey, I really like you two. Playing the villain for a friend, earning money for a friend and opening him a martial school—those aren’t things just anyone can do. Come, a toast to friendship.”

Only the three of them raised their cups and drank.

It was the first time Geom Mugeuk had seen Ian talk so much since meeting her.

So when Ian became the main character, she talked this much.

That was why it was a precious day.

Of course, Ian had not made this gathering merely to play with them.

When their hearts had loosened enough, Ian asked casually,

“By the way, I heard there was an instructor who passed away not long ago?”

In that instant, the two flinched. That reaction alone told her that the two knew about Im Hyeon.

“How did you know Instructor Im died?”

At Gyoseok’s question, Ian answered as though it were no big deal.

“I heard.”

“From whom? A gag order was issued about that matter.”

It seemed they had ordered the students who knew of the incident never to speak of it.

“It seemed like everyone knew.”

She guessed that if these two knew, the others would know as well. Sure enough, they could not refute it.

“We’re friends now, aren’t we? Can’t you at least tell me that much?”

As Yugwang seriously agonized over it, Gyoseok said,

“Don’t. If it’s found out that we violated the gag order, we could be expelled from the academy. Let’s go.”

Gyoseok grabbed the wavering Yugwang and pulled him to his feet.

He hurried outside with Yugwang.

Yugwang, who had gone as far as the entrance of the inn, strode back again. Gyoseok could no longer stop him and followed behind with a sigh.

“Instructor Im died in an accident while sparring with Bang So.”

“Who is Bang So?”

“He’s in our White Dragon Class. He’s a classmate who entered shortly before Instructor Im died.”

Ian was surprised, and so was Seojin, who had been listening. It was surprising enough that Im Hyeon had died in a sparring match as they said, but she had never imagined the opponent would be a student of the White Dragon Class.

“Was Bang So at the training ground earlier?”

At Ian’s question, Yugwang shook his head.

“No. Ever since that incident, he’s been skipping classes.”

Yugwang added one more impression.

“After that happened, something changed.”

Gyoseok frowned and shook his head.

“You’re spouting nonsense again.”

“Don’t you feel it? That feeling like something has sunk down, that unpleasant feeling for no reason—don’t you feel it?”

Because Gyoseok did not believe in such feelings of his, Yugwang had really wanted to tell someone about this mood.

“Something definitely changed.”

Ian looked at him differently. Sensing a shift in the current that no one else could feel was truly an incredible talent. Though he was now trembling while receiving a cup of wine, she thought he might one day become a great master.

Ian expressed her gratitude to him.

“Thank you for telling me.”

The reason Yugwang had spoken honestly was not simply because she was beautiful. It was because he had been moved when Ian had said earlier that he was the kinder person.

People seeing them for the first time all thought Gyoseok was kinder based on appearance alone. There had also been many who regarded him as a ruffian based solely on first impressions.

But Ian had recognized him accurately.

“I was grateful too. See you tomorrow.”

At least for his final farewell, he did not tremble and left with confidence.

Gyoseok said,

“You absolutely must not say you heard it from him.”

“I promise.”

After the two left the inn, only the three of them remained there.

“Could this Bang So be those people’s underling?”

At Ian’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered,

“We’ll have to find that out. But this much is certain. Since he died in a sparring match with a White Dragon Class student, anyone would think this matter was a simple accident.”

They would think a greenhorn had mishandled his sword and killed an instructor.

Geom Mugeuk added something unexpected.

“If this Bang So is their hired blade, he’ll start attending classes tomorrow.”

“What? Why all of a sudden?”

At Ian’s question, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward Seojin.

“Because someone else has appeared whom they need to kill.”

* * *

Come now, that was absurd! Of course, she thought Geom Mugeuk had been joking.

But astonishingly, the next day, Bang So came to class.

Bang So had drooping shoulders and a gloomy expression. Since he kept his head lowered without meeting anyone’s eyes, it was hard to tell what he looked like.

When he appeared after so long, the attention of the White Dragon Class students focused on him. Everyone pitied him. What if they themselves had caused an instructor’s death? It was something they did not even want to imagine.

Yugwang and Gyoseok exchanged glances. The two were greatly surprised. He had appeared right after Yugwang had told her about him yesterday.

When they looked at Ian, she had already seen through Bang So’s true nature.

—He’s hiding his skill.

—Good eye.

Not only Seojin, but no one else noticed that hidden ability. He was a master far beyond the instructors’ level.

—Surely not? Could he really have appeared to kill Squad Leader Seo, just as you said, Young Master?

—If not? Is there any reason to have that man come back here?

—That’s idiotic, isn’t it? If a man who has killed once commits murder again, attention will be drawn to him.

—I actually think it’s a clever choice.

—What do you mean?

Geom Mugeuk pointed out the part she had not considered.

—Seojin, a blood relative of the Ghost Gate, entered the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy to investigate Im Hyeon’s death.

—That’s right.

—The person who killed Im Hyeon is Bang So. Now imagine she is killed by that Bang So.

—!

Only then did Ian understand what Geom Mugeuk meant.

Seojin, who had come to investigate the death of an elder brother from her hometown, and Bang So, who had accidentally killed an instructor. Bang So, feeling pressured by her investigation, commits murder? If afterward they dealt with Bang So as having committed suicide as well, or had him disappear, everything would be neatly resolved.

—Since they have a grudge between them, no one will try to look for another force behind it.

Pyo San was speaking with Bang So. He seemed to be checking whether he was all right.

After a long conversation, Pyo San said to the students,

“Today, we will have prearranged sparring for the first time in a while.”

The students thought he was deliberately having them practice sparring to help Bang So overcome the shock of the previous bout.

Ian’s expression turned cold.

‘These insane bastards!’

Geom Mugeuk said nothing more and left it to Ian.

‘Now, Ian. How will you deal with this?’

Chapter 570: I Survived Thanks to That Imagination
--------------------------------------------------
Ian conveyed her honest feelings to Geom Mugeuk.

—I’m afraid I’ll handle it as I please and end up ruining everything.

Geom Mugeuk knew her better than anyone. If she were alone, she would have handled the matter better than anyone, without such fear. It was burdensome because he, the Young Cult Leader, was watching. There would be pressure to do well, too.

—Ian, what was your weakness again?

—Even in this urgent situation, isn’t my weakness this carelessness of mine that wants to joke that it’s my pretty face?

—Nine tails and carelessness don’t exactly go together.

—Then what is my weakness?

Ian was curious what Geom Mugeuk thought her weakness was.

—Your weakness is me.

Ian was startled by the unexpected answer.

—You, Young Cult Leader?

—Yes, it was me. Your sense of duty and spirit of sacrifice, believing you had to protect me, were the greatest weakness in your life.

Ian’s gaze toward Geom Mugeuk trembled slightly. She knew it, too. That was why Geom Mugeuk had removed her from his guard and placed her as leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit. Of course, that was entirely from Geom Mugeuk’s perspective.

‘That sense of duty to protect you was the driving force of my life, Young Cult Leader. Even now, it isn’t very different.’

Geom Mugeuk’s sound transmission continued.

—Your second weakness was your lack of inner force. But that has been resolved as well. The third was experience, and I believe you have filled that to some extent recently by leaving the cult on your own.

Geom Mugeuk knew that what Ian needed right now was not a clue as to how to handle this matter. So what if she made a mistake? So what if she took a bit of a detour? He was protecting her. That, too, would become a great lesson for her.

What she truly needed right now was this.

—Do you know how strong you are?

With her weaknesses supplemented, she had reached great mastery of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

—How strong am I?

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned to Pyo San, standing ahead of them, then back to Bang So.

—People like that are not even worth comparing. The Seventh Master of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall? The Chief Master? Even comparing you to them is an insult to the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Even people she had yet to meet were all swept away with those words.

—If I were to speak of your strength, I would have to ask it like this.

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk said,

—Within how many moves could you kill the Master of the Yellow Dragon Hall here?

Because she had never properly fought true masters yet, she did not know how strong she was. The stronger her opponent was, the more she would come to realize how strong she herself was.

Geom Mugeuk instilled in her the greatest confidence he could.

—Ian, this martial world is yours now.

It was fine to say that to Ian. She was not someone who would grow arrogant in any situation.

Ian’s eyes trembled. It was probably the first time in her life she had heard such words.

—It’s far too burdensome. This martial world—how much will you pay for it?

Seeing her joke, he felt that her tension had eased.

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and nodded. Her eyes said,

‘Yes, Young Cult Leader. I’ll try.’

While the two were conversing by sound transmission, the instructor Pyo San personally paired them up for sparring, two at a time.

Geom Mugeuk and Ian became one pair, and as expected, Bang So was paired with Seo Jin.

Geom Mugeuk could be certain.

‘They really intend to kill her.’

Bang So, who had killed Im Hyeon, just happened to return today, and just happened to be paired with Seo Jin. There was no way such coincidences would pile up like this.

If the sparring proceeded as it was, Bang So would certainly kill Seo Jin. Unless she used ghost arts from the very beginning, Seo Jin’s current martial skill was still not enough to face Bang So.

‘Ian, what will you do?’

He had thought she would use some excuse, any excuse, to switch partners and face Bang So herself.

But for whatever reason, Ian was simply standing by and watching.

“The arranged sparring will proceed in three stages. Attack, block, and counterattack. Newcomers who haven’t learned yet are to follow the demonstration!”

Those giving the demonstration were Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok, the longest-standing members of the White Dragon Class.

“Begin carefully so that neither side gets hurt!”

Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok began the arranged sparring.
