They blocked swords thrust toward the agreed-upon points, then in turn the other thrust out a sword. In that way, sword and sword came and went between them.

When the two drew close, Yu Gwang whispered.

“I feel like something’s going to happen.”

“Shh! Be quiet!”

“I’ve got a bad feeling!”

“Please!”

At the source of that bad feeling as well, the agreed-upon movements were continuing.

Bang So was looking for an opportunity to kill Seo Jin. After killing her, he was supposed to slip out of this place.

The higher-ups would take care of the aftermath. With a fake corpse that had died from falling off a cliff, the madman of the White Dragon Class would be dealt with as dead.

‘Farewell!’

Just as he was about to kill her with his next move, Bang So’s eyes flew wide in shock.

Something was stuck to the back of the hand holding his sword.

The moment he confirmed what it was, he let out a scream without realizing it.

“Aaaaargh!”

It was a large centipede with brilliant, multicolored markings. It was not an illusion. There was even a repulsive sensation.

He was not someone who would scream at something like a centipede, but it had been too sudden. Moreover, the instant he saw those colors, he instinctively sensed it was a centipede carrying a vicious poison, which shocked him even more.

The next instant, his body flew toward Seo Jin.

‘What is this?’

His body would not obey him. An invisible force was constricting him and driving him forward.

“Aaaaaaaargh!”

At Bang So’s scream, everyone stopped moving and stared at him.

To others, Bang So appeared to be charging toward Seo Jin while letting out a bizarre shriek. To anyone watching, he looked as if the shock of the previous incident had revived and driven him mad.

Shwik!

Bang So’s sword grazed past Seo Jin’s face.

At that very instant.

Thud!

Seo Jin’s sword sank into his heart.

When Bang So, even while impaled by the sword, tried to swing his blade again, Seo Jin pulled her sword free and retreated.

Papapapak.

Bang So sprayed blood in all directions and collapsed where he stood. Until the moment he died, he could not understand this situation.

In his rapidly cooling eyes, he saw the centipede still clinging to the back of his hand burst with a pop and scatter in dazzling five-colored brilliance. It was a sight visible only to his eyes.

Several hall members screamed. Those farther away ran over, while those nearby stepped back.

Of course, the person most shocked was Pyo San. When Bang So first screamed and charged forward, he had thought it unexpected. That fellow could even put on such realistic acting?

But then he had died absurdly, impaled by Seo Jin’s sword.

Pyo San stared at Seo Jin with a shocked expression.

‘She was a master capable of killing Bang So in one move?’

In truth, the centipede stuck to the back of Bang So’s hand was Seo Jin’s ghost art.

Before the arranged sparring began, Ian had sent Seo Jin a sound transmission.

—Group Leader Seo, can you use ghost arts to make him scream?

—Yes, I think I can.

The Ghost Gate’s ghost arts could show the illusion of bugs crawling all over one’s body, and could even make the sensation feel real.

The moment Seo Jin activated her ghost art.

Ian suppressed Bang So’s body with Remote Object Manipulation and sent him flying toward Seo Jin.

She was confident she could do it, but she could not be certain it was truly possible. If Geom Mugeuk had not filled her with confidence, it was something she could not have attempted.

And there was also the final trust that Geom Mugeuk was watching.

In the end, startled by the ghost art and unable to overcome Ian’s inner force, he moved as Ian controlled him and lost his life.

Ian had turned their plan against them. They had tried to show everyone the sight of an enraged Bang So killing Seo Jin, but instead, it became Seo Jin killing Bang So because she had no choice. The enemies themselves had created a stage for revenge that could be carried out openly.

“What on earth happened?”

Pyo San asked Seo Jin, pretending to be flustered. No, he actually was flustered.

“I don’t know either. He suddenly screamed and rushed at me.”

She, too, had no need to act shocked and flustered.

She had resolved to kill him, but now that she truly had, the hand holding her sword trembled. The slash of wind from his blade as it missed and passed by her face was vivid. Of course, it had been a situation Ian staged realistically through Remote Object Manipulation.

Seeing her appear shaken, Pyo San was inwardly puzzled.

‘No matter how I look at it, it doesn’t seem like she killed him with skill.’

Then why on earth had Bang So died? At least when it came to killing people, Bang So had been someone he could trust.

In any case, he had to settle this situation first.

Pyo San calmly warned the hall members.

“Everyone, return! If any of you speak of this matter, you will be expelled from the hall immediately!”

For now, he dismissed the entire White Dragon Class.

“You stay.”

Pyo San kept Seo Jin behind.

First, he intended to take her away and lock her up, then report to Gi Seok. If the conclusion was that she absolutely had to be killed this time, they could handle it by saying she had taken her own life out of guilt while under investigation. There was still a chance.

At that moment, Ian stepped forward.

“I will go with you. I witnessed it from right beside them. My testimony will be more useful than that of my flustered friend.”

Pyo San found Ian suspicious as well. Could it really be a coincidence that such a beautiful woman entered the hall immediately after Seo Jin did?

“If you step back, I’ll call you later.”

“Before I step back, there is something I’m curious about.”

Before Pyo San could ask what her question was, Ian spoke first.

“Why did you put someone so mentally unstable together with a newcomer who only just entered?”

Pyo San’s expression hardened.

“Shouldn’t both of them have been paired with someone skilled?”

Everyone’s eyes turned toward Pyo San. They had all been too distracted by Bang So’s death to think much of it, but after hearing Ian’s words, it certainly did seem strange.

Even though she had pointed out the part that could become the greatest weakness in this incident, he remained composed.

“Are you saying I deliberately had Bang So killed?”

Of course, the hall members thought that could not be the case. There was no reason for it.

“Surely you all know that unavoidable accidents have nothing to do with skill.”

He was referring to Instructor Im’s death. It meant that even if one was paired with an instructor, a person who would die would die.

“Go back at once.”

Just as the hall members were about to turn away, someone shouted.

“There’s a white snake tattoo on this man’s chest!”

Pyo San turned around in shock, and at some point Geom Mugeuk was crouched beside the corpse, rummaging through it.

“Get your hands off him!”

Geom Mugeuk flinched in fright and withdrew his hands.

Pyo San felt as if he had been caught off guard. He had only been paying attention to this side for a very brief moment, so when had that bastard gone over to the corpse?

“It’s a coiled white snake!”

At that, Ian said,

“Isn’t the organization that uses a white snake as its symbol in the martial world the White Snake Group?”

At the words White Snake Group, the hall members began to murmur. The White Snake Group was one of the notorious contract-killing organizations in the martial world.

“Everyone, be quiet!”

Pyo San roared. The composure with which he had treated the hall members until now shattered.

Pyo San walked toward Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk retreated with a thoroughly frightened expression.

“Who told you that you could touch the corpse as you pleased? Before we even determine the cause of death, who said you could?”

“Didn’t we all see the cause of death?”

Pyo San’s expression hardened even further.

“I think what’s important right now isn’t whether or not someone touched the corpse, but figuring out why the White Snake Group is in the White Dragon Class.”

Geom Mugeuk stammered with a frightened face, but he said everything that needed to be said. And even added a ridiculous joke.

“We’re White Dragons, not White Snakes, aren’t we?”

Pyo San glared at Geom Mugeuk, then said coldly,

“You will pack your things at once and leave the martial hall! We don’t need someone like you!”

At that moment, Seo Jin was the first to step forward.

“That is an unjust measure.”

Ian also stepped forward, saying it was unjust. For her, of course, it was a situation where she naturally would.

Yu Gwang, who had drunk with them the night before, suddenly stepped forward.

“It is unjust.”

When Ian stepped forward, he had shouted without realizing it.

Pyo San roared.

“You three pack your things as well!”

Gyo Seok’s expression hardened. Yu Gwang had stepped forward before he could stop him. He agonized over it. Should he step forward or not? Yu Gwang shook his head, telling him not to.

‘You bastard! If you do that, how can I not step forward?’

Gyo Seok also stepped forward and spoke. He did step forward, but in a different way.

“My fellow students spoke without thinking. Please forgive them!”

The other hall members did not dare step forward in this atmosphere.

Rage surged up in Pyo San. This was not how things were supposed to proceed. It had gone wrong—thoroughly wrong.

“Everyone withdraw and await disciplinary action.”

Just then, a man appeared there.

“What is going on?”

The person who appeared there was none other than Yeon Baekjin.

At his appearance, Pyo San truly felt like shouting. Of course, without showing any of that, he greeted him politely and reported.

“There was an accident during sparring training.”

“Again?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk shouted.

“There was a member of the White Snake Unit in the White Dragon Class!”

Truly, Pyo San had to force himself to restrain the urge to run over and drive his sword into Geom Mugeuk’s heart.

Yeon Baekjin glanced at the fallen corpse. Even at the mention of the White Snake Unit, he was not greatly shaken. What he focused on was the opponent.

“Who was he sparring with?”

Seo Jin stepped forward and said,

“Me.”

Yeon Baekjin looked at her.

“Are you all right?”

“Yes.”

He asked nothing else.

Yeon Baekjin’s gaze turned back to Pyo San.

“It happened during your class, so you will be the first one who must undergo questioning. Follow me.”

Pyo San’s expression hardened. There was no way Yeon Baekjin had simply been passing by and stopped in by chance. If so, Bang So’s death made sense as well.

‘This man helped them.’

It was a situation that could easily lead him to such a misunderstanding.

Yeon Baekjin, who had started walking ahead, looked back at Pyo San.

“Do you intend to refuse my order?”

“No.”

Pyo San quietly followed behind him.

The two of them walked past the trainees.

The place Yeon Baekjin’s gaze slid toward was Seo Jin.

Pyo San, following behind, glared only at Geom Mugeuk.

‘That bastard made this bigger!’

The sight of Geom Mugeuk avoiding his gaze as if to say, goodness, how frightening, made him even more hateful.

‘Just you wait!’

And so the two of them left the place, and warriors who appeared to be Yeon Baekjin’s subordinates came and carried away the corpse.

“See? I was right, wasn’t I? Didn’t I say something would happen?”

At Yu Gwang’s words, Gyo Seok forcibly dragged him away. Just thinking about how his conspiracy sickness would only get worse now made his head ache.

Like that, all the trainees scattered, and only three people remained there.

Ian spoke cautiously to Geom Mugeuk.

“This was the best I could do. With my meager imagination, I couldn’t think of any other way to deal with someone who came to kill Squad Leader Seo.”

At that, Seo Jin expressed her gratitude to Ian.

“I lived thanks to that imagination.”

“It was also the first moment the Ghost Shadow Unit worked together to defeat an enemy.”

The two women looked at each other and smiled.

This was the life Geom Mugeuk had wanted for Seo Jin. For her to live in this murim, smiling alongside a good person like Ian. It was also the reason he had run all the way here with Ian.

“What are you going to do now?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, Ian seemed to ponder for a moment.

“You learned it in the arranged sparring match, didn’t you? Attack, block, and counterattack. Since we blocked the attack……”

Ian looked toward the buildings of the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy visible in the distance and said,

“Now it’s time to counterattack.”

Chapter 571: My Family Has Something to Inherit Too
---------------------------------------------------
"How will we counterattack?"

To Geommugeuk's question, Ian mentioned one person's name.

"Through Yeon Baekjin."

Ian had accurately grasped that he was at the center of this matter.

"The fact that he approached Section Leader Seo, and that he appeared here today. He definitely knows something."

Geommugeuk and Seo Jin nodded.

"First, we have to find out whether that person is an enemy or not."

Ian's gaze turned toward Seo Jin.

"And surely, Yeon Baekjin will approach Section Leader Seo again."

"I'll find out his intentions then."

"No! There's no need to go that far. That guy gives me a bad feeling somehow. The way he looks at Section Leader Seo is unusual too."

Geommugeuk could tell.

Ian had felt it too. A certain ominousness in his eyes. A premonition of catastrophe carried by the fate of those two.

Right now it was nothing more than simple fondness, but that emotion would grow beyond their control and eventually lead them both to ruin.

Ian, we have to change that fate.

Ian spoke to Geommugeuk.

"Young Sect Leader, please find out what Yeon Baekjin uncovers from Pyo San."

"I will."

To whom else could she make such a request? And who else could answer so readily?

She felt it anew. She was fighting this battle with the finest sword and shield in the martial world. That was also why this was a battle she could not afford to lose.

"In the meantime, I'll go with Section Leader Seo to Tongcheongak and the Eunwol Branch to gather the information we need. The counterattack will begin based on that information."

The reason she was going to Tongcheongak and the Eunwol Branch with Seo Jin was to show her how to make contact with them and how to receive information.

"Then see you later."

As Geommugeuk was about to leave first, Ian spoke from behind.

"Thank you, Young Sect Leader."

"What for?"

"Everything."

Ian knew better than anyone how difficult it was to watch over someone from the side like this. It would be much easier to step forward herself. But Geommugeuk was giving her a chance. A chance to gain experience and grow.

Geommugeuk was grateful for Ian, who always thanked her like this. She probably didn't know. How much power a single word from a loved one held. The fact that it was the ultimate strength that let you endure until the very end.

The power of that one word of thanks.

We know it, yet we make mistakes. We say those words several times a day to others, yet we don't say them to the very person we should for years, all because they are close.

"If you're grateful, pay me back with money!"

Geommugeuk leaped away lightly.

Watching her figure vanish in an instant, Ian spoke as if to herself.

"Then even if I went bankrupt dozens of times, I couldn't pay you back."

* * *

When Pyo San came to his senses, he was sitting in a chair with his acupoints suppressed.

He had suddenly lost consciousness while following Yeon Baekjin, and when he woke up, he was here.

'Where is this?'

The sweltering heat felt throughout his whole body, weapons hanging everywhere, scorched walls, and the furnace flames glowing red-hot like lava.

Pyo San realized. This place was none other than the forge directly operated by the Hwangnyongmugwan.

Because the martial hall had so many officers, dozens of weapons were damaged or destroyed each day. So Hwangnyongmugwan operated its own forge to supply weapons, and sold the surplus externally.

'Why was I brought here?'

There wasn't a single worker from the forge around, and one person stood before him with their back turned.

He could tell who it was at a glance by the snow-white hair.

Yeon Baekjin was looking at the weapons piled up in a corner but noticed Pyo San had woken up without turning around.

"Everything piled up here is defective. They look fine on the outside but are unusable. Later they'll be melted down in that inferno and reborn as new weapons."

Pyo San spoke cautiously.

"No matter that you are the Hall Lord's younger brother, surely you shouldn't treat an instructor this way?"

Yeon Baekjin didn't turn around, but Pyo San could feel that he was smiling coldly.

"One who tries to kill an officer is no instructor."

"Kill an officer? What do you mean?"

Pyo San retorted firmly, but if that had worked, he wouldn't have been brought here in the first place.

Yeon Baekjin turned around and approached Pyo San. He met eyes with Pyo San, who was seated and hunched over. He gazed into Pyo San's eyes with an incomparably cold gaze. The surroundings were scorching hot, yet only Yeon Baekjin felt cold.

Pyo San suddenly remembered what Giseok had said.

—Yeon Baekjin is the most calculating and ruthless person of all.

Pyo San intuited that his life was hanging by a thread.

"I want only one answer."

It wasn't about Seo Jin, nor about the deceased Im Hyeon, nor about Bangso of the deceased Baeksadan.

What Yeon Baekjin truly wanted was one location.

"Where is that place?"

"!"

Pyo San's heart sank.

'He knew. Yeon Baekjin already knew.'

Pyo San deliberately met Yeon Baekjin's eyes calmly and answered.

"I have no idea what you're talking about."

Displeasure flared openly on Yeon Baekjin's face. He didn't hide his emotions.

"I am not one to ask twice. But just this once, I will ask again. Where is that place?"

Pyo San could feel it. That if he didn't answer, he would truly die.

But he also knew. Even if he answered, he wouldn't be spared. Furthermore, his superiors wouldn't let him live after leaking a secret here.

He truly hadn't expected Yeon Baekjin to act so boldly.

With no answer, Yeon Baekjin rose without hesitation and turned away.

"In this place, the fate of unnecessary things is only one."

Yeon Baekjin's subordinates came and pulled him up.

They dragged him toward the furnace where flames shot up, as if to burn him alive.

"Wait! Great Hero Yeon! Everyone in the Baekryongban saw me being taken away by you. If you kill me, you won't be safe either!"

Yeon Baekjin stood silently with his back turned.

'He wouldn't kill me.'

But the subordinates pushed his face toward the inferno.

Pyo San felt extreme terror at the scorching heat that assailed him.

Even if they didn't kill him, his face would be completely ruined.

"Please! Don't do this! I know nothing!"

As if they weren't merely trying to scare him, the martial artists kept pushing. He desperately resisted, but how could he, with his internal energy suppressed, overcome the strength of martial artists?

"Uwaaaack!"

It was too hot. They say dying by fire is the most painful. He hadn't known his life would end burning alive like this.

It was then.

"Do you truly intend to burn him to death?"

A calm voice resonated.

At those words, the martial artists pushing Pyo San stopped.

"Uwaaaaah!"

Pyo San fell backward, away from the flames.

One side of his face was red as if half-cooked. If they had been even a little later, his face would have been completely ruined. Pyo San gasped for breath.

The one who appeared was none other than Yeon Baekjin's older brother and the Hall Lord of Hwangnyongmugwan, Yeon Baekin.

He was no ordinary person. Someone who hadn't seen him might think he was just a martial hall lord no matter how large the organization, but from him who revealed himself directly, the bearing of an absolute master could be felt.

He was Yeon Baekjin's older brother by a year, and true to their blood relation, the two resembled each other in appearance. However, the atmosphere they exuded was quite different.

Unlike Yeon Baekjin who gave off a free-spirited feeling, Yeon Baekin felt well-organized and restrained. Also, his hair was jet black.

Behind him lined up personal guards who protected the Hall Lord, and Giseok, the leader of the Chilsabu and Pyo San's superior, could be seen as well.

Even at his brother's appearance, Yeon Baekjin wasn't very surprised. He was so calm that he looked like someone who had expected Yeon Baekin to come.

At their appearance, Yeon Baekjin's subordinates all quietly retreated through the back door.

"What brings a noble person to such a shabby place?"

"What are you trying to do right now?"

The gazes of the two brothers intertwined in the air. There was so much they wanted to say to each other, but both were holding back.

"What do you mean? I was in the middle of a conversation with Instructor Pyo."

Yeon Baekjin walked toward Pyo San. He looked at the flames that had nearly burned Pyo San to death moments ago and said,

"It was a very heated conversation between men, wasn't it? Instructor Pyo."

Pyo San said nothing, but his eyes were full of resentment.

Giseok stepped forward and asked Pyo San.

"Tell the Hall Lord what happened."

Pyo San politely bowed to Yeon Baekin and recounted today's events.

"Today during the sparring match of the Baekryongban, an accident occurred where an officer died. Great Hero Yeon seized me over that matter and tried to force a confession that I was involved."

Because there were many eyes watching, he omitted any mention of the location he had asked about.

Yeon Baekin asked his younger brother.

"Is that true?"

"Everything except that the officer who died today was from the Baeksadan."

Pyo San spoke urgently.

"One cannot conclude that from a mere tattoo."

"You also failed to mention that he has a record of already killing an instructor."

At those words, Pyo San could say nothing.

Yeon Baekin spoke calmly.

"I, as the Hall Lord, will personally investigate this matter."

Then Yeon Baekjin said with a smile.

"You would."

As if implying that his brother was on the same side, Yeon Baekin's expression hardened.

"Take Instructor Pyo to the Hall Lord's residence."

The subordinates behind him took Pyo San out.

Yeon Baekin warned sternly.

"Do not interfere in martial hall affairs any further! If this happens even one more time, I will not forgive you either."

After saying one cold word, as Yeon Baekin was about to turn around, Yeon Baekjin spoke from behind.

"Brother, are you even interested in martial hall affairs?"

Yeon Baekin faltered and stopped his steps.

When he turned around, Yeon Baekjin was staring only at the furnace flames with his back turned.

Yeon Baekin looked at his younger brother for a moment, tried to say something but swallowed it again, then resumed his steps.

For a moment, Yeon Baekjin stared blankly at the fire like that.

Then, the moment he turned his head to leave that place, he flinched and stopped moving.

Someone stood in front of the defective weapons piled in the corner.

Yeon Baekjin was inwardly startled. No matter how complicated and dazed his mind was while staring at the fire, how could he not have known someone entered? If that person had tried to attack him, he could have been struck.

"Who are you?"

Then a man wearing Baekryongban martial attire turned toward him.

It was Geommugeuk. For whatever reason, she had revealed herself directly to him.

"Ah, Great Hero Yeon!"

Yeon Baekjin's gaze turned cold automatically.

"What were you doing here?"

"Ah, I was checking if there were any weapons among these that could be reused. Some are originally in good condition but were unluckily mixed in and destined to melt away, aren't they?"

Their gazes intertwined in the air.

Yeon Baekjin was convinced that Geommugeuk was no ordinary person. Moving together with a beauty like Ian, becoming friendly with Seo Jin immediately upon entering the hall, and now appearing here.

'This one deliberately revealed her presence. If not, she would have disappeared quietly. How much did she hear?'

Yeon Baekjin asked Geommugeuk.

"So? Did you find anything worth fixing?"

Geommugeuk stared at Yeon Baekjin and said.

"Just by looking, I can't tell. Most of them probably look fine at first glance, but once you try to use them, you realize they're trash after all."

Geommugeuk walked over to the flames beside where Yeon Baekjin stood.

Yeon Baekjin secretly raised his internal energy and prepared for a surprise attack.

But Geommugeuk crouched in front of the furnace. After gazing at the fire for a moment, she recalled old memories.

"I remember playing with fire with my older brother when I was young."

Yeon Baekjin looked down at her with cold eyes and merely listened.

'Full of openings.'

Everything was an opening. He felt like he could kill her with a single blow right now. The back of her head, her neck, her back. They all felt like openings.

But he felt no desire to subdue her with a single strike. He strangely didn't feel like fighting. He wasn't the type to let such a strange guy go in such a situation.

"I remember being scared that my brother might push me into the fire. That anxiety still remains with me."

At the mention of an older brother, Yeon Baekjin's expression stiffened. Clearly she had brought this up after seeing him meet his brother.

'She was there since then.'

That meant neither his brother nor the other martial artists had noticed her hiding.

"You don't seem close to your brother."

"We tried to kill each other. Our family has quite a bit to inherit."

"How much?"

"Quite a lot."

"Then your brother must be dead by now."

Flames flickered at the corners of Geommugeuk's smiling mouth.

"No. He's alive. In fact, we're quite close now."

"Then there must not have been much."

Geommugeuk spoke with a meaningful smile.

"I could teach you the secret if you wish. The way to avoid bloodshed between brothers in a power struggle! What do you say? Buy me a drink and I'll tell you."

"Not necessary."

She was clearly no ordinary person, but the nonsense was so excessive that it wasn't easy to discern her true nature.

Geommugeuk, who had been crouching, rose from her spot and stood before the original pile of scrap metal again.

"Shall we look together? Maybe there's at least one thing that doesn't need to be melted?"

He was a truly strange guy. Regardless of how strong she was, he hadn't expected someone to make such a proposal.

"Why are you so fixated on this?"

"Partly because too many things are thrown away in this world."

Geommugeuk turned to him and added.

"Isn't it unfair? To be thrown away like this when perfectly fine."

The furnace flames flickered fiercely across Yeon Baekjin's face as he stared at Geommugeuk.

"Who are you?"

Chapter 572: Even If I Go to Hell, I'll Become the Greatest Under Heaven
------------------------------------------------------------------------
“I am Geom Yeon, a new recruit of the White Dragon Class. Not the yeon as in fate, but…”

Whoosh.

Before he could even finish speaking, Yeon Baekjin launched a surprise attempt to seal Geom Mugeuk’s acupoints.

But Geom Mugeuk lightly twisted his body and avoided the attack.

“I use the yeon as in acting. Once this matter is over, I’m someone who will disappear. Forever.”

Looking at his empty hand clutching at the air, Yeon Baekjin felt a strange tremor. Was it fear? No, it was clearly an emotion different from fear.

He knew. If he had seen someone without openings as being full of them, then in the end, those openings had been his own.

“Ah, of course, I didn’t come to Hwangryong Martial Academy to learn martial arts.”

“Then why did you come?”

“To help a friend.”

“Who?”

There were two reasons Geom Mugeuk had revealed himself today.

First, he had confirmed that Yeon Baekjin stood on the side opposed to Pyo San.

Not every enemy of one’s enemy was an ally, but in this urgent situation, where the enemies were even trying to kill Guimun’s own flesh and blood, he judged that joining hands with Yeon Baekjin was not a bad choice.

The second reason, of course, was Seojin. If the wheel of fate carrying the two of them rolled all the way toward ruin, he intended to willingly climb onto that wheel with them. To do that, no one else mattered, but he had to get close to Yeon Baekjin.

“I am not someone who belongs to Guimun, but personally, I owe Guimun a great debt.”

It was an answer that he had come to help Seojin. It was not a lie. He truly did owe Guimun a great debt.

“Surely you didn’t think the Guimun Lord would let his daughter wander around alone?”

“Then that woman called Ian as well?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“We are protecting her.”

These people were protecting the daughter of the Guimun Lord?

It was believable. The day after Seojin entered, the two of them enrolled. After that, they had been going around together.

“I showed myself because I thought the target of Seojin’s revenge and Great Hero Yeon’s enemy might be the same person.”

Geom Mugeuk took out another weapon from the pile and handed it to him.

“Do you feel like searching together now?”

Yeon Baekjin threw the sword back into the heap of scrap iron.

“I refuse.”

His gaze turned toward the new weapons hanging on the wall.

“So many new ones pour out every day. There’s no need to rummage through scrap metal.”

Even if what the other man said was true, he could not simply trust him at face value.

Yeon Baekjin turned and left the place.

“If you’re a new recruit, don’t be late to class.”

As Yeon Baekjin walked toward the entrance, one thought suddenly came to him.

‘But did he hear even the part where I pressed Pyo San about where that place is?’

Yeon Baekjin looked back.

But Geom Mugeuk was nowhere to be seen, as if he had vanished at some point.

* * *

“Great Hero Yeon has noticed the existence of that place.”

Pyo San’s burned face throbbed with heat, but he did not even have the leisure to apply medicine.

“Given Great Hero Yeon’s personality, he’ll keep digging.”

Yeon Baekin, the master of Hwangryong Academy, merely stood with his hands clasped behind his back, silently looking out the window.

Giseok, their chief, shook his head to tell him not to say any more, but Pyo San could not hold back and added one last remark.

“As long as Great Hero Yeon knows that you will forgive him no matter what he does, Master, he will keep trying.”

Truly, if he had been alone with Giseok, curses would have come out first.

Yeon Baekin let out a light sigh, then turned toward Pyo San.

“Yes, I suppose he will.”

Then he walked toward Pyo San with a concerned expression.

“Your burns are severe. Let me see. Are you all right?”

“I’m fine. Thank you for your concern—”

Crack.

At Yeon Baekin’s fingertips, Pyo San’s neck twisted at an unnatural angle, then returned to its original position.

It was a move so swift that before he could even register it, his cervical bones snapped, and he collapsed where he stood.

Looking down at the corpse, Yeon Baekin let out a low, anger-filled groan. Mmm, the rage that seeped out with that sound was not directed solely at Pyo San.

Giseok, on the other hand, showed no reaction even at the death of his own limb.

In this situation, now that Yeon Baekjin had become involved in earnest, he deemed it appropriate to remove him. Only, now that the lips were gone, he would have to grit his chilled teeth.

“You said the excavation is in its final stage?”

At Yeon Baekin’s question, Giseok answered respectfully.

“Yes. They say they have almost broken through. They will find the entrance soon.”

Passion flashed through Yeon Baekin’s eyes. Yes, they had to find it. For that moment, he had become an academy master who twisted an instructor’s neck.

“We’re nearly there now.”

Yeon Baekin looked down once more at Pyo San’s corpse, then gazed far out the window.

“Yes. Hell Gate must be waiting for us.”

At Yeon Baekin’s self-mocking reply, Giseok bowed his head respectfully.

“Even if you go to hell, you will go as the greatest under heaven.”

* * *

At the inn, Geom Mugeuk was drinking alone as he waited for Ian and Seojin to return.

Yeon Baekjin strode in to where he was sitting and placed an old iron sword on the table.

“There was one sword worth keeping.”

He had gone to the scrap heap piled up in the smithy and found a usable sword.

Geom Mugeuk looked up at him. He felt different from when they had parted earlier. Compared to then, he seemed a little more agitated.

“Surely you didn’t really go back and look for it?”

“I found it myself.”

“I wouldn’t have known even if you’d bought it on the way here.”

Yeon Baekjin snorted. It was a moment that offered a slight glimpse into his character.

“Why did you change your mind?”

It was because of one person’s death.

“Pyo San is dead. They say he hanged himself.”

Yeon Baekjin knew. His brother had killed him. It was the moment when his wavering suspicion turned into certainty.

“What does he take me for?”

Despite the shocking news, Geom Mugeuk remained calm.

“Did you expect it, by any chance?”

“I wouldn’t say I expected it, but it isn’t surprising. They are people who tried to kill even Guimun’s own blood. Cutting off one exposed limb wouldn’t be a problem for them. Now, please sit. If you haven’t eaten, have a meal as well.”

Yeon Baekjin sat down. When he had come here, his thoughts had been complicated and he had not been certain. But once he actually saw Geom Mugeuk, he felt he had done well to come. This young fellow had a strange way of putting one at ease.

“Just a cup of wine.”

Geom Mugeuk poured him wine.

“One more.”

After emptying three cups in succession, he revealed an unexpected fact.

“Instructor Im came to see me before he died.”

The reason Yeon Baekjin had become entangled in this matter was none other than Im Hyeok.

“He said something strange seemed to be happening inside the academy.”

Yeon Baekjin conveyed exactly what Im Hyeok had told him.

The first time Im Hyeok took notice of this matter was two years ago.

There had been an extremely talented student he had personally taught since the White Dragon Class two years earlier. He was skilled, and he had a strong desire for success.

At that rate, he was naturally certain to pass the Hwangryong Class graduation exam, but one day, he came to him and said he was quitting the martial academy, offering his farewell. He said that at least he wanted to say goodbye to his instructor.

Im Hyeon tried to stop him, saying that if he endured just a little longer, he would definitely be able to graduate, but the student left, saying there was something he absolutely had to do.

At that moment, he sensed an indescribable yearning in the student’s eyes. What was it that he had tried to gain by throwing away all the time he had spent suffering until then?

That incident passed, and this year, something similar happened again. This time as well, it was a student whose passing of the graduation exam was guaranteed, and in him, he saw again the yearning he had seen in the eyes of that student back then.

No matter how he thought about it, it was strange, so he began to investigate in secret. And he discovered that in the last three years, many students who were fully capable of passing the graduation exam had quit.

Because so many people attended the academy, no one had paid attention to this fact. If they said they were quitting, what more needed to be said? They must have had their own circumstances.

“He came to me saying it was strange no matter how he looked at it. He asked me to look into it. To be honest, even when I heard him say that, I didn’t think it was all that strange.”

He emptied his cup again.

“Two days after he came to see me, Instructor Im died.”

Yeon Baekjin’s gaze, which had been on his empty wine cup, turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“That was truly strange.”

Around that time, he had been feeling that his elder brother, the academy master, had changed greatly from before. He had somehow become sensitive and cold.

Even so, he had not thought that his brother was involved in killing an instructor. But today, with Pyo San’s death, it became clear. His brother had been involved in Im Hyeon’s death as well.

“That place you asked about when you were pressing Pyo San earlier—what does it refer to?”

“So you did hear it.”

He had come here already intending to tell him everything.

“I found out after secretly investigating the academy’s finances. Money had been spent buying materials needed for a large-scale construction project.”

That was why he had pressed Pyo San about where the place was. To find out where, exactly, and what kind of construction was taking place.

“That is all I know.”

“Thank you for telling me.”

Thanks to that, he finally learned why Im Hyeok had lost his life. And he had also obtained precious information that some secret construction project was underway.

Yeon Baekjin silently emptied his wine cup. It was guilt he did not necessarily need to carry, but Geom Mugeuk could sense that he felt some sense of debt over Im Hyeon’s death.

The Yeon Baekjin of now was not an evil man. No matter how he looked at him, he was not an evil man pretending to be good either.

Why had such an ordinary person walked that path? When Seojin took her revenge, his brother had already been dead as well. At the time, he had naturally assumed Yeon Baekjin had killed him.

Why did you, such a man, walk the path of fate where you killed your brother and even the woman you loved?

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze lowered to the iron sword Yeon Baekjin had brought. In any case, one thing was certain. The moment he chose this sword and brought it here, the wheel of fate must have begun to turn slightly in another direction.

Slowly drawing the iron sword, Geom Mugeuk said,

“You found a good one. This sword is now fated not to melt in a furnace.”

* * *

The mechanical lift was descending endlessly underground.

Yeon Baekin was riding alone in the space, which was just large enough for one person.

He was staring at his own face reflected in the metal of the mechanism. A face that had become unfamiliar at some point.

Thud.

The stopped mechanism’s door opened.

A martial artist was waiting in front of it. A familiar face. He was a man from Hwangryong Martial Academy.

“Please step only where I step and follow me.”

Yeon Baekin walked after him.

Lines were drawn all over the floor like a Go board, and each was painted a different color.

Yeon Baekin stepped where the guide stepped.

Along the way, sections of the walls had been torn open here and there. The complicated devices visible inside were terrifying mechanisms.

Some had already been completely dismantled, while others remained as they were, so stepping on the wrong part of the floor could cost one’s life.

There were places they had to cross on a single rope, and there were even places where they had to climb up, only to slide back down.

There were places where they had to crawl through small passages, and they even had to swim through water. It was truly a maze that could never be navigated without a guide. He had come several times over the past three years, but the paths changed as well, so he was confused every time he came.

And then they arrived at the final space.

In that wide place like a plaza, those who had once been students of Hwangryong Martial Academy were working diligently.

There were those raising sword energy to cut into the walls, those looking down at blueprints and busily writing something, and those carrying boxes.

They had not been dragged here. Every one of them had chosen this of their own will.

They were being paid a monthly sum that could not be compared to the jobs they might have chosen after leaving the academy. Once this work was over, it was an amount that would let every one of them fulfill their dreams.

And there he was.

The man who had changed not only his own life as the master of Hwangryong Academy, but the lives of everyone here.

The man’s first words when he came to him had been this:

—You’ll have to dig some ground with me.

He was a very handsome middle-aged man with a genial impression. There was ease in his eyes, and mischief clung to the slightly upturned corners of his mouth.

“Careful there!”

The man had his hair tied tightly back, his upper body bare, and was running here and there barefoot.

The countless scars on his body told what kind of days he had lived through.

“I said, be careful there!”

The instant the man’s shout burst toward one young man—

Whoooooosh!

A massive lump of iron several times the size of a person fell toward his head.

Craack! Kwaaang!

Yeon Baekin thought that since the lump of iron was so huge, they would not even be able to find traces of the corpse.

Fwooooosh.

When the rising dust settled, everyone stared at the spot in astonishment.

The massive lump of iron had been split in two, and between the halves stood two people.

The man was standing beside the young man.

When he had shouted, he had been quite far away, yet in an instant he had flown there and cut the lump of iron in half.

The cut surfaces were so clean and smooth they seemed to shine. Astonishingly, the man had cut this enormous lump of iron without emitting sword energy.

Nor had he cut it with a treasured sword. The sword in the man’s hand was an old iron sword one could find anywhere in the marketplace.

At the man’s divine might, not only Yeon Baekin but everyone there revealed awe. The move he had shown was truly the pinnacle of swordsmanship.

The man lightly struck the young man on the back of the head.

“Were you trying to die before even spending the money?”

“I’m sorry. Thank you for saving me.”

The young man bowed deeply in thanks. The other young men also expressed their gratitude. There was not a single one among them who had not received this man’s help at least once.

“Everyone, clear this first!”

“Yes!”

The young men gathered to move the lump of iron. They raised sword energy and began cutting the iron into pieces. When they had first entered this place, most of them had been unable to raise sword energy, but now all of them handled it skillfully.

Only after standing there for quite some time and watching over the cleanup of the iron lump did the man turn toward Yeon Baekin.

“It’s been a while since you came.”

The man brushed off the dust covering his body with his hands and walked over with a bright smile.

“Did something interesting happen in the outside world?”

Chapter 573: A Demon More Fearsome Than a Ghost Has Followed Us
---------------------------------------------------------------
When I first saw him, I thought he was a madman.

He came wearing a shabby martial arts uniform and an old iron sword at his waist, and abruptly suggested we dig into the ground together.

But he wasn't mad. Not the slightest hint of madness could be felt from the man.

'Then has he come to kill me?'

Did his suggestion to dig together mean he intended to bury me there?

"Where exactly are you suggesting we dig?"

Even as he asked the question naturally, Yeon Baekin launched a surprise attack at the man.

In the martial world, there were many martial artists who refused to acknowledge instructors or masters of martial halls. Some even mocked him, saying he swindled money from snot-nosed brats. He had worked harder than anyone knew to avoid hearing such words.

Through endless effort, he had reached the pinnacle in the world of martial halls.

Shhhk.

It wasn't a position he had attained easily.

Whish.

In the next moment, Yeon Baekin stared at the man with a blank face. To be precise, he looked at his own sword held in the man's hand.

The problem wasn't that his sword had been snatched away. The problem was that he had no idea how it had been taken.

The man's hands or wrists should have been restrained somehow. But they were perfectly fine. Yeon Baekin hadn't even felt the slightest touch, yet his sword was gone.

With such skill, the man could have killed him, or proceeded with threats.

But the man turned the sword around and offered him the hilt.

"You have quite the temper."

Looking back, this very moment was when the first button of their relationship had been fastened.

Because of that single, simple act—a powerhouse who could have subdued his blood points or threatened to snap his neck, instead turning the sword and offering him the hilt.

"In life, shouldn't there be a day when good fortune comes knocking on its own at least once?"

Just as he had smiled brightly at him that day, the man was smiling the same way today.

The man approached Yeon Baekin's side, washed his face, hands, and feet in the water trickling down from the ceiling, and even rinsed his mouth.

"This dust is something else. By the way, have you eaten?"

"I have."

After washing up briefly, the man lifted the cloth covering the corner. The basket was piled full of rice balls.

The man devoured the rice balls.

"Is that enough for you?"

"There are also days when I eat meat and drink my fill of wine."

When this work had begun, Yeon Baekin had tried to provide every comfort. He had offered to furnish the room splendidly, bring courtesans to comfortable sleeping quarters, and prepare feasts for every meal. But the man had refused. These were his words at that time:

"One must be somewhat uncomfortable and lacking to grow stronger faster."

He had confirmed just moments ago that those words were true. He saw it in the hall members wielding sword qi freely.

They were no longer greenhorn hall members, but proper martial artists.

They had learned things in this cramped, uncomfortable space that they couldn't learn in the wide training grounds outside.

And more than anything, those words applied most of all to this man.

Because he could feel that the man had grown stronger than when he first came to him.

Eating the same food as the hall members and sleeping in the same place, perhaps the man had even made this place his own training ground.

While the man ate the rice balls, Yeon Baekin recalled the day they first met.

"Deep beneath the Hwangryong Martial Hall, there is a place where treasure and peerless divine arts are stored."

A master of this level wouldn't speak nonsense. Naturally, one question arose.

"With your skill, you could kill me and seize the Hwangryong Martial Hall."

"That's right. But if the master of the Hwangryong Martial Hall changes, the eyes of the martial world will focus here. I wish to proceed with this matter quietly."

"Can you guarantee you won't kill me and silence the witnesses after the work is done?"

"There is none. You have no choice but to trust me."

At first, he had joined hands with him out of necessity. To refuse meant death.

At first, he hadn't trusted him. He had pretended to trust him while looking for a way to survive.

And now?

Yeon Baekin looked at the man. Over the past days, the man had shown through actions, not words, that he was someone worth trusting.

Did he trust him? Yes, he did.

But still, when he thought of the moment he would discover the door he sought beyond that wall, his heart beat like mad.

"The peerless divine art there shall be yours."

"Why do you not want the secret manual?"

"What does it matter what I learn? I'll be stronger regardless."

The man cackled as he spoke. These were the words of a man who had snatched away the Hwangryong Hall Master's sword in a single move, without him even knowing how.

"What I want lies elsewhere."

What could it be that he wanted?

Would he look at him the same way even in the treasure-filled chamber? Would he spare him, and spare all those hall members as well?

Or would that iron sword be drawn, killing everyone here in a single move?

Whether this encounter with the man would become a nightmare or a stroke of fortune, the result was not far off.

The man who had finished his rice balls washed his hands in the flowing water. Though he dressed in shabby clothes, he was a clean and tidy person.

"Why have you come down?"

"I heard the excavation is nearing its end, so I came down. And to see your face after some time."

The man, finished washing, turned toward Yeon Baekin. Under the man's gaze fixed upon him, Yeon Baekin felt a crushing pressure that gripped his heart. From the first day they met, he had been unable to lie while looking into this man's eyes.

"You must be troubled over your younger brother."

In that moment, Yeon Baekin knew. The man knew everything happening outside. That he was worrying over his younger brother.

Yeon Baekin tensed inwardly when the man brought up his brother. His brother was trying to find this place.

"The situation has grown complicated with the blood kin of the Ghost Gate entering the hall."

At that, the man spoke as though he knew Seo Jin was here as well.

"The problem is not the little ghost of the Ghost Gate."

"What do you mean?"

"A demon far more fearsome than a ghost has attached itself to her."

"Who are you referring to?"

The man merely smiled an inscrutable smile without telling him who.

"Our hall master is struggling with one little ghost, so I suppose I must go up."

Yeon Baekin was startled by his words. Just who had come?

"Don't do that. Shouldn't you remain at your post at such a critical time?"

At that, the man shouted.

"Let us rest as well!"

Yeon Baekin was bewildered by the truly unexpected words.

All the hall members looked this way with expressions saying 'Exactly!'

"Of course you should rest."

Looking at the flustered Yeon Baekin, the man put on a sly smile as if amused. He could tell the man was joking.

"The kids are exhausted. To break through the final gate, we'll need new personnel as well."

"How many do you need? I will select and send some excellent ones."

But for some reason, the man refused.

"There's no need. This time, I will choose and bring them myself."

Then the man shouted to the hall members.

"Come and eat!"

The hall members rushed over and took the rice balls.

"Wash your hands before eating! Tsk, you filthy lads!"

At the man's cry, some washed their hands, while some took the rice balls with a laugh. Perhaps because they had suffered together, the mood was truly good.

Some of them were hall members Yeon Baekin knew well. Being with this man, their eyes and auras had changed completely, as if they were different people.

The man shouted to them.

"I'll be out for a few days. Rest well in the meantime! Prepare to receive the newcomers."

The hall members cheered in joy.

The man, who had been going barefoot, put on his shoes and walked briskly toward Yeon Baekin.

"Let us go up."

"Very well."

As they walked to the mechanism that led upward, the man asked.

"Is this place important? Or is your brother important?"

Yeon Baekin couldn't answer. Because he didn't want to give this man justification.

"You didn't choose your brother?"

Yeon Baekin let out a sigh. He had already answered, for all intents and purposes. He should have said his brother was important immediately.

The man gave Yeon Baekin's back a light slap.

"You should have at least said it was your brother."

Yeon Baekin couldn't say a word.

The man spoke calmly.

"Do not worry. I will resolve this favorably."

Yeon Baekin looked at the man.

Just looking at his profile set his mind at ease. Even if he were to die, in this moment, there was a unique sense of trust this man gave.

And it was likely this side of the man that had kept fastening the buttons of their relationship.

* * *

"What kind of person is the Young Sect Leader?"

At Seo Jin's question, I An turned her head to her. The two women were on their way back after stopping by the Tongcheon Pavilion and the Eunwol Branch.

Just by the words and gazes with which Geom Mugyeok and I An treated each other, one could tell. How deep the affection they held.

Therefore, wasn't I An the person who knew the Young Sect Leader best? Seo Jin thought.

I An expressed Geom Mugyeok in exactly one word.

"Dream. He is truly like a dream."

"A dream?"

"He is someone who makes me dream of becoming like him."

If there was a good dream, there was also a bad one.

"He is also someone who makes me feel uneasy—that all of this might be a dream when I wake up one day."

Just as he had suddenly changed one day, wouldn't he suddenly return to how he was? Such a vague fear existed in her heart.

It was a fear she hadn't spoken of to Seo Daeryong or Jang Ho, yet it was the first time she brought it out to Seo Jin.

"What if it is a dream? As long as you don't wake until you die."

At those words, I An smiled. She was glad Seo Jin had entered as a team leader. They were both women, and they got along well. Cheongmyeon had been relatively difficult to deal with after all.

"Anyway, just be prepared to be surprised. Because he shows amazing sides every time."

Then Seo Jin spoke.

"I think I'm starting to understand a little already."

Inside the inn, Geom Mugyeok and Yeon Baekjin were drinking wine.

The usually stiff-looking Yeon Baekjin was talking with Geom Mugyeok with a flushed face. He didn't look guarded at all.

"Well, that's nothing much."

The two women entered the inn.

They sat together at the table where Geom Mugyeok and Yeon Baekjin were seated.

"We're back."

After greeting Geom Mugyeok, they greeted Yeon Baekjin as well.

Geom Mugyeok spoke to the two women.

"I was honest with Brother Yeon. I told him we followed to protect Seo Jin."

That was enough to explain what kind of relationship they had told Yeon Baekjin.

Yeon Baekjin's gaze turned to Seo Jin.

He had learned of this matter when Im Hyeon came to him—a conversation he should have had with her, but had already had with Geom Mugyeok. Since there was no need to repeat it, he rose from his seat.

This was also why Geom Mugyeok had revealed himself before him. Because he did not wish to become deeply involved with her. Because he hoped this connection would pass by like a fleeting wind.

Geom Mugyeok spoke to Yeon Baekjin as he tried to leave.

"Try speaking with your brother."

Yeon Baekjin was startled by the unexpected words.

He stared at Geom Mugyeok for a moment, recalling what he had said before.

"You said if I bought you a drink, you'd teach me how brothers could avoid bloodshed in a power struggle?"

"I did."

"Surely the solution isn't simply talking to my brother?"

Is that all you're saying the method is? Such emotions showed on his face.

"Yes, that's right."

Yeon Baekjin scoffed. No matter how much he thought about it, this wasn't something that could be resolved through conversation.

"I can't buy you that drink yet."

Geom Mugyeok spoke to him as he turned to leave.

"There may seem to be countless ways for martial artists to resolve conflicts, but most are resolved by one of two things."

"And those are?"

"Words and swords."

Geom Mugyeok added calmly.

"Whether it works or not, meet him first. The more hopeless a relationship seems, the more unexpectedly well it may turn out."

* * *

The next day, the atmosphere in the White Dragon Class was gloomy.

Already two people had met their deaths in the White Dragon Class. Added to that were ominous rumors that the instructor, Pyo San, had committed suicide.

"They said a temporary instructor is coming before the new instructor is assigned today."

As the hall members talked about who the temporary instructor would be, Yu Gwang was trembling with anxiety.

"I feel like something even bigger is going to happen."

Now, Gyo Seok spoke as if it were no big deal.

"Who dies this time? Me? You?"

"I'm not joking."

"Neither am I. So what? Should I quit the hall and go back to my hometown?"

"Sorry."

Yu Gwang felt sorry toward Gyo Seok. When feeling anxious, he should just swallow it. There was no need to make others anxious too.

Then someone spoke from behind.

"It's better than not knowing when something ominous will happen, isn't it?"

It was I An. I An was grateful to Yu Gwang for bravely stepping forward yesterday, and she believed this talent of his was an instinct essential for surviving as a martial artist.

"I think it would be good to properly cultivate that instinct instead."

Coming from none other than I An, Yu Gwang was moved.

"About that shield you mentioned back then..."

Gyo Seok covered Yu Gwang's mouth.

"Don't."

He stopped him because Yu Gwang was about to ask if they were still recruiting for that shield.

"I won't! I said I won't! Do I look that stupid?"

"Exactly that much! No, even more!"

I An smiled at the two. She truly wanted to take them to the Ghost Shadow Corps. Of course, that was impossible. She could not have these simple, honest fellows join the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

Then the noisy area fell silent.

"There, the new instructor is coming."

Someone was walking this way.

It was a man with his hair tied tightly, wearing a shabby martial uniform and carrying a single iron sword, yet his atmosphere was bright.

He had the brightest expression of any instructor or master seen here.

The moment he saw him, Geom Mugyeok knew.

Why Yeon Baekjin had no choice but to walk such a fate.

It wasn't because of his brother.

His brother too was ultimately a victim.

Because the man before his eyes was one of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings.

And not just any of them.

The First King among the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings.

The Sword King, Akgunhak.

Although the era's Three Absolutes had died and Hwamugi had gone into seclusion, he had ascended to the position of the world's strongest during the era of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings. He was truly the strongest martial artist.

Geom Mugyeok calmed his surging heart and steadied himself by gripping the hilt of the Black Demon Sword. Lest it cry out on its own in response to the man's energy. Though he had met many of the Twelve Earthly Branch Kings until now, he had never been as tense as he was now.

The Sword King, having arrived before the hall members, introduced himself to everyone.

"Until a new instructor is assigned, I will be your temporary Instructor Ak."

The Sword King spoke to the hall members with a bright smile.

"Now then, shall we start with introductions, one by one?"

Chapter 574: Who Knew Martial Halls These Days Were This Good
-------------------------------------------------------------
The Sword King’s gaze was gentle.

The trainees liked their first impression of this new instructor.

Was it because of the ease in his expression? Or was it because of his shabby martial robes, his iron sword, and the hair tied back behind him? There was something free-spirited about him.

How to put it? He looked like the sort of man who would roam the Central Plains with a single sword at his waist, savoring his freedom.

And that was why Geom Mugeuk grew even more tense.

Because he knew very well what level that sense of freedom and ease came from.

Geom Mugeuk took one step forward, moving in front of Ian and Seojin.

He positioned the two women behind him, to his left and right.

If he had been alone, he would not have been this tense. But this place was full of trainees, and more than anyone, Ian and Seojin were here.

Was there any guarantee that the Sword King would not attack him here and now?

Considering everything that had happened with the force behind the scenes until now, nothing that occurred here would be strange in the slightest.

Ian’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk’s back. Ever since the temporary instructor had appeared, he had not taken his eyes off him.

She had watched the Young Cult Leader since childhood. Now, she felt as if she could tell his mood just from the way he stood. And right now, she sensed that Geom Mugeuk was somehow tense.

‘He’s truly someone who couldn’t be further from tension.’

Naturally, Ian’s gaze turned toward the newly arrived instructor.

She only felt that he was clearly different from the instructors in the martial academy, who all gave off similar impressions. She could not sense any particularly special aura from him.

If Geom Mugeuk was truly tense because of that instructor, then it meant he was an expert far above her own level.

‘Surely not? Could he be an expert from the White Snake Corps?’

Since she had never imagined that the hidden force Geom Mugeuk had been dealing with until now would appear here, she wondered if the opponent might be from the White Snake Corps.

“Now, you introduce yourself first.”

The trainee closest to the Sword King stated his name first. The Sword King asked the trainees all sorts of questions. How old are you? Why did you enter the martial academy? What will you do after you graduate? Do you have a girlfriend?

If one only looked at him like this, he was unmistakably an instructor of the martial academy. The sort of person who took great interest in other people’s business and meddled in everything.

Then it became Geom Mugeuk’s turn.

“I’m Geom Yeon.”

There was no theatrics in today’s name introduction.

Seeing that, Ian was certain her feeling had been correct. If it had been the usual Young Cult Leader, he would certainly have said that it was not the Yeon of bonds, but the Yeon of acting.

This was the decisive reason Geom Mugeuk was tense.

‘He came here knowing me.’

How could it be coincidence that he and one of the Twelve Zodiac Kings had met? And not just any of the Twelve Zodiac Kings, but the First King.

The problem was that the Sword King had come forward openly. If he had known Geom Mugeuk was here, he could have moved in secret. Just as most of the Twelve Zodiac Kings had done until now.

But the Sword King had stepped proudly before him.

Because he thought Geom Mugeuk wouldn’t recognize him?

Impossible. No matter how much he hid his aura, how could one fail to recognize that presence? This was the Sword King’s confidence. A declaration that they should clash properly.

The Sword King, who had been staring at Geom Mugeuk, called out in delight.

“Hey! So it’s you!”

To Geom Mugeuk, those words sounded as if he were saying this:

So I’ve finally met the bastard who’s been giving our organization so much trouble until now!

Of course, the Sword King gave a different reason.

“The rumors are already everywhere in the martial academy. They say there’s a new recruit who goes around with two beautiful women. You enviable bastard!”

He truly looked envious. At that, the trainees watching from all around burst into laughter. It was their first time seeing an instructor openly envy someone over the beauty of female trainees. They had already begun lowering their guard toward this temporary instructor.

Geom Mugeuk tapped his own chest twice and said boastfully,

“That’s right. I’m that handsome new recruit.”

“Let me have a closer look, then. Let’s see just how handsome you are.”

The Sword King walked toward Geom Mugeuk.

At that moment, the Heavenly Demon Body-Protecting Art sensed Geom Mugeuk’s tension and activated on its own.

Even as he approached, step by step, the Sword King revealed no aura whatsoever.

Coming toward him with a broad smile, he truly looked like an instructor who enjoyed joking around with his disciples.

The Sword King came right up in front of Geom Mugeuk’s nose.

They were now at a distance where, if either drew his sword, he could cut the other’s throat.

Geom Mugeuk’s heart pounded.

His opponent was the Sword King.

The Sword King took one more step closer even from there.

Then he slowly raised his hand and extended it toward Geom Mugeuk.

At that sight, Ian’s heart, standing behind him, pounded as if it would burst even more than Geom Mugeuk’s.

If the two of them began fighting fiercely, what should she do? Should she help the Young Cult Leader? Or should she trust the Young Cult Leader, take care of Seojin, and retreat?

The hand extended toward Geom Mugeuk reached its destination.

Tap.

The Sword King placed his hand on Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder.

“You’re handsome.”

Until then, Geom Mugeuk had stood still in place. Rather than an intense fight, doing nothing and watching him place his hand on his shoulder was even more difficult. Since he had no idea when or how the attack might come, the strain on his mind had reached its limit.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“You’re more impressive, Instructor.”

“I am?”

“Usually, people burn with jealousy. But you’re telling me I’m handsome.”

At that, the Sword King came even closer to Geom Mugeuk. Geom Mugeuk silently stared as the man’s two eyes drew nearer. This time was the same. Enduring this without revealing the slightest aura was even harder.

The Sword King whispered by Geom Mugeuk’s ear.

“To be honest, I’m jealous. I’m a man too.”

Then he looked at Geom Mugeuk and grinned.

The Sword King was different from what he had imagined. Far too different.

‘So the Sword King was this kind of person.’

He surely knew as well. That Geom Mugeuk did not see him as an ordinary instructor. That was why this behavior of his was all the more remarkable.

Even then, the Sword King’s hand remained on Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder. The moment even the faintest trace of true qi was felt at his fingertips, a mad battle would erupt.

The Sword King’s gaze turned toward Ian, who stood behind Geom Mugeuk.

When their eyes met, Ian bowed her head in greeting and stated her name.

“I’m Ian.”

The Sword King removed his hand from Geom Mugeuk’s shoulder and this time took one step toward Ian.

Geom Mugeuk turned along with him.

‘If you cut Ian down, you die too.’

But he would not take such a risk with Geom Mugeuk behind him. He, too, must know what level the Young Cult Leader’s abilities were at.

As expected, the Sword King did not attack her. On the contrary, he looked at her and exclaimed in admiration.

“Remarkable!”

It seemed as if he were admiring her appearance, but Geom Mugeuk knew.

‘He saw through Ian’s achievement.’

In other words, he had instantly seen through Ian’s martial realm, after she had achieved great completion in the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

Even though she had shown no sign of tension at all and had thoroughly concealed her aura, she could not deceive his eyes.

The Sword King stared intently at Ian, then this time looked at Seojin.

“I’m Seojin.”

There was no lust or desire in the Sword King’s gaze as he looked at the two women.

That was what made it even more of a problem. The Sword King returned to his original place and called them out.

“Ian, Seojin. Come forward!”

This was a clear provocation aimed at Geom Mugeuk.

After all, it was perfectly natural for an instructor to tell trainees to come forward.

Now, what will you do?

Ian, who had taken one step forward, looked at Geom Mugeuk.

From the way Geom Mugeuk looked at the instructor, Ian could now tell for certain.

‘That man is an expert who can make the Young Cult Leader this tense!’

She sensed that Geom Mugeuk was worried. Worried that they might be put in danger.

Seojin, on the other hand, did not know how serious the situation was, so she looked at Ian with a gaze that said they should go forward together.

At that moment, one of Ian’s nine tails unfurled.

She grabbed Geom Mugeuk’s wrist.

When Geom Mugeuk looked at her in surprise, Ian pulled him along and went forward.

“Let’s go together.”

Seojin followed behind them. She, too, had instinctively sensed that something unusual was happening.

The Sword King watched this with a strange smile.

“I believe I told only the two of you to come out?”

Ian said to him,

“There’s a reason I brought this friend with us.”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“This friend promised to be the sword and shield at our side for the rest of his life.”

Originally, he had said he would become a sword and shield for her, but now she said “our.” Right now, they were in a situation where Seojin had to be protected as well.

The trainees, hearing those words for the first time, grew noisy. Most of them frowned rather than laughed, and they jeered rather than applauded.

“If you’re a martial artist, you should carry a sword and shield wherever you go.”

Even as she spoke, Ian’s heart was pounding wildly.

She had thought of it on the spot, so she had no idea whether this method would work or not. Even so, it was the method she had chosen because she wanted to help Geom Mugeuk somehow.

The Sword King walked up to Ian.

“What will you do if that shield gets smashed apart?”

A brief silence passed. Soon, Ian smiled brightly and answered,

“That is why we are always recruiting shields.”

The Sword King stared blankly for a moment, then burst into loud laughter.

“Good, that’s amusing. Is there an age limit on that shield, by any chance?”

Since the joke carried the meaning of whether he himself could join, the trainees burst into laughter.

“All three of you may go back.”

Perhaps accepting her wit, the Sword King allowed them to return.

They were about to go back to their original positions when the Sword King called Geom Mugeuk.

“You said your name was Geom Yeon, right?”

“Yes.”

“There are two women, but only one shield. What will you do if both of them are attacked at the same time?”

He asked it like a joke, but it was a meaningful question. If these two women were attacked at the same time, which one would you protect?

It was truly a difficult question. If not for these two women, Geom Mugeuk could not have been standing here. If Ian had not blocked for him with the Whole-Body Petrification Art, he would already be dead, and if Seojin had not told him about the Great Regression Art, he would not have returned.

Geom Mugeuk smiled and turned around.

“I probably won’t need to save either of them.”

“Why not?”

“What kind of fool in this world would stab such beautiful women to death? Would you stab them, Instructor?”

At the unexpected answer, the Sword King laughed aloud once again.

“I wouldn’t have been able to stab them. Instead, I would have stabbed you three times.”

“Why three times?”

“I’d have to stab you for the women’s share too.”

This time, Geom Mugeuk laughed and looked at Ian.

“Fellow entrant, you heard him, right? Your shield is a shield exposed to this kind of danger.”

Ian smiled and answered,

“I’ll find a way to block one of them. So just get stabbed twice.”

Then Seojin, on the other side, said,

“I’ll work hard too, so you only have to block one.”

Geom Mugeuk looked back at the Sword King.

“That’s what they say.”

The two men looked at each other. The Sword King’s face was full of ease, and Geom Mugeuk wore a relaxed smile.

The other trainees continued introducing themselves. As if to show that he had not given only Geom Mugeuk and the two women special treatment, the Sword King asked the trainees all sorts of questions again. What is your family situation like? If you earn money, what will you spend it on?

In the meantime, training time ended. As soon as the bell signaling the end of training rang out, the Sword King left without even looking back.

“See you tomorrow!”

The sight of him hurrying away made him look like a slacking instructor who had managed to coast through another day.

Only after he had gone far away did Geom Mugeuk finally let out a light sigh. Though he truly had not used even a strand of inner force, he had fought a fierce battle with him. And it had been more exhausting than any other fight.

Ian cautiously asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Did I mess things up, by any chance?”

“No. You did well.”

Only then did Ian let out a sigh of relief.

“You told me before, didn’t you? That if I wanted to describe my strength, I should ask how many moves it would take me to kill the master of this Yellow Dragon Hall! But just now, my heart almost burst because of a mere instructor!”

At her joke, Geom Mugeuk also answered with a joke.

“I didn’t know the level of martial academies these days was this high.”

Of course, Ian knew. That the instructor from a moment ago was an expert from the hidden force Geom Mugeuk had been dealing with until now. An expert from the White Snake Corps would not have been able to make Geom Mugeuk this tense.

“When your skills reach about your level, enemies like that start appearing in front of you.”

“Then what should I do?”

Geom Mugeuk gave her the answer.

“You have to achieve great completion of the Twelfth Star.”

Ian nodded and resolved that she would absolutely achieve great completion of the Twelfth Star.

“You mustn’t say this when the time comes! ‘Sorry, now you have to face an even stronger enemy!’”

Geom Mugeuk smiled with an expression that said he might very well do just that.

Seojin, standing beside the two of them, also felt a desire to become stronger.

“I’ll work hard too.”

“Yes. Now we don’t need a baby ghost, but a real ghost.”

The Sword King’s question about whom he would save had instead become a powerful motivation for Ian and Seojin to grow stronger. They were both the type who would rather protect someone than live a life where someone else protected them.

Ian looked in the direction the Sword King had gone and said,

“Dangerous lessons will begin tomorrow.”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a meaningful smile.

“The more dangerous it is, the more there will be to learn.”

Chapter 575: A Few Bruises on the Chest Are Fine, Right?
--------------------------------------------------------
The next day, as they were on their way to attend the White Dragon class, Seojin asked worriedly,

“What if that man takes us hostage again and tries to kill the Young Cult Master?”

At that, Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk’s back as he walked ahead of them and said calmly,

“The Young Cult Master will stop him.”

But Seojin knew just how close to the brink yesterday’s atmosphere had been.

“If you’re trying to stay because of me, I’m all right.”

Seojin was worried that Ian might be forcing herself to remain because of her revenge.

When Ian stopped walking, Seojin stopped as well.

“You asked what kind of person the Young Cult Master is, didn’t you?”

“Yes.”

“If he had judged that we needed to return, the Young Cult Master would have packed our bags himself and sent us back. Honor? Justification? Pride? He isn’t someone who overextends himself for things like that. If the Young Cult Master says nothing, then it’s fine.”

After returning from class yesterday, Geom Mugeuk had gone out somewhere and come back. She had not asked where he had been, but it had surely been an outing to prepare for this matter.

And at dawn, Ian had heard the window of Geom Mugeuk’s room open. He had been awake until then.

Lying on her bed, Ian had looked at the stars visible through the half-open window. Perhaps Geom Mugeuk was looking at those same stars right now.

“If the Young Cult Master says the sun won’t rise tomorrow, I’ll bet my entire fortune that it won’t.”

It was Ian’s absolute faith in Geom Mugeuk.

“When you make that bet, please be sure to tell me.”

“So you can bet with me that it won’t rise?”

“No, I couldn’t do that. Now that I’ve left home, I have many places to spend money too.”

Ian and Seojin looked at each other and laughed.

Just then, up ahead, Geom Mugeuk looked back at them.

“What are you doing? Hurry up!”

The two women began walking again. As they went, Ian offered another optimistic theory.

“The Young Cult Master isn’t just an ordinary master. With his skills, and if the opponent is skilled enough to threaten someone like him, then hostages won’t mean anything. It’s like this: if the Young Cult Master can beat him, he’ll win even if we’re taken hostage. If the Young Cult Master can’t beat him, then the opponent wouldn’t bother taking hostages in the first place.”

Seojin had lived as a blood relative of the Ghost Gate, lacking nothing. And now she was feeling an unfamiliar yet intense longing.

“I want to become strong too. Truly.”

She wanted to fight alongside them in this world of the strong. Not as a hostage, but as a protagonist.

How could Ian not understand her feelings? She, too, had once felt the same longing and had thrown herself into training like a madwoman.

When one stayed beside Geom Mugeuk, one naturally became this way without realizing it. It wasn’t because anyone ordered them to. Perhaps it was because the path that man walked was one that could not be shared unless one ran until one’s heart felt like it would burst.

“If you want that, then absolutely don’t doze off during class!”

* * *

The Sword King’s second class began.

He looked a little different from yesterday. The shabby martial robes, the tightly tied hair, and the single iron sword were the same, but today he had come barefoot.

“Try walking around barefoot from time to time. You can feel the energy of the earth, and it’s quite nice.”

The trainees laughed at the sight of him hanging his shoes on the side opposite his sword.

On top of that, he had dragged in a large box.

“I brought it from the storage.”

When he opened the box, thick with piled-up dust, it was filled with training wooden swords.

In the early days of the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy, students had trained with wooden swords until several years after entering.

But as the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy became famous and began selecting students through examinations, wooden sword classes had vanished at some point.

“Now, take one each.”

Until now, they had trained with their own real swords at their waists, so the trainees were bewildered when wooden swords were suddenly handed out.

“You brats, who do you think you’re good enough to cut down that you’re already using real swords? Even wooden swords are too good for you.”

The trainees took up the wooden swords, inwardly dumbfounded. From what he had shown yesterday to his bare feet and now this out-of-nowhere wooden sword lesson, he was the very image of a slacking instructor.

As Ian picked up a wooden sword, she sent a sound transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—This is unexpected. I thought he would create a frightening atmosphere with a class that felt even more like real combat.

Then an unexpected sound transmission came back from Geom Mugeuk.

—I find this more frightening.

—Pardon? Why?

—Because they’ll swing freely, thinking they’re only wooden swords.

—!

The wooden sword in the Sword King’s hand will be sharper than a treasured blade, Ian.

Geom Mugeuk looked down at the wooden sword.

‘Come to think of it, how long has it been since I last held a wooden sword?’

He had once recalled the day he first grasped a real sword in his father’s training hall.

Now that he thought about it, he had considered the day he first held a real sword special, yet had completely forgotten the day he first held a wooden sword. Perhaps that moment had been the more important one.

The martial art taught at the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy was the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.

The Yellow Dragon Sword Art had played the greatest role in making the Yellow Dragon Martial Academy the foremost martial academy in the Central Plains, but it was not because its swordsmanship was overwhelmingly powerful. It was a martial art created so that its sword techniques could be performed without conflict no matter what inner cultivation method one had learned.

“The Yellow Dragon Sword Art consists of twenty-six forms in total.”

At that, the trainees said,

“It’s twenty-nine.”

“Ah, was it?”

The trainees wore expressions that said, I knew it, but the Sword King was not embarrassed at all.

“If you learn this sword art properly, you’ll be able to give the Demonic Cult’s Young Cult Master’s backside a thorough beating.”

The trainees laughed at that. The one who laughed the loudest among them was Geom Mugeuk. He was enjoying the Sword King’s joke without feeling the slightest displeasure.

Ian could sense it. The two of them were engaged in an invisible battle of wills. And both of them were enjoying it.
