“Now, today we will learn the first three forms of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.”

The Sword King held up his wooden sword and stood before them all.

Only Geom Mugeuk properly felt it. Though it was only a wooden sword, the moment he held a sword, his presence changed completely.

If he drew a real sword, what sort of presence would he reveal? The Sword King with a sword in hand felt like a different person, enough to make such fear arise on its own.

“I don’t usually teach things like this just anywhere, you know.”

The trainees took him as an instructor merely making glib remarks, but Geom Mugeuk focused on every word he spoke and every gesture he made. The trainees did not know who was teaching them swordsmanship at that moment.

The Sword King’s movements were truly gentle. They seemed to have no strength in them at all, to the point that they looked almost like a dance.

Everyone tilted their heads. The Yellow Dragon Sword Art they had learned until now was a martial art performed with fast, forceful movements.

So they all thought the same thing. Looks like he’s trying to get through this carelessly, just like yesterday.

He even changed the movement midway through performing a form.

“Ah, it would be better to do this like this.”

Everyone shook their heads, but it looked different in Geom Mugeuk’s eyes.

He could tell that the Sword King had reinterpreted the fierce Yellow Dragon Sword Art on a foundation of softness.

Had he ever properly learned the Yellow Dragon Sword Art for a long time? He had likely only glanced at the manual briefly in order to act as a temporary instructor, yet he had reinterpreted a martial art and presented it as something else.

After performing the forms, the Sword King sat on the box from which he had taken the wooden swords and brushed the dust from his feet.

“Did you all get a good look?”

As he said that, the Sword King’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

From the look in his eyes as he stared at him, Geom Mugeuk understood.

‘The fight has begun.’

I performed it this way. Now how will you show me this Yellow Dragon Sword Art?

It was a probe meant to gauge Geom Mugeuk’s skill, a game played by one who had reached the pinnacle of swordsmanship, and at the same time the beginning of a fight in which either could cut off the other’s head at any moment.

“Now, everyone try it!”

The trainees thrust out their wooden swords in unison. As if saying, This is what the Yellow Dragon Sword Art is, they performed the forms quickly and powerfully, exactly as they had already learned them.

A few tried to imitate what the Sword King had done, but soon thought, What am I doing? and returned to performing the martial art in its original way.

In this place, the only ones following exactly as the Sword King had performed were Geom Mugeuk, Ian, and Seojin.

Of course, the only one who accurately understood the Sword King’s reinterpretation was Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk’s movements were identical to the Sword King’s.

Softly, like flowing water.

With all his sincerity poured into each and every movement, he performed the three forms of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.

A strange light flashed through the Sword King’s eyes as he watched Geom Mugeuk. He really succeeded after seeing it just once?

“Geomyeon, come forward.”

The Sword King called Geom Mugeuk out.

Ian was tense inwardly, but Geom Mugeuk walked forward fearlessly with the wooden sword in hand.

“Show us a demonstration.”

Geom Mugeuk perfectly reproduced exactly what the Sword King had shown.

He did not include his own interpretation in this martial art. He performed the martial art exactly as the Sword King had taught it.

One’s own interpretation should only be applied when one could perfectly perform the original.

Knowing that originality could sometimes become a trap that hindered completion of the essence, Geom Mugeuk strove to remain faithful to that essence.

Laughter broke out here and there. To the trainees’ eyes, it looked ridiculous. The instructor’s demonstration had been strange to begin with, and they thought he was imitating those awkward movements as if trying to curry favor with the instructor.

Some trainees openly sneered. After becoming Shield Geomyeon rather than the new Geomyeon, he had been receiving jealousy and resentment, making it even worse.

When the demonstration ended, the Sword King asked the trainees,

“Now, who wants to try a prearranged spar with the forms we learned today?”

As everyone was glancing at one another, one person stepped forward.

“I’ll try.”

It was Yu Gwang. He had no personal grudge against Geomyeon.

There were two reasons he stepped forward. Half of him wanted to catch Ian’s eye even a little, and the other half was curious why Ian kept this fellow by her side. What was so special about him?

“Now, face each other and perform the forms we learned today.”

The two of them performed the forms.

While Yu Gwang executed them quickly and powerfully as he usually practiced them, Geom Mugeuk moved with unsurpassed gentleness, just as he had learned today.

The problem was that Yu Gwang’s speed was faster, so it seemed the prearranged spar would not work.

And yet the two men’s prearranged spar matched perfectly.

When Geom Mugeuk performed alone, he had truly looked slow, but when he actually matched moves with Yu Gwang, he was not slow at all.

So everyone thought that Geom Mugeuk had performed slowly earlier and was now executing the forms quickly.

But only Ian recognized it accurately.

‘His speed is the same as before. It only looked slow because it appeared so smooth.’

What truly surprised Ian, however, happened next.

When two people faced each other and performed the Yellow Dragon Sword Art forms, their swords were meant to collide on the third form.

The moment the two wooden swords struck each other in midair—

Saaak.

The wooden sword Yu Gwang held was smoothly cut apart.

Not only Yu Gwang himself, but everyone watching was startled.

If a wooden sword were to break, it should have snapped with a crack. Yet it had truly made the sound of paper being cut by a blade.

Yu Gwang, the person involved, was the most shocked. He looked down at his severed sword. The cut surface was as smooth as if it had been sliced by a blade.

There had not even been any impact when the swords clashed.

‘Was it cut from the beginning?’

He had no choice but to understand the situation in that way.

Aside from the Sword King, the person who realized the secret was, as expected, Ian.

Geom Mugeuk’s movements were as fluid as water, and that water was not water that could be taken lightly.

The instant that water focused on a single point, it became water capable of cutting through anything. Water that shot forth so quickly it appeared instead to be standing still.

The Sword King’s reinterpretation, planting extreme hardness within softness, had been contained in the forms Geom Mugeuk performed.

Emotion flashed through the Sword King’s eyes. Geom Mugeuk had perfectly accepted his interpretation after watching a single demonstration.

The Sword King strode toward Geom Mugeuk.

You are qualified to die by my sword!

His steps made it seem as though he would draw his sword at any moment.

As always, the hardest part in a fight against the Sword King was the moment when one had to do nothing at all.

Fortunately, there was no surprise attack this time either.

Instead, the Sword King stopped in front of him and extended his wooden sword forward. From that action, Geom Mugeuk understood. It meant he wanted to try a prearranged spar with him.

‘What were you doing in this martial academy? With what thoughts did you appear before me, and with what thoughts are you giving a lecture on martial arts?’

After gazing at the Sword King for a moment, Geom Mugeuk nodded.

‘Are you truly a man who keeps his promises?’

As if they had made a promise, the two moved at the same time.

Their movements were identical. The place their swords aimed, their speed, the movement of their feet—it looked as though they were performing the forms while looking into a mirror. If the two of them had been overlaid, it would have seemed as if a single person were moving.

The wooden swords brushed past each other’s shoulders and grazed their faces. They executed the prescribed forms exactly as they were. If either of them had altered the angle of his hand even slightly, he would have stabbed the other in the face, yet both unfolded the forms precisely. Even so, their eyes remained fixed only on each other’s.

At last, when they reached the third form, their wooden swords collided.

Saaak!

The wooden sword in Geom Mugeuk’s hand was sliced cleanly away, just as Yugwang’s had been earlier.

Carrying that momentum, the Sword King’s wooden sword flew toward Geom Mugeuk’s chest.

It was the wooden sword of none other than the Sword King. If it struck properly, it could be a fatal blow.

Saaak.

Before it could touch Geom Mugeuk’s chest, the Sword King’s wooden sword, too, was sliced apart like paper and fell to the floor. It had only fallen a step later; from the beginning, the two wooden swords had been cut together.

“Ah! What a shame!”

The Sword King hopped barefoot as if he truly found it regrettable.

What bloomed on his face was not anger. It was an indescribable ecstasy. The joy of having someone who had cut his sword standing before his eyes.

“I was only going to stab you three times with this wooden sword. A man trying to protect a woman can afford a few bruises on his chest, can’t he?”

As if bruises would have been the end of it.

If he had been stabbed three times by the Sword King’s wooden sword, he would not have remained standing.

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“It’s a good thing the wooden swords were old.”

“Then shall we try it with real swords?”

At the unexpected suggestion, Geom Mugeuk returned to the Sword King the very words he had said to the students earlier.

“How could a mere newcomer draw a real sword and try to take someone down?”

“That’s why this instructor is saying he’ll face you, isn’t it?”

Shrrrng.

The Sword King began drawing his iron sword.

“I heard that in this class, an instructor died and a student died as well.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the iron sword stopped halfway out of its sheath.

“Then who will die this time?”

“If we go in order, it would be the instructor’s turn.”

The two were smiling broadly at each other, but an invisible tension was mounting.

Just then, the bell signaling the end of class rang out.

Clack.

The Sword King’s iron sword returned to its sheath.

As soon as the bell rang, he turned sharply and left without looking back.

“See you tomorrow!”

The sight of him leaving so happily was no different from the first day: a slacker instructor who had been waiting only for the bell to ring. All anyone could see were the shoes dangling at his waist.

The students gathered and examined the cut wooden swords.

“They were cut beforehand with a sword.”

After all, even the instructor of the White Dragon Class couldn’t possibly cut a wooden sword like this with another wooden sword.

“Ah, come to think of it, the instructor brought these wooden swords, didn’t he?”

“Obvious. Too obvious.”

Everyone concluded that the instructor had cut the wooden swords in advance.

They even looked at Geom Mugeuk with suspicion, wondering if the two had perhaps planned it together. They thought the two had conspired to pretend to be experts. The conversation they had exchanged at the end, too, had not been the sort of conversation a normal instructor and student would have.

But Ian could see it.

On Geom Mugeuk’s face, the very same ecstasy that had bloomed earlier on the Sword King’s face was blooming now. That face was not the face of the Young Cult Leader, but the face of a martial artist.

In a situation where everyone believed it was a prank, something arranged in advance.

‘Those two are really fighting.’

Chapter 576: Villains Go On While Being Cursed
----------------------------------------------
It was a sudden visit from his younger brother.

In truth, Yeon Baegin had been worrying over his brother’s affairs until just moments ago. Could he really entrust his brother to the man who had come up from underground, or should he step in and resolve it himself somehow?

And then Yeon Baekjin had come to him first.

“It’s been a while since I was here.”

Yeon Baekjin looked around the office. After so long without visiting his elder brother, it felt awkward.

“Sit.”

The two of them sat facing each other at the reception table.

Yeon Baegin poured the cooled tea from the pot on the table into his own cup and his brother’s.

An awkward silence drifted between them for a moment. The first to speak was Yeon Baekjin.

“Someone told me this. That the only ways martial artists resolve conflict are through conversation and blades.”

Yeon Baegin toyed with his teacup as he asked,

“So which did you bring?”

“That depends on your answer, Brother.”

Yeon Baekjin stared at him. In truth, what he wanted to ask was this:

—This was all your doing, wasn’t it?

Instructor Im’s death, and Pyosan’s death too. He wanted to shout like this:

—Just what in the world are you plotting?

But Yeon Baekjin forced himself to ask calmly,

“Did you know that the White Serpent Corps had infiltrated the White Dragon Class?”

He had deliberately asked indirectly, but Yeon Baegin’s reaction was cold.

“Why are you suddenly taking an interest in the academy’s affairs? You never cared.”

Yeon Baekjin’s expression hardened.

‘Why do you think I never cared about the academy?’

Why he had not cared, why he had pretended not to care—others might not know, but his brother had no right to say that.

“Answer my question first.”

“And if I did know? Is the solution a blade?”

He had hoped for a denial. That he had misunderstood everything. But his brother did not deny it. That irritable reaction could only mean one thing.

“I told you, didn’t I? From now on, don’t interfere in the academy’s affairs at all.”

“How could I not get involved? This is our academy!”

“Our?”

At his brother’s incredulous reaction, Yeon Baekjin realized something in that instant. He had been gravely mistaken about one thing all this time.

Yeon Baekjin had thought his brother would feel sorry toward him. For the fact that he had not fought over power in the academy and had meekly stepped aside.

But judging by that reaction now, his brother seemed to have considered him nothing more than a good-for-nothing younger brother with no interest whatsoever in the academy.

Ah! Come to think of it, they had never once spoken about that. He had assumed his brother would understand.

“Leave the academy.”

“What?”

“I said go far away. I’ll give you enough money to live comfortably for the rest of your life.”

After glaring at his brother for a moment, Yeon Baekjin asked,

“And if I don’t leave? Will you kill me?”

The obvious answer—what are you saying, of course not—did not come. Naturally, Yeon Baekjin’s voice rose.

“Don’t be mistaken. This academy isn’t yours, Brother. It holds Father’s lifetime of toil, and the dreams of countless students.”

As he listened to those words, Yeon Baegin came to realize one thing.

That the reason he had been worrying over his younger brother’s problem until just moments ago had not been because he feared his brother might die. Rather, he had been worried that the man from underground might resolve matters by keeping his brother alive.

Yes. He was the sort of person who could not forgive even his own brother if he touched what was his. He was a good person only when things could remain amicable.

In truth, he had known. That someday the day would come when he had to kill his younger brother.

That thought had been hidden deep within his heart, inside a chunk of ice that would not easily melt.

It did not melt easily, but the ice was transparent, so he could always see it. He had merely pretended not to.

“Brother, you’re making me burn right now. I would have gone cold forever otherwise. I will never stand by and watch you ruin the academy Father left behind.”

Yeon Baekjin did not know. The one who had truly grown heated was Yeon Baegin, and the ice that had concealed his murderous intent had all melted away.

Just as the two of them were silently staring each other down amid that taut tension, a new visitor arrived.

It was the Sword King, who had come after finishing his lesson.

The Sword King greeted Yeon Baekjin warmly when he saw him.

“Oh, if it isn’t Great Hero Yeon!”

Yeon Baekjin, on the other hand, was seeing his face for the first time today.

“I have taken on the role of temporary instructor for the White Dragon Class.”

Had there been an instructor like this in the academy? There were so many instructors, after all.

When Yeon Baekjin looked back at his brother, Yeon Baegin was silently staring down at his teacup.

Yeon Baekjin left the office without even a greeting. Then, at the doorway, he glanced back at the Sword King.

‘But what business would an instructor have coming to Brother’s office? A temporary instructor, at that?’

His brother usually dealt only with the Seven Masters. Matters concerning instructors were handled by the Seven Masters, especially Giseok, whom he used like his own limbs.

But now was not the time to question that, so he left the place as he was.

As he walked, he recalled Geom Mugeuk’s advice.

‘As if conversation would solve anything. Damn it!’

After he left, the Sword King sat down in the seat where Yeon Baekjin had been.

Pouring himself some cold tea from the pot, he drank and said casually,

“I will make sure your younger brother never appears before your eyes again.”

Taking it to mean that he would kill his brother, Yeon Baegin was startled.

At that, the Sword King smiled playfully.

“Be honest. Your heart fluttered a little just now, didn’t it?”

Realizing it was the Sword King’s joke, Yeon Baegin turned his displeased gaze out the window. In any case, the man was not someone he could get angry at, and this was the sort of joke the man made without the slightest hesitation.

With his gaze still turned away, Yeon Baegin changed the subject.

“Did the lesson go well?”

Yeon Baegin knew that this man had come up to find that so-called devil. Since he had said he would become an instructor for the White Dragon Class, that devil more frightening than a ghost must be in the White Dragon Class.

The Sword King said with a broad grin,

“The lesson was extremely enjoyable.”

Yeon Baegin could feel that this man was more excited than he had ever been since they met.

“Who is that devil?”

“It’s better that you don’t know. You already have enough worries as it is.”

In truth, Yeon Baegin’s worries were over. All that remained was how to convey it to him.

Yeon Baegin used these words to speak his mind.

“……It did flutter.”

He lowered his head and could not meet the Sword King’s eyes. The man would understand exactly what those words meant.

Then came the Sword King’s voice.

“You are trying to have both again. You want to be the elder brother who tries to kill his younger brother, but also the elder brother who still has some shred of conscience.”

Struck where it hurt, Yeon Baegin could not refute him. Because it was true. Because he wanted to appear not as someone killing out of greed, but as someone forced to kill because he had no other choice.

“Raise your head.”

Yeon Baegin raised his head and looked at the Sword King.

“That is worse than a shameless villain. Because it means you intend to become a villain while protecting even your own heart.”

After draining the tea he had been drinking in one sip, the Sword King rose from his seat.

“A villain is someone who walks a single path to the end while being cursed.”

* * *

When Giseok entered the office after being summoned, Yeon Baegin was standing with his back turned, looking out the window.

“You called for me?”

Even at his greeting, Yeon Baegin merely continued looking out the window in silence.

Giseok had served Yeon Baegin for a long time. In a situation like the present, he could guess what this silence meant.

Giseok asked carefully,

“Have you finally made your decision?”

Sure enough, Yeon Baegin slowly nodded.

It was the decision Giseok had been hoping for. The person who would become the greatest obstacle in this matter was none other than Yeon Baekjin. Because of him, Pyosan, who had been one of Giseok’s own limbs, had ultimately died as well.

Now Yeon Baegin offered no worries, no excuses, no explanations.

“Send word to the White Serpent Corps.”

Yes, if he had made up his mind to walk the path of a villain.

“Tell them to send their best, so they do not fail.”

* * *

When the Sword King left the office, he saw Geom Mugeuk in the academy’s training ground.

In the training ground, after all the students had left, he was practicing martial arts alone.

Geom Mugeuk was barefoot, holding a wooden sword.

The martial art Geom Mugeuk was performing was the Yellow Dragon Sword Art. It was different from what the Sword King had taught him earlier.

Based on the Sword King’s teaching, he was displaying a newly reinterpreted Yellow Dragon Sword Art of his own.

If the Sword King’s martial art earlier had been fluid like water, concealing the utmost strength within softness, then the martial art being displayed now was a Yellow Dragon Sword Art interpreted upon the foundation of wind.

Geom Mugeuk’s wooden sword, which had been moving slowly, grew faster. The quiet breeze that stirred reeds became a gust of wind climbing up a valley, and soon turned into a typhoon that swept through the surroundings.

The Sword King felt freedom in the Yellow Dragon Sword Art Geom Mugeuk displayed. A wild freedom that blew as it pleased, drifting gently one moment and suddenly whirling the next.

When Geom Mugeuk finished all three forms, the Sword King walked toward him.

“Is this rebellion?”

He meant that Geom Mugeuk had not done it as he was taught.

“This is all something I learned from you, Instructor.”

He was referring to the fact that the Sword King had reinterpreted the original Yellow Dragon Sword Art and taught it to him. Geom Mugeuk had simply reinterpreted that reinterpretation.

As if he had been waiting to show him, Geom Mugeuk was not surprised by his appearance at all.

“You were waiting to show me, weren’t you?”

And he did not try to hide it.

“You are the only one who would recognize what this is, Instructor.”

Even though they knew each other’s identities, Geom Mugeuk treated him as an instructor, and the Sword King treated him as a student.

The Sword King’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk’s bare feet. He had said that bare feet were good, but Geom Mugeuk was probably the only one who had actually put it into practice.

“It’s good training barefoot.”

It was not simply because he could feel the energy of the earth. The sensation of stepping through footwork was so vivid that he could grasp the placement of his feet more accurately.

“Show me that form you performed earlier one more time.”

Geom Mugeuk showed him the three forms of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art he had reinterpreted. After watching them all, the Sword King held out his hand.

“Wooden sword!”

Geom Mugeuk turned the wooden sword around and respectfully offered him the hilt. It was as if he were handing over a real sword.

Taking the wooden sword, the Sword King reproduced Geom Mugeuk’s forms exactly.

With just a single look, he too made the martial art Geom Mugeuk had reinterpreted his own. To reinterpret a martial art, and then reinterpret that reinterpretation again. It was something possible only because of the realm the two of them had reached.

Then, all of a sudden, the Sword King charged at Geom Mugeuk in a surprise attack.

Shwaaaak!

The Sword King’s wooden sword tore through the air as it flew toward Geom Mugeuk. It was not some other martial art. It was none other than the First Form of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art. And he executed it exactly as Geom Mugeuk had interpreted it.

The three forms followed in an instant. The strong wind became a gale and stirred up a typhoon.

Whoooosh. When the dust that had risen settled, the two of them could be seen. Geom Mugeuk had evaded every one of those terrifying attacks.

“Ugh, this wretched dust.”

The Sword King waved his hand.

Both of them behaved as if the surprise attack just now had never happened. Geom Mugeuk did not ask why he had attacked him, and the Sword King did not apologize.

“Yours is better.”

The Sword King acknowledged it honestly. Geom Mugeuk’s reinterpretation was more complete.

At that, Geom Mugeuk sighed.

“Why sigh when I said yours is better?”

“That’s not why I sighed.”

“Then why?”

“When I see someone more impressive than me, I habitually sigh.”

He said it like a joke, but he meant it. Someone on the level of the Sword King did not easily admit that he was lacking. But the Sword King had acknowledged his insufficiency without the slightest hesitation.

“Why the sudden flattery?”

“I’m a shield, aren’t I?”

He meant that he had someone to protect.

“Since I’ve already started flattering you, I’ll finish it. The reason mine had no choice but to be better is because I reinterpreted it based on something far more outstanding.”

The Sword King stared intently at Geom Mugeuk, and his gaze deepened.

“I think I understand a little now.”

Though there was no explanation before or after, Geom Mugeuk could guess what he meant. Perhaps he meant that he now understood why the affairs of his organization had been blocked time and time again by the young cult leader of the Demon Cult.

“You say you are a shield, yet what are you doing here instead of protecting the women you ought to protect?”

“A shield needs to rest a little too. Now that I’ve actually tried it, being a shield isn’t something just anyone can do.”

Even playing the role of a shield mostly in words was this exhausting. Ian was like that, Uncle Hwi was like that. His own guards were like that too. It must truly have been difficult—he felt that anew once more.

“Being a shield doesn’t suit the temperament of someone as full of worries as I am at all.”

At that, the Sword King asked directly,

“That woman named Ian—what is she to you?”

“She is like my heart.”

At his honest answer, the Sword King looked puzzled.

“Is it all right to speak so openly of such a great weakness?”

“She is not my weakness. She is my enemies’ weakness.”

“What does that mean?”

“If they touch her, they will die by my hand in the most miserable way in the world. So she is a weakness that scatters my enemies’ nerves, because they must restrain their desire to touch her to the very end.”

The Sword King laughed loudly and pleasantly.

“That was a fresh bit of sophistry.”

The Sword King began walking on his way again. Looking at the shoes dangling from his waist, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Why don’t you kill me?”

Surely he had appeared as an instructor in order to kill him.

“Why are you putting it off?”

The Sword King stopped walking for a moment and looked up at the sky. It was as if he meant to find the answer up there.

Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky with him.

And so the two of them looked up at the sky for a long while.

“What did you ask me?”

“What did I ask? Ah, why you aren’t killing me?”

“It’s been a while since I had some fresh air outside, for one.”

As the Sword King started trudging away again, he added,

“And because there are far too many boring bastards in this martial world.”

Chapter 577: Because It Looks Too Old
-------------------------------------
When Geom Mugeuk opened the window of his guest room, cold air flowed in. A pale dawn mist was drifting through the alley in front of the inn.

He had entered the Time-Space Transposition Art in the evening and had only just finished.

In terms of time, four sijin had passed, but the time he had actually spent training was a full two days. That was because the steadily advancing Secret Art of Time was now providing him with six times more time than reality.

For two days, he had exhausted all his internal energy, recovered it, exhausted it again, and recovered it again. He had no idea how many evil spirits had appeared and vanished over and over.

And at the end of his training, he unfolded a space within the Time-Space Transposition Art meant for him alone.

It was a hot spring amid a snowy plain, where rising steam created a dreamlike atmosphere.

By soaking his body there, the fatigue of a day and a half of relentless training melted away.

Truly, he had repeated the Nine Hells Demonic Art so many times that he felt as though evil spirits might appear through the rising steam, asking to loosen up their bodies too.

He could feel it coming. The seventh level of the Nine Hells Demonic Art was about to rise another step.

The experience and insight he had gained from his battle with the Dark King were merging with the realization he had felt while reinterpreting the forms of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art, and his martial realm was beginning to stir.

This experience of reinterpreting the Sword King’s reinterpretation granted him an unexpected and special enlightenment. After all, he had never before newly interpreted a martial academy’s martial art together with an expert like the Sword King.

‘I have to keep doing this.’

He could feel that this work with the Sword King would bring him great achievement.

Just then, the window of the next guest room opened, and someone stuck out her head.

“You’re not sleeping?”

It was Ian.

“What about you?”

“I was cultivating my inner art, and before I knew it, the time had gotten this late.”

She had sensed that the atmosphere around them these days was anything but ordinary. After all, an enemy had appeared who made Geom Mugeuk tenser than anyone else in this place full of novices. How could she possibly sleep?

“Training is good, but sleep is important too.”

“Look who’s talking.”

She knew Geom Mugeuk always trained until dawn. Of course, since he trained quietly in his room, she assumed he must be cultivating his inner art as well. What she did not know was just how much sword qi and sword aura were rampaging in the room right next to hers.

“I’ll sleep all I want after I’m dead!”

At her words, Geom Mugeuk smiled faintly.

‘You’ve already slept all you wanted.’

So in this life, stay awake.

Stay awake until you grow old and become a white-haired old woman, watching the world with those beautiful eyes.

Ian with white hair would surely age beautifully. Would they still be able to stand side by side like this then, looking at the world together?

“That man… he’s strong, isn’t he?”

It was the first time Ian had asked about the Sword King.

Geom Mugeuk nodded honestly.

“How strong?”

“Have you ever seen a real expert up until now who went barefoot?”

“No.”

“He’s exactly that strong.”

She understood that he meant the Sword King was that special an expert, but Ian deliberately spoke as if it were no big deal.

“Well, if it’s just that much, it should be fine. Young Master, you’re not lacking in specialness anywhere either, are you?”

As she said that, she tapped the left side of her chest twice.

“To pierce Young Master’s heart, they’d have to pierce not one, but two hearts.”

At her half-joking, half-serious remark, Geom Mugeuk laughed as if amused. But the look in his eyes as he gazed at her grew deeper. Because what she had just said was something she had already experienced.

“So this is the advantage of someone with two hearts?”

“Just trust me!”

“Your heart absolutely must not be pierced! I’m going to sleep trusting only in that heart!”

“Sleep deeply for my share too.”

Of course, in Geom Mugeuk’s heart, the order was reversed.

To pierce her heart, they would first have to pierce his.

There would never be a day when his heart was pierced and that blade reached Ian’s heart. All his efforts were to prevent such a moment from ever coming.

While Ian was looking up at the stars in the dawn sky, in the room next door, a fierce battle in which the earth split and cliffs collapsed was unfolding once more, soundlessly.

* * *

The Sword King came to class on time the next day as well.

He had even prepared the next three forms of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.

“Now then, today I will teach the fourth through sixth forms.”

Just what does this man want?

As Geom Mugeuk looked at him, that thought naturally came to mind. But at the very least, he could tell why the Sword King was teaching the Yellow Dragon Sword Art again.

The look the Sword King gave him said this:

—Let’s have another bout!

Yesterday’s reinterpretation had been his defeat. He had admitted that the form Geom Mugeuk reinterpreted was better. That was why he intended to challenge him again today.

—No matter how much you base your reinterpretation on mine this time, you won’t be able to make it better.

Geom Mugeuk answered him with his eyes.

‘Let’s do it. Even if we kill each other when the time comes, for now, let’s enjoy this.’

They both instinctively knew. As martial artists, meeting such an opponent might happen only once in a lifetime, if at all.

The Sword King demonstrated the fourth through sixth forms.

The academy disciples watching him turned to one another and whispered.

“What is that?”

“It’s different from yesterday too.”

“He’s doing whatever he wants.”

The interpretation of the forms the Sword King was displaying today was yet another interpretation.

The Yellow Dragon Sword Art he had shown yesterday had been soft and fluid as water. It had even looked like a dance.

But today, it was indifferent. There was no way to describe it except to say it was truly indifferent. He swung his sword indifferently. He strode forward and thrust with a dull stab. Movements continued one after another as though he were casually kicking a pebble lying on the ground.

Of course, the academy disciples could not grasp the deep essence of martial learning contained in each and every move, so this too looked to them like a halfhearted demonstration.

Geom Mugeuk, on the other hand, even took off his shoes and followed his forms exactly, barefoot.

At that sight, the academy disciples frowned even harder.

They had already thought his claim that he would become Ian and Seojin’s shield was a ploy to look good in front of them. Now they thought he was training barefoot to curry favor with the instructor.

“Shouldn’t we call the seniors from the Black Dragon Class and have them teach him a lesson?”

“He really is detestable.”

Some even began exchanging such whispers.

Of course, not all the academy disciples were like that.

Yugwang and Gyoseok were diligently following along. Gyoseok was doing it because of his friend, and Yugwang because of Ian. With Ian following along so earnestly, he could not just stand by and watch.

“Do you like that woman that much?”

When Gyoseok shook his head, Yugwang shook his own even more fiercely.

“That’s not why.”

“Then?”

“I’ve been so uneasy lately that I can barely sleep. But whenever I look at Miss Lee, my heart becomes calm. Strangely, all my uneasiness just disappears. There’s something about that woman.”

“It’s just because she’s pretty.”

“I said that’s not it!”

Yugwang tried hard to follow along. Gyoseok also pretended he had no choice and followed the movements. He scolded his friend every day to stop indulging in delusions, but he knew there was something special about him.

And so the second day’s class ended.

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King met again at the place where they had met on the first day.

Watching the martial art Geom Mugeuk unfolded, the Sword King smiled with satisfaction.

Yesterday, Geom Mugeuk had reinterpreted what he had taught. Today, he was performing it exactly as he had taught it.

“The forms you reinterpreted today are perfect, Instructor. It would be discourteous to ruin such excellent forms out of a petty desire to win.”

When he honestly admitted defeat, this time the Sword King let out a sigh.

“Why are you sighing when I’m the one saying I lost?”

“Because you acknowledged it too cleanly.”

Then unexpected words flowed from him.

“A Young Cult Leader like you would be hard to see ever again in the history of the Demonic Cult. Since I must kill with my own hands someone granted by the heavens, I can’t help but sigh.”

He was still filled with confidence that he could kill Geom Mugeuk.

“The one who dies will be you, Instructor. Just like those comrades of yours who died by my hand before.”

It was the first time he had brought up the other Twelve Zodiac Kings to him. How would he react?

“Comrades? Who are you calling my comrades?”

The Sword King did not consider the Twelve Zodiac Kings who had died before him to be his comrades. Rather, the man who had always been composed showed displeasure for the first time at being placed on the same level as them.

“I’ll show you my comrades.”

In the next instant, the Sword King released his aura. It was the first time he had revealed his aura since they had met.

Saaaaaa.

Everything around them was glittering.

At a glance, it looked like jewels sparkling in sunlight.

But that was not it. They were all sword blades.

Blades were protruding from the walls, the floor, the ceiling—everywhere in all directions. Hundreds, thousands of blades. Truly, blades filled every side so densely that even the slightest movement seemed likely to cut him.

When Geom Mugeuk turned his head to the side, a blade was aimed at his face. Blades protruded on the opposite side as well. There were blades in front of him and above his head too.

Geom Mugeuk reached out and touched a blade.

Slice.

His finger was cut by the blade, and blood flowed. The aura was so sharp that it had actually cut his finger. Of course, it was also because he had not deployed protective gang qi.

Far beyond the flashing blades, he could see the Sword King.

“What do you think of my friends?”

It meant that only swords were his friends.

Where, in this place so full of loneliness, are these friends of yours?

Geom Mugeuk smiled pleasantly and said to him,

“You have so many friends that there’s no room for me to slip in.”

Perhaps he liked the answer, for the Sword King laughed as well.

Sssss.

As the Sword King’s aura vanished, the surrounding swords disappeared in an instant.

“Later, before you die, will you grant me one request?”

Geom Mugeuk could sense it. The reason the Sword King had shown himself before him, the reason he was so leisurely giving and receiving lessons like this, might perhaps all have been for the sake of that request.

“Very well. Before I kill you, Instructor, I’ll grant your request.”

Of course, he did not simply agree meekly.

“In exchange, please grant one request of mine as well. I’ll tell you mine right here and now.”

“Speak.”

“Please continue reinterpreting the Yellow Dragon Sword Art as you have been, all the way to its final form. Keep wagering with me.”

A trace of doubt naturally passed over the Sword King’s face.

“No matter how well I reinterpret it, it should be a martial art you have no need for.”

“There are three reasons.”

“Three, you say?”

“First, this wager is too enjoyable.”

He thought this enjoyment was surely the same for the Sword King. Otherwise, they would not be having this conversation here now.

“Second, there is someone to whom I want to pass on the martial art completed in this way.”

And lastly.

“This is just my intuition, but by the time this process ends, I think my martial realm will rise.”

The Sword King smiled and asked with an expression that seemed to say, And yet you’re asking me to teach you?

“Where in the world did you learn this shameless degree of honesty?”

“From people who weren’t petty. Those people did not take my honesty as a weakness.”

A strange emotion flowed across the Sword King’s face. Yesterday, when they had parted, he had said there were too many petty people in this murim.

“Didn’t we meet as academy disciple and instructor? Since fate has brought us together like this, I want to learn everything I can.”

Though it was surely not an easy request, the Sword King did not ponder for long.

“If I’m to become someone who isn’t petty, I suppose I can’t refuse. Fine, I’ll grant your request.”

“Thank you.”

The Sword King was about to turn away when he suddenly asked,

“What will you do if the request I make of you is one you cannot grant?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled apologetically and answered,

“That’s why I’m asking first, isn’t it?”

* * *

Even though Geom Mugeuk had told him outright that his intention was to grow stronger first and kill him if necessary, the Sword King taught him every form of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art through to the final one, just as promised. It was something that had not been planned at all when he first became a temporary instructor.

On some days, Geom Mugeuk reinterpreted better, and on other days, the Sword King did. Sometimes they combined and supplemented each other’s versions, and sometimes they created something entirely new.

The forms flowed like water, then the wind blew, then blazing flames surged up, and on other days, lightning struck.

It was as if the two of them were playing a game of baduk, move by move. It was a pleasure that would never come again in their lives, and at the same time, a battle of pride.

The academy disciples sensed that something was happening, but they could not tell exactly what it was. None of them could grasp the profound essence of that martial learning.

And so, on the day he learned the final form of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art, the Yellow Dragon Sword Form was newly completed.

The forms the two men completed were different from each other. Though they had endlessly influenced one another, they had arrived at different interpretations to the very end.

While they had been doing it, they had burned with competitive spirit, but once it was over, neither of them cared who had won more often.

Geom Mugeuk held out something wrapped to the Sword King.

“What is this?”

“A gift.”

“A gift of death? If I open it, will Formless Deadly Poison spread out?”

“I wrapped it far too carelessly for that.”

The Sword King opened the wrapping. What was inside was something he truly had not expected. It was a pair of shoes.

“They looked too worn out.”

For the first time, bewilderment passed through the Sword King’s eyes. But soon, the Sword King turned the shoes upside down and shook them. He looked as though he were checking whether there were hidden projectiles inside.

“Why are you giving me this?”

To the curious Sword King, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“My martial art has reached the eighth level.”

The realm of the Nine Hells Demonic Art, which had been surging restlessly, had finally reached the eighth level the night before.

Geom Mugeuk could not say how delighted he had been at the power of the Nine Hells Demonic Art, now even stronger. In truth, he had reached the eighth level in the shortest time of any cult leader in history.

Though it was news that should have made him afraid and wary, the Sword King instead smiled brightly.

“Why are you smiling? Are you not worried?”

“If I win, defeating the eighth level is more boastworthy than defeating the seventh. And if I lose, losing to the eighth level is less shameful than losing to the seventh, isn’t it?”

Geom Mugeuk could clearly feel it. Facing this man would be more difficult than facing the Fighting King and the Dark King combined. In every sense.

The two gazed at each other in silence. The Sword King asked in a calm voice,

“You’ve grown stronger, so why are you offering me shoes instead of a sword?”

Geom Mugeuk treated him with honesty to the very end.

“It seems I want to become even stronger.”

Chapter 578: Someone Would Think It Was the Demonic Cult, Not a Martial Hall
----------------------------------------------------------------------------
The Sword King’s gaze fell to the shoes in his hand.

When had he last received a gift like this from someone?

“I don’t even wear shoes much.”

“That’s all the more reason you should wear clean ones.”

“What do you mean?”

“Since you go around barefoot, don’t the shoes you have dangling from you stand out even more?”

“Now that I think of it, that’s true.”

The Sword King had yet to say thank you for the shoes. He was clearly more flustered than when Geom Mugeuk had drawn his sword.

“If I accept something like this, it’ll make things awkward when I ask you for a favor later.”

Geom Mugeuk did not ask what favor he meant. Instead, he took the words as a joke.

“When you ask me for a favor, be sure to look down at your waist!”

The Sword King stared at Geom Mugeuk for a moment, then sat down and slapped the soles of his feet clean with his hands.

Then, right in front of Geom Mugeuk, he put on the shoes.

“They fit perfectly.”

“My eye for such things is hardly ordinary, is it? It’s the same eye that will one day pierce through your forms, Instructor, and strike your heart with perfect accuracy.”

“Then I suppose I’ll have to stab those eyes out first.”

The Sword King rose from his seat and dusted off his backside.

“But what if I’d tampered with those shoes? How could you just put them on like that?”

“If I die because my eye failed to recognize someone so petty, then I die for my own lack of discernment. I’d deserve it.”

The Sword King took out the old shoes he had been carrying at his waist.

“I wore these for a long time too.”

They were so worn that it was impossible to tell when he had first put them on.

“Do those shoes hold some meaning?”

The Sword King shook his head.

“If these old shoes have any meaning, it would be that I lived my life paying that little attention to what others thought.”

“I envy you. I’m terribly conscious of other people.”

“You are?”

Geom Mugeuk seemed as though he would be freer than anyone.

“I want to be a fine person to that one, and look witty to that one. In a dangerous moment, I want them to think of me first. I want to be the star of this gathering. I am conscious of all sorts of things. Even speaking honestly like this is because I’m conscious of you, Instructor. I want to seem like a sincere person.”

The Sword King could feel that Geom Mugeuk was not lying.

“Isn’t living like that exhausting?”

“It is exhausting, but it’s rewarding too. If I hadn’t been this conscious of things, would you be having a conversation like this with me now?”

He would not. They would certainly have gone through an entirely different process from now. No, perhaps the conclusion would already have been reached.

Whoosh.

The Sword King raised blazing yang qi in his palm and burned the shoes he was holding.

After looking for a moment at the shoes blazing fiercely, the Sword King left the ash behind and strode away.

Geom Mugeuk shouted after his retreating back.

“You mustn’t stab my eyes! They’re what I’m most confident in!”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk called Ian and Seojin to a deserted clearing.

“From now on, I will pass the Yellow Dragon Sword Art on to Team Leader Seo.”

Seojin looked at Geom Mugeuk in surprise. She knew. Over the past ten days or so, something had been exchanged between the instructor and Geom Mugeuk through the medium of the Yellow Dragon Sword Art. And now he was saying he would pass the fruits of that on to her.

Startled, she looked at Ian. Her expression seemed to ask whether she could accept it so readily, and Ian smiled and nodded.

Only then did Seojin look at Geom Mugeuk and express her gratitude.

“Thank you very much.”

Lately, she had been thinking constantly that she wanted to become stronger. But the Ghost Arts were not martial arts that produced results overnight, and she was not in a position to learn a new martial art either.

How grateful could she be, then, that Geom Mugeuk had somehow understood her heart and was now trying to pass swordsmanship on to her?

“If you are teaching swordsmanship to Team Leader Seo, then I will withdraw.”

Ian tried to tactfully step away. After all, it was originally improper to be present when someone was passing martial arts on to another.

If Geom Mugeuk had meant to send her away, he would have called only Seojin in the first place.

“No. You learn it with her.”

“Pardon? Me too?”

“If you learn it as a supplementary sword art, it will certainly be of help.”

He himself used the Nine Flames Demonic Art as his main martial art, but there were times when he used the Flying Heaven Sword Art. Enemies who did not require the Nine Flames Demonic Art were handled with the Flying Heaven Sword Art. She, too, would often find herself in similar situations.

“There is something you must pay special attention to as you learn. Think about why the existing Yellow Dragon Sword Art was reinterpreted in this way.”

And the most important part of this instruction was this.

“How you would reinterpret it.”

Ian asked with a surprised expression.

“Am I qualified to do that?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“More than enough.”

Geom Mugeuk was certain that the process of that contemplation would help Ian more than the sword art itself.

Ian could tell. This training, too, was an extraordinarily precious fortuitous opportunity. Geom Mugeuk was always giving her moments like this.

Looking into her trembling eyes, Geom Mugeuk nodded once.

Instead of saying thank you, Ian bowed her head.

If reinterpretation was more important for Ian, then for Seojin, the meaning was different.

“Team Leader Seo will go with this sword art until the day she dies.”

He was certain there was almost no chance she would obtain a better sword art. The new Yellow Dragon Sword Art, blended from his and the Sword King’s interpretations, had been completed as a sword art of unmatched excellence.

She needed proper swordsmanship. Once she became a true master of the Ghost Arts, she might not need any other martial art, but until then, she had to survive. No, even when that time came, knowing proper swordsmanship would be far more useful.

Geom Mugeuk passed on to the two women the Yellow Dragon Sword Art he had reinterpreted, just as it was.

He explained it truly simply. As with any field, only those who truly understand can explain simply; the essence of that profound martial learning was conveyed to the two women in easy words.

Ian was intelligent, of course, and so was Seojin. Because of that, the transmission of the martial art proceeded without obstruction. Whenever they did not understand something, they asked questions on the spot and discussed it together.

Since Ian had learned it with her, if there were parts Seojin did not understand in the future, they would be able to discuss them together as they trained.

As the transmission was drawing to an end, a man came and handed Ian a small piece of paper before disappearing. He was a martial artist from Silver Moon.

Previously, Ian and Seojin had gone to Tongcheon Pavilion and Silver Moon and told them to examine all information related to the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall. And today, Silver Moon had obtained urgent information.

Ian relayed the information written on the paper.

“The White Snake Squad is coming here.”

Geom Mugeuk was not surprised at all, as though he had expected it.

“Estimated arrival time?”

“They say it will be around midnight tonight.”

Geom Mugeuk looked down at Ian’s Sun-Moon Sword. She had wrapped the scabbard in old cloth so it would not stand out.

“It seems the time has finally come to stain that sword with blood.”

* * *

A single carriage was racing along.

There were three people riding in the carriage.

One of the men among them opened his mouth.

“This is the first time in years I’ve gone on an operation as the youngest.”

The man was White Eight Snake, a member of the White Snake Squad.

After entering the top ten of the hundred vipers belonging to the White Snake Squad, every mission had revolved around him.

But today’s mission was different. Three people were riding in the carriage, and the other two were both ranked above him.

White One Snake and White Five Snake.

These numbers, naturally, denoted their ranks. In other words, White One Snake was the strongest master in the White Snake Squad, excluding the squad leader. White Five Snake was the fifth-ranked master.

Even White Five Snake commanded a truly expensive fee, so how much more so White One Snake?

There were several legendary contracts White One Snake had accomplished. The tale of how he had killed the Martial Alliance’s elder master, the Purple Clear Sword, alone and escaped was passed down within the White Snake Squad like a legend.

“Is there something about this contract that I don’t know?”

He had finished another contract and joined this carriage yesterday, and he had been truly surprised when he saw the two people already riding inside. Perhaps White Five Snake had been just as startled when he had joined earlier and seen White One Snake.

In any case, when he considered the target of this contract, it seemed like he could have gone alone.

“I doubt it’s a contract that requires all three of us.”

As he said that, White Eight Snake observed the other two. This was the first time something like this had happened since he had begun taking contract work.

At last, White Five Snake opened his mouth.

“Of course not.”

White Five Snake looked at White One Snake, who was seated beside him, and said,

“If it were something that needed all of us, would we even be able to come back alive?”

It would be a mission of certain death.

Of course, White Five Snake did not think that was the case. Not when he considered the squad leader’s personality as he had seen it until now.

“The squad leader smelled money.”

At White Five Snake’s words, White Eight Snake immediately understood the situation.

The squad leader intended to properly squeeze money out of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.

The moment White One Snake was mobilized, the contract fee became incomparably more expensive. White One Snake had symbolic value by name alone.

In short, they were using an ox-slaughtering knife to kill a chicken so they could charge an outrageous price.

And would that greedy White Snake Squad Leader really end it with this single contract? He would scheme somehow to extort more money.

‘He’ll suck up every penny they earned running that martial hall.’

The problem was that a man who merely ran a martial hall had joined hands with the White Snake Squad in the first place. How could that naive man have known that once he set foot on that path, there was no turning back? The beginning of vileness was always exceedingly proper and trustworthy.

In that case, he would have to handle this contract himself. He could not possibly make his seniors, who were like the heavens above him, do the work after coming along with him.

White Eight Snake glanced at White One Snake. White One Snake had his eyes closed and said nothing.

‘How much will he receive for this contract?’

Three times as much as him? Five times? He would be the one doing all the work. Once you became the best, you earned money just by showing up.

Of course, he had no complaints. Someday, he too would sit in that seat and earn money while doing nothing but putting on airs.

White Eight Snake asked toward the driver’s seat,

“When will we arrive?”

“We should arrive before midnight.”

White Eight Snake said to the other two,

“When we arrive, please have a drink and wait. I will go handle it and return.”

At that, White One Snake opened his eyes. In his cold, snake-like gaze, there was not the slightest trace of carelessness.

“The squad leader…”

In his low voice, one could feel profound internal energy.

“If he had sent White Two Snake, then it would have been because of money. But the fact that he sent me means there is something troubling him.”

White One Snake gently closed his eyes again and said,

“If you want to return alive, you had best move together.”

White Eight Snake politely bowed his head and answered that he would, but he regarded what White One Snake had just said as embarrassment over earning money for nothing.

‘Why are you being so grave? Anyone watching would think we were going to face the Demon Cult, not a martial hall.’

* * *

“Anyone watching would think you were learning some peerless divine art.”

The person who entered the clearing was Yeon Baekjin.

Geom Mugeuk had suddenly contacted him and asked to meet. He had said it was an important matter involving life and death, and that he must not be late for the appointed time and had to meet him here.

‘What on earth is going on?’

He had come to the meeting place inwardly tense, only to find Ian and Seojin performing the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.

Geom Mugeuk, who was teaching, and the two women, who were learning, were all exceedingly serious.

“But it seems a bit different from the Yellow Dragon Sword Art.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and said,

“They only learned it a few days ago, so they are still unskilled.”

“That’s not the reason, is it? It must be because the instructor taught it strangely.”

Yeon Baekjin could not stop thinking about the temporary instructor he had met in his elder brother’s office. So he looked into him and found that he truly was serving as a temporary instructor in the White Dragon Class.

But the students’ complaints were no small matter. He had been teaching the Yellow Dragon Sword Art strangely.

“I met that man called an instructor in my brother’s office. My brother does not deal with instructors, yet that man had come there.”

“So you met your elder brother.”

Yeon Baekjin nodded. Since Geom Mugeuk had also advised him to meet his brother, he summed up the situation of that day in a single sentence.

“I went to talk, but my brother made me use a blade.”

And he guessed that the reason Geom Mugeuk had called him here today was also related to his brother.

“Why did you ask to see me? You said it involved life and death?”

Truly, he was not this sort of person. He was not someone who would come running like this just because someone told him to, especially not because a mere student had called him. If it involved life and death, he was the sort of person who would say, Then you should come to me.

Was it because of Geom Mugeuk? Because of Seojin? Or because of that beautiful woman? For some reason, he felt as though he kept being swayed by them.

Geom Mugeuk delivered startling news.

“Masters of the White Snake Squad are coming to the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.”

It was completely unexpected, so Yeon Baekjin was startled.

“Is that information certain? The White Snake Squad is not a place that leaks information easily. How did you find out?”

“A White Snake Squad martial artist died by the sword of a mere White Dragon Class student. Did you think they would sit still? So we released people to watch them.”

Yeon Baekjin thought that if it was the Ghost Gate, they might well be able to display that level of intelligence-gathering ability.

If what he said was true? His gaze naturally turned toward Seojin.

“I will protect you.”

He was not someone who said such things to others, especially not to women.

But for some reason, his gaze kept going to her. Besides, the beginning of this matter had been his brother’s fault.

It was only natural that she should be grateful, yet Seojin showed no reaction at all.

Feeling that something was strange, Yeon Baekjin looked at Geom Mugeuk.

From Geom Mugeuk’s mouth came a truth even more shocking than the fact that they were coming.

“The targets of the contract include not only Seojin, but you as well, Hero Yeon.”

Yeon Baekjin stood there blankly for a moment, too shocked, before the delayed impact washed over him. The words “I can’t believe it” rose to his throat, but he could not spit them out.

When he had gone to meet his brother, he had felt that it might be possible. But he was really trying to kill him? He had truly placed a contract with the White Snake Squad?

“Do you know when they are coming?”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze went behind Yeon Baekjin. He had deliberately called Yeon Baekjin to this secluded place at just the right time.

Because he thought they would not miss such a fine opportunity, with their contract targets gathered in such a remote place.

“It seems they have already arrived.”

Masked men revealed themselves from within the darkness.

Yeon Baekjin turned around, stepped in front of Seojin, and said,

“If you want to live, stick close behind me!”

Chapter 579: There’s No Way the White Snake Will Come
-----------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk looked at the back of Yeon Baekjin, who had stepped in front of Seojin.

This was the kind of person he had always been. A man willing to face death for someone else.

But before the regression, he had lived on the dark side opposite that heroic image. Perhaps back then, his elder brother, the Sword King, and everyone else had all been looking at the reverse side of the coin.

Seojin asked Yeon Baekjin,

“Why are you trying to save me?”

He could not exactly say that, for some strange reason, his eyes kept being drawn to her and his heart with them.

“Because it’s something my brother did wrong.”

Yeon Baekjin turned to Geom Mugeuk and said,

“You said you came out to protect Seojin too, didn’t you?”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“Help me protect her!”

He was agitated. He knew that the more moments were like this, the calmer one had to be, but the three masked men who had appeared before them did not possess ordinary auras. The fact that they had revealed themselves like this meant they were experts so skilled they had no need to resort to ambush.

‘They sent the very best among the White Snake Squad.’

His hand trembled as he drew his sword. It was not simply because he was afraid. The object of his fury now was his elder brother. Sending experts of this caliber meant he was determined not to fail.

‘So you really mean to kill me.’

If he was going to die anyway, Yeon Baekjin wanted at least to save this woman.

“I may only be the younger brother of a mere martial hall master, but the woman beside me is blood kin of the Ghost Sect. If you bastards kill that woman, the Ghost Sect will slaughter every last one of you.”

Seojin, who had been standing behind Yeon Baekjin, stepped forward and stood side by side with him.

“I don’t think they’re the sort to withdraw because of that.”

Yeon Baekjin looked at Seojin. She had every reason to be frightened, yet she was calm.

‘Is it because she’s from a prestigious family after all?’

Part of it was Seojin’s innate temperament, but the reason she was calm was that she trusted the two people standing behind her. With Geom Mugeuk and Ian behind her, what was there to fear?

Yeon Baekjin’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk and Ian behind them. He misunderstood the two of them.

‘Are you just going to stay there instead of stepping forward?’

The thought crossed his mind, but he did not say it. Fine. At their young age, how frightened must they be? He understood them.

The first of the three masked men to speak was Baek O-sa.

“Those who are about to die by our hands only ever say three things. They threaten, they hate, or they beg.”

Baek O-sa’s eyes, fixed on Yeon Baekjin, held mockery.

“In my experience, the ones who threaten are always the weakest.”

Yeon Baekjin did not waver. He set aside his anger toward his brother for the moment. In this instant, he had to remain calm.

“You know this isn’t a threat, don’t you? Do you think the Ghost Sect Master would simply overlook his daughter’s death?”

This time, Baek Pal-sa spoke.

“The younger brother of the Hwangryong Martial Hall master, bewitched by the beauty of the new trainees, violated and murdered them before vanishing.”

With those words, it became clear how they intended to handle this matter.

Rage surged up inside Yeon Baekjin. As if losing his life were not enough, his reputation would be dragged down into the mud as well.

Yeon Baekjin extended his sword forward and spoke coldly.

“Try it.”

Baek Pal-sa drew his sword and looked back at Baek Il-sa. All that remained was Baek Il-sa’s permission.

At that moment, Baek Il-sa was looking at one person. That person was Ian.

Baek Pal-sa and Baek O-sa thought he was staring because she was so beautiful, but that was not it. Baek Il-sa was seeing the true nature that the two of them could not.

‘Now I understand why the Sect Master sent me.’

Yeon Baekjin and Seojin were opponents Baek Pal-sa could handle well enough.

The problem was the young man and woman standing behind them. The feeling Geom Mugeuk and Ian gave off was strange. The issue was that he could not be certain of their ability. If they were experts, they should feel like experts; if they were weaklings, they should feel like weaklings. Yet what he sensed made them seem at times stronger than him, and at times as if they were nothing special.

‘The woman is the stronger one!’

Baek O-sa, on the other hand, reached a different conclusion.

“A woman too beautiful to kill.”

It seemed Baek O-sa had judged her to be nothing much.

At last, Baek Il-sa’s order fell.

“Kill him first.”

Baek Pal-sa stepped forward.

“I’ll take care of it.”

Baek Pal-sa rushed at Yeon Baekjin. He did not even give his opponent a chance to prepare himself.

His sword flew in, aiming for Yeon Baekjin’s neck, heart, and vital points.

Clang, clang, clang!

Fortunately, Yeon Baekjin responded calmly. An exchange continued in which a single mistake would cost him his life.

Ian focused on Yeon Baekjin’s movements.

Yeon Baekjin was a man who had achieved mastery of the Hwangryong Sword Art. Through his fight, she could see how the original Hwangryong Sword Art was used in actual combat.

Only by knowing the original Hwangryong Sword Art precisely could she understand how it had been reinterpreted.

In the blink of an eye, more than thirty exchanges passed. Yeon Baekjin, who had mastered the Hwangryong Sword Art, was not a weak man.

But as the exchanges went on, he grew increasingly disadvantaged. The martial art he had learned was, in the end, the orthodox swordsmanship of a martial hall, while Baek Pal-sa’s martial art was designed to kill opponents. On top of that, Yeon Baekjin’s real combat experience was comparatively far too lacking.

Blood sprayed from Yeon Baekjin’s arm.

At the sight of blood, Yeon Baekjin became agitated, and the situation worsened further.

Not missing the opportunity, Baek Pal-sa pressed him hard.

Just when he could no longer endure—

Hiiiiiiieek.

Suddenly, with a ghostly wail, Yeon Baekjin’s shadow surged up and engulfed Baek Pal-sa.

The person who helped him in that moment of crisis was Seojin, with her ghost art. She was still only able to cast ghost arts that showed illusions or limited an opponent’s field of vision rather than launch direct attacks.

But in this fight, that one move was an opportunity capable of reversing victory and defeat.

Because of the shadow covering his face, Baek Pal-sa lost sight of Yeon Baekjin for an instant.

Yeon Baekjin did not miss that chance. Rather than retreating, he slid diagonally forward, charged in, and swung his sword.

Shiiik.

Slash!

Blood sprayed from Baek Pal-sa’s side before he could block.

Now the situation had been reversed. As Baek Pal-sa staggered backward, Yeon Baekjin was about to rush in and cut off his breath.

At that moment, Baek O-sa, who had been watching, scattered hidden weapons. They were aimed at both Yeon Baekjin and Seojin.

Swaaaaaak!

As the hidden weapons flew in, Yeon Baekjin instinctively threw himself toward Seojin. He could not let the woman who had saved him die.

‘Is this the end?’

He expected several hidden weapons to bury themselves in his back.

Clang, clang, clang, clang, clang!

From behind him came the sound of hidden weapons being deflected.

When Yeon Baekjin turned back, Ian was already standing in front of them, blocking the way.

‘She knocked down all those hidden weapons?’

It had seemed as though dozens of them had been scattered at once. Yeon Baekjin stared at Ian in astonishment. And that was not all.

Thud.

Baek Pal-sa toppled forward just like that. The hidden weapons Ian had deflected were lodged in his neck and heart.

Baek O-sa stepped forward.

“So you were hiding your skill.”

He looked back at Baek Il-sa. The way she had deflected the hidden weapons was no ordinary technique, so his gaze said they should attack together. But Baek Il-sa did not step forward.

‘Is that noble pride of yours making you refuse a joint attack?’

He would rather that were the case. If Baek Il-sa was not stepping forward because he truly sensed that the opponent was not easy to deal with, then they had really fallen into a difficult situation.

Meanwhile, Seojin staunched the bleeding from Yeon Baekjin’s arm. When the hidden weapons had flown earlier, he had thrown himself toward her.

Watching them, Geom Mugeuk was certain their fates had completely changed. Now they had saved each other’s lives.

‘It’s done.’

This was the moment he achieved the greatest purpose for which he had come to Hwangryong Martial Hall.

Soon, the fight between Baek O-sa and Ian began.

Because Ian fought using the Hwangryong Sword Art, Yeon Baekjin, watching her swordsmanship, was inwardly astonished.

‘It’s similar to the Hwangryong Sword Art.’

It was similar, yet different from what he had learned.

‘That instructor taught it to her!’

In those forms, Yeon Baekjin saw a river flowing at ease, and he saw a fierce wind raging. Flames blazed hotly, and lightning struck.

As the fight continued, Yeon Baekjin’s eyes grew wider and wider. Only today did he learn that the Hwangryong Sword Art could be this powerful and beautiful.

He did not know. The swordsmanship unfolding before him was something she had only just learned today. And she was using it this magnificently in actual combat.

In the earlier fight against Yeon Baekjin, the more exchanges passed, the more advantageous Baek Pal-sa had become. But in this fight, the more exchanges passed, the more advantageous Ian became. Through her first real battle, her Hwangryong Sword Art was becoming incomparably more stable with every move.

When they first exchanged blows, Baek O-sa thought it was a fight worth trying. But in an instant, the balance collapsed, and he began to be pushed back.

The moment Baek O-sa shouted for help—

Shiiik!

Thrust!

Ian’s sword pierced Baek O-sa’s chest. From the moment she had heard the word “violate and murder” from Baek Pal-sa earlier, she had felt no hesitation whatsoever in killing them.

“……You will die miserably. That man is ranked first in the main squad.”

Even at Baek O-sa’s words, Ian showed no fear at all.

“What you say before dying is the same after all.”

It was what Baek O-sa himself had said earlier.

Before dying by his hand, his opponents threatened, hated, or begged. He, too, had failed to escape those categories.

Papatatata!

When Ian pulled out her sword, he collapsed, spraying blood.

Geom Mugeuk looked at her with satisfaction.

The fact that she could display this much skill with a martial art she had learned only today plainly showed just how outstanding her talent for martial arts was, and also just how high her current realm had become.

‘Become stronger, Ian.’

In this martial world swarming with all manner of villains, if she was to live while showing that face to the world, she would have to become so strong that no one would dare challenge her.

Ian did not rejoice or feel exhilarated. She merely looked down at her sword in silence.

Both were firsts.

Killing with the Hwangryong Sword Art, and staining the Ilwol Sword with blood.

The reinterpreted Hwangryong Sword Art truly felt as though it did not contain a single unnecessary element. And though its power was weaker than the Bicheon Sword Art, it consumed far less internal energy.

Because of that, there could be no better auxiliary sword art. There would be times when she had to hide her identity, and times when she had to fight while conserving her internal energy.

Baek Il-sa’s expression had turned completely stiff. This was truly an unforeseen situation.

But that did not mean he felt fear. He was Baek Il-sa, after all.

“Who are you people?”

Geom Mugeuk answered leisurely.

“We’re new members of Hwangryong Martial Hall’s White Dragon Class, aren’t we? You must have investigated us before coming.”

The investigation had been completely wrong.

‘Squad Leader! If there was something bothering you, you shouldn’t have accepted the commission.’

But it was not the squad leader he should resent. When he had come here, he too had indulged in every manner of arrogance, thinking that being sent instead of Baek I-sa meant there was something troubling about this job.

“Ranked first in the White Snake Squad, are you? To think the number one rank is facing off against a trainee from Hwangryong Martial Hall’s basic White Dragon Class! If it were me, I’d be too ashamed to reveal my rank.”

Geom Mugeuk stood at the very back, provoking and mocking him with words, but Baek Il-sa did not take the bait.

He faced Ian cautiously. As he always had, he intended to kill them all, one by one. In the end, the one left alive would be him.

Swaaaaaak!

Sword energy burst out from Baek Il-sa’s sword in a surprise attack.

Boom!

The sword energy released from Ian’s sword collided with Baek Il-sa’s. Dust rose with an ear-splitting explosion.

Clang, clang, clang, clang!

As sword met sword, sparks flared within the dust.

Baek Il-sa was strong. He was on a different level from the two who had died earlier.

Baek Il-sa’s martial art was a sword art that had crawled up a tower of murder and reached its peak.

But Ian’s Bicheon Sword Art, which she had mastered, was a martial art that such a tower of killing intent could not withstand even if it reached the heavens.

Her Bicheon Sword Art was completely different from the Hwangryong Sword Art she had displayed earlier, and also different from the Bicheon Sword Art that Geom Mugeuk used.

Her swordsmanship was delicate and graceful, and at the same time sharp and powerful.

Astonishingly, when Baek Il-sa exchanged the first move with her, he already sensed his defeat.

He thought it would have been nice if Baek Pal-sa and Baek O-sa were still alive. They needed to see this fight so they could think, Ah! So this is why we lost.

No, even if they saw it, they would not understand. They would not understand just how strong this woman was.

Shaaaaaak.

Her sword cut through the air, drawing a dazzling trajectory he could not possibly follow.

In that instant, he saw it. In Ian’s eyes, the countless people he had killed flashed past like a revolving lantern.

Shiiik.

Thrust!

Even though the sword had pierced through his chest, he felt no pain.

His final words were neither a plea, nor hatred, nor a threat.

“Perfect.”

His final words were praise for Ian.

With those words, Baek Ilsa toppled over and died.

Ian was bewildered. She had thought it would be a much harder fight than this. Why else would she have sent Geom Mugeuk a voice transmission like that?

―Is this man really ranked first in the White Snake Squad?

Geom Mugeuk’s pleased reply came back.

―Do you understand your own skill now?

Ian’s level was not something the likes of the White Snake Squad could do anything about.

Yeon Baekjin stared blankly at Baek Ilsa’s corpse. That woman had killed the top-ranked master of the White Snake Squad?

In a trembling voice, Yeon Baekjin thanked Ian. He no longer spoke down to her carelessly.

“Thank you for saving me.”

“Great Hero Yeon, you tried to save our Seojin too, didn’t you?”

Rather than joy at having survived, Yeon Baekjin felt dejected. He had not known his own brother was trying to kill him, and he had failed to recognize that this woman was such an extraordinary master.

‘I deserved to die.’

His gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

Ever since they had met, the man had given him a strange feeling. Perhaps he was an even greater master than this woman.

“You killed their number one. The White Snake Squad won’t let this pass. The entire White Snake Squad will come swarming in!”

At Yeon Baekjin’s worry, Geom Mugeuk smiled in a way whose meaning was hard to read.

“As life goes on, all kinds of animals may come to Hwangryong Martial Hall. But the white snake will never come again.”

“How can you be so certain?”

Geom Mugeuk said to Ian,

“Send word to the White Snake Squad. Tell them not to interfere in our affairs anymore.”

“Yes.”

Yeon Baekjin asked in a trembling voice,

“Who in the world are you people?”

Was he saying that the fearsome White Snake Squad would stay out of it simply because they were told to?

Only then did Geom Mugeuk reveal his identity.

“I am Geom Mugeuk, Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“!”

In that instant, Yeon Baekjin froze.

Ian and Seojin also revealed who they were.

“I am Ian, Captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

“I am Seojin, Second Squad Leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

Yeon Baekjin turned deathly pale and could not say a word.

Could one believe that a blood relative of the Ghost Gate was serving merely as a squad leader in some unit? Yes, if it were only one place, it could be believed.

A person who traveled with the greatest beauty under heaven, a woman who could easily kill the top-ranked master of the White Snake Squad? Yes, if it were only one person, it could be believed.

‘Even so, the Young Cult Leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult!’

He had treated him so carelessly until now.

Toward that confusion and disbelief, Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly.

“Whether I disappear from your life like smoke, or become a vicious Young Cult Leader, is now up to you.”

Chapter 580: You Turned Your Back Twice
---------------------------------------
Yeon Baekjin had never once imagined that, in his life, he would ever meet the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult.

Now the three people before him looked completely different.

And the first question that came to mind was this. His words and manner had become exceedingly courteous.

“Why is the young cult leader of the Demonic… no, of the Divine Cult here?”

Because of you.

To keep you from living out the fate of killing Seojin.

“As Great Hero Yeon knows, I came because of our Squad Leader Seo.”

The young cult leader himself had moved for the sake of a mere squad leader? At the natural doubt that appeared on Yeon Baekjin’s face, Geom Mugeuk looked at Seojin and said,

“Squad Leader Seo is someone special to me.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Seojin bowed his head politely. He felt it from time to time. That the young cult leader gave him an affection far beyond what he deserved. Yet it was clearly not the fondness one felt toward someone of the opposite sex. Was it because he cherished Ian so much?

Yeon Baekjin looked at Seojin.

Then that meant his elder brother had killed the hometown elder brother of a squad leader of the Demonic Cult’s Ghost Shadow Unit. How close the two were did not matter. What mattered was that, because of it, the young cult leader of the Demonic Cult had come here.

For the young cult leader to have come was no different from saying that the entire Demonic Cult had come. With a single word from him, the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall could be wiped out overnight.

Yeon Baekjin’s body trembled faintly. He had lived thinking himself rather bold, but he could not hide the fear that came rushing in.

Yeon Baekjin asked cautiously,

“What should I do?”

Instead of answering, Geom Mugeuk threw out a question.

“What do you intend to do about your brother?”

For an instant, Yeon Baekjin’s expression hardened.
