Since his brother had tried to kill him, the answer was already there. He was not the sort of person who would meekly let himself be killed simply because the person trying to kill him was his brother.

“It’s not as though I can die for that, can I?”

He had no choice but to become ruthless as well.

At Yeon Baekjin’s answer, Ian thought of Geom Mugeuk and the Eldest Young Master.

Even in a mere martial hall, such bloodshed between kin could not be avoided, yet he had brought the succession struggle of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult to a peaceful end. Only now did Ian feel anew just how much effort Geom Mugeuk must have made.

Yeon Baekjin remembered that Geom Mugeuk knew about the place he was trying to find.

“Could it be that the young cult leader is also trying to find that place?”

It was a question born from his inability to truly believe that he had come all this way purely for the sake of a mere squad leader.

“That’s right. I intend to resolve that matter as well.”

Yeon Baekjin’s fear grew even greater. The Demonic Cult had its eyes on the very place with which his brother was currently obsessed. Would they really leave them alive? The Demonic Cult he knew was not such a place. Every story he had heard about the Demonic Cult from childhood until now had been stained with blood. Beneath those handsome and beautiful faces, monsters must be hiding.

But what changed his mind were the corpses of the White Serpent Corps strewn across the floor.

‘If not for them, my corpse would be over there.’

Thinking that his life had been as good as forfeit anyway, his fear subsided.

His gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk.

“What must I do for you to become not a vicious young cult leader, but one who disappears like smoke?”

“Two things will suffice.”

“What are they?”

“Be honest. And trust us.”

Yeon Baekjin was silent for a moment. Now that he knew who the other party was, neither of those two things was easy.

“I cannot say I trust you yet, but I will speak honestly.”

That honesty came half from gratitude for saving his life, and half from fear.

“My brother is currently possessed by something. It must be connected to the secret construction. And what he was promised in return…”

He had grasped the situation accurately.

“It is certainly a martial arts manual.”

“Why do you think it is a manual?”

“My brother could not stand martial artists looking down on our martial hall’s martial arts. He would not have been able to resist the temptation of a peerless divine art.”

Geom Mugeuk combined all the information he had gathered so far and reached a conclusion.

“There is a treasury beneath the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall where a peerless divine art is kept, and the hall members who left without graduating despite having the ability are digging there?”

And the one managing that place was the Sword King.

With the Sword King’s martial prowess, he would not go to such lengths for a mere martial arts manual.

‘What is it that you want?’

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened. Clearly, the Sword King was trying to use him to do something.

Even if he gave in and moved according to the Sword King’s intentions, he had to stay one step ahead. If only he could sneak into that place.

“Is there anyone besides your brother who might know about that place?”

“There is one person. Giseok, one of the Seven Masters, is my brother’s arms and legs. He should know something. However, his martial arts…”

He was about to say that Giseok was far stronger than the other masters, but stopped. Just from seeing the martial arts of the woman accompanying him, he could tell what the young cult leader’s martial prowess must be like.

“His loyalty to my brother runs deep, so he will never open his mouth.”

“Then Giseok must have been the one who brought in the White Serpent Corps.”

“My brother would not have contacted them directly, so that must have been the case.”

Though Yeon Baekjin answered his questions obediently, there was one part on which he could not compromise.

“My brother… I will take care of my brother’s matter myself.”

He had resolved to kill him with his own hands. He did not want his brother to be used miserably and then killed by those bastards. Nor did he want him killed by the Demonic Cult.

If he could not kill him, he was prepared to die together with him.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been quietly gazing at him, said something unexpected.

“Meet your brother again.”

Yeon Baekjin was startled.

“Did you not see? He called the White Serpent Corps to kill me. And their highest-ranked expert at that! If not for you, I would have died here today.”

“That is why I am telling you to meet him.”

Yeon Baekjin guessed the reason like this.

“You mean to use my brother’s guilt! If I go to him now, he will think the White Serpent Corps has not yet carried out the commission. Since I am a younger brother who will die soon anyway, he might tell me something.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“That is not it. If he were the sort of person who would let information slip out of guilt, he would not have called the White Serpent Corps in the first place.”

“If not that, then why?”

“Go and say everything you want to say. Only then will you have no regrets as you live on.”

For a moment, Yeon Baekjin wore a blank expression.

‘What? He’s saying this for my sake?’

Yeon Baekjin could not believe it. No, he could not understand it.

“You are asking me to believe that, in a situation like this, you are looking after me? What am I to you?”

At that, Geom Mugeuk brought up something Yeon Baekjin had forgotten.

“You showed your back twice. Once to us, and once to the enemy. Both times, it was to protect Seojin.”

The first time, when he had stood in front of Seojin. The next, when he had blocked the hidden weapon for him.

“What are you, you ask? You are a person willing to give your life to protect my subordinate.”

It was truly consideration purely for him.

“If things end without you saying what you need to say, you will think of your brother for the rest of your life. Even if you live on as the master of the Yellow Dragon Hall, there will be a shadow over your heart forever.”

Of course, there was another reason as well.

If Yeon Baekjin lived brightly, that would be a good thing for Seojin too. Geom Mugeuk was still trying until the very end. To him, Seojin was worth that much.

“With the relationship between the two of you, you may regret it whether you speak or not, but this time, the regret of not speaking will be greater. So not for your brother’s sake, but for your own, go say everything and come back.”

* * *

Yeon Baekin stood by the window of his office, gazing at the night scenery.

Though it was late, lights were lit throughout the martial hall, and figures could be seen training until late.

“They arrive today?”

At Yeon Baekin’s question, Giseok, who stood behind him, answered respectfully.

“Yes. They should have already arrived.”

Yeon Baekin asked no further, but Giseok spoke in place of what was in his heart.

“Great Hero Yeon will likely find it difficult to see tomorrow morning’s sun.”

Yeon Baekin’s expression hardened reflexively, then he suddenly recalled the Sword King’s words.

—A villain is one who keeps walking a single path while being cursed.

Yes, there was no need to be ashamed. Trying not to be cursed was, rather, the more shameful sight.

“Seeing how thick the stars are, tomorrow will be clear.”

A smile formed at the corners of Giseok’s mouth. He considered this sort of leader far better than one who was indecisive and pretended to be kind.

“Has that man still been going to the White Dragon Class training lately?”

“He has gone every day recently. They say he has taught a Yellow Dragon Sword Art entirely different from the existing one.”

Giseok added cautiously,

“I do not know what on earth he is thinking.”

He had said it in hopes of hearing whether Yeon Baekin knew what he was thinking, but Yeon Baekin said nothing. He had not told Giseok about the demon the man had spoken of.

Just then, a subordinate’s voice was heard from outside.

“Your younger brother has come to see you.”

Yeon Baekin and Giseok looked at each other. At this late hour, it was an unexpected visit.

“Shall I stay?”

At Giseok’s question, Yeon Baekin shook his head.

“Show him in.”

When the door opened, Yeon Baekjin entered.

As he came inside, the smell of alcohol wafted strongly from him. He held a liquor bottle in one hand. The moment he saw Giseok, instead of greeting him, he spoke like a drunken man.

“Our Master Gi’s tail must have worn away to nothing.”

It was a rude remark, but Giseok showed no sign of displeasure at all. Rather, he smiled and said,

“I only wish my loyalty truly reached that extent. Then, please talk.”

After offering his respects, Giseok left the room.

One might have expected him to say a word about being rude to Giseok, but Yeon Baekin treated him more gently than usual.

“What brings you here at such a late hour?”

The instant he saw that, anger surged up in Yeon Baekjin. He had called the White Serpent Corps to kill him, and yet he made that face?

If he were human, should he not look at least a little flustered out of guilt? At the very least, should he not be as cold as usual?

Yeon Baekjin perched on the guest table and drank straight from the bottle. In doing so, he quelled the anger rising within him.

“Couldn’t sleep. Had something to say.”

The one who had advised him to have a drink before going was Geom Mugeuk. It would probably loosen his nerves. It had truly been wise to bring it. If he had not, he might have shouted just now.

Yeon Baekjin held out the liquor bottle.

“Want some?”

Yeon Baekin shook his head. Yeon Baekjin drank alone again.

“So, what is it you wanted to say?”

After sitting blankly at the table for a moment, Yeon Baekjin brought out the words he had thought of on the way here. They were also words he had kept in his heart for a long time. Words he spoke not for his brother, but for himself.

“To be honest, I thought I had yielded the position of hall master to you.”

Yeon Baekin listened to him in silence.

“Since I was young, I often heard that I had greater martial talent. I thought that if I set my mind to it, I could become hall master.”

Without looking at his brother, Yeon Baekjin continued speaking as he looked down at the liquor bottle in his hand.

“Of course, you are far stronger now, but even that, I thought, was because I had yielded. I deliberately stopped trying because I did not want to become stronger than you.”

Yeon Baekin had had no idea that his younger brother had been thinking this way. And this thought came to him: Is he insane?

“It was all my delusion. This martial hall was never mine, not even once.”

At his younger brother’s confession, Yeon Baekin spoke with a pleasant expression.

“I only thought of myself and failed to understand your heart. From now on…”

“From now on?”

“Let us get along. We are each other’s only brothers, are we not?”

Had his brother always been this brazen and cold-blooded?

“Thank you, Brother.”

For leaving me with this sight at the end.

Yeon Baekin could feel for himself that his heart was colder than ever. He felt as though he could say any number of kind words.

‘You should have acted like this from the start. You should have either lived pretending not to know, as you have until now, or helped me with loyalty like Giseok’s. Then I would not have had to do this, would I?’

But he knew his younger brother was not someone capable of that. Once he sobered up, he would begin investigating him from behind again.

“Let us at least eat together tomorrow.”

What meal? My funeral offering? At those words, Yeon Baekjin’s anger crossed its limit. Because of that, he instead smiled and asked,

“What kind of martial art is it?”

Yeon Baekin flinched in surprise.

“What do you mean?”

“I am asking what kind of martial art it is, the one you are possessed by.”

Yeon Baekjin asked with a smile, but his eyes were cold.

The smile vanished from Yeon Baekin’s face.

Yeon Baekjin could tell. This martial art was like a reverse scale to his brother. Well, of course it was. He must have staked everything on it.

“You have not received that martial arts manual yet, have you? They said they would give it to you once you finished digging up that treasury, didn’t they?”

Yeon Baekin’s expression grew increasingly stiff, but Yeon Baekjin kept pressing him.

“Who in the world would just hand over a peerless divine art? And you believe them when they say they will? Brother, are you an idiot?”

Yeon Baekin did not lose his temper to the end. After all, once his younger brother left this room, he would never see him again. Rather, this was good. Even the faint trace of guilt he had felt toward his brother vanished with this.

Yeon Baekjin was truly furious. To be deceived by such a person, to call the White Serpent Corps to kill his own younger brother. In the end, he would die by Yeon Baekjin’s hand—Brother, are you truly such a fool!

Yeon Baekjin rose from his seat and, feeling stifled, flung open the office window.

He stood by the window like that for a long while, staring outside.

“If you are done talking, leave.”

Yeon Baekjin glanced once at his brother, then left the place without a word.

Left alone, Yeon Baekin opened the wall cabinet and took out a liquor bottle. It was an expensive liquor he normally savored.

After filling a pure white cup to the brim, he drank it down.

“Now get out of my life.”

* * *

When Yeon Baekjin met Geom Mugeuk again, he was excited.

“This is only my feeling, but I think I have found where the entrance is.”

A short while ago, when he had stood by the window of his brother’s office and looked outside, he had seen the night view of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall.

There were brightly lit places, and there were places less bright. Even so, lights were lit here and there, but there was one place that was particularly dark. Since he had never had the chance to stand there at night and look outside, he had forgotten that place existed. It was a place that, normally, every member of the hall forgot.

A place directly visible from the office, yet used only once a year. A place considered sacred in the martial hall, so no one could approach it in ordinary times. And above all, the place his brother valued most.

“It is the sparring arena where the graduation exam is held.”

Chapter 581: You Haven’t Forgotten My Request, Right?
-----------------------------------------------------
The area around the dueling platform was dark.

The one who descended soundlessly onto the stone floor, scarred with countless marks, was Geom Mugeuk.

Before coming down here, Geom Mugeuk had released his qi in all directions. Countless strands of qi spread out like a spiderweb, searching to see whether anyone was hiding nearby.

In Yeon Baegin’s unlit office, visible from here, in the other windows of the building, and in the darkness around this dueling platform, there was no one concealed at all. That meant the entrance was well hidden.

First, he climbed onto the dueling platform and examined the floor, but he could not find the entrance.

He had not thought from the beginning that the entrance would be on the dueling platform. If a fight grew intense, the ground could split or be gouged open and expose the entrance, so they would not have made it in a place like this.

If the person who made the entrance did choose such a place? If it was someone who placed meaning in symbolism?

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the seats of honor in the stands. There was a separate area prepared where the hall master, masters, and instructors sat to watch. With the large, ornate hall master’s seat at the center, the seats for the masters and instructors were arranged to the left and right.

Geom Mugeuk examined the hall master’s seat first. The large chair, made like a grand master’s chair, was fixed to the floor.

‘It has to be here.’

Through all the experiences he had before his regression, Geom Mugeuk had become quite well versed in mechanical devices.

The most important mindset when searching for a mechanism was to be certain that it existed. Strangely enough, if one was half in doubt, a hidden device would never reveal itself. Only when one searched with the conviction that it was definitely here would it be seen.

Geom Mugeuk slowly ran his fingers over everything, even beneath the chair, meticulously inspecting even the finest crack.

After searching the hall master’s seat, he examined the next chair, the master’s chair. He searched and searched again, then moved on to the next chair.

Then, at the third chair, he felt a faint crack. A circular crack. It was such a fine crack that one would not even sense it unless one believed it had to be there.

Geom Mugeuk slowly pressed down on that spot.

He felt it sink inward slightly, but no entrance was revealed.

An ordinary person would have assumed this was not it and moved on, but Geom Mugeuk knew.

‘It’s a door opened by a double mechanism. This is the first device.’

Geom Mugeuk returned to the hall master’s seat.

Just as he sat in the chair and began examining this and that again, the yellow dragon ornament on the armrest, which had not moved earlier, turned sideways. This time, too, only very slightly.

At that very moment!

With a smooth sliding sound, the lower section opened, and while he remained seated in the chair, it descended.

When he rose from the chair, the chair returned to its original place, and as the ceiling door closed, the surroundings became pitch-black.

Geom Mugeuk used the Divine Eye Art and examined the area. When he pressed the device right beside the place where he had descended, the ceiling opened again and the chair came down. After enough time had passed for someone to sit in the chair, it automatically rose again. It was a top-grade mechanism, operating with truly refined and smooth precision.

Geom Mugeuk looked around again. It was a space of only about three or four pyeong, and on one wall there was a mechanism that led underground.

Geom Mugeuk carefully walked toward the mechanism. Since he did not know what the situation below was like, he did not rashly board it.

Geom Mugeuk examined the gaps in the mechanism. If there was a space through which he could descend, he intended to jump down through it. No matter how deep it was, with his martial prowess, he would be able to land without making a sound.

But the mechanism here had been designed so that anyone going down had no choice but to ride this device. If he boarded it, it would certainly be arranged so that those below would know someone was coming down.

It was just then.

Sssrrrk.

The door above opened again, and the chair descended. Astonishingly, the one who came down was the Sword King. He held an armful of items in both hands—wine and meat.

After stepping off the chair, he climbed onto the mechanism.

Standing in the place the Sword King was looking was Geom Mugeuk within the Space-Time Transposition Art. The instant the ceiling opened, Geom Mugeuk had vanished at the same time.

Because Geom Mugeuk could see outside, he was watching the Sword King, but the Sword King was merely looking into an empty space.

Inside and outside the mechanism, the two men stood facing each other.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze went to his feet. He was wearing the shoes Geom Mugeuk had given him as a gift.

Soon after, the mechanism carrying the Sword King descended.

Wooooong.

Only after he rode the mechanism down did Geom Mugeuk emerge from the Space-Time Transposition Art and reveal himself.

Geom Mugeuk silently looked down into the endless darkness where the mechanism had descended.

‘What are you searching for down there?’

* * *

When Geom Mugeuk returned, Yeon Baekjin was waiting in the guest room with Ian and Seojin.

“The entrance was there.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Yeon Baekjin’s expression brightened. His intuition had been correct.

“Knowing my brother’s personality, I thought he would have put the entrance somewhere visible.”

“You found it well.”

In the current situation, Yeon Baekjin was glad that he had personally discovered a place that would greatly influence his fate.

“Let us go in there together at once. I will go with you.”

Yeon Baekjin wanted to enter that place himself. He wanted to see whether an unparalleled divine art truly existed there.

But Geom Mugeuk firmly shook his head.

“Even though we know the entrance, we cannot enter rashly.”

The reason was something Yeon Baekjin had not expected.

“There is an expert near your brother whom even I cannot handle carelessly.”

Naturally, Yeon Baekjin was startled. There was an expert whom even the young cult leader of the Demon Cult could not do anything about? Had he ever seen such a person in the martial hall?

At that moment, as if by a lie, one person came to Yeon Baekjin’s mind.

“Could it be that temporary instructor?”

He did not know why that man came to mind. But the person he had seen in his brother’s office kept bothering him. That very man who had taught strange techniques to the members of the White Dragon Class.

“That’s right. It’s him. As long as he is there, killing your brother is impossible. If we move rashly, you would be the first to die instead.”

“Then why did he not order that man to kill me from the start? There would have been no need to call the White Snake Group.”

“Because he is not someone your brother can treat carelessly. He is probably more afraid of him than of the White Snake Group.”

Yeon Baekjin felt suffocated. His brother was trying to kill him, and around that brother was an expert whom even the young cult leader of the Demon Cult had to be cautious of.

Ian asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What will you do?”

“We have to go in when that man is not there.”

So first he had to find out what the Sword King was trying to excavate. If possible, he had to seize it first. This was a separate matter from his personal feelings toward the Sword King.

If the Sword King was putting this much effort into it, then it would certainly be something extremely threatening to the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

On his way back here, Geom Mugeuk had already made one plan.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Yeon Baekjin and said,

“You will have to die. If the one ranked first in the White Snake Group came to kill you and you did not die, would that not be strange?”

Was he finally revealing his true colors? As Yeon Baekjin was shocked out of his wits, Geom Mugeuk followed with an incomprehensible action.

Geom Mugeuk asked Ian and Seojin,

“Which of you draws snakes better?”

* * *

When Giseok awoke and opened his eyes, someone was looking down at him.

It was a man wearing a mask with a bamboo hat pulled low, and through the parted collar of his clothes, a coiled white snake could be seen.

‘The White Snake Group!’

Of course, the man disguised as a member of the White Snake Group was none other than Geom Mugeuk. The person who had drawn the picture on his chest was Ian. Naturally, Seojin had assisted from the side. It needs to coil one more time, the tail should rise higher. When the two women put their heads together, a fairly convincing white snake was born.

Giseok struggled to maintain his composure. The man had probably not come to kill him. If he had, Giseok would never have opened his eyes.

“How did the commission go?”

At Giseok’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered in a low voice.

“It ended well.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke in a deep, altered voice.

“But why have you come to me? I heard the White Snake Group does not normally contact clients.”

“This time is an exception.”

Geom Mugeuk went to a chair placed in the corner and sat down. In the meantime, Giseok sat up on the bed. The other party had not moved Giseok’s sword, which stood beside the bed. That meant he was that confident in his skill.

“Yeon Baekjin said something strange before he died.”

At those words, Giseok’s heart sank, but he answered as if it were nothing.

“When a man is about to die, what would he not say?”

Geom Mugeuk quoted the words of Baek Osa of the White Snake Group, who had died.

“Usually, they show obvious reactions. They threaten, hate, or beg. But Yeon Baekjin said something else.”

The words Giseok desperately hoped would not come out flowed from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“He said you people were digging up treasure underground?”

Inwardly, Giseok was furious.

‘Damn it! He should have just died, but he had to spout useless nonsense.’

For now, Giseok flatly denied it.

“I do not know what you mean.”

Geom Mugeuk said calmly,

“It was strange from the start. Calling me, the one ranked first in the White Snake Group, just to kill the younger brother of a mere martial hall master.”

Giseok was startled again.

‘So the one who came this time was the top-ranked man of the White Snake Sect.’

He knew that the hall master had asked the White Snake Group to handle this matter thoroughly. Even so, they had sent the one ranked first?

This had happened because the leader of the White Snake Group had felt an inexplicable sense of foreboding, but Giseok misunderstood it like this.

‘They sent this man to threaten us and extort money.’

They had looked down on them because they were a martial hall. From the reputation of being the Central Plains’ Number One Martial Hall, what they had sensed was the smell of money. They must have come to squeeze more money out of naive martial hall fools, and Yeon Baekjin had ended up saying that.

What could one expect from those who killed people for money? They were people who should never have been entangled with from the start.

Sure enough, the other party poured out a cold killing intent and demanded money.

“Since I moved personally, I must receive an additional three hundred thousand taels.”

At that absurd demand, Giseok predicted how this matter would proceed.

‘Because of this greed, both the top-ranked man and the hall master will all be replaced.’

They would never dream what kind of extraordinary expert was on this side.

“This is not something I can decide. First, I must report to the hall master.”

Geom Mugeuk coldly delivered his words and turned away.

“Bring three hundred thousand taels to the White Snake Group’s main headquarters within five days. For every day you are late, I will add another hundred thousand taels.”

* * *

After receiving an urgent message, the Sword King entered Yeon Baegin’s office.

From Yeon Baegin’s expression alone, he could already tell that a serious problem had arisen. The Sword King pretended not to know and sat down in a chair, saying,

“I drank with the children yesterday. I think I have rested enough now, so I was considering resuming work. I have also roughly chosen the members to take underground.”

Yeon Baegin said cautiously,

“Before you go, there is one urgent matter I must ask of you.”

“Speak.”

Yeon Baegin spoke in as composed a tone as possible.

“My younger brother was killed by the White Snake Group.”

The Sword King seemed to have expected this situation and was not surprised at all.

Yeon Baegin had expected him to look at him with eyes like these.

Was he not killed by the White Snake Group, but by you? I told you to say proudly that you killed him!

But the Sword King before him wore an oddly inscrutable expression. There was even a smile at the corners of his mouth. What was certain was that it was not a sneer directed at him.

“But a problem has arisen. Before that bastard died, he revealed the existence of the underground treasury to a member of the White Snake Group.”

It was something that should have become a major problem, but the Sword King remained calm.

“What did they demand?”

“Three hundred thousand taels.”

Yeon Baegin took a thick envelope from his bosom and placed it in front of the Sword King.

“Here is three hundred thousand taels.”

He was not asking him to take this money to them. He was asking him to accept this money and deal with them. Their demands would not end at three hundred thousand taels.

“Normally, I would have handled it myself. But in the current situation, I do not have the time to find people who can deal with the White Snake Group.”

What he feared even more was that he might draw in another wolf.

Yet he could not simply leave them alone. The White Snake Group, having smelled money, would never let this pass.

“I am ashamed to have to ask this of you.”

Yeon Baegin lowered his head.

The Sword King stared at him with an unreadable expression, then smiled.

“When one undertakes a great task, flies are bound to gather. I will handle it.”

If anything, it was Yeon Baegin who was flustered. He had not expected him to accept so readily. He had steeled himself to be cursed at, and had even been prepared to kneel.

Yet the Sword King even smiled as he tucked the envelope containing three hundred thousand taels into his robe and rose from his seat.

“When do you intend to leave?”

The Sword King asked as they stepped out of the office.

“I should finish my class first.”

* * *

“Today is my final lesson.”

The Sword King had everyone perform the Yellow Dragon Sword Form. Since most of them had not learned it properly, they performed the original Yellow Dragon Sword Form instead.

After the trainees had gone through the Yellow Dragon Sword Art in a flurry, the Sword King said to them,

“There are only a few of you who listened diligently to my lessons.”

He pointed out several people. Geom Mugeuk, Ian, Seojin, and even Yugwang and Gyoseok, who had followed along earnestly.

“Only the five of you learned it properly.”

The gazes the other trainees cast at them were far from kind. They believed those five had followed along with such absurd forms either because they were infatuated with a woman, or because they wanted to curry favor with this temporary instructor.

“You will come with me. I’ll show you a new world.”

The Sword King turned on his heel and strode away. He did not even bid farewell to the remaining trainees.

The five who had been singled out followed after him.

The place the Sword King arrived at was the sparring platform for the Yellow Dragon Class’s exit examination. Climbing onto the platform, the Sword King asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Where did everyone else go, and why are you the only one here?”

The only person who had arrived there was Geom Mugeuk.

“I sent them a voice transmission telling them to run. By now, Ian should be taking them to a safe house inside the main school.”

Geom Mugeuk also climbed onto the platform and stood before him.

“Why, all of a sudden?”

The Sword King feigned ignorance, but Geom Mugeuk knew what he was thinking.

“Because I realized that you had seen through my plan, Instructor.”

There was only one reason he had come here, of all places, and had brought not only him but all the other trainees as well. He had intended to take them all underground.

For Geom Mugeuk, that situation would have been an immense burden.

“After all, one shield cannot protect four people, can it?”

The Sword King burst into loud laughter. He did not seem the least bit disappointed that the other four had not followed. If anything, his expression suggested he had expected things to turn out this way to some extent.

“If you had been on our side, we would have achieved our goal long ago.”

Geom Mugeuk did not ask what that goal was. Their relationship had not yet reached the point where he would tell him. That question, at least, had to be saved. So that when he asked it just once, the question would have power.

The Sword King took an envelope from his robe and handed it to Geom Mugeuk.

“What is this?”

“You said you wanted it, didn’t you?”

When he opened it, there were three hundred thousand taels inside.

“You’re giving it to me even after realizing my plan?”

“It’s not my money anyway, is it? I can act like I did that man a favor by handling this matter, and act like I did you a favor by giving you the money.”

As expected, this man was no ordinary person.

“If I’d known this would happen, I should have asked for around a million taels.”

“Then you’d only have had more regrets. You’ll die before you can spend it all anyway.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed as he tucked the money away. It seemed the shoes he had given him were worth three hundred thousand taels.

“But how did you know? That I was the one who set this up.”

“I knew the moment the hall master told me Yeon Baekjin was dead.”

This was exactly why.

“You’re not the sort of man who lets someone you’ve formed a bond with die so easily, are you? There’s no way Yeon Baekjin would have died to the likes of the White Snake Corps.”

He had understood him precisely.

“You think far too highly of me.”

“I’m still underestimating you. That’s why I’m leaving you alive like this.”

And that sentiment was also connected to the reason he had called Geom Mugeuk here today.

“You haven’t forgotten that you agreed to grant me a request, have you?”

“Of course not.”

What kind of request was he going to make?

Since they were speaking atop the sparring platform, Geom Mugeuk had a feeling it would be a request related to fighting.

But the Sword King’s request was something he had truly never expected.

“Dig the ground with me.”

Chapter 582: Take Off Your Shoes First, Then Speak?
---------------------------------------------------
Go underground together?

At a time when hiding it deliberately still wouldn’t have been enough, he wanted to dig for the underground treasure vault together?

“Are you serious?”

Even without asking, even if the man had not nodded like that right before his eyes, Geom Mugeuk could feel that he meant it. It seemed he had intended to go underground with him from the very beginning.

“Why are you trying to take me with you?”

Naturally, it had to be because he needed him.

The problem was what would happen when he found what he was looking for.

There was no way he intended to share it with him, so did it mean he would use him to his heart’s content and kill him then? Was he not worried about the possibility of having it taken from him?

The Sword King pointed a finger at Geom Mugeuk’s chest.

“Because of that.”

When Geom Mugeuk lowered his head, a white snake drawn on his chest showed between the open lapels of his robe. It seemed a waste to erase the snake Ian had drawn for him, so he had not wiped it off yet.

“I didn’t know you liked snakes.”

Of course, he knew very well that the man had not said it because he liked snakes or the White Snake Squad.

“You found the entrance down, didn’t you?”

At the Sword King’s question, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“Yes, I found it.”

Geom Mugeuk walked over to where the chairs were arranged and operated the first mechanism hidden beneath the seats meant for the instructors.

Then, after sitting back down in the Hall Master’s seat, he operated the armrest.

Srrrk.

Geom Mugeuk descended together with the chair, then rose back up again.

In the meantime, the Sword King had come to stand before the Hall Master’s seat. Looking down at Geom Mugeuk, the Sword King did not hide the admiration that crossed his face.

“You found this entrance, which is difficult to locate, and in that short span of time even carried out a scheme to send me outside. I need a clever man like you. I’m in need of clever shoveling.”

“If that’s the case, you’ve chosen extremely well.”

“I do have a good eye for people.”

The Sword King sat down in the instructor’s seat beside Geom Mugeuk.

The two of them stared wordlessly at the empty training ground.

“Who drew that picture for you?”

“My heart drew it for me.”

He had already introduced Ian to him as someone like his heart.

“It was quite a good idea. If I had left my post for even a moment, there’s no telling what you might have done down below.”

That was not the only praise the Sword King gave.

“And on your way here, with instantaneous judgment, you let the four who could have become hostages escape.”

“I know you wouldn’t have taken them hostage.”

“Even so, it would have bothered you. That alone would have been enough.”

Both of their gazes were still turned toward the empty sparring platform.

“I understand that you promised the Hall Master an peerless divine art. Then what will you give me?”

“Greedy, aren’t you! You’ve already learned the greatest martial art under heaven.”

“You may not have known, but my nickname is the Incarnation of Greed.”

After a brief pause, the Sword King spoke.

“You said it yourself. That you wanted to become stronger.”

What he intended to give was also truly unexpected.

“Down below, you will be able to become stronger.”

If that was true? Then you’ll die by my hand.

Geom Mugeuk sensed that he would not be able to hear anything more detailed from him.

“As expected, you are an excellent instructor.”

At last, Geom Mugeuk accepted his proposal.

“I made a promise, so I should keep it. Let’s go.”

“Aren’t you afraid? How do you know what’s down there?”

“There will be earth to dig and hall members exhausted from digging it.”

“There may also be an instructor who will lead you into a death trap.”

At last, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze, which had been fixed on the sparring platform, turned toward the Sword King.

“I am cautious, and I’m not the sort of person to stake my life over pride or curiosity. Even so, the reason I say I’ll go down is because there is one thing I believe.”

The Sword King’s gaze also remained on Geom Mugeuk as he waited for his answer.

“I believe that the place where you intend to fight me is, at the very least, not beneath this earth.”

And then came an unexpected reason.

“Because when you die, you’ll want to die looking at that sky, not at a suffocating ceiling of dirt.”

“!”

“The same goes for me. So let’s do our digging toward death or our schemes of drawing snakes on chests beneath this sky. No, how about we have our final fight atop that sparring platform? Both you and I are people of this martial hall, aren’t we?”

The Sword King, looking at Geom Mugeuk, said nothing.

“Now then, the clever shovel is going down.”

Geom Mugeuk descended with a srrrk.

As he went down into the space below, Geom Mugeuk’s eyes deepened. He had spoken that way, but he must not forget for even a single moment that the man could kill him down here.

Originally, it was an adventure he would not have taken. But this time, he thought he might be able to obtain information through the Sword King about Hwa Mugi and that organization. He judged it worth taking the risk. And he had also prepared something, just in case.

As Geom Mugeuk waited in the space below, the Sword King also descended on the chair.

The two of them climbed onto the mechanism that led underground. Once they boarded, it was packed full.

Wooooong.

The mechanism carrying the two began to descend.

Facing forward, the Sword King said,

“You’re too sentimental.”

His face, reflected in the metal of the mechanism, was smiling. Geom Mugeuk’s smiling face was reflected there as well.

“Say that after you take off those shoes, why don’t you?”

Yes, for now, these moments of enjoyment with him were still pleasant.

Because we had not yet peered into the pitch-black holes in each other’s hearts. Because we still did not know what we would see in that abyss.

And so the mechanism carrying the two of them continued descending into the darkness.

* * *

“Where are you taking us?”

In front of the martial hall, Gyo Seok asked Ian. They had been following the instructor, and then suddenly Ian and Seo Jin had taken them out of the martial hall. In that instant, even their mute acupoints and internal energy had been sealed, and they had been dragged along without being able to resist once. He had truly thought they were being kidnapped.

Only after they left the martial hall did Ian apologize and release their mute acupoints and internal energy.

“You’ll know when we get there.”

Unlike the anxious Gyo Seok, Yu Gwang obediently followed where Ian led.

They soon met a man hiding in a private house on the outskirts, a little away from the marketplace. It was Yeon Baekjin, who had been treated as having been killed by the White Snake Squad this time.

“Great Hero Yeon!”

Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok were startled to see Yeon Baekjin hiding in a place like this. Only then did they realize that something serious had happened.

Of course, they had still expected it to be something like a conflict between brothers over the position of Hall Master, but from Ian came words they had never imagined at all.

“We are going to our cult’s safe house right now.”

Our cult? Safe house?

At those unfamiliar words, Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok looked at each other, their eyes round. They were words they knew, but had never once used in reality or heard directly.

To the shocked pair, Ian spoke calmly.

“I’m sorry you got dragged into this. But from now on, you have to listen to me.”

Whether they wanted to listen or not, they had to. The aura Ian revealed was not that of an ordinary member of the White Dragon Class.

They had already thought she was no ordinary person, but now she was exuding an even greater presence than Yeon Baekjin, the Hall Master’s younger brother.

A carriage arrived there. It was driven by a farmer who looked ordinary. He looked so ordinary that, on the contrary, he felt extraordinary. In this situation, there was no way an ordinary farmer would come.

When the five of them climbed into the rear compartment of the shabby cargo wagon, which did not even have windows, the carriage set off.

Gyo Seok asked cautiously,

“What in the world is going on?”

If this had been the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult before Geom Mugeuk, they would already have been loaded in after even their paralysis and sleep acupoints had been sealed. Even if their lives had been saved, they would not have known who the other party was or why this was happening to them.

But fortunately, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult they were experiencing was the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult after Geom Mugeuk.

“A martial world conspiracy is underway here at the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall. We came to resolve that matter.”

Yu Gwang turned to look at Gyo Seok with an expression that said, See?

But right now, Gyo Seok had no time to pay attention to his conspiracy-theorist friend.

“Who are you people?”

Ian answered calmly and plainly.

“You’ll find out when we arrive.”

Gyo Seok was about to ask where that place was, but Yu Gwang, beside him, tugged at his sleeve and stopped him.

“We go where destiny leads!”

More like where the pretty woman leads!

That pretty woman, like destiny itself, quietly closed her eyes and recalled Geom Mugeuk’s sound transmission that she had heard at the last moment.

—There is an entrance beneath the Hall Master’s seat at the sparring platform. Go to the safe house and relay this message.

At the same time, he had transmitted another message to her.

After hearing all of it, Ian had been puzzled.

Just who was she supposed to relay this important message to? A warrior of the safe house? Or was someone waiting at the safe house?

How long had they ridden like that? After changing carriages twice and checking whether they were being followed, the carriage at last entered a manor.

When they arrived there, only Ian and Seo Jin sensed that they had even passed through a formation. Rather, Seo Jin sensed it more accurately.

Because Seo Jin had learned ghost arts, she was also well versed in formations, so she knew more precisely how remarkable the formation they had just passed through was.

The Martial Alliance and the Evil Path Alliance were likely the same, but in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult as well, safe houses in particular were protected with utmost thoroughness.

In the courtyard of the safe house, a man stood with his back turned.

At the sight of that familiar broad back, Ian flinched in surprise. Soon, her expression brightened radiantly. The person she had missed so dearly was waiting there.

“Father!”

The person who turned toward her was none other than the Fist Demon.

“Ian.”

Ian ran over and threw herself into the Fist Demon’s arms. When she had left the cult and returned to the main cult, she had barely managed to exchange greetings before parting from him, and now they had met again like this.

On the Fist Demon’s fearsome face blossomed a welcome permitted to only one person.

Of course, the other three were terrified by the Fist Demon’s frightening appearance. Even Seo Jin was overawed by his imposing looks. It was her first time meeting a Demon Venerable as well.

‘He’s the captain’s father? How could a daughter like that come from a face like his?’

Not only Seo Jin, but everyone looked back and forth between the two with similar thoughts.

At that moment, a woman walked out of the building. She was a woman with beautiful features that were completely opposite to the Fist Demon’s.

With deep eyes that suited her snow-white hair perfectly, she was none other than the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Seeing her, Ian cupped her fist respectfully and greeted her.

“I greet the Sword Venerable!”

“Captain Lee, it’s good to see you.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable welcomed Ian with a smile. She knew very well how much Geom Mugeuk cherished Ian, and now Ian was the Fist Demon’s daughter. Naturally, the gaze she turned toward Ian was gentle.

The presence of the two Demon Venerables was tremendous. They were two people who had recently attained achievements in martial learning through blood-soaked training. And that effort was still ongoing even now.

On the day Geom Mugeuk confirmed that the Sword King was at the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, he had gone out and returned. At that time, Ian had guessed that his outing was to prepare for this matter. Geom Mugeuk had sent urgent messages asking the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable for help.

Once Geom Mugeuk knew the opponent was the Sword King, he did not intend to face him alone. All the more so because Ian and Seo Jin were also with him.

The reason he called the Fist Demon was because of Ian, and the reason he called the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was because the opponent was the Sword King. He had sensed that this battle would become a great stimulus to everyone who wielded a sword.

Everyone held their breath as they watched Ian exchange greetings with the two.

A man more terrifying than anyone in the world and a woman more elegant than anyone in the world?

‘Just who are they?’

Ian first revealed her and Seo Jin’s identities to the two friends.

“I am Ian, captain of the Ghost Shadow Unit of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, and this is Team Leader Seo Jin.”

Shocked, Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok froze and could not say a word. It was the moment they finally understood with certainty the meaning of “our cult” and “safe house.”

“You two… no, you two esteemed ones are from the Demonic Cult—no, the Divine Cult?”

Gyo Seok trembled as he stammered. He should have known from the moment Yu Gwang started saying, “Ominous, ominous.” This is all your fault! That bastard’s conspiracy theories had drawn in the most terrifying beings in the world.

“Yes. As I said earlier, we came out because there was something to investigate.”

But the surprises began from here. Ian formally introduced the two Demon Venerables.

“This is Lord Fist Demon, one of our cult’s Eight Demon Venerables, and this is Lady Ilhwa Sword Venerable. Seo Jin, greet them formally first.”

At the word Sword Venerable, she had thought, Surely not, but they truly were Demon Venerables. Seo Jin respectfully greeted the two.

“I am Seo Jin, Team Leader of the Ghost Shadow Unit. It is an honor to meet such esteemed personages.”

Her voice trembled. Truly, it was the moment she was seeing the Eight Demon Venerables she had only heard about in words, and two of them in one place at that.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable was especially impressive. She was incomparably beautiful and elegant, to the point that Seo Jin naturally thought she wanted to age like that.

If even Seo Jin was this shocked, how great must Yu Gwang and Gyo Seok’s shock have been? How could they have known they would meet the Eight Demon Venerables in their lifetimes?

“Oh my!”

“Please spare us!”

The two of them prostrated themselves flat on the ground. It truly felt like they were dreaming.

Of course, they were not the only ones surprised. Yeon Baekjin, who had been standing awkwardly, also bent at the waist and greeted them. He, too, was startled enough for his heart to drop.

Originally, he should have been more shocked when Geom Mugeuk revealed his identity, but he was more shocked now.

When he saw Geom Mugeuk, it had not felt very real. Geom Mugeuk felt far too removed from the image of the Demonic Cult’s young cult leader.

But now it was different. He realized he was dealing with the real Demonic Cult and its demons. Had he ever in his life met people who were this frightening and gave off such an intense presence?

He could now feel that this fight had entered a new phase. It was not a situation where he could step forward and speak recklessly. The vague Demonic Cult had now revealed its substance before his eyes.

“Now, everyone, go inside and rest.”

Ian called a warrior of the safe house and had the others taken inside.

Now only Ian, the Fist Demon, and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable remained there. Ian told the two of them everything that had happened so far, exactly as it was.

“It was the first time I had ever seen the Young Cult Leader so tense.”

At those words, the Fist Demon and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at each other and exchanged glances. They sensed that this enemy was stronger than any they had faced before.

“Where is the Young Cult Leader now?”

At the Fist Demon’s question, Ian shook her head and only relayed the message she had been told to convey.

“He told me to tell you that there is an entrance leading underground beneath the sparring platform.”

What Geom Mugeuk had told her to relay at the very end was this.

“And he said that if he does not appear at this safe house even after ten days, you should come rescue him.”

Chapter 583: One Against a Hundred
----------------------------------
“At least he seems confident he can hold out for ten days.”

Whatever he felt inside, the Fist Demon did not show any worry outwardly.

The same was true of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“I don’t know what sort of enemy he’s facing, but it won’t be easy for anyone to spend ten whole days with the Young Cult Leader either.”

At her joke, the Fist Demon and Ian smiled together. Yes, Geom Mugeuk was the kind of person who could make people laugh even in a situation like this. That was why he and the Demon Venerables trusted the Young Cult Leader.

“Since Mugeuk set a deadline, why don’t we wait?”

At the Fist Demon’s courteous suggestion, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable readily agreed.

“Let us do that.”

From the circumstances, it appeared that Geom Mugeuk had gone underground together with the enemy.

Of course, that did not mean the Fist Demon would simply sit back and wait. He said to Ian,

“Instead, send word to Tongcheon Pavilion and tell them to report every movement related to Yellow Dragon Martial Academy. Also confirm that Mugeuk is not at the academy or anywhere nearby.”

“Yes, I will.”

Not only Tongcheon Pavilion but even Silver Moon had already mobilized every information network for this matter. If even the slightest strange sign appeared, word would come here at once.

The Fist Demon did not let down his guard. He spoke again to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“We should also keep in mind the possibility that other powerful enemies may come to aid them.”

If the opponent was someone strong enough to make Geom Mugeuk wary, then they might have exceptional subordinates, or they might call in another enemy.

“They may try to conduct a drive hunt.”

He meant they could have driven Geom Mugeuk underground and then come swarming in as a group.

At the Fist Demon’s words, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable answered politely.

“I will make preparations as well.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable knew. The Fist Demon was not merely a man with a frightening face. Since his youth, he had gone through every kind of hardship in the martial world alongside the Cult Leader. When the situation was urgent, his judgment could be trusted.

Only after the conversation with the Ilhwa Sword Venerable ended did the Fist Demon finally attend to his daughter.

“Are you hurt anywhere?”

“No, I’m fine.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable stepped away so the father and daughter could speak comfortably.

“Then I will go in first.”

Ian quickly said to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable,

“Sword Venerable, if anything is inconvenient or if there is anything you need done, please tell me.”

“I will.”

After hearing her consideration, before turning away, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable added a word.

“Unit Leader Ian, you’ve made progress in your martial arts, haven’t you?”

As expected, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had noticed Ian’s achievement. Having reached great accomplishment in the Flying Heaven Sword Art and greatly increased her internal energy as well, there was no way the Ilhwa Sword Venerable would fail to notice her change.

“I was fortunate.”

“Congratulations. Considering your age, it is a remarkable achievement. Even I had not reached that level at your age.”

“You praise me too highly.”

Ian cupped her fist and bowed her head politely. She did not want to miss this good opportunity to meet the Sword Venerable.

“I still have so much to learn. If you could spare even a brief moment to teach me when you have time, I would be truly grateful.”

It was a difficult request for her to make, but the Ilhwa Sword Venerable readily allowed it.

“That is precisely what I would hope for.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable was not merely saying so out of courtesy; she meant it sincerely. She knew well that the enlightenment gained while teaching could be just as great as the enlightenment gained while learning.

In that sense, there would be no better person than Ian. She was still thirsting for training.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable walked toward the building.

After entering the building and walking down the corridor, she saw the Fist Demon and Ian conversing far beyond the window.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s gaze turned toward the smiling Fist Demon.

‘Can he really be that happy?’

In all the years they had served together as Demon Venerables, had she ever seen the Fist Demon make such an expression? Even on the way here together, she had not seen him smile once.

Suddenly, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon came to mind. Now he cherished Geom Mugeuk like a son and was fond of him, but there had also been a moment when fate could have granted him a child of his own. And herself as well.

She did not regret it. At the time, she had lived believing that was the best choice.

But today, as she watched that smile bloom on the Fist Demon’s face—a smile rarer than a spirit herb—she found herself wondering, for some reason, what it might have been like to live a different life at least once.

* * *

The mechanism arrived underground.

When the door opened, a young man was waiting in front of them.

“You’ve come?”

The man, who had bowed politely to the Sword King, glanced at Geom Mugeuk.

“Take in the new recruit.”

At the Sword King’s words, delight flashed across the man’s face. But that delight soon turned into disappointment.

“Only one this time?”

“Is that not enough?”

“No, sir.”

They had rested for a while, but everyone was very exhausted. He had thought the Sword King would bring down at least five or six people this time.

The Sword King took off his shoe and smacked the man on the rear with it.

“Fix your expression! He may be one person, but he’s worth a hundred.”

As soon as he came down here, the first thing he did was take off his shoes.

“This place is so rough that shoes tear up in no time.”

After tying the shoes at his waist, the Sword King walked off first.

The man’s eyes, as he looked at Geom Mugeuk, were filled with doubt.

You’re really worth a hundred?

The handsome fellow before him looked far too removed from the rough work done here.

The man turned around and followed the path the Sword King had taken.

“You have to step exactly where I step and follow me. If you step wrong, you die!”

The path inward was as complicated as could be. They had to pass through places where mechanisms had been dismantled, and even the floor they had to step on changed frantically from yellow to red, then to blue.

“Don’t even think about coming out alone.”

The man was certain no one could memorize the path after following it only once, but in truth, an exact map was being drawn in Geom Mugeuk’s mind. Since there might come a time when he had to escape this place, Geom Mugeuk committed the path to memory with all his might.

This place was truly dangerous. Everywhere was filled with mechanisms that had not yet been completely dismantled, and blades jutted out here and there in every direction.

“You have to jump over there in one go. You can do it, right?”

“Wait a moment. Let me catch my breath.”

Geom Mugeuk put on an exaggerated show of weakness. It was because he wanted to talk to the man for a moment.

“My name is Geom Yeon.”

“I’m Hong In.”

Hong In asked Geom Mugeuk with a slightly pitying look.

“What do you need money for that you came in here?”

“I came in because of… the Instructor.”

“The Instructor? You mean Eldest Brother?”

The Sword King was being called Eldest Brother by them. Then again, since he probably had not taught them before, he would never have been their instructor. Eldest Brother. From that title, one could guess how the Sword King treated them.

“There’s something about him that pulls people in.”

As if he agreed with that point, Hong In nodded.

“Still, if he’d said he wouldn’t pay you, you wouldn’t have come, would you?”

At the look that said, In the end, you came for the money, so don’t say pointless things, Geom Mugeuk obediently acknowledged it.

“That’s right. It’s because of the money.”

“Now, jump.”

With an expression that said, I knew it, Hong In lightly launched himself forward. Geom Mugeuk jumped after him.

“You jump just fine, yet you put on that act!”

Hong In opened the way again. Geom Mugeuk realized there were far more mechanisms installed here than he had imagined. There must have been countless sacrifices in the process of breaking through this place.

Then the two arrived in a broad space. The martial academy members had gathered around the Sword King, who had arrived first.

Their gazes naturally turned toward Geom Mugeuk. Their eyes were wary as they wondered who this person joining them could be.

Geom Mugeuk also slowly looked them over.

First of all, they were exhausted. Fatigue could be felt throughout their entire bodies.

But there was passion and heat in their eyes.

They were all people who had come voluntarily to earn money. They were all dreamers.

But Geom Mugeuk read something else in them as well.

It was madness.

These youths were enduring in this stifling place, in this perilous place where their comrades died around them, thinking only of money. How could they possibly endure without going mad?

They probably thought they were holding up well, but Geom Mugeuk could feel that the look in their eyes was not normal. Since they were gathered among people just like themselves, they would not know what state they were in.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Sword King.

‘Can you not see that exhausted madness?’

Was he pretending not to see it for the sake of his goal, or was he unable to feel it because he was among them as well?

Geom Mugeuk greeted them loudly and brightly.

“I’m the new recruit, Geom Yeon, from the White Dragon Class. I look forward to working with you!”

At the mention of the White Dragon Class, everyone began murmuring. It was the first time someone from the White Dragon Class had come here.

Even though the Sword King was present, one of the academy members could not hold back and asked,

“How did someone from the White Dragon Class come here?”

“Exactly. Just how smart and strong in martial arts do you think I must be? Ah, you’re asking why someone with that kind of skill entered the White Dragon Class?”

Under the gazes focused on him, Geom Mugeuk remained relaxed.

“There were circumstances.”

“What circumstances?”

“When a man makes a choice other men can’t understand, what reason could there be? It was a woman problem.”

At that moment, several academy members laughed. Some laughed in disbelief, others in sympathy.

It was the first time he had seen their expressions loosen. When they laughed like this, they looked similar to the academy members aboveground.

In any case, the Sword King had brought him, and the fact that he spoke so boldly in the Sword King’s presence made them think, at the very least, that he was not deceiving them.

“Where do I sleep? Where’s my spot? Ah, I came down in a hurry, so I couldn’t bring anything with me.”

The Sword King did not order a subordinate to do it, but personally stepped forward to guide him.

“Follow me.”

The Sword King first took him to the sleeping quarters.

A large room had been carved into the wall, and shabby wooden beds were lined up inside. As if everyone here was on equal footing, there were no better spots and no worse spots.

“The beds are too small and too hard.”

Geom Mugeuk sat on a bed and bounced his hips a little.

“If they’re too comfortable, you only get more idle thoughts.”

“Isn’t that the thinking of the side making people work?”

“I sleep here too.”

“That isn’t a reason it’s fine for them to be uncomfortable.”

The Sword King stared at Geom Mugeuk and gave another reason.

“Thanks to this, they’ve become much stronger.”

“Ah! Then I should lie down.”

Geom Mugeuk flopped onto the bed.

The Sword King stood beside him, looking down at him. Geom Mugeuk lay on the bed and silently looked up at the ceiling.

“As expected, coming underground already feels stifling.”

The Sword King began walking again, and Geom Mugeuk followed behind him.

Even while walking, one had to be careful. Blades jutted out here and there throughout the living space. He felt they had been left as they were to remind everyone not to let down their guard.

Drip, drip, drip.

Following the sound of water, they arrived at a place where underground water flowed down.

“If you need to wash, wash here.”

The Sword King took out basic necessities from a wooden box in the corner and gave them to him. He knew where everything was in every corner of this place.

The final place he guided him to was where the excavation had stopped.

An iron wall blocked the way ahead inside the cave.

The Sword King had described it as digging through the ground, but to be precise, they had to dig through this iron wall. The wall was covered in unknown characters and patterns, and for some reason, it gave off a dangerous feeling.

“This is the final barrier.”

“How do you know it’s the final one?”

“Because I’ve done enough. Because I don’t want to do any more.”

Since it was a joke filled with sincerity, Geom Mugeuk laughed out loud.

After staring quietly at the iron wall, Geom Mugeuk placed his palm against it. The cold energy seemed to warn him like this.

Do not come any farther.

This wall would not be everything. A dangerous final barrier would be waiting.

‘What lies at the end?’

The Sword King had said it. That he could become stronger here. Just what was there?

“Who made this place?”

“I don’t know either. I only know it was made a very long time ago. But I’m certain there is a peerless divine art and incredible treasure inside.”

You’re not after that, are you? You’re someone who handed me the enormous sum of three hundred thousand nyang without hesitation.

Geom Mugeuk turned with his back to the iron wall.

“Now that we’ve come this far, please tell me. Why do you need me? It isn’t as if I have some special talent for breaking through this barrier.”

The Sword King’s answer was unexpected.

“To tell the truth, I’m tired too. This final barrier will be truly dangerous. Many sacrifices will occur.”

As if that was all, he said nothing more.

Startled, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“Don’t tell me you mean for me to save the academy members?”

Surprisingly, the Sword King nodded.

“It would be too much for me alone. It’s one of the biggest reasons I called you.”

As if he had said all he needed to say, the Sword King turned and began walking away. Then Geom Mugeuk asked from behind him,

“Do you truly intend to let these academy members live?”

The Sword King’s steps stopped.

“When the treasure vault containing a peerless divine art and all manner of treasures opens, the academy members will see it too. Of course, with you present, Instructor, they wouldn’t dare covet it. But once they return to the world, they will spread rumors someday. That you obtained a peerless divine art and treasure here, Instructor. That they were there.”

Then the Sword King answered,

“So what?”

Yes, if it was the Sword King Geom Mugeuk had seen so far, he might truly not care, just as that answer suggested. What if rumors spread? He possessed the skill to keep anyone from taking it from him.

But there was one reason Geom Mugeuk had brought this up.

Before his regression, there had never been any rumor that such treasure had been discovered. Nor had he ever heard that the person who spread such a rumor was a member of the martial academy. Had all these people kept the secret?

‘Or did you kill them all?’

The Sword King turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

“So? Are you saying I should kill them all to silence them?”

Frightening words were exchanged, but the taut tension between the two men actually disappeared.

“Then you take charge of that as well.”

The Sword King laughed, and Geom Mugeuk laughed along with him.

“I was joking. You know my reputation, don’t you?”

The Sword King gazed at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Yes. If you are the Young Cult Leader of the rumors, you will save those children.”

Their gazes intertwined in midair.

How much was truth, and how much was falsehood?

Geom Mugeuk felt that as long as the Sword King stood barefoot before him, and as long as they did not go all the way beyond this wall, he would not be able to find the answer.

Geom Mugeuk turned back around and looked at the iron wall, then this time quietly pressed his ear against it.

“Do you hear something?”

“I do.”

The Sword King asked in surprise,

“What sound?”

“If we are to complete the work safely…”

Geom Mugeuk even gently closed his eyes and concentrated.

“It says we need good beds and soft bedding.”

Chapter 584: No Meat No Work
----------------------------
That afternoon, the full-scale work of dismantling the mechanisms began.

The officials gathered in front of the iron wall. The labor here was well divided, and those gathered now were mainly tasked with probing the mechanisms.

Hongin was in command of this operation.

"The mechanisms here are expected to be even more sensitive than before, so from now on, the use of internal energy is forbidden except in special circumstances. As you all know, if you touch even one thing wrong, everyone dies!"

Hongin tried hard to keep the officials from relaxing. A mistake meant death.

"Begin work!"

The officials began working while tense. Starting from the ground before the iron wall, they began probing the earth with thin, long, awl-like tools. Their hand movements were extremely careful.

Geomugeuk stood a short distance away, watching their work. Hongin didn't give Geomugeuk any orders. Because he knew the Sword King was treating him specially.

Even if that weren't the case, he couldn't entrust such important work to someone participating for the first time.

How much time passed like that? One official found something.

"I think it's here."

Then other officials brought chisels and hammers and carefully began digging there. More than half of it was stone floor, and they had to dig without using internal energy, so the work proceeded slowly.

Geomugeuk watched the work in silence. At this rate, they wouldn't be able to remove even the gate at the entrance after ten days.

Geomugeuk glanced at the Sword King. As if telling him to handle it on his own, he said nothing.

"I'll help as well."

When Geomugeuk stepped forward, Hongin looked at the Sword King. The Sword King gave a slight nod. Meaning to put him to work.

Hongin was displeased with this situation. To entrust such dangerous work to Geomugeuk, who had just arrived! Even if he was an expert, this work required experience.

Because he found Geomugeuk unreliable, he told him several times to be really careful.

"Don't worry!"

Because it seemed like he would start swinging the hammer wildly at any moment, Hongin inwardly prayed, Please! Don't cause an accident!

Clang! Clang! Clang! Clang!

Geomugeuk's hand movements were fast and skilled.

At that sight, not only Hongin but the other officials as well were startled.

"Stop!"

"It's fine!"

Geomugeuk's hand movements grew even faster.

Hongin urgently looked at the Sword King, asking for help.

But the Sword King merely watched in silence.

Once Geomugeuk stepped in, the work speed became incomparably faster. At a glance, it looked like he was digging roughly, but how could Geomugeuk's hand movements be so crude?

Geomugeuk was channeling qi through the tip of the chisel to check what was beneath the ground while digging. Detecting something underground with qi was several times more difficult than emitting qi into the air. Yet he was accomplishing such a task casually and skillfully.

How deep had he dug?

Finally, part of the mechanism revealed itself there.

Hongin called another official who had been waiting in the back.

"The mechanism has revealed itself."

Then one official came this way.

Geomugeuk could sense it.

'This guy isn't an official of Hwangryong Martial Hall.'

He was concealing a special qi, not that of ordinary officials. He might have been able to avoid the eyes of others, but he couldn't avoid Geomugeuk's eyes.

Indeed, while other tasks might not require one, dismantling mechanisms would demand a proper mechanism expert. Since Hwangryong Martial Hall was frequented by so many people, ordinary officials wouldn't even know if this man was the Sword King's direct subordinate.

After examining the exposed mechanism, the man reported to the Sword King.

"It's a mechanism designed to prevent forcibly breaking the wall or digging through the ground to enter."

Then he spoke to Hongin.

"Please expose it as much as possible. Our team will handle the rest."

"Understood."

Without realizing it, Hongin looked at Geomugeuk. As if he had been waiting, Geomugeuk went to the official working across from him.

"I'll take this side. Please finish up my side."

After swapping with him, he quickly began digging out that side.

The gazes of the officials, who had been watching with concern at first, changed. The amount of work Geomugeuk had already done was what should have taken several days.

The mechanism on the other side was revealed as well. He wasn't just digging randomly. The mechanism here was installed at a different height than the place he'd worked on earlier, yet he had dug it out precisely without a single scratch.

Hongin couldn't hide his surprise.

"Not bad?"

He should have said, "That's incredible," but somehow praising this newbie felt like it would excite him and make him put excessive force into his hammering.

"I've always had sensitive hands. I'll do that side too."

Geomugeuk began skillfully hammering around all sides of the revealed mechanism.

Geomugeuk focused on the work. He didn't think about what might be beyond that wall, what the officials would do, or what the Sword King was feeling. He devoted himself to hammering as if in a trance.

The Sword King merely watched silently as Geomugeuk displayed a concentration that was almost frightening.

The officials' gazes, which had initially been filled with worry, changed to admiration before anyone knew it. Geomugeuk now looked like an artist carving a sculpture.

Just as the earth around all sides was carved away and the hidden mechanisms had considerably revealed themselves.

"We stop here for today. It's mealtime."

At Hongin's voice from behind, Geomugeuk set down his hammer.

"Has it already gotten so late?"

The officials gathered around Geomugeuk.

Because they were engaged in life-or-death work, the person they liked most was neither someone with a good personality nor someone who was kind to them.

Their favorite person was someone who worked well. Because they were doing work where one person's mistake could kill many. Because if the work went well, they could escape this hellish place even a day sooner.

In that regard, who knew how much Geomugeuk had shortened the working hours today. Moreover, he had a good personality.

"I'm starving to death! Give me meat, meat! If you don't give me meat, I'm not working from tomorrow!"

Geomugeuk sat around eating with the officials. Meat really came out.

Several came to his side and introduced themselves. Since they were from martial hall backgrounds, they were not fundamentally evil people.

This was always the first thing they were curious about.

"Why did you come here?"

You look strong, and you're skillful with your hands. No matter how I look at it, you're not someone who should be in a place like this.

At someone's question, Geomugeuk's gaze turned to the Sword King, who was sitting across from them eating.

"I came because I heard I could get stronger here."

The officials nodded. They didn't deny it. Many of those who had endured until now had become experts capable of emitting sword qi.

"I heard you're a one-man army?"

"Does human greed have an end? I want to become one against a thousand, one against ten thousand. Senior, what brought you down here?"

As Geomugeuk naturally mingled with them, his first day's work down underground came to an end like that.

* * *

That night, something unexpected happened as well.

The Sword King brought several large bundles and distributed them to the officials.

"Everyone, take one."

When what had been tightly compressed by internal energy was unleashed, it billowed open like a blooming flower—surprisingly, it was a cotton blanket.

No matter how tightly the blankets had been compressed, he must have gone up and down many times to carry so many of them.

Geomugeuk cheered with delight.

"New blankets!"

He immediately took a cotton blanket, laid it on his sleeping mat, and threw himself onto it.

"Uwa! It's so fluffy!"

Because this behavior from the Sword King was so unexpected, the officials looked at him with surprised faces.

"A difficult task has begun, so I prepared these so you can sleep comfortably. Take them."

Only then did the officials relax and take the blankets. Who would dislike being given new blankets? Everyone was happy.

Of course, the newbie's voice was the loudest.

"The smell of new blankets is so good!"

* * *

At dawn, when everyone was asleep, Geomugeuk met the Sword King at the place where they had worked during the day.

The Sword King was sitting astride the exposed mechanism without a care, staring blankly at the wall. Even without turning around, he knew Geomugeuk had come.

"You weren't going to sleep anyway, so why the fuss about blankets? It wasn't because of you, was it?"

"The work is hard, so you should at least sleep properly."

The Sword King asked without turning his head.

"Tell me the real reason."

He was convinced that Geomugeuk had another reason. He had brought the blankets to hear that reason as well.

"You might be offended if you hear it."

"It's fine. I've lived hearing plenty of curses."

"You, Master? Who would dare curse at you?"

"Aren't you preparing to curse me right now? So speak comfortably."

Geomugeuk revealed the reason.

"Because everyone looked so exhausted."

The Sword King turned his head to look at Geomugeuk. At his gaze asking if that was offensive, Geomugeuk spoke calmly.

"If I were their parent, I would have cursed you out, Master."

As if he understood even those words, the Sword King showed no agitation.

But a needle-like remark was added.

"But you only selected officials without family from this place."

"!"

Like carving stone with a chisel, Geomugeuk's hammering began.

"They call you the chief here, don't they?"

The Sword King's expression hardened slightly as he turned his gaze back to the wall.

Certainly, the word "chief" held a feeling closer to family, beyond merely signifying a superior. That's likely how it had sounded to him.

To someone without family, being called "chief"—aren't you now doing something that would earn you curses from their parents?

"Didn't I say you might feel bad?"

"I don't feel bad. They all chose this themselves. And it's work that earns big money."

"That would make them feel even worse. Because their child was lured here for nothing but money."

The Sword King whirled his head to look at Geomugeuk again.

"Of course, the one who planned and executed that must have been the Hwangryong Hall Master."

Geomugeuk pretended to offer a carrot, then cracked the whip.

"But you knew, didn't you? What kind of officials he had gathered."

He was the same person who had told Yeonbaegin that villains walk their path while being cursed. Yet now, he didn't display that shamelessness.

"You can't strike there in anger. It'll explode."

The Sword King, who had been sitting still, suddenly threw the shoes he had tied at his waist at Geomugeuk.

"At your young age, what do you know about playing the parent!"

Geomugeuk caught the flying shoes in succession.

"Am I not from an exceptional father-son relationship? I know a thing or two about a parent's heart."

Geomugeuk understood. The Sword King hadn't said he was sorry, but by throwing the shoes while losing his composure, he had apologized.

Geomugeuk took the shoes and placed them beside the mechanism where he had been sitting.

"Still, thank you for the blankets."

The Sword King looked down at the shoes placed side by side and answered.

"I didn't bring them because of you. You said you heard a voice from beyond that wall? Asking for blankets."

At his joke, Geomugeuk smiled, walked to the wall, and pressed his ear against it again.

"I hear it again."

"What is it saying this time?"

Geomugeuk closed his eyes tightly.

A moment later, unexpected words flowed from Geomugeuk's mouth.

"The officials say they want to see the stars."

In an instant, the trace of a smile vanished from the Sword King's face.

"Nonsense!"

"Why is it nonsense?"

Geomugeuk pulled his ear from the wall and turned to the Sword King.

"Are you worried they'll run away if they go out? Or worried their hearts will waver?"

After a brief pause, Geomugeuk added.

"So what? Those were your words, Master. When I asked what if the officials spread rumors about this place, you said, 'So what.'"

The Sword King said nothing.

"So what if some run away? So what? They're people you'll release after seeing what's inside anyway. Surely you don't intend not to release them?"

"Why do you go so far to show kindness to the officials? It's not something they want or will appreciate."

The Sword King picked up the shoes beside him. It was a sign that if the answer didn't satisfy him, he would throw them again. This time, it would be anger, not an apology.

Geomugeuk answered calmly.

"Because for some of them, it may be the last sky they see while alive."

The shoe did not fly from the Sword King’s hand. Nor did he put it down.

“And this matter is not a favor bestowed upon them. It is a favor bestowed upon Master.”

To the Sword King, who wore an expression asking what he meant, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“I am giving you a chance. To do what you must do, even now.”
