One shoe flew back toward Geom Mugeuk.

“You, I said you were too sentimental, didn’t I?”

Holding the remaining shoe, the Sword King asked.

“So? What do you gain if I become a better person?”

Geom Mugeuk answered with a smile.

“You told me before. That even in victory, defeating an Eighth Star is more glorious than defeating a Seventh Star, and even in defeat, losing to an Eighth Star is less shameful than losing to a Seventh Star. It is the same for me. I too wish to fight someone who is even a little bit better.”

In the end, the Sword King could not throw the remaining shoe he held. It had been only a day since Geom Mugeuk came down; to think he would make such a joke.

“Hey! Can’t you just go back up?”

* * *

Gwonma opened his eyes wide and asked again.

“He bought what and took it in?”

The one who answered his question was Ian.

“They say it was blankets.”

Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, who had been listening to the report together, confirmed once more.

“Blankets? You mean the blankets we know of?”

“Yes, the blankets you cover yourself with.”

The expert said to have made even Geom Mugeuk nervous—bought blankets?

At present, the supreme warriors of Tongcheon Pavilion and Eunwol were intensively monitoring Hwangryong Martial Hall.

They had been keeping thorough watch on the entrance to the underground Bimudae in particular, and it was reported that the Sword King, emerging from there, had bought blankets in bulk at the marketplace.

It was said that he had made no particular effort to hide his tracks.

The strange reports did not end there.

“And last night, dozens of people are said to have come out of there.”

“Dozens? And then?”

Had they moved somewhere? Had they embarked on some kind of mission? Or if not?

But the answer that came from Ian was incomprehensible.

“They gathered at the Bimudae and then went back down, apparently.”

In fact, Ian, who was making the report, also could not understand it. So he had asked the warrior from Tongcheon Pavilion several times. Was that really correct?

“They just went back down?”

“Yes, they stood there looking at the night sky and then went back down, apparently.”

Gwonma looked at Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, but she was no different in her incomprehension. This was truly a report as bizarre as the matter of buying blankets.

“Was the Young Sect Leader among them as well?”

“Yes, it is said that the Young Sect Leader and his master were together with them.”

At the mention that Geom Mugeuk had been with them, Ilhwa Sword Sovereign smiled and spoke. She could not imagine what on earth was happening, but one thing was certain.

“Did I not tell you so from the beginning? Even without knowing who the enemy is, it will not be easy to remain with the Young Sect Leader for even ten days.”

Chapter 585: If They’re Strong, It’s Difficult
----------------------------------------------
It was an unforgettable night.

Everyone stood on the sparring platform and looked up at the night sky. Gazing at the stars pouring down above them, not one of them opened their mouth.

They simply stared at the stars in silence. It was a moment to which silence was best suited.

When they had suddenly been assembled and brought outside, many of them had thought the same thing. So, now that the excavation is in its final stages, they’re going to kill us all!

After all, not once had they been allowed outside since entering this place.

But that was not it. The Sword King had made them all look up at the night sky.

At first, everyone was bewildered, but gradually, emotion welled up within them.

The beautiful stars, the outside wind they smelled for the first time in so long.

In that moment, they truly felt that they were alive.

At least for that moment.

If they survived, then with that money! With that hope! They could escape all the idle thoughts that began from there.

The stars and the wind seemed to be saying this.

You are alive.

Had they been alone, the feeling would not have moved them so deeply. Because the people who had endured that hell together were looking up together, tonight’s starlight felt even more profound.

Among them was the Sword King.

He, too, was looking at the stars. For a very long time.

Then, suddenly, the Sword King turned his head and looked at Geom Mugeuk. Though he had come out saying they should show the men, Geom Mugeuk was the one gazing at the stars most intently.

With those deepened eyes holding the stars, what was the Young Cult Leader thinking?

Everyone’s thoughts were different, but at the very least, this much would be the same.

They would never be able to forget these stars, this night air.

* * *

When Hongin opened his eyes in the morning, what had happened the night before felt like a dream. In fact, he had dreamed about it, too. Of course, in the dream, while they were gathered like that, a meteor fell and sent everyone flying in every direction, which woke him up.

His dream was to use the money he had earned here to build a small martial hall in a kindhearted village, meet a good woman, and live happily for the rest of his life.

One thing had been added to that dream.

That he would survive no matter what and look at the stars every night.

As Hongin tried to rise from his bed, he looked down at the snow-white blanket covering him.

Naturally, his gaze turned to one place.

Geom Mugeuk was sitting cross-legged atop his blanket, circulating his qi. Had he stayed up all night like that?

All of this had started after that newcomer came in. Was it really because of that newcomer?

“All right, everyone up!”

Hongin shouted as he got down from his bed.

The men washed and ate breakfast as usual. It was the same morning as yesterday, yet the atmosphere flowing among them was strangely different.

After they finished preparing, they gathered once more before the iron wall.

“All right, today of all days, I don’t need to tell you why we have to concentrate, do I?”

At Hongin’s words, all the men laughed. It occurred to him that it had truly been a long time since they had laughed together like this.

Hongin spoke to the Sword King as their representative.

“Thank you very much for yesterday.”

The men standing behind Hongin also bowed their heads to the Sword King all together.

The Sword King gazed at them, then spoke quietly.

“I was far too late, wasn’t I?”

A resounding answer of “No, sir!” rang out.

The Sword King’s gaze turned to Geom Mugeuk. At the look in his eyes that seemed to ask, Are you satisfied now? Geom Mugeuk answered with a bright smile that said, Isn’t this so much better?

“All right, then. Begin work!”

Clang, clang, clang.

Starting with Geom Mugeuk’s cheerful hammering, the men began their assigned tasks.

The excavation team’s work proceeded like this. When Geom Mugeuk rapidly dug something out, the men finished the work.

Excavating the mechanism was even more difficult than on the first day. Complicated devices were revealing themselves here and there.

Even so, Geom Mugeuk’s hands still moved lightly. As the work grew harder, he had also become that much more accustomed to it.

That did not mean he greedily tried to do everything alone.

“Please help me here.”

Whenever necessary, he asked the men for help. If it seemed too difficult for them, he asked the Sword King to help as well. Because of that, the work advanced smoothly and without delay.

In that way, the mechanism buried underground gradually revealed more and more of its true form. It looked as though it were a living creature, stretching out here and there.

Geom Mugeuk, who possessed a certain degree of knowledge in mechanical formations, could tell.

‘This is not a mechanism made in recent times.’

It was a mechanism made long ago, yet its delicacy could not be compared to anything in the present. It had undoubtedly been made by an extraordinary craftsman even in the era in which it was created. Of course, that did not mean dismantling it would be more difficult. The study of mechanical formations had developed brilliantly over all those years.

And just as the work continued like that—

Clank! At the sound, everyone’s gaze turned toward the spot. One of the men wore a startled expression. He had accidentally touched something wrong.

Srrrk.

The walls on both sides opened, revealing dozens of holes.

“Be careful!”

The very instant Geom Mugeuk’s shout rang out—

Shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish-shwish!

Dozens of deadly hidden weapons poured out from both walls at tremendous speed.

In that instant, Geom Mugeuk’s sword spun.

Whoooooosh.

A small circle expanded in an instant, and wind whirled violently.

Whirrrr-rurururururu!

It was Geom Mugeuk’s sword wind. He had stirred up sword wind because if he tried to knock them away with his sword, he felt he could not afford to miss even one.

Papapapapapapapak!

The incoming hidden weapons were caught in the sword wind and sent flying back to where they had come from.

But he had not blocked every hidden weapon. What Geom Mugeuk blocked were the hidden weapons that had flown from the left wall.

The men on the right side should have screamed as the hidden weapons from the right wall struck them, yet not a single scream rang out.

When Geom Mugeuk looked back, he saw the Sword King’s back. He had charged in at once and swung his sword, deflecting every hidden weapon.

Because they had their backs to each other, neither of them could see how the other had blocked the hidden weapons. The men’s eyes likely had not been able to follow it, but it must have been truly fantastic. Geom Mugeuk regretted not seeing the technique for himself.

But had he been confident he would not miss even a single one? Or had he thought it would be fine if one person died?

“Is anyone hurt?”

At the Sword King’s question, Hongin answered loudly.

“No, sir. Thank you for saving us.”

The men looked at one another in relief. Was it because they had seen the stars the previous night? The emotion of having survived felt greater than ever. Not one of them reproached the comrade who had made the mistake.

Their gazes turned toward the floor. Dozens of hidden weapons deflected by Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King were scattered across it.

They had taken it for granted that the Sword King could block them, but they had not expected Geom Mugeuk to block all those hidden weapons.

As they stared at him in astonishment, Geom Mugeuk said,

“I told you I was worth a hundred men, didn’t I?”

It was the moment they confirmed with their own eyes that Geom Mugeuk truly was an extraordinary expert. Yes, they should have realized it when the Sword King gave him special treatment.

After expressing their gratitude to the Sword King, the men gathered around Geom Mugeuk.

Hongin thanked him as their representative.

“Thank you.”

Now Hongin’s words and attitude toward Geom Mugeuk had become courteous.

“Please treat me comfortably as a newcomer, just like before.”

How could he do that when Geom Mugeuk was the best at the work and his martial arts were this strong? Especially when he had even saved their lives.

“I actually enjoy being the youngest who hides his strength! So please treat me comfortably!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, not only Hongin but the other men as well burst into laughter.

Hongin did as Geom Mugeuk wanted.

“Thanks, youngest.”

Work resumed again.

As if that accident had never happened, Geom Mugeuk immersed himself in the task.

With Geom Mugeuk leading the work, a task that no one could have guessed how long would take was already finished.

At this pace, it seemed they could completely excavate the mechanism and even dismantle it by sometime tomorrow.

“Work’s over!”

At Hongin’s shout, Geom Mugeuk cried, “I’m hungry!” It would have been nice if it had ended there.

“But aren’t you going to hold a welcome party for the newcomer?”

The Sword King’s hand naturally went to the shoe hanging at his waist. But he could not throw it in front of everyone watching.

Especially not at the person who had saved them all.

* * *

“This time, he bought liquor and meat?”

At Ian’s report, even the Fist Demon let out a dumbfounded laugh. Whenever a report related to Geom Mugeuk came in, expressions kept appearing on that solemn, frightening face that did not suit it at all.

“It seems the Young Cult Leader is still all right. It’s not as though he bought medicine or coffins.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable picked up Ian’s joke.

“Wouldn’t medicine be better, rather? From the enemy’s point of view.”

Ian laughed, knowing what she meant. After all, it would be easier to block Geom Mugeuk’s sword energy than to deal with him at a drinking party.

“Then I will report to you again as soon as the next report comes in.”

She left the room and came out into the courtyard.

Though she had come out smiling on the surface, inwardly, she was worried about Geom Mugeuk.

He had bought blankets, watched the stars, and bought liquor.

But how could Geom Mugeuk possibly be playing around? If anything, she was even more worried. Because she knew better than anyone what kind of person Geom Mugeuk was.

‘Even there, Young Master is truly doing his utmost.’

The stranger the reports, the harder he must be trying.

At that moment, someone spoke from behind her.

“Are you worried about the Young Cult Leader?”

When she turned around, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was walking toward her.

Ian answered honestly.

“The Young Cult Leader saw something in him that I could not see.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable nodded.

“Yes, that is the kind of person he is.”

If Ian had viewed the fact that Geom Mugeuk had seen through the Sword King negatively, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable viewed it positively. He was someone who saw what others could not, so there was no need to worry.

“You know well what kind of person the Young Cult Leader is, do you not?”

Yes. Because she knew, she worried—and because she knew, Geom Mugeuk was also someone she did not have to worry about at all.

“Yes! Who am I to worry about whom? From now on, I will wrap my worries up tightly and stuff them under the blanket.”

The two women looked at each other and smiled. The Sword Venerable could have been difficult and awkward because they had not done much together before, but Ian was deeply grateful that she treated her so comfortably like this.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable was about to continue on her way, but then stopped.

“Captain Yi.”

“Yes, Sword Venerable.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable gave her unexpected advice.

“When the moment comes that you must make a decision, do not hesitate.”

Ian was inwardly flustered by the sudden advice. Those words just now did not mean she should not hesitate when executing martial arts.

They meant that when she had to make some decision in life, she should not hesitate. And it did not sound like she meant to decide quickly, but to decide properly.

“Yes! I will never hesitate.”

At her spirited answer, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable smiled, then walked away.

Ian stood there for a moment, watching her from behind. A moment ago, she had seen regret in her eyes. It had been an impulsive remark born from that deep regret.

‘What decision did you hesitate over, to regret it so much?’

* * *

The mechanism had completely revealed itself.

Its appearance looked like a monster that had been hiding underground, now exposing its bones and entrails.

All the men underground gathered and looked at it. It was a truly moving moment. They had suffered so much in order to see that.

Of course, none of them knew what awaited beyond that iron wall. There might be even more dangerous things lined up one after another, or it might simply end inside there. They knew nothing.

They only hoped this process would end even one day sooner.

The male expert in mechanisms spoke to the Sword King.

“This mechanism was made with very old techniques.”

“Can you dismantle it?”

The man looked at the mechanism, then nodded with a confident expression.

“I can.”

When the Sword King nodded, the man spoke vigorously to the men under his command. They were all people he had taught.

“We begin the work.”

Geom Mugeuk stood side by side with the Sword King and watched the dismantling team work from nearby.

“Thank you for the welcome party yesterday.”

The Sword King shook his head. Yesterday’s welcome party had been utter chaos. Was it because they had taken in the outside air once? Once Geom Mugeuk took the lead in setting the mood, everyone had been too busy laughing and chatting to know what to do with themselves.

When Geom Mugeuk, drunk, brought up that he wanted to hear their big brother sing, the Sword King had immediately risen from his seat, so he had no idea what happened afterward. Still, at the time, he had muttered this to himself: Insane. It had been directed at Geom Mugeuk and also at himself. At himself, who had even held a welcome party.

“Honestly, I’m still confused. Whether this is your sincerity, or whether it’s just for show.”

Geom Mugeuk answered without hesitation.

“It’s for show.”

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“I don’t think I would have done this if there weren’t someone there to see me do it. Even now, I am making an effort toward you, Master. Asking you to see what makes me special.”

“While saying your intentions out loud like this?”

“Confession is also part of the effort.”

“Fine, let’s say that’s true. Why are you making the effort?”

After a brief silence, Geom Mugeuk spoke honestly.

“I suppose I’m afraid of fighting you, Master.”

The Sword King’s gaze as he looked at Geom Mugeuk deepened.

“So perhaps I’m trying to build a good relationship with you, even like this. In hopes that later, right before you kill me, you will hesitate even for an instant. And because of that hesitation, you will die by my hand.”

The Sword King said with an incredulous expression,

“If you say everything so honestly, doesn’t that make it meaningless?”

“This, too, is all part of the strategy.”

Watching Geom Mugeuk put on a sly smile, the Sword King looked back at the dismantling work.

Then the Sword King abruptly said,

“It is not easy for a person to change their fate.”

After a short pause, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“With your skill, Master, could you not change even that fate?”

The Sword King gave no answer.

Yes, how could Geom Mugeuk not know? Jo Chunbae, who did business in Maga Village, the Sword King before his eyes, and himself. How difficult it was to change one’s life.

If one was strong, it was difficult because one was strong; if one was weak, it was difficult because one was weak.

“You were angry before, weren’t you? When I described the people I killed as your comrades. Then what are you doing with people who are not even your comrades?”

Then he asked the question he had truly wanted to ask.

“Who is above you, Master?”

Geom Mugeuk’s heart pounded. Would Hwa Mugi’s name come out of the Sword King’s mouth?

But the Sword King said nothing. Geom Mugeuk did not ask again. This, too, was a question he had to save and cherish.

At that moment, the male mechanism expert came and reported.

“The mechanism has been dismantled.”

From everyone there, he could feel astonishment, tension, fear, and anticipation. All sorts of emotions spread out in every direction.

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King stood at the very front of the iron wall. The other men gathered behind the two of them. May all that long suffering end here, at last.

“Then I will open it.”

Grrrrrk.

The sound alone told them that it had not been opened in a very long time.

As everyone watched, the iron wall slowly began to open.

Chapter 586: A Villain Like This Is a First
-------------------------------------------
The moment the door opened, old air burst out.

It was as if the space had finally let out the breath it had been holding.

From that musty smell of accumulated years alone, one could tell that no one had entered this place for a very long time.

A long passageway showed faintly within the darkness.

Looking inside with the Divine Eye Art, Geom Mugeuk could see that the walls on both sides of the passage were also engraved with all manner of patterns, letters, and images like those carved into the door.

As though they had been etched into intestines, the darkness swallowed all of it and exuded an indescribable energy as it tempted those before it.

Come in. Hurry and come in.

That energy was difficult to put into words, but one thing was certain. It was an utterly unfamiliar energy, one that no person living in the martial world of the present age had ever felt.

If you go in, you will die.

Yet despite the warning of his instincts, Geom Mugeuk felt that he had to enter that place.

It was not resistance born of fear of the unknown, nor a spirit of challenge.

He felt that coming to Hwangnyong Martial Hall, meeting the Sword King, and the Sword King telling him they should come down here had all been the pull of fate.

Geom Mugeuk asked the Sword King standing beside him.

“Do you feel it?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Sword King nodded.

Just then, one of the hall members stepped forward as if possessed by something. As he was about to pass Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King and enter, Hongin rushed over and grabbed him.

“Snap out of it!”

Smack!

Hongin struck his cheek without mercy. Only then did the man come back to his senses.

The darkness had tempted him and called him in. Among the hall members, he had the weakest internal energy and the frailest mental strength, and so he had been the first to succumb to that temptation.

Soon after, another hall member also tried to walk forward, but the member beside him grabbed him, and he regained his senses.

Geom Mugeuk sent a voice transmission to the Sword King.

—It seems the time has come to send the hall members back.

He had a premonition that if they entered this place, these people would not survive.

—Shall we go in, just the two of us?

He had thought the Sword King would readily agree, but the Sword King could not make a decision right away.

Geom Mugeuk did not provoke him with words like these.

Was saying you would spare these people a lie?

He believed that the Sword King was hesitating not because he planned to take them inside and kill them, but for some other reason.

He had clearly felt that the Sword King cared for them. If he had not, he would never have shown them the stars.

As expected, the Sword King’s concern was for the hall members.

—I wanted to show them the final place.

They had endured hardship unto death and come this far. It seemed that, leaving aside money, he wanted to show them what they had risked their lives for.

But Geom Mugeuk did not think taking them into that ominous darkness was the right choice.

—What they see in there will not help them live their lives from now on. Unless you intend to share that treasure with them. Will you?

The Sword King shook his head.

When the Sword King gave some instruction by voice transmission to the man who was an expert in mechanisms, the man ran off somewhere.

Then the Sword King turned to the hall members standing behind him.

“Your mission ends here.”

At those words, the hall members were startled. They had sensed that this excavation was nearly finished, but they had not imagined it would end with the opening of this door. They had thought they would enter that darkness and dismantle more mechanisms.

But they could feel it. They could feel just how dangerous the darkness beyond that door was. They could tell just by seeing their comrades walk forward as if bewitched. If the temptation at the entrance was already this strong, what would it be like inside?

When they thought it was over now, all sorts of emotions surged up within them. After their surprise came joy. And the emotion that dominated them in the end was fear.

They had served the Sword King as their elder brother, believing in him and following him, but at this final moment they could not help but feel anxious that they might be killed to silence them. Now they would learn whether their trust had been correct.

At that moment, the mechanism expert who had received the order returned and handed envelopes to everyone. Inside were the bank drafts promised to them.

Originally, in the beginning, they had been paid their monthly wages. If they asked for money to be sent to someone, it had been sent on their behalf as well. Worried that they might not be paid later, they had accepted the money without fail, but since they could not leave anyway, keeping it with them was meaningless.

And so the hall members had trusted the Sword King and asked to receive the money when they left. Now he had truly given them the promised sum.

At the sight of the money, all the hall members grew choked up. They had gone through all that suffering until now to earn this money.

“You’ve all worked hard.”

At the Sword King’s words, tears flowed from the eyes of several hall members. It was the moment this long, sickening ordeal came to an end.

Of course, there were also many hall members who had wanted to go all the way beyond that door. Just what kind of treasure was there for such mechanisms to have been installed?

Along with that, there was one more expectation. Perhaps they might obtain even a little of the treasure.

But when they saw their comrades being bewitched, those thoughts vanished. All they wanted was to get out of this wretched place as quickly as possible.

Once outside, they wanted to deposit the money at a money house first, then have a drink at a tavern. After that, they would sleep all day at an inn. Others thought of leaving for a place where no one knew them. Each of them wanted something different.

They gathered before the Sword King to offer their final farewell. Among them were some whose lives the Sword King had saved several times.

Representing them all, Hongin spoke to the Sword King.

“Thank you, truly, for everything.”

Behind him, the hall members cried out their thanks in unison.

The Sword King brushed the dust from Hongin’s clothes and said,

“Be happy. I hope the day comes when I stop by the martial hall you run.”

The Sword King had not forgotten that his dream was to run a martial hall.

Hongin bowed his head respectfully and said,

“I will never forget the grace you have shown me.”

The Sword King looked around at the hall members and gave them his final advice.

“Keep what happened here a secret. You won’t die by my hand; you’ll die at the hands of some other greedy bastard. Just say you inherited the money.”

The hall members all nodded, knowing well what he meant.

Geom Mugeuk’s voice transmission flew to the Sword King again.

—I’ve never seen a villain like this before.

Geom Mugeuk still could not understand. Why had there been no rumors about this place in his life before the regression?

Was it because the hall members had gone through that door and all died? Or had they been sent away like this, and, astonishingly, all lived while keeping the secret? Or had something else happened?

In any case, in this life, they had all survived. Some might belittle them, saying they had risked their lives for money, but Geom Mugeuk did not think so. They were brave survivors.

Before leaving, Hongin looked back at Geom Mugeuk one last time. He was the one who had saved them from the attack of the mechanism. Was it because Hongin thought they had seen the stars and slept under cotton blankets thanks to that newcomer? Perhaps he thought that being able to leave safely like this now might also be thanks to that newcomer.

Hongin bowed respectfully to Geom Mugeuk. When he raised his head, he tossed out one last joke. Originally, it was a joke he would have made to the Sword King, but he had not dared.

“Please be sure to come to my martial hall. I’ll teach our youngest brother’s children for free for three months!”

At his final joke, the surrounding hall members laughed together.

And so Hongin and the hall members left that place.

Perhaps by the Sword King’s order, the mechanism expert also followed them out.

Now only Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King remained there.

“Now I can throw a shoe at you without worry.”

At the Sword King’s joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed and answered,

“It’s dark in there, so be careful when you throw it.”

The Sword King, who had been silently gazing at Geom Mugeuk, asked,

“Why aren’t you seeing them off? What if I have my men kill them?”

“Because someone who truly intends to kill them wouldn’t say something like that.”

Of course, that was not the reason. The true reason was this.

“What meaning would there be in sending them safely out of here? If you were determined to kill them, you could kill them as much as you wanted even after they left this martial hall.”

He knew better than anyone just how large an organization they were.

“Perhaps they lived thanks to you.”

“If that is the case, then it was not thanks to me, but thanks to you, Instructor.”

At the look in his eyes asking why, Geom Mugeuk answered,

“Have you already forgotten? When I asked why you were taking me underground, you said it was because you wanted to save those people. I merely faithfully followed those words, so in the end, you were the one who saved them, Instructor. So you may take pride in it and say, ‘I saved all those hall members.’”

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk without a word. Since he revealed no emotion, it was impossible to know what he was thinking.

“Shall we go?”

“Good. Just a moment.”

Geom Mugeuk brought a rope from the box where the work tools were kept.

He tied it around his own waist and handed the other end to the Sword King.

“Tie it around your waist. It may use some method to separate us.”

It meant they should not be scattered from each other. Taking the rope, the Sword King asked with a surprised expression,

“Why are you doing this?”

“You have to protect me, Instructor.”

The Sword King stared at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“Isn’t it the opposite?”

He had wondered whether this Young Cult Leader before him was trying to tie them together in order to protect him.

And what he found even more surprising was this.

“You are not greedy. Most would try to enter alone by any means necessary and seize whatever is inside.”

He had thought Geom Mugeuk would try to shake him off instead, yet here he was offering a rope.

“Don’t they say that every treasure of the martial world has an owner? If it is my fate to receive it, it will come to me even if I am not greedy.”

In his previous life, Geom Mugeuk had felt it keenly. When one sought to accomplish something, one only needed a single drop of greed.

What allowed the first step taken with greed to continue all the way to the end was will and effort. From the second step onward, greed only tripped one’s feet.

The Sword King tied the rope around his waist. They left enough slack so that the two of them could move freely.

“Now, let’s go.”

The two slowly walked into the passage within the darkness. They heightened the senses of their entire bodies to the utmost. They took one step after another, but nothing happened.

They had walked to the middle of the passage when it happened.

Shiiiiiii.

Just as the letters and patterns carved into both walls seemed to glow,

Shak-shak-shak-shak-shak!

As if dropping down from the heavens, martial artists holding spears appeared before them.

Blocking the passage in a single line, they leveled their spears all at once.

Clack-clack-clack-clack-clack!

There were five in all!

Martial artists also appeared behind them and leveled their spears. The same number that had appeared in front had appeared behind as well.

Geom Mugeuk knew.

‘They’re the martial artists from the pictures on the walls.’

They looked exactly like the figures in the paintings, but of course they were not living beings. An illusion art meant to guard this place had been activated.

“I’ll take the rear.”

The Sword King took the rear, and Geom Mugeuk took the front.

Clack-clack-clack-clack-clack!

Like trained soldiers, the martial artists began walking forward with their spears leveled. The energy emanating from them was by no means ordinary.

Whoosh.

Geom Mugeuk’s sword energy flew toward them.

The sword energy that flew out passed through them. It should have swept through them, but it literally just passed by. They felt like beings without substance, like smoke.

The things that had approached thrust their spears.

Clang-clang-clang-clang-clang!

But the spears that struck his sword were real.

Geom Mugeuk swiftly swung his sword, trying to cut the spears apart.

Whoosh.

But just like when he had launched the sword energy, his sword passed right through the spears.

When they attacked, they became real spears, and when Geom Mugeuk tried to cut them, they changed into illusions. It was the first time he had ever experienced such an illusion art.

Clang-clang-clang-clang-clang!

He blocked the thrusting spears and launched surprise attacks, but his sword likewise passed through their bodies and cut only empty air.

Geom Mugeuk retreated one step at a time. The situation behind him was the same. Moreover, they were not puppets blindly thrusting spears. They displayed proper spear arts.

The two, forced back, eventually stood with their backs against each other.

“And you wanted to bring the hall members to a place like this?”

If they really had brought them, they would all have died here. It would truly have become a scene of carnage.

Whish-whish-whish-whish-whish!

The way the creatures fought changed. They hurled their spears at high speed.

The two of them swiftly swung their swords, each blocking in turn. The spears that were knocked away shattered like light and vanished, only for new spears to appear in their hands once more.

The one fortunate thing for Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King was that they did not have to worry about their backs.

‘If we can’t find the method to break this, we’ll die here after our internal energy runs dry!’

No matter what illusion art or sorcery it was, there was always a way to break it. That was an absolute principle that never changed. If so? The method to break it had to be somewhere in this space.

But even with the Blood-Eye Essence and the Divine Eye Art, that method could not be seen.

As he deflected their attacks, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the paintings on the wall. He activated the Divine Eye Art and looked at the painting of the spear-wielding martial artists.

Then one painting caught his eye.

It showed spear-wielding martial artists standing as though guarding a single pattern at the center.

‘Could it be?’

That pattern was drawn here and there across the walls. Geom Mugeuk sprang into the air and thrust his sword into one of those patterns.

Flash!

At that instant, one of the spear-wielding martial artists turned into a cluster of light and disappeared.

Swish! Swish! Swish! Swish!

Deflecting the spears that came flying in ferociously, Geom Mugeuk shouted.

“I found the method to break it. Hold them off for me!”

Geom Mugeuk thrust his sword into another pattern drawn elsewhere. Once again, a martial artist vanished into a cluster of light.

From the front and the back, spears flew at Geom Mugeuk all at once.

The Sword King launched himself forward and knocked aside the spears flying toward Geom Mugeuk.

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk found the patterns in the mural one by one.

Each time he thrust his sword into a pattern, they disappeared. But the fewer their numbers became, the more violently they attacked Geom Mugeuk.

They attacked like madmen, but their opponents were Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King. How could their dwindling numbers stop men who had already found the answer?

And so Geom Mugeuk found the final pattern and stabbed it with his sword. The last remaining spear-wielding martial artist turned into a cluster of light and vanished.

At the same time, a hidden door at the end of the passage opened with a clang.

“How did you know the method to break it?”

“The stronger an illusion art is, the simpler its method of breaking it tends to be. Fortunately, it was an old illusion art, so the method hadn’t been twisted around like they do these days. I was able to deal with it easily.”

But the Sword King could tell. It was not as easy as he made it sound. If it had been him, would he have been able to infer the method from that painting?

“If I had come alone, I would not have been able to break it.”

When the Sword King admitted it honestly, Geom Mugeuk thought inwardly. If that’s the case, then how did you pass through here in your previous life? Who did you come with then?

“Then will you give me half of the treasures in this place?”

He asked half in jest and half in earnest, but unexpectedly, the Sword King nodded.

“I will.”

“You mean it, right? You’re not just acting generous because you’re going to kill me anyway, are you?”

Like that, the two of them cautiously entered through the door that had opened at the end of the passage.

In that instant, both of them stopped in their tracks, and for a while, neither of them said a word.

At last, the one to break the silence was Geom Mugeuk.

“You must not break that promise you just made.”

Chapter 587: Cutting It Off Cleanly Only Makes Me Think of It More
------------------------------------------------------------------
The interior was filled with a dazzling radiance.

In the center, gold ingots were piled up. Truly, they were stacked high.

And the jewels displayed within the cases surrounding them on all sides—top-grade night pearls, of course, as well as red jade, blue jade, jadeite, pearls, and crystals. All manner of gems flaunted their splendor.

It was truly a treasure vault in every sense of the word.

“The very people I wanted to see in my dreams last night showed up, and now I’ve hit the jackpot!”

Geommu Geuk glanced at Geomwang. He too was astonished by the tremendous treasure, yet no greed dwelled in his eyes.

That composed expression alone made it clear that what he pursued was not wealth. That was why this fight would be difficult. He was no ordinary villain.

“Do you regret agreeing to split things half and half?”

At Geommu Geuk’s question, Geomwang nodded.

“It’s far more than I expected.”

“No take-backs.”

Geommu Geuk flung himself down atop the piled gold.

“To sleep amidst gold! I doubt there is a more intoxicating bed than this.”

Then Geomwang’s voice reached him.

“But you like cotton quilts more than gold, don’t you?”

While lying down, Geommu Geuk raised only his head and asked as if arguing the point.

“Who says that?”

“Is that not so?”

“I am called the very incarnation of greed, you know?”

“Who calls you that? You probably just go around saying it yourself.”

Looking at Geomwang, who was staring at him, Geommu Geuk spoke firmly.

“You’re slowly working a scheme to avoid giving me the treasure, but it won’t work. Half of this is mine.”

Geommu Geuk laid his head back comfortably.

Such a high and ornate ceiling in an underground place like this? Gazing up at it, the question naturally arose.

Who could have made this place? Could it have been some wealthy magnate’s secret storehouse from ages past?

No. There was no way Geomwang had been digging through the earth just to seize a magnate’s treasure vault. What he wanted lay elsewhere.

Then who had built it?

The outer formation array, the illusion arts in the passage—this was a place that contained the greatest power of its era.

While still lying down, Geommu Geuk looked at Geomwang. Come to think of it, the rope that had connected their waists had been severed. Noticing his gaze at his waist, Geomwang answered.

“I cut it while we were fighting earlier.”

“I know you cut it so I could fight freely, but somehow I feel upset, as if the bond between master and disciple has been severed. Surely you aren’t going to cut off your relationship with me just as cleanly?”

Of course, Geomwang was not upset in the slightest.

“As you can see, I cut with a single stroke. I’m the type to sever things cleanly and forget.”

Geommu Geuk let out a feigned sigh.

“Human relationships are harder to cut than to tie.”

Then he posed an unexpected question.

“If you cut off a relationship with a single stroke, doesn’t that make you think of the person even more?”

Geomwang’s gaze turned to him, waiting for the explanation.

“If you cut them off suddenly because you can’t stand the sight of them and want to avoid them, you end up thinking about them constantly. Ugh, you think, ‘It’s good not to see them,’ yet they come to mind. You think, ‘I cut ties well,’ yet they come to mind. You wonder, ‘Why didn’t I cut them off sooner?’ and still they come to mind.”

This time, Geomwang asked.

“Then how do you end things?”

“The more I hate them, the better I plan my retreat.”

At the word “operation,” Geomwang let out a hollow laugh.

“You need to plan a strategy just to end one human relationship?”

“Of course. If you cut things off abruptly, you can never forget them for the rest of your life. That’s why most relationships that aren’t even worth remembering remain in your heart until the end.”

“!”

“Cutting them off suddenly doesn’t make you forget quickly. You slowly remove them from your heart, one handful at a time. Slowly, without rushing, until you forget that you were ever tied to that person. The more you hate them, the slower you must go.”

“If you invest that much effort, you’ll think of them even more.”

“You will. Yet the quality of those thoughts is different.”

“How so?”

“They don’t cling to you. They don’t come rushing in unexpectedly. They don’t suddenly make you clench your fists.”

Geomwang gazed silently at Geommu Geuk for a moment.

“So don’t try to cut me off too cleanly. If you do, you’ll keep thinking of me.”

Geomwang placed his hand on the shoes at his waist and asked.

“After all this, you’re going to cut me down with a single stroke, aren’t you?”

“Ah! How are you so quick to catch on?”

Geomwang snorted and walked toward a corner. There was a door there.

“Let’s go.”

“What’s the rush? Let’s enjoy these treasures for a bit before we leave.”

“What we are meant to enjoy is not in this room.”

Geommu Geuk rose and walked toward it.

“Surely we don’t have to break through another barrier when we open that door?”

Geomwang opened it.

Then the dark passage they had seen earlier revealed itself once more.

An energy far more powerful than before was flowing out. The force that beckoned them to enter was likewise stronger than before.

“What in the world lies at the end of that?”

Then Geomwang spoke of this place for the first time.

“Something that can change the fate of this martial world.”

Still looking into the darkness, Geommu Geuk asked.

“For the better? Or for the worse?”

After a brief pause, Geomwang answered.

“That depends on what kind of people we are.”

The two walked back into the darkness.

* * *

“Track them all down and eliminate them!”

Gi Seok flinched for a moment at Yeon Baek-in’s words. He had thought the same needed to be done, but he hadn’t expected Yeon Baek-in to make such a decision so easily.

At the report that all the members who had been underground had left, Yeon Baek-in’s heart sank.

‘He just let all those people go?’

He had thought it was a false report at first. He couldn’t imagine that the man underground would simply release them. He had naturally assumed they would be massacred. Yet he sent them away, even giving them money?

That man might not care, but he himself was different.

There is a treasure vault under the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall!

If even one person let it slip, packs of jackals would swarm this place. Yeon Baek-in did not believe they would keep the secret.

Even if the place was empty now, they would come to verify it with their own eyes.

It was perhaps a ploy by the man underground to corner him.

‘He really got me.’

In any case, he would find them all and kill them. Hadn’t he said so himself? A villain is one who goes on while being cursed.

“We can’t use the Baeksa Group, so I will look for another contract organization to put in the request.”

“As quickly as possible.”

Now Gi Seok knew for certain.

Yeon Baek-in hadn’t turned evil; he had been this kind of person from the start. An order to kill dozens was not something anyone could give easily.

Just then, a subordinate’s voice came from outside.

“A guest has arrived.”

“Who?”

“They say they are the parents of a White Dragon Class member.”

At the subordinate’s report, Yeon Baek-in and Gi Seok looked at each other. He did not meet with members’ parents directly. The subordinate should know that. Why report it?

Then the door opened. It was unheard of for the door to open before he even responded. In other words, it meant someone no one dared to stop.

The one who opened the office door and entered was a middle-aged woman.

The moment he saw her, Yeon Baek-in’s eyes widened. She was a truly elegant woman who captured one’s gaze in an instant.

“I’ve come because of my daughter.”

At her gentle voice, Yeon Baek-in’s stiff expression softened.

“Who might your daughter be?”

“It is Yi An.”

The woman who had entered was none other than the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

Gi Seok, standing beside him, sent a voice transmission.

—The child who used to associate with Seo-jin of Guimun.

Even without the voice transmission, Yeon Baek-in knew of Yi An. Rumors had been rampant that a truly beautiful member had joined.

‘So her beauty takes after her mother.’

Yeon Baek-in spoke to her willingly.

“Very well. Please come in.”

He had thought she came alone, but someone else followed her in.

Yeon Baek-in’s eyes went wide at the person who entered, filling the doorway. A muscular man came in like a hunk of moving iron, and he had so fearsome a face that one could hardly dare meet his eyes.

“I am the father of that Yi An.”

The true father of Yi An: Gwon Ma.

Only now did Yeon Baek-in understand why the subordinates outside had failed to stop them beforehand. That terrifying face was one thing, but those massive fists?

Gwon Ma asked Yeon Baek-in as soon as he entered.

“Where is my daughter now?”

The reason Gwon Ma and the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had come here was twofold.

First, there had been a report from the Tongcheon Gak. A report that all the members who had been underground had emerged in secret.

They could now tell without needing to hear it that Geommu Geuk had spared them and sent them away.

Bedding had been sent down, they had come out and looked at the stars, and the finale was him sending them all away alive. It was truly a move befitting Geommu Geuk.

The problem was the Hall Master before them. Judging from his actions thus far and the words of his younger brother, Yeon Baek-jin, it seemed unlikely he would let the members who had come out of the underground survive.

So they had come together to save them.

We entrust them to the two Demon Venerables.

That was surely what their hearts had meant. At this moment, the two Demon Venerables shared a mind with Geommu Geuk.

The second reason they had shown themselves was to pressure and warn the forces behind him.

We are here, so do not act rashly! We stand beside the Young Cult Leader.

They wanted to prevent another expert from lending strength underground. Just as they were watching the other side’s movements, so too were they being watched.

Gwon Ma asked bluntly.

“I heard that after following her instructor, she hasn’t returned?”

Yeon Baek-in was inwardly flustered. He hadn’t expected such a fearsome father and such an elegant mother to come looking. With parents like these, they needn’t have sent their daughter to a martial hall.

Gi Seok’s voice transmission flew in again.

—Yi An is currently underground.

After Geomwang had left with Geommu Geuk, Yi An, Seo-jin, Yu Gwang, and Gyo Seok, they had not attended classes.

Since they didn’t know the children were inside the Heavenly Demon Cult, Yeon Baek-in naturally assumed the Geomwang underground had taken them.

“Aren’t I asking? Where is my child?”

Normally he would have demanded what rudeness this was, but he didn’t dare do so to Gwon Ma. Though Gwon Ma revealed no demonic energy, his aura pressed down on Yeon Baek-in so heavily he could barely breathe.

Then the Ilhwa Sword Venerable stepped forward.

“Please understand, Hall Master. This person’s temperament is rather fiery.”

How could a couple be so contrasting? Yeon Baek-in found himself smiling at the woman and frowning at the man.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable asked in a gentle tone.

“We came to see our daughter, but not a single person knows where she has gone, so I am quite worried.”

Though she rarely went out, she was more skilled at dealing with people than anyone.

Then Gi Seok stepped forward in his place to answer.

“She has gone out for outdoor training with the White Dragon Class instructor.”

“But the other White Dragon Class members were attending class?”

“A few were specially selected to go. It seems they displayed exceptional skill.”

Gi Seok answered calmly without panicking.

“She will return in a few days.”

“Do members go out for external training like this?”

This time, Yeon Baek-in answered.

“Even I, the Hall Master, cannot interfere with how an instructor teaches.”

Yeon Baek-in looked at the two with confident eyes.

“If you entrusted her education to us, you must trust us.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable stood up with a pleasant expression.

“We were impolite in our haste. We shall take our leave for today.”

Gwon Ma left one last remark before leaving.

“That’s right. We entrusted our child to you because we trusted you.”

Those meaningful words were a final chance for Yeon Baek-in. A warning not to touch the members.

But could the current Yeon Baek-in grasp the meaning contained in those words?

Only after the two left the office did Yeon Baek-in feel relieved. Gwon Ma’s momentum had been so intense that his heart was still pounding.

“It is because the member called Yi An is so exceptionally beautiful.”

He understood Gwon Ma’s reaction. If he had such a beautiful daughter, he would naturally be on edge.

But to come here and throw his weight around like this? It was all because he looked down on the Hall Master of this martial hall. If only he mastered a peerless divine art, he would never suffer such humiliation.

“Then the only White Dragon Class children underground now are the ones taken away afterward.”

“That is correct.”

He couldn’t fathom what that man was thinking. In any case, judging by the fact that he had released the members, the end was surely not far off.

“What will you do about that couple? They didn’t seem like ordinary experts.”

On the other hand, Yeon Baek-in already had it all figured out.

“The one who has their daughter should take responsibility.”

He meant he would leave it to Geomwang to handle.

“You take care of the members as quickly as possible.”

* * *

Gi Seok, wearing a pressed bamboo hat, walked hurriedly.

This time, the side he was putting in the request with was the Black Tiger School. Like the Baeksa Group, it was a contract organization that would do anything for money. Since relations with the Baeksa Group were not good, they were the perfect choice for this request.

Usually, when meeting with a contract organization, one would meet in a very secluded place or in a crowded area like this.

Today, they had agreed to meet at a tavern on the main street.

As Gi Seok walked among the pedestrians, he looked at the tavern ahead. A man was waiting at the window seat they had agreed upon.

As he was walking toward him.

‘Huh? Those people?’

In the distance were Yi An’s parents, whom he had seen in Yeon Baek-in’s office. The woman recognized him and happily waved her hand.

Gi Seok, who had unconsciously moved to greet them, flinched in surprise.

‘How did she recognize me when I’m wearing a bamboo hat?’

The man beside her didn’t wave, but instead slowly extended his fist toward Gi Seok.

A fist slowly extended from afar, through the crowd.

‘What is he doing?’

Gi Seok thought it was a warning to him. Bring my daughter back quickly.

And that warning was ticklish. No, it was actually ticklish.

Gi Seok felt an unbearable itch in his chest.

Unable to endure it, he opened his collar and looked down at his chest.

“!”

Gi Seok’s eyes went wide.

His chest began to cave inward, and a shape began to form.

Astonishingly, it was the shape of a massive fist.

A giant fist filling his chest.

But soon, that outline began to fade.

When the fist-shaped outline completely vanished like a lie, Gi Seok, who had been standing blankly in the middle of the street looking down at his chest, slowly sank to the ground. His head dropped limply, already dead.

Not a sound of its flight, nor a scream, nor any trace left upon the body—a silent fist.

Chapter 588: When You Realize Being Alone Is Harder
---------------------------------------------------
Geomugeuk and the Sword King walked down the dark passage.

This time as well, all manner of patterns, characters, and drawings were inscribed on the walls. The style was both similar to and different from the paintings they had seen earlier. It did not seem as though the same person had drawn them, but judging by the similar feeling they gave off—

‘Were they fellow disciples?’

He thought that might be the case.

Having found the method to break through in the previous paintings, he did not pass over even a single character lightly.

“Look here.”

There was a drawing where Geomugeuk pointed. The very pattern that had served as the key to breaking through earlier was drawn here as well.

“It seems to be a pattern that held meaning for the ones who made this place.”

The Sword King merely nodded once and said nothing else.

It was when the two of them had reached roughly the center of the passage.

Ssssss.

The instant he felt the formation activate, their surroundings changed in the blink of an eye.

‘Where is this?’

A blue sky reflected beneath his feet. This place, where it felt as though he were standing in the sky, was the same place that had appeared when Geomugeuk released his qi force.

And so below was the sea. Above, an endlessly clear and peaceful sky; below, an unfathomable abyssal sea.

“Master!”

Geomugeuk called out, but the Sword King was not there.

“Should we have tied the rope again?”

Of course, it was a joke. Even if they had tied a rope, he would not have been able to enter this space together with the Sword King. This trial was undoubtedly one each person had to overcome on their own.

“You aren’t making only me suffer while you sit back and watch for fun, are you?”

Just as Geomugeuk’s voice rang out resoundingly, someone appeared before him.

Amazingly, the person was Geomugeuk himself. Yet even though another version of himself had appeared, Geomugeuk was not surprised.

He knew well that old illusions and formations often used this method, making one’s own self appear. This trial was likely one that tested the strength of the inner self.

This place is inside my own heart. So trust yourself.

Steeling his heart like that, Geomugeuk approached himself first.

After staring quietly at that face, Geomugeuk spoke to himself.

“It’s been a while.”

At that greeting, the other Geomugeuk looked puzzled.

“I see you from time to time.”

If someone else heard it, they might think he was mad, but in truth, whenever Geomugeuk took time to rest alone, he would sometimes stand face-to-face with his lonely self from before his regression.

Once, he had even stood across from himself with a vast hole between them and told him not to fall into it.

“You came today to put me in trouble, didn’t you?”

The instant the other self’s eyes flashed, the place where he stood changed in the blink of an eye.

Geomugeuk was running.

‘Where is this?’

The place he was heading toward was the Heavenly Demon Hall within the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.

‘Father!’

It was that day. The day all the people of the Heavenly Demon Hall were killed by Hwa Mugi!

Calling desperately for his father, Geomugeuk was blindly running toward the Heavenly Demon Hall. He should not have done that, but the self of that time had been far too agitated to make any other judgment.

Corpses lay collapsed everywhere. Not only martial artists, but even those who did not know martial arts—all the people of the Heavenly Demon Hall had fallen. The sight only drove him into greater agitation.

‘The outer court is intact, so how in the world was the Heavenly Demon Hall breached?’

As if answering that question, a single person appeared.

A man stood on top of the wall surrounding the Heavenly Demon Hall, arms folded as he looked down over the place. He was the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable of the past.

It was a sight he had not seen that day in the past. An illusion visible only now, inside this formation.

Because he had regarded him as a traitor, his figure must have appeared. After all, this place was his own heart.

In the distance, Hwa Mugi’s figure appeared.

His face could not be seen. But Geomugeuk could feel that he was smiling coldly in his direction.

A single streak of sword qi flew at him without a word.

He also saw Ian hurl himself in front of him.

‘Ian.’

He felt the sword qi that pierced Ian’s chest pass through and pierce his own chest as well.

Because they had fallen collapsed one atop the other, Ian’s hot blood flowed down to his own chest.

That day, which he had tried not to recall because he had been so utterly powerless, was being vividly reenacted within the formation.

Then someone walked over to his fallen self and looked down at him. It was neither Hwa Mugi nor the Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable.

It was the Blood Heaven Saber Demon, with a great saber strapped to his back. His eyes were colder than anything.

Beside him stood the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, looking down at him. The smile beneath his mask felt unfamiliar. The other Demon Venerables gathered around his dying self.

He saw the Fist Demon’s massive fists, and the heavily made-up face of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. The stench of alcohol poured from the Drunken Demon, and from the Demon Buddha’s body radiated not golden light, but a crimson glow. The drawings on the poison pouches at the Poison King’s waist were not their current cute forms, but had been shaped into something terrifying.

They were all looking down at him, laughing at him.

Geomugeuk could tell. This formation was trying to deceive him. Telling him that everyone that day had been a traitor.

—Be enraged!

It was trying to make his blood boil like lava.

If he had been his ordinary self, not someone who had regressed, that anguish and hatred would have dominated him. The unknown energy emitted by the formation was endlessly numbing his reason, drawing out suspicion and rage. It was an energy an ordinary person could never withstand.

This formation was the Blood Wave Mad Soul Formation, which drove people mad by stimulating the anger and hatred deep within their hearts.

But it did not work on Geomugeuk.

‘From the very beginning, when I first regressed, I thought all of them were traitors anyway.’

He had come this far with that in his heart.

And yet, why had he treated them with such sincerity? Because this life was a different life from the last.

It did not matter who had betrayed him in his previous life. The Demon Venerables of this life were different people from back then. He had made them into different people. All their fates had changed.

Looking up at the sneering Demon Venerables, Geomugeuk instead smiled brightly.

“Is there something interesting to see?”

Geomugeuk rose to his feet. Before he knew it, Ian’s corpse had vanished, and so had the wound that had pierced through his body.

When Geomugeuk faced them with a smile, the formation dug into another weakness.

—Do you truly intend to protect them all?

When he turned around, others had appeared before he knew it.

Not only the Demon Venerables, but Ian, Seo Daeryong, Jang Ho-hyung, and even his friends had appeared. Bisa-in, Jin Hagun, Jin Haryeong, Han Seol as well. The escort warriors and even Jo Chunbae. Everyone he knew had appeared.

—You are arrogant. Life is difficult enough when one simply protects oneself and survives, yet you say you will live while protecting all those people.

Another version of himself walked toward Geomugeuk.

—Admit it. That it’s hard. Admit that it’s truly so hard you can’t bear it anymore.

Splash.

The Demon Venerables began grabbing Geomugeuk by the arms and legs and hanging on. Geomugeuk began to be dragged down into the abyss beneath his feet.

All the others rushed in as well and began pulling Geomugeuk down. They were all shouting for him to save them.

And so Geomugeuk sank into the abyss.

Just as the other Geomugeuk looked down beneath the water with a satisfied expression—

Fwaaaaaak.

Geomugeuk burst back up from within the water.

Everyone who had been pulling him down had lifted him up and risen to the surface with him.

They were not the people created by the formation who had been dragging Geomugeuk down moments ago. They now wore the expressions of those who were together with Geomugeuk in the present.

The formation could not dominate Geomugeuk’s heart.

“I am not struggling.”

—Do not deceive yourself.

Geomugeuk could feel it. The moment he admitted that it was hard, the person caught in the formation would collapse.

At the same time, he understood that the way to break this formation was to refuse to admit it. In a way the formation itself could accept.

“It’s not as hard as I thought. Once you learn that being alone is harder.”

“!”

This time, Geomugeuk walked toward the other version of himself.

Behind this Geomugeuk stood everyone, while the other Geomugeuk was alone.

Geomugeuk looked at the other self. He was not merely an illusion created by the formation. He was Geomugeuk’s self from before the regression.

“Because you existed, I was able to make these people. I mean that.”

That was why the gaze with which he looked at himself was gentler than anything.

“Is it not hard? It is. Do I not want to go somewhere alone? At times, I do. But if you are me, then you know too.”

Now Geomugeuk was no longer speaking to himself, but to the one who had created this trial.

“You must never have lived a life alone. You must never have passed so many nights by yourself, nor walked an endless road while speaking only to your own footprints. If you experience that dreadful loneliness, you come to understand. That time alone is not so wonderful, nor does it give such a sense of freedom. Only when there are people walking with you does that time alone finally gain value. Surely you know that too, do you not?”

With an even gentler gaze, Geomugeuk spoke to himself, and to the formation.

“This formation does not work on me, so withdraw now.”

The moment those words ended, astonishingly, the formation began to break.

Ssssss.

The people around him began to vanish one by one.

Geomugeuk turned and looked at them as they disappeared. They had been born from his heart, and were now disappearing back into his heart.

“I’ll see you all back at the cult!”

The others disappeared first, and the Demon Venerables vanished last.

Because they had been created by his own heart, they disappeared in the forms Geomugeuk wished for.

The Blood Heaven Saber Demon vanished while sitting with his great saber planted in the ground, holding out a cup of liquor; the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon while removing his mask. The Drunken Demon staggered drunkenly and fell flat. Sorry, hyung. The Poison King vanished crouched down, staring at a venomous insect; the Ilhwa Sword Venerable while drawing her sword and aiming it at him. The Demon Buddha vanished while making a Buddha statue. The Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable while greeting him warmly.

And his father, as always, while wearing that sneer.

The moment his father disappeared, the formation vanished completely, and Geomugeuk was once again standing in the dark passage.

“Phew.”

After letting out a light sigh, Geomugeuk slowly moved forward and walked to the end of the passage.

Clank.

Then the door at the end opened.

He had passed through this trial even more easily than the first. Because his heart was firmer than anyone’s, the Blood Wave Mad Soul Formation had been unable to dig into his mind.

But the Sword King had still not appeared there.

Geomugeuk did not enter through the door. Instead, he sat down with his back to it, looking at the passage he had come through.

“Stop wandering and come out already.”

Geomugeuk sat there and closed his eyes.

How long did he wait like that?

“Phew.”

Letting out a long sigh, the Sword King appeared.

From that sigh, Geomugeuk could tell how difficult this trial had been for him. Who had it been that had seized him and dragged him into the abyss?

“Fortunately, you shook them off.”

Seeing that Geomugeuk had come out first, the Sword King asked with a surprised expression.

“Was it not hard for you?”

“My life is quite simple, after all.”

The Sword King gave him a look that seemed to ask, Is that really so? Then, looking at the open door behind him, he asked again. The scent drifting out from there was one no martial artist could endure: the distinctive pleasant fragrance given off by spiritual medicines.

“Why did you not go in alone?”

“Because I have enough loyalty to withstand that much.”

As the Sword King approached Geomugeuk, he said,

“In recognition of that loyalty, I’ll give you half again this time.”

The Sword King flung the door wide open.

When Geomugeuk and the Sword King looked inside, they were once again unable to say a word.

An even greater emotion than in the first room blossomed across both their faces.

“What you said was right. That room was not the one we were meant to savor.”

This room was the true space for a martial artist. All manner of spiritual medicines were displayed on stands on every side.

Geomugeuk slowly stepped forward.

“May I truly take half?”

The Sword King nodded.

“These are items one cannot buy even with money, and still?”

No matter how much money one had, obtaining the spiritual medicines here would not be easy. Of course, it was possible. If one paid tens of thousands of nyang, hundreds of thousands of nyang, or millions of nyang for a single spiritual medicine.

The Sword King nodded and said,

“I have that much loyalty too. Now, I’m hungry, so let’s eat some first. If we leave it behind, it will only benefit someone else.”

The Sword King tossed a spiritual medicine in front of him to Geomugeuk.

The spiritual medicine he had casually thrown was, astonishingly, the Divine Fire Pill, which possessed effects equal to ten-thousand-year Snow Ginseng.

“I never refuse spiritual medicines offered to me.”

“I told you, half is yours.”

“I will eat it with gratitude.”

Geom Mugeuk chewed and swallowed the Shinhwadan, then asked,

“Is it all right to just hand out elixirs to someone you’re supposed to fight?”

“That’s why I’m eating one too, isn’t it?”

The Sword King also chewed up a spirit pill.

Geom Mugeuk sat down first and began circulating his qi, assimilating the medicinal effects.

The Sword King was about to do the same, but instead approached Geom Mugeuk.

He stood in front of him and silently looked down at him.

Since Geom Mugeuk was in the middle of circulating his qi to dissolve the medicinal effects, now was the perfect chance to attack. Naturally, a strange tension hung in the air.

“What would you do if I ambushed you while you were circulating your qi?”

Geom Mugeuk kept his eyes closed and said nothing.

The Sword King bent at the waist, peered into his face, and spoke again.

“You can talk while circulating qi, can’t you? You can move too.”

When there was still no answer, the Sword King abruptly jabbed toward his eyes. At that moment, Geom Mugeuk snapped his head aside to avoid it and opened his eyes.

“I trust that you’re not that kind of person.”

The Sword King shook his head with an expression that said, as expected. Speaking while circulating qi, moving freely—he was a young cult leader who had already surpassed ordinary martial artists.

When the Sword King sat cross-legged beside him, this time Geom Mugeuk asked,

“What if I finish first and attack you?”

The Sword King closed his eyes and said,

“Then do as you like.”

As if he truly trusted Geom Mugeuk, he began regulating his breath and circulating his qi.

After watching him for a moment, Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes again.

Thus, the two of them focused on circulating their qi and fully assimilated the medicinal effects.

After finishing, the Sword King tossed another elixir to Geom Mugeuk.

“This one tastes good too.”

This elixir was the Jeogundan (Accumulated Cloud Pill).

“Judging by the nature of their medicinal effects, I think these two can be assimilated together.”

He tossed another spirit pill to Geom Mugeuk. This one was the Somyeongdan (Radiant Clarity Pill).

“You really want us to eat them all?”

“You don’t want to? If you don’t, then don’t.”

When the Sword King held out his hand as if asking for them back, Geom Mugeuk popped the pills in his hand into his mouth one after another.

“As if that would be the case.”

The two of them repeated the process of taking elixirs and assimilating them, then taking more and assimilating them again. To think there would come a day when they felt full from eating elixirs.

When they finally finished circulating their qi, both their eyes were clearer and deeper than ever.

In particular, Geom Mugeuk’s internal energy was just like his aura. It was so clear and deep that its quantity could no longer be measured.

Even so, there were still elixirs left there, so Geom Mugeuk set aside his share.

“Who are you gathering all those for?”

“Surely there are people I could give them to.”

“It’s all wasted effort, no matter whose mouth you put them in. Just eat them all yourself while you can.”

But Geom Mugeuk carefully tucked them into his robes, then stood before the door again.

Beyond it lay a passage of darkness, exuding an even more intense energy than before.

Geom Mugeuk could instinctively sense it. This was the final trial.

What the Sword King desired must be beyond that. The core of this underground treasury was not the treasures or the elixirs, but that place.

“At this point, you should confess. ‘In truth, I was capable of passing through all these trials, but I pretended to struggle in order to test you. Now that your test is over, I’ll simply let you pass through the final trial.’”

The Sword King made a different confession.

“I nearly died back at the last trial.”

“……”

“……”

Geom Mugeuk entered the dark passage first and asked him,

“Will you give me half this time too?”

Chapter 589: Now You Seem Like the Demonic Cult's Young Leader
--------------------------------------------------------------
Yeon Baek-in was silently looking down at Giseok’s corpse.

When he first received the report that Giseok had died, Yeon Baek-in did not believe it. So the first thing he did was burst out shouting, asking what nonsense that was.

Even though he knew a subordinate would never dare spout nonsense in front of him, he simply did not want to believe it.

On top of that, they said Giseok had been walking along the road when he suddenly slumped down and died. He possessed skill that could not even be compared to ordinary instructors, so it was truly an absurd death.

The physician who had examined Giseok’s corpse entered the place where he was. He was the most skilled physician in this region.

“How did he die?”

Skipping even the greetings, he asked for the cause of death first. The physician answered cautiously.

“When I examined his insides, his five viscera and six bowels had been completely destroyed.”

Yeon Baek-in thought, Of course.

“Was it poison?”

“No. It was caused by a powerful impact from outside.”

Yeon Baek-in’s gaze turned toward Giseok’s corpse. There was no trace of impact anywhere on his body. Not even the faintest bruise.

“It seems a powerful internal striking technique was used to shock only the inside, but this is the first time I have seen no external traces to this extent.”

Moreover, according to the witnesses, no one had directly struck him.

Then that meant someone had thrown a punch from a distance and done this? It was a truly unbelievable realm.

When Yeon Baek-in thought about who the culprit might be, two people came to mind.

The first person who came to mind was the husband from that couple who had come to his office. Perhaps because Giseok had not died from being stabbed by a weapon but from an external blow, that man’s enormous fist came to mind. He had been that memorable an opponent.

‘But he has no reason to kill Giseok, does he?’

The next person who came to mind was that man underground.

‘Could this possibly be his doing?’

Had he released the officials because he had anticipated something like this? Was he trying to use this matter as an excuse to discard him? Or was he truly angry because Yeon Baek-in had tried to kill the people he had released?

Whatever the motive, the only person nearby capable of killing Giseok with such astonishing skill was him. Even if he had not killed him personally, he was also the most likely person capable of sending an expert who could.

‘Even so, how dare he kill one of my own limbs like this!’

This death felt like a warning aimed at him. You are next.

After fear came a surge of anger.

‘This is my martial hall. My land, my people.’

Leaving that place, Yeon Baek-in headed straight for the sparring platform.

He had to find out for certain whether it truly was his doing. If it was, then even if he had to kneel and beg forgiveness, he had to appease his anger. After all, he had already killed his younger brother on his way here.

When he arrived at the sparring platform, someone there blocked him from going down underground.

It was the man who specialized in mechanisms, one of the Sword King’s aides.

“You cannot go down.”

“Move aside. I must meet him.”

He intended to go down even if he had to use force. At that moment, the mechanism expert looked to the side. Yeon Baek-in naturally followed his gaze and turned his head that way.

Far off in the spectator seats, a man was sitting there. Had he been there from the beginning? No matter how urgent his mind had been, how had he failed to notice a person there?

The man sat in a chair with both arms resting over his knees, his head lowered. Was he wearing something on his hands? Only those things, reflecting white in the sunlight, caught his eye.

“Please turn back.”

Just as he was about to turn back to the mechanism expert and say he could not do that—

At an inexplicable sensation, Yeon Baek-in turned his head again.

Before he knew it, the man who had been sitting far away was standing right beside him. He had not sensed him coming here at all. It felt as though the man had moved through space—or rather, as though he had been standing here from the very beginning.

When one saw a person, one naturally looked at their face first, but with this man, Yeon Baek-in’s gaze went to his body first.

Long arms and long legs. He truly had an exceptionally well-formed body. And Yeon Baek-in had never seen anyone with muscles like his. If there was such a phrase as not having a single unnecessary muscle on one’s body, then he thought this man ought to be the owner of that expression.

The man’s fists were tightly wrapped in bandages, and it was impossible to tell whether he was injured or whether he was protecting his hands.

Only after looking over his body did Yeon Baek-in finally look at his face. In truth, he should have looked at his face first. If he had, he would not have been leisurely scanning this man’s body in front of him.

The moment he saw the man’s two eyes, which seemed hollow, Yeon Baek-in felt his entire body freeze. It was not because the other man was releasing any aura.

His eyes showed no emotion, yet they were the eyes of a man who had killed truly many people. Behind this man, Yeon Baek-in saw a hell where people screamed within a pit of fire.

And when he came to his senses, Yeon Baek-in was hurrying back the way he had come. He must have been so frightened after meeting those eyes that his memory had briefly cut out. He could not remember how he had turned around.

When he had put some distance between them and looked back again, the man was nowhere to be seen.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King walked together down the dark passage.

What kind of trial would be waiting this time? If it was stronger than the previous one, passing through it would not be easy.

The walls of this passage, too, were covered in all sorts of letters, patterns, and drawings that could not be understood.

“That pattern is here as well.”

The pattern they had seen earlier was drawn in several places again. Was it a pattern representing the clan of those who had designed this trial? Or did it have some other meaning?

“You know what this pattern is, don’t you?”

“Why do you think I know?”

“It’s just a feeling. You don’t seem like someone who would do something he didn’t understand well.”

“I appreciate you thinking highly of me, but I’m not that remarkable a person.”

“And you’re even modest.”

“I said I’m not!”

Their conversation flowed naturally, but both of them were sharply observing their surroundings as they advanced carefully.

And this time as well, when they reached about the middle of the passage, the trial began.

Sssssss.

The formation activated, and their surroundings changed.

A desolate wasteland revealed itself. The scorching heat rising from the ground shimmered at the distant horizon, and the wind carried dust up into the sky.

“I like this formation!”

The reason Geom Mugeuk was pleased was because the Sword King was beside him.

“It seems to be a trial we have to break through by joining forces. I like that it’s not lonely. Look over there.”

Where Geom Mugeuk pointed, a signpost stood.

—You cannot leave unless you kill them all.

“Since it’s even warning us in advance, it looks like we’ll be seeing a lot of blood.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Sword King nodded.

Far away, enemies were rushing toward them, kicking up clouds of dust. Their footsteps shook the ground, and shouts rang out from every direction.

“I understand why the elixir room was placed before this.”

The number charging at them was tremendous. They were swarming in like a horde of ants.

“You seem pleased right now.”

“Seeing this many enemies after so long makes me a little excited.”

Of course, he was not excited out of fear. It was the joy of being able to display his skill to his heart’s content. They were not real people but enemies created by the formation, so he could cut them down without hesitation.

“Seeing you excited at the thought of bloodshed, you finally seem like the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult.”

“It was hard keeping it hidden.”

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword.

“I’ll take this side. Ah, do you know what’s good about fighting together? It means I can fight with my back to a wall.”

It meant he trusted the person behind him that much.

“What will you do if that wall collapses toward you?”

“It will be as soft as a cotton blanket.”

The moment he finished speaking, a streak of sword qi flew from the Black Demon Sword.

Shiiiiiiik.

Those running at the front threw themselves aside to avoid the sword qi. But those behind them could not avoid it in time and were swept away.

Geom Mugeuk had gauged their level of martial arts by observing how they dodged the sword qi.

With this single move, Geom Mugeuk was certain.

“They’re the first stage. Conserve your internal energy as you deal with them.”
