He was certain that no matter how many there were, they were not the end.

Geom Mugeuk charged toward them. The Black Demon Sword cut them down. Wherever he passed, enemies stabbed through the heart fell one after another.

Conserving internal energy did not mean it was wise to never use sword qi at all. That would consume just as much stamina.

Swaaaaaak!

They could not avoid the sword qi fired at close range. Dozens of them were swept up in the sword qi and killed all at once.

But they kept pouring in endlessly over the fallen corpses. Just looking at them was enough to be overwhelming.

In the middle of the fight, Geom Mugeuk looked toward the Sword King.

The instant the Sword King’s sword flashed, over a dozen enemies fell at once. Even though he was not emitting visible sword qi, the necks of enemies at distances his sword could not reach were severed.

‘Formless Sword Qi!’

Geom Mugeuk recognized the Sword King’s divine prowess at once. Before his regression, what had raised the name of the Sword King, who became the greatest under heaven, was precisely that Formless Sword Qi.

Because the sword qi could not be seen, avoiding it was several times more difficult than avoiding ordinary sword qi.

Was it confidence? In this battle that Geom Mugeuk could witness, the Sword King was showing him the use of Formless Sword Qi without trying to hide it.

No matter how many enemies came swarming in, on this side there was Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King, who could even use Formless Sword Qi.

In an instant, all those many enemies had fallen.

Sssssss.

All the piled-up corpses disappeared.

Without giving them even a moment to rest, enemies far away were once again raising dust as they charged toward them. They were running faster than the first group.

“This is my first time seeing Formless Sword Qi.”

“Do you think you can dodge it?”

“I think I’ll need to come up with more strategies.”

“What kind of strategies?”

“The kind that will make you hesitate at the final moment, Instructor.”

At that, the Sword King gave an inscrutable smile.

“If so, then you’re falling for my strategy.”

“What do you mean?”

“I’ll act as if I’m going to hesitate, then cut you down without hesitating.”

“And you’re telling me that?”

“To confuse you.”

His fight with this man would happen after they entered the final room.

He had clearly brought Geom Mugeuk because he needed him. He had not brought him merely to pass through these trials. There was definitely another reason.

As much as he was curious about what lay in the room the Sword King was trying to reach, Geom Mugeuk was curious about that reason.

‘So make sure you survive and enter that room with me.’

Meanwhile, the second group of enemies came surging in, hundreds of blades flashing. Their numbers had decreased compared to before, but their momentum was even sharper.

The two of them calmly dealt with them. Geom Mugeuk did not use the Nine Flames Demonic Art, and the Sword King, too, only used Formless Sword Qi and did not use his unique martial arts.

If they had been ordinary experts, this would have been a situation difficult to endure.

The numbers pouring in were great, and their skill was by no means trivial. And the greatest problem was that they knew no fear.

Moreover, even if they dealt with them well, internal energy was still a problem. Most experts would have run dry of internal energy before finishing off this second attack.

But Geom Mugeuk was overflowing with internal energy. Astonishingly, the fighting up to now had barely left any sign that he had used internal energy at all.

The Sword King, too, was full of vigor. The levels of the first and second groups of enemies were clearly different, but the second group also fell just like the first.

Before the martial arts of Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King, that difference in ability held no meaning at all. Judged coldly by relative standards, the first enemies had been weaklings, and the second were weaklings as well.

The two of them once again finished them all off.

This time as well, all the piled-up corpses disappeared.

Enemies were coming in yet again. This time, they advanced more slowly than before. As though they were saying they would come slowly and kill them.

“This seems to be the last one.”

No matter that it was a formation, there were definite limits. Just the attacks so far must have required an enormous amount of money, internal energy, and mental effort to create this formation.

“You sound disappointed that it’s the last.”

“To be honest, I am. Fighting together with you is so much fun, Instructor. I’d like it if ten more stages came out from now on.”

“You just want to steal a look at my martial arts.”

“All that happens is my intentions keep getting exposed, and I keep learning how sharp your eye is, Instructor.”

The Sword King silently stared at Geom Mugeuk, then brought up the situation outside.

“Aren’t you worried about the outside? The shield is here, but the people you have to protect are out there, aren’t they?”

“Should I be worried?”

After a brief pause, the Sword King spoke. A trace of hesitation showed in him, as though he were debating whether to say it or not.

“Someone else should have arrived by now.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. By someone else, he meant another of the Twelve Zodiac Kings.

“They may come down here, or they may try to hold your people back.”

It meant he himself did not know how they would move. From this, one thing became clear: the Twelve Zodiac Kings were moving toward a single goal, but they also acted quite independently. He had known before that they sometimes disagreed among themselves, but today it became even clearer.

“You say they might take your people, so why are you smiling?”

“Because I’m happy. You’re telling me because you’re worried about me right now, aren’t you?”

With an expression that asked whether this was really the time to be so relaxed, the Sword King brought up Ian.

“Did you not say she was someone like your heart?”

Meaning she might be in danger. This time, Geom Mugeuk answered honestly.

“It’s all right. Other shields have arrived.”

The Sword King asked in surprise.

“The Demon Supremes are here?”

“I called them on the very day I first met you, Master.”

He had called them because he knew the man would later become the First King among the Twelve Zodiac Kings, but to the Sword King now, it must have sounded as though he had called them because he held him in such high regard.

In the meantime, the third group of enemies launched their attack. These enemies emitted sword qi. They were more skilled than the ones before.

Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!

Dozens of strands of sword qi flew in from all directions, tearing through the air. The powerful killing intent carried in their sword qi chilled the surrounding air.

Through the sword qi embroidered like a spiderweb, Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King traversed the air. No matter how densely they wove their net of sword qi, they could not catch the two of them.

Amid the sword qi they had created, sword qi that seemed to declare, This is what true sword qi is, flew out.

Each time their swords flashed, the net of sword qi was torn apart. The great net became smaller, and smaller still.

The last enemy to unleash sword qi was pierced at the same time by two swords, one from the front and one from the back. Geom Mugeuk had thrust his sword in from the front, and the Sword King from behind.

The blades that had pierced through the body were aimed at each other.

Sssssss.

Along with the fallen corpses, the final enemy impaled on their swords also disappeared.

And so the two men were left with their swords pointed at each other. After silently looking at one another, Geom Mugeuk smiled first and withdrew his sword.

“You’ve worked hard.”

“As have you.”

As if the formation truly ended there, no more enemies surged in.

“We probably set the record for breaking this formation the fastest since it was created.”

But though all the corpses had vanished, the wasteland around the two of them remained exactly as it was. The formation had not been broken.

Geom Mugeuk looked around. He checked to see if there was perhaps an enemy they had not killed.

But the corpses had already all disappeared, and there was no one anywhere around that vast wasteland. No new enemy appeared either.

The only thing left there was the sign they had seen at the beginning.

—You cannot leave unless you kill them all.

Chapter 590: Haven’t You Lived Your Life Running Nonstop?
---------------------------------------------------------
If we don’t kill everyone, we can’t leave?

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King looked at each other.

“It means we’re to kill even the person we came here with.”

The Sword King’s expression hardened at once, and anger poured out of him.

“What kind of damn formation is this?”

Bang!

The Sword King kicked the signpost without mercy. The shattered signpost scattered everywhere. As if that was not enough to vent his anger, he even yanked out the stake of the signpost and snapped it into pieces.

Geom Mugeuk, on the other hand, watched the Sword King in silence.

“Aren’t you angry?”

“You got angry enough for me as well.”

“We absolutely hate this kind of thing.”

The Sword King, who had been raging like that, seemed to calm himself for a moment.

Shhng!

He smoothly drew his iron sword.

Swoooosh.

Geom Mugeuk tilted his head back. Formless sword qi brushed past his face.

The Sword King spoke with his sword drawn.

“Shall we do it now?”

“No, why are you taking your anger out on me?”

The instant he drew his sword, the air around them changed completely.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. The Black Demon Sword wanted to cry out. Ever since meeting the Sword King, he had kept the Black Demon Sword suppressed.

‘Not yet.’

Geom Mugeuk shook his head.

“I won’t do it.”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk saw it. For the briefest instant, a certain emotion flashed through the Sword King’s eyes. It was unmistakably close to relief.

“If we don’t? Then what do you propose?”

Then Geom Mugeuk said something utterly unexpected.

“Then we just don’t leave.”

“What?”

“Let’s not leave. Let’s live here.”

Geom Mugeuk looked up at the sky and shouted. It was a cry directed at the one who must have created this place.

“Hey! We’re not leaving!”

At that, the Sword King could not hold back and burst into laughter. He might not have meant it sincerely, but the way he did not lose his composure even in a situation like this truly pleased him.

“You really are…”

The Sword King did not finish his sentence.

Geom Mugeuk looked around in every direction, then said to the Sword King,

“Please lend me one of your shoes for a moment.”

“Are you about to say something that will make me throw my shoe at you?”

“No. If that were the case, I’d have asked for both.”

With a look that asked, What on earth is he trying to do? the Sword King handed over the shoe hanging at his waist.

Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes for a moment as if praying for something, then threw the shoe upward. The shoe rose high into the air and fell to the ground. Looking in the direction the shoe pointed, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Let’s try going this way.”

The Sword King shook his head in disbelief. He had never imagined Geom Mugeuk would tell their direction by tossing a shoe.

“Why not use your own shoe?”

“It would get dirty.”

When Geom Mugeuk shook the dirt off the shoe and returned it, the Sword King finally said what he had held back earlier.

“You’re a madman.”

At last hearing those words, Geom Mugeuk smiled happily.

“Why are you smiling when I called you a madman?”

“Most of the people who called me a madman ended up on good terms with me.”

“Whoever said that must have been a madman too.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed even louder and began walking.

After sheathing his sword again, the Sword King followed after him. Was it because they did not have to fight right away? The Sword King’s expression looked at ease.

“I’ll have to correct what I said earlier. We may end up taking the longest to break this formation since it was first created.”

“In any case, we’ll be the first and last to enter this formation.”

“I suppose that’s true.”

It did not seem likely that this formation had been installed anywhere else.

The two of them continued walking. The end of the wasteland was blocked by a tall cliff.

“So this is as far as the world created by the formation extends. The other directions are probably all blocked by cliffs after walking about this far too.”

The Sword King could tell that Geom Mugeuk had walked in order to confirm the extent of the formation.

‘What exactly is he trying to do?’

He clearly seemed to have some kind of plan. Then Geom Mugeuk once again said something unexpected.

“It’s getting dark, so let’s spend the night here.”

As he said that, he picked up dry branches lying nearby and lit a campfire. He looked like someone who had forgotten that they were inside a formation.

If it had been anyone else, the Sword King would have asked what kind of lunacy this was. No, to begin with, there was no need to compare him to others. Anyone else would never have come down underground with him in the first place.

The campfire rose.

Sparks crackled and flew up into the air with the smoke, and the dried rocks and grass were dyed by the flickering firelight.

“Well, one day should be fine!”

The Sword King flopped down beside it. Watching him, Geom Mugeuk also lay down comfortably on the other side of the campfire.

“What do you mean, one day? We can rest for a whole year. Let’s take a break.”

The Sword King’s face, too, took on an expression that said, Yes, let’s do that. It had been truly a long time since he had rested. He had never imagined that rest would be inside a formation, and with the Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult at that.

“Hasn’t your life been one where you’ve run without rest? Rest well.”

“How do you know I’ve run without rest?”

“Because you met me. I’ve also been running without rest. Those who run recognize others who run.”

A faint smile formed on the Sword King’s lips.

The two of them lay there like that and looked up at the sky. As time passed, the color held by the sky changed. When the light of twilight had completely darkened, a new light began to embroider the sky.

“The stars that day… I liked them too.”

Geom Mugeuk turned his head and looked at the Sword King. What had he thought while looking at the stars that day? Whom had he remembered?

“Why did you join hands with them?”

He was clearly different from the other Twelve Zodiac Kings Geom Mugeuk had seen so far.

If it had been before, he would never have told him. But today was different.

“I owed a debt.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze deepened as he looked at him.

“It must be a great debt.”

The Sword King nodded slightly as he lay there.

Then he tossed out a single sentence.

“Nothing changes.”

Contrary to his words, regret flashed through the Sword King’s eyes. If they had not met as enemies? The Young Cult Leader of the Demon Cult was certainly someone who made him think such thoughts.

Then Geom Mugeuk’s voice came from beyond the campfire.

“Don’t decide that in advance. It is your life, Instructor, and my life. We can decide our own lives.”

After a brief pause, Geom Mugeuk added,

“And debts can be repaid.”

The Sword King gave no answer.

As the campfire died down like that, the night only grew deeper.

* * *

There was a new report from Tongcheongak.

The master of Hwangnyong Martial Hall had come to the dueling platform leading underground, and they said an extraordinary expert had appeared there. In particular, at the report that his fists were wrapped in bandages, the Fist Demon’s eyes gleamed.

He had heard that two absolute masters had joined forces in the last battle at Cheonhwaru, so this time as well, another expert must have come out in support.

“Not only that, experts who appear to be their subordinates are gathering at Hwangnyong Martial Hall one after another.”

The bastards had begun to move in earnest.

After finishing the report, Ian conveyed her thoughts to the Fist Demon.

“It seems they’re waiting for the Young Cult Leader to come out. The fact that they’re guarding the entrance without going underground means that whatever is happening down below right now is that important. Or else, they trust the person who went with the Young Cult Leader.”

The Fist Demon, who had been nodding silently, made a decision. His gaze turned to the Ilhwa Sword Venerable. In this kind of situation, the decision a man called the Fist Demon made was precisely this.

“We will wait for the Young Cult Leader.”

It meant they would wipe out the bastards and be the ones to greet Geom Mugeuk when he came up to the surface.

A smile formed on the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s lips.

“I was waiting for you to say that.”

Unlike that smile, her eyes shone as coldly as her sword.

Ian had expected her father to make such a decision.

Because this was surely the reason Geom Mugeuk had called the Demon Venerables. Because he must have believed they would take care of it on their own without being told.

Yes, if one had to choose the word least suited to the title Demon Venerable, it would be safe house. The two of them had only been staying for a while for her and Seojin’s sake.

But she had not expected even this decision from her father.

“You come with us too.”

She had thought he would not take her because it was dangerous.

“A martial artist should be on the battlefield. Just as the Young Cult Leader is underground right now.”

This was the Fist Demon’s view of life.

He also knew well that the more precious a child was, the more hardship one had to make them endure. Holding them close was actually easy. Sending them out was the difficult thing.

“Father! Thank you.”

Ian expressed her gratitude with a bright face.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable opened the door first and looked back at the two of them.

“Let us go. We cannot allow enemies to stand above the Young Cult Leader’s head.”

* * *

The next morning, when Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes, the Sword King was standing alone, looking at the rising sun.

“You may be the only person who could sleep so soundly in a situation like this.”

“I haven’t been able to sleep lately because you’ve kept me so tense, Instructor.”

Geom Mugeuk stretched broadly and stood side by side with the Sword King.

“If you had killed me, you would have broken the formation and left.”

“Don’t we at least have enough loyalty not to stab each other while we sleep?”

“Of course. That’s why I slept soundly.”

Of course, part of the reason he slept was because he trusted the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art he had mastered to completion.

“What a shame. I wanted to fight you on that dueling platform.”

Their gazes, which had been fixed on the rising sun, turned toward each other.

“You intend to kill me.”

“I cannot be the one to die.”

“Now, think whether you’d feel relieved after killing me or feel regret. I’ll confess first. If I kill you, Instructor, I think I’ll regret it for a long, long time. I’ll regret it and remember it for the rest of my life.”

“You’re running a scheme until the very end.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

“Even if we fight when the time comes, let’s at least see the final room together before we do. You know, don’t you? How hard I dug.”

“I know. But this damned formation…”

In that instant, the Sword King flinched and stopped speaking. He had sensed that Geom Mugeuk had some kind of plan. That premonition was correct.

“We don’t have to fight here.”

“How?”

“As you must have sensed, I have some basic knowledge about illusions and formations.”

And the solution to this problem was truly in the most basic of basics.

“In illusions and formations, there must always be a method to break them. That is my first principle.”

The Sword King nodded with an expression that said he knew that much too.

“But the method to break a formation cannot, from the start, be killing the person who entered the formation with you. Because that would violate the premise of the foremost principle: that if you break the method, you can live.”

“!”

Geom Mugeuk had precisely seen through the thing that must not be missed.

“That signpost was a trick.”

“!”

“In a situation where you’ve defeated the enemies, yet the formation has not been broken, if a signpost like that remains, of course you would think you have to kill even your companion. It’s the formation of the final gate, so surely it must be truly cruel, right? It’s a trick aimed at that very mindset.”

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the sun rising in the distance.

“The remaining enemy is hiding somewhere here. If we eliminate that last one, then we will have eliminated everyone.”

The Sword King was truly surprised and impressed. If they had not seen through this trick, the companions who had come together would have killed each other. If the Young Cult Leader’s words were true, then this formation was a truly vicious formation that toyed with people’s hearts.

Ah! So that was why he went all the way to the end of the formation to find out how large this place was!

“Then why didn’t you say this yesterday?”

“I wanted to light a campfire with you once, Instructor. Isn’t this all a memory too?”

The Sword King said nothing. He had been about to say, half in jest and half in earnest, Weren’t you trying to pry my secret out of me? But from Geom Mugeuk’s expression now, he could tell. He had truly only wanted to camp with him for a day.

If he had been trying to pry out his secret, he would have persistently tried to learn it somehow. But yesterday, he and Geom Mugeuk had spent most of their time looking up at the night sky.

This time, the Sword King himself took the shoe at his waist and threw it upward.

Picking up the shoe that had fallen to the ground, he said,

“Let’s search from this side.”

And there really was one.

Moreover, it was not far from their starting point. Astonishingly, the remaining enemy was a single person.

“I’ll cut him down.”

Emotion was carried in the Sword King’s hand.

Since one remained, they had thought a truly strong enemy would have been left behind, but that was not the case. He was on the same level as the martial artists who had come in the first of the three attacks.

That was why this formation was even more infuriating. All they had to do was find and kill such a weakling. But by the time they learned that fact, they would already have killed all the companions who had come with them.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. The person who created this formation was mocking humans, and martial artists. It was a formation in which that malice could be felt vividly.

When the Sword King cut down the opponent, the formation finally began to break. It was the moment they cut down everyone the formation had spoken of. Those who had entered the formation were not included in that “everyone.”

Ssssssssss.

When the formation disappeared, the two of them were standing in a dark passage.

Clank.

Far away, the door at the end of the passage opened.

The Sword King looked at Geom Mugeuk with complicated eyes.

“We passed another gate thanks to you.”

The Sword King began walking toward the door first.

“Still, I cannot give you half this time. This is a place where you either gain everything or gain nothing.”

Following behind him, Geom Mugeuk said,

“It’s a shame, but it can’t be helped.”

The two stopped before the door at the end of the passage.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned toward the top of the door. A single pattern was drawn there.

It was not the pattern he had seen on the walls or in the rooms of the previous passage.

This was a pattern he was seeing for the first time.

And yet it drew Geom Mugeuk’s gaze more intensely than any picture he had seen so far.

With a square at the center, six small circles surrounded it.

Each circle was a different color.

Black and white, blue and red, and yellow and purple.

‘No way?’

The square in the center looked like the secret coffer, and the six circles around it looked like the orbs the secret coffer absorbed.

When Geom Mugeuk stared fixedly at the pattern, the Sword King asked,

“What is it?”

“I take back what I said earlier.”

Slowly opening the door, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Give me everything in this room.”

Chapter 591: If It’s Wrong Because It’s Upside Down
---------------------------------------------------
When Yeon Baegin entered his office, his heart sank.

In the brief time he had been away, someone had entered his office. This silent intruder stood by the window as if he owned the place, looking outside, his broad back blocking the entire office window.

Just from his back, Yeon Baegin knew who it was.

It was Ian’s father, the man who had hounded him about where his daughter had gone.

The fact that he had come in like this despite Yeon Baegin’s subordinate standing guard perfectly fine in front of the door showed just how formidable his skill was. He had not come in through the window, either. A body that large could never have fit through it.

Even when Yeon Baegin had seen him earlier, he had thought the man was no ordinary person, but seeing the huge fists clasped behind his back somehow made it feel hard to breathe.

Even so, Yeon Baegin drew up his internal energy and spoke coldly.

“Who are you?”

As always, a fight was about momentum. If you were pushed back at the start, you would be pushed back until the end.

But he regretted it at once.

The moment the Fist Demon turned and looked at him, every fine hair on his body stood on end. Though the man had not released any particular killing intent, his presence was not something Yeon Baegin could face with courage or resolve. He even looked more terrifying than he had that day.

“I have come to ask a favor of you.”

The Fist Demon was courteous. That made him all the more frightening.

“What favor?”

“Send all the students out at once.”

Yeon Baegin flinched in surprise.

“What do you mean by that?”

“Yellow Dragon Martial Hall is closed for the day.”

Yellow Dragon Martial Hall was far too large a martial hall to ignore the students and fight. If a battle broke out, countless students would surely be caught up in it and die.

Yeon Baegin could tell by instinct. Something terrible was going to happen at Yellow Dragon Martial Hall today.

“What on earth are you planning?”

The Fist Demon looked at him with cold eyes and asked in return.

“And what about you?”

Yeon Baegin was frightened by the Fist Demon’s gaze alone, but he did not retreat meekly. If his own skill was lacking, there was still the man underground.

“Just who are you?”

“Did I not say I am Ian’s father?”

“Reveal your true identity.”

A mere student’s parent would never do something like this.

“I asked who you are!”

The Fist Demon chose the quickest method. He revealed his demonic energy. Even when there had been no particular presence from him, Yeon Baegin had been so frightened that every hair on his body had stood on end; now that the man revealed his demonic energy, Yeon Baegin’s legs began to tremble.

“I have come from the Divine Cult.”

“!”

The moment he heard the words Divine Cult, Yeon Baegin felt his insides churn and dizziness wash over him.

His ironclad belief that, Whoever you are, he will take care of you, changed in that instant into the doubt, Can he truly take care of this? To the martial world, the Demonic Cult was that kind of existence.

He had been at ease because this matter was taking place deep underground, and no one could possibly know. So why, at this final moment, was the Demonic Cult getting involved?

‘It was them!’

The students who had left the underground! They must have spread word to the Demonic Cult. That was how he misunderstood it.

In any case, he had to get through this moment.

“Why is the Divine Cult threatening us?”

“I believe you know that better than I do.”

He had been about to feign ignorance, saying he did not know what the man meant. But when his eyes met the Fist Demon’s, he did not dare. The Fist Demon revealing his demonic energy now felt a hundred times more frightening than on the first day, when he had pressed him about where his daughter was.

As if he would exchange no more words, the Fist Demon turned back toward the window.

“Hurry and close the martial hall. Soon, a new gate will open.”

Yeon Baegin could tell that the gate meant the gates of hell.

* * *

All the masters and instructors of Yellow Dragon Martial Hall came out and began sending the students home.

“Everyone, go back for today.”

The students were all startled. Never once had they been sent away like this.

“Has something happened?”

“That is none of your concern. Leave the martial hall immediately!”

Even the masters could not tell them, though they wanted to. They themselves did not know the reason.

Thus, hundreds of students began leaving the martial hall. Even those who ate and slept inside the hall were sent out, and the cooks and menials were sent away as well.

As if poisonous smoke had truly spread through the martial hall, Yellow Dragon Martial Hall was completely emptied in an instant.

At the end, the masters and instructors stood before the building and reported to Yeon Baegin, who was looking out over the main training ground.

“As you ordered, we have sent everyone out.”

They thought they would now be able to hear the reason.

“You men leave as well.”

“Yes?”

All of them were shocked. They had never imagined he would send even them away. They had thought he would say a powerful enemy was coming, and that they should fight alongside him.

If not that, then what did he intend to do alone?

“What is happening?”

“I will tell you later.”

After sending even the masters and instructors away, Yeon Baegin returned to his office.

The Fist Demon was looking out the window at the sparring grounds. There had been no one there a short while ago, but once the students left, martial artists began gathering there one after another. As if replacing the students, they gathered in that place, and their numbers were not small.

Among them, the Fist Demon’s gaze was directed at a man sitting in the spectator seats.

As the report from Tongcheongak had said, the man had bandages wrapped around both hands. The man was also looking this way, and his presence was far from ordinary.

Moreover, he must have clearly sensed the Fist Demon’s gaze, yet he did not move from his seat.

Then Yeon Baegin’s voice came from behind him.

“I did as you wished.”

The Fist Demon silently tried to leave the office. At that moment, Yeon Baegin mustered his courage and spoke. He had to at least know what the man intended to do.

“Do you truly mean to turn my martial hall into hell?”

“That is why you sent them all out, is it not? The people who should not be in hell.”

After leaving one final remark, the Fist Demon left the room.

“That is why you remained.”

* * *

Geom Mugeuk and the Sword King entered the room.

The moment they stepped inside, they could tell. This was the final room.

No jewel, no miraculous elixir could compare to the mysterious energy emanating from this place.

The first thing that caught their eyes was the pattern on the floor. It was the same as the one that had been on the door earlier: a square and six small circles were drawn on the floor.

Not only the floor but the surrounding walls were covered in various patterns made of squares and circles, their colors black and white, blue and red, and yellow and purple.

Even without that, there was something else that made them certain this place was related to the secret coffer.

The thing placed in the center of the room was, astonishingly, a secret coffer.

Its color and shape were identical. The only difference was that it was a secret coffer made as large as an adult’s height, unlike the one Geom Mugeuk carried around.

The moment he saw the giant secret coffer, the mysterious energy in his body began to react. The energy that had been asleep inside him started to stir and awaken.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Sword King. The Sword King, too, was staring at the secret coffer with trembling eyes. He was overwhelmed with emotion. At last, he had arrived at the place he had wanted to reach.

“Is that it? The thing you were trying to find?”

Then came an unexpected answer.

“I do not know either.”

It seemed he, too, had come here without knowing what was in this place.

“I was only to bring back what was being kept here.”

At first, Geom Mugeuk thought that someone like the Sword King would never have done this without even knowing what he was looking for. But thinking back on what he had said in the formation, he realized it might be possible.

“If you bring that back, your debt will be repaid.”

The Sword King nodded. Who was the person he owed? Hwa Mugi? Or someone else entirely?

The Sword King walked slowly toward the secret coffer. He looked slightly flustered. He had probably never imagined that what awaited him here would be such a large lump of iron.

The Sword King carefully placed a hand on the secret coffer. But nothing happened. It was merely cold iron.

After examining the front, he moved on to inspect the side. Then he walked around to the back, circled it once, and returned to his original position.

Geom Mugeuk silently watched what he was doing. He had said on the way in that he wanted everything in this place, but the Sword King held priority over what was in this space. Geom Mugeuk acknowledged that.

Srrr.

The Sword King’s body rose into the air. He gently landed atop the secret coffer and examined its upper surface. Then he came back down.

“I see no device that can open it.”

He seemed to have judged that this box was not all there was to it.

“It is fine, so try touching it.”

Only after the Sword King said that did Geom Mugeuk step forward. The reason the Sword King felt favorably toward Geom Mugeuk was not merely because of his eloquence. It was because of this very side of him: he knew how to restrain his greed and wait.

Geom Mugeuk placed his hand on the secret coffer. Then the energy inside his body reacted.

‘This secret coffer is not merely imitating the outward appearance.’

The Sword King asked from behind him.

“Could it be ten-thousand-year cold iron?”

It was a question of whether he himself had failed to recognize it properly. But how could the Sword King be mistaken?

“No. It is made of ordinary iron.”

A faint look of dismay crossed the Sword King’s face.

Even if it had been made of ten-thousand-year cold iron, it did not seem like the box itself was the goal. Ten-thousand-year cold iron could not change the fate of the martial world.

Of course, one could think that selling such a large piece of ten-thousand-year cold iron might change the fate of the martial world, but if it had merely been about money, he would not have been sent underground.

“There must be a way to open this.”

At the Sword King’s words, Geom Mugeuk nodded in agreement.

“I think the same. There must be some secret. Let us solve it slowly together.”

The Sword King nodded. Somehow, he felt that if it were Geom Mugeuk, he would be able to unravel any secret, no matter how difficult. Indeed, he was already thinking of things the Sword King had not.

“First, let us flip it over and look at the underside.”

The Sword King flinched in surprise. He had not thought of that.

It was clearly a good idea, but the Sword King hesitated for a moment. He was worried that something might go wrong if they flipped it over, and Geom Mugeuk immediately saw through that feeling.

“If something goes wrong just because it was turned upside down, could anything truly remarkable come out of it?”

At those words, the Sword King laughed. He was right. Had he been alone, he would have agonized over it for several double-hours, yet this man so readily reached an answer.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk was treating that huge secret coffer the same way he treated the one in his robes. Had he only turned it upside down? Even drawing a smiling face on it had not mattered.

“Very well, let us flip it over.”

The two of them turned the secret coffer over. There was no passage beneath it as they might have hoped, and the bottom surface was the same as the other sides.

“I only made you waste your effort for nothing.”

“No. It was a good thought.”

From the very beginning, Geom Mugeuk’s way of thinking differed from his own.

Geom Mugeuk walked toward one wall. A small display cabinet stood there, with books placed inside. There were not many, only about ten volumes.

“Something important might be hidden in a place like this as a feint within a feint.”

The Sword King came over and examined them with him. Even apart from that, he thought there might be a way to open that lump of iron, but everything placed there was a secret martial arts manual.

“There are manuals here that the hall master would like.”

Seeing that there were secret martial arts manuals, Geom Mugeuk realized that the promise made to Yeon Baegin had not been entirely empty. Of course, that did not mean they were sincerely looking out for him.

“I will give you half of the manuals here.”

It meant he would give Yeon Baegin some of what was left over.

And Geom Mugeuk sensed one more important thing. The words I will give you half of the manuals implied that nothing else could be given.

For the moment, Geom Mugeuk took what was offered.

“I do not need half. I will just take one.”

Geom Mugeuk took one manual from among them. It was a demonic art called the Heavenly Dipper Body, which protected the body with astral energy.

“This is a demonic art known to have been lost long ago, but here it was.”

The Heavenly Dipper Body was a body-protection demonic art that raised astral energy to protect the body. What made this martial art excellent was that it could be used in conjunction with other body-protection arts.

That meant it could be used at the same time as the Heavenly Demon Body-Protection Art or the Vajra Asura of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

Of course, normally, focusing one’s internal energy on the strongest body-protection art would be the right choice. But Geom Mugeuk possessed such vast internal energy that he could allocate extreme amounts of it among multiple body-protection arts. For that reason, the Heavenly Dipper Body could be called a martial art that might grant him yet another life.

“Just how much stronger do you intend to become?”

“I really must have anxiety about death. When I see martial arts like this, I cannot help myself.”

After putting away the manual, he carefully examined the patterns on the walls. The Sword King also examined the opposite wall.

They were not inside a formation, so if worst came to worst, he intended to bring food and endure here while studying the place.

How long had they examined it like that?

Geom Mugeuk turned around with his eyes squeezed shut.

“Ah! I stared so hard my eyes are about to fall out. I am taking a break. A break.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Sword King replied bluntly without turning around.

“Even if we find it, I cannot give you this one thing.”

“I know.”

“Then why are you working so hard?”

“First, we find it. Then I persuade you, Instructor.”

The Sword King wanted to know where this greed came from.

“Why are you suddenly being greedy?”

“It is not sudden. I have always been a greedy person. If I had no greed, I would not have come all the way down into this underground place.”

Geom Mugeuk sat down, leaning against the secret coffer.

This time, Geom Mugeuk asked the Sword King, who was examining the patterns on the wall.

“You said there was something here that could change the fate of the martial world.”

Geom Mugeuk added calmly and asked,

“Then why did you bring me down here?”

The Sword King gave no answer. He merely looked at the wall with deepened eyes.

“Huh? Huhh?”

Thinking he was about to play some prank again, the Sword King did not turn around.

“Instructor!”

Only at that urgent cry did the Sword King turn toward Geom Mugeuk.

In that instant, the Sword King’s eyes flew wide open.

Geom Mugeuk was being sucked into the secret coffer. He had already been almost completely drawn in.

‘Secret coffer, your father is eating us.’

Perhaps because it was nothing else but being absorbed by the secret coffer, even in this urgent moment, Geom Mugeuk felt no fear at all. No, instead, he felt comfortable. That was why he did not forcibly resist the power pulling him in.

Swoooosh.

The Sword King shot forward and tried to grab Geom Mugeuk. But by the time he arrived, Geom Mugeuk had already completely disappeared into the secret coffer.

The Sword King struck the spot where Geom Mugeuk had been sucked in. But as if it had never absorbed a person, the wall was nothing but hard, cold iron.

Chapter 592: Bi Gui, What Do You Think?
---------------------------------------
The man with bandages wrapped around both hands sat in the spectator stands, looking down at the empty martial arena.

The one who cautiously entered was Yeon Baegin.

“I am Yeon Baegin, master of the Hwangryong Martial Hall.”

First, he introduced himself to the man. Of course, the other party surely knew who he was. After all, he was sitting there in the place Yeon Baegin cherished and loved most.

Even so, the reason he greeted him was to show proper courtesy to this terrifying man.

Earlier, he had been so frightened upon seeing him that his memory had briefly cut off, and he had fled. That was how deeply the murderous aura this man exuded stirred primal fear.

“I have joined hands with the person underground, so I suppose that means I am on the same side as you as well.”

Normally, he should have run away. Even if he did not run, he should have hidden in his office and come out only after all this fighting was over.

But the final words the Fist Demon had spoken in his office had driven him to come here.

Did I not send out everyone who did not belong in hell? If you remained, then you remained because you belong in hell.

Yeon Baegin interpreted those words to mean that he intended to kill him. Now that the Demonic Cult had intervened, he knew this was not something that could be resolved by begging. In any case, they would take everything from him and kill him.

He judged that trusting the man underground would give him a greater chance of saving his life. And if this terrifying man before him was on the same side, perhaps he would get rid of those Demonic Cult bastards.

‘I will never give up.’

The reason the Demonic Cult bastards looked down on him, and the reason this man gazed at him with such contempt, was all because he was weak. It was because they did not acknowledge the master of a martial hall as a true martial artist.

‘If only I obtain the secret manual!’

He would work hard enough to die and become strong without fail. Then, in this very moment, he would meet those eyes looking down on him with contempt and say this:

—Lower your eyes.

Because he harbored this burning desire, he was able to stand there so confidently.

“The reason I came is to convey important information. Our opponents are people from the Demonic Cult.”

Even at the words Demonic Cult, the man showed no agitation whatsoever. Seeing that he was not surprised in the least, Yeon Baegin could tell that he already knew.

‘These bastards didn’t even tell me.’

It was uncomfortable to keep looking at the man, who still said nothing, so Yeon Baegin turned his gaze aside.

Here and there around them stood people who appeared to be the man’s subordinates. Their number reached several dozen. Instead of weapons, they wore fist gauntlets on both hands.

‘They are all fist art users.’

If there was a saying that subordinates resembled their leader, this was precisely the case it was meant for. Every one of the martial artists revealed an aura as cold as ice, without even a trace of mercy. Yeon Baegin could feel it. They were not ordinary subordinates, but killing weapons raised without any emotion at all.

‘Who on earth are you, that you do not even fear the Demonic Cult?’

Neither Yeon Baegin, nor the Fist Demon, nor the One-Flower Sword Venerable, nor anyone else knew, but only Geom Mugeuk knew his true identity.

One of the Twelve Kings, the Fist King, Gang Hu.

He was the Fist King, the last and twelfth of the Twelve Earthly Kings.

When they ruled the martial world, a saying had been popular.

The Twelve Earthly Kings begin with the sword and end with the fist.

Just because he was the last, the twelfth, did not mean his skill was last. Aside from the First King, the Sword King, their order had been decided regardless of skill.

In his life before regression, countless people had died at Gang Hu’s hands even after he ascended to the seat of Fist King. The martial artists of that era had lived in a time when no one could stop those fists imbued with innate killing intent.

Just then, Gang Hu’s gaze turned toward one place. It was the first moment emotion entered that indifferent expression.

When Yeon Baegin turned around, a young man was walking in. Around his hands were wound crimson bandages, as if they had been dipped in blood and pulled out. It was a stark contrast to Gang Hu’s white bandages.

“I have returned.”

When the man respectfully cupped his fist in greeting, Gang Hu nodded.

The aura of the newly arrived young man was no ordinary thing either, and he introduced the two people who had come with him.

“These are the ones dispatched to assist with this matter.”

Both looked young, but one of them was dressed in an unusual manner.

He wore red martial robes, and the shape of a ghost had been drawn on them in white lines. The moment Yeon Baegin saw that attire, he knew who the other party was.

‘Ghost Blade!’

He was an absolute master of the unorthodox faction, called Ghost Blade because he wielded his saber like a ghost.

‘Ghost Blade was that young? From what era is Ghost Blade?’

Could he have rejuvenated and returned to youth? Or had some other young expert imitated his clothing? Yeon Baegin could not know.

The other man had long, narrow eyes, and his hands and feet were unusually long. He did not wear his sword at his waist or on his back, but held it against his chest.

The two men were the sort whose appearances would not be easily forgotten after a single glance.

In Yeon Baegin’s eyes, Gang Hu seemed like someone who would not so much as greet anyone, and yet he accepted the two men’s greetings.

As the two respectfully cupped their fists, he too cupped his fists from where he sat. Seeing that, Yeon Baegin felt an even greater sense of humiliation. While he had been speaking so much, the man had only sent him a look of contempt.

After finishing their greetings, the three men passed by Yeon Baegin and walked toward the spectator seats beside where Gang Hu sat. As they went, they did not spare Yeon Baegin a single glance.

Yeon Baegin realized that one thing the terrifying demon had said was accurate. Cold and merciless. The people gathered here were well suited to hell.

And before he knew it, he himself had become someone who mingled with them.

It was just then.

Everyone’s gaze began to turn toward one place.

There were people walking toward them with an overwhelming presence.

The person in the lead was the Fist Demon, and to either side of him followed the One-Flower Sword Venerable and Ian. With these three walking over, how could all eyes not be drawn to them?

Even though there were dozens on this side, they strode forward without hesitation.

An immense murderous aura poured out from Gang Hu’s subordinates. It was the first time Ian had faced such powerful killing intent in actual combat.

Yeon Baegin, who stood closest, met the Fist Demon’s eyes. Yeon Baegin was afraid, but he did not want to be overwhelmed in spirit. So he did not avoid the Fist Demon’s gaze and stared back at him.

“Did you not say so yourself? That I belong in this hell too. Did you think I would run away in fear just because you are the Demonic Cult?”

The Fist Demon said nothing to him and looked at Gang Hu. Thinking he had been ignored yet again, Yeon Baegin flared up, but he did not dare argue while looking at that terrifying face.

At last, for the first time since Yeon Baegin had come here, Gang Hu opened his mouth. This was a man who had not even spoken to those dispatched to support him.

“Fist Demon, I have always wanted to see you at least once.”

The Fist Demon merely nodded once and did not ask who the other party was. He did not seem curious either.

Instead, the one who reacted to those words was Yeon Baegin. He was startled out of his wits.

‘What? Fist Demon? Did he say Fist Demon? That man was a Demon Venerable?’

He had thought he was merely an expert of the Demonic Cult. He had never dreamed that one of the Eight Demon Venerables of the Demonic Cult would have come here. And he had just spoken so boldly to such a person?

There was even more than one Demon Venerable.

“Even the One-Flower Sword Venerable. As expected, the Young Cult Leader is the Young Cult Leader.”

Yeon Baegin had no time to be shocked by the words One-Flower Sword Venerable. At the words Young Cult Leader, his mind went blank.

‘Young Cult Leader? What Young Cult Leader? Surely not the Young Cult Leader of the Demonic Cult?’

As Yeon Baegin nearly fainted from shock, the gazes of the Fist Demon and Gang Hu intertwined in midair. Yeon Baegin could tell that he truly was a Demon Venerable. The murderous aura of the fearsome Gang Hu no longer felt quite so terrifying when it met the Fist Demon. Killing intent and demonic energy stood taut against each other.

This time, the Fist Demon spoke for the first time.

“The martial hall is closed today.”

The Fist Demon slowly swept his gaze over all of them and spoke coldly. It was the sort of thing Yeon Baegin would have wanted to say even in his dreams.

“So from this moment on, everyone is to exit through the door we open.”

* * *

“We’re here, everyone get up!”

At the sound of someone waking him, Geom Mugeuk started and opened his eyes. The instant he did, a truly unexpected situation unfolded before him. It was the first time he had opened his eyes after being absorbed into the secret coffer.

Rattle, rattle.

The place he was in was a moving carriage.

A foul smell hit him all at once. A dozen or so men were packed tightly together in the cargo compartment of a carriage without even a window.

Seeing that they all wore swords, it did not seem they were being dragged somewhere.

‘Did some formation activate inside the secret coffer?’

A dangerous formation that threatened him?

But when he had been sucked into the secret coffer, he had felt extremely calm and pleasant. It had not seemed as if the secret coffer was trying to kill him.

Geom Mugeuk first examined the condition of his body.

He checked first whether his arms and legs were properly attached. Arms normal, legs normal. His eyes saw well, and his ears heard well.

His body was in good condition. The problem was his internal energy.

He had only about as much internal energy as someone who had trained for a few years. It was not enough to use the Nine Flames Demonic Art or the techniques of the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

‘Thank goodness, thank goodness.’

It was fortunate that he had even a few years’ worth. Even a handful of internal energy could allow one to avoid death in a crisis. That was the level of his skill.

Next, he examined his sword. It was not the Black Demon Sword, but an ordinary iron sword. Of course, once he escaped this place, it would turn back into the Black Demon Sword. Only one thing remained unchanged. The secret coffer was still in his breast pocket.

‘Secret coffer, what sort of test is your father trying to put me through?’

Geom Mugeuk thought this situation was some kind of test the secret coffer was giving him.

If this was the secret coffer’s test?

There would definitely be a way to clear it, and there would definitely be a reward as well. Yes, he should think positively.

Shrrng.

Geom Mugeuk drew his sword slightly and reflected his face in it.

‘It’s my face.’

For now, he had not entered someone else’s body.

Seeing that, the man sitting in front of him asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What did you say your name was?”

He was the owner of the voice that had woken him a moment ago.

After wondering what he should answer, Geom Mugeuk decided that, in this place, he was a body that would vanish like smoke anyway.

“Geom Yeon.”

“Your first time?”

He had no idea what exactly was supposed to be his first time, but.

“It is.”

“I knew it. I told them so many times not to let rookies volunteer.”

Hearing the word volunteer, he roughly understood the situation.

“I wasn’t told that.”

“If you don’t keep your head on straight, you’ll die. Rookies like you are the first to get killed.”

“I’ll keep my wits about me.”

He had truly never dreamed the secret coffer would absorb him, and now, to make matters worse, he was in this situation?

Suddenly, he thought of the Sword King outside.

‘If he saw me get sucked in here, he won’t just stand by, will he?’

What worried him was that the Sword King might destroy the secret coffer in an attempt to rescue him, but that probably would not happen.

Not only was he not that reckless by nature, but since he had seen Geom Mugeuk being absorbed, he would be certain that this lump of metal had truly been the intended object.

He was probably trying to find a way inside somehow.

But Geom Mugeuk had a feeling he would not be able to enter. He suspected that one needed to have the secret coffer he possessed, or else have the energy absorbed by the secret coffer inside one’s body, in order to enter.

He had clearly been invited by the secret coffer.

At last, the carriage came to a stop.

The curtain was drawn back, and dazzling sunlight poured in.

“Now, everyone get down!”

Geom Mugeuk got out of the carriage. It was a vast plain below a mountain, with dozens of tents spread out and hundreds of martial artists coming and going.

People shouting, people carrying the wounded, people running somewhere. From the sight, he could tell immediately.

‘It’s a battlefield!’

Those who had come in the carriage with him had all volunteered for the war.

“Now, you lot are this way.”

The martial artist who took charge of them divided the people who had arrived by carriage among several tents.

“New recruits.”

Geom Mugeuk also entered one of those tents.

Inside were three or four martial artists. Some were sleeping, some were eating something, and one sat on a cot, maintaining his weapon. All of them had weary, exhausted faces.

One of the men frowned when he saw Geom Mugeuk.

“Damn it! They sent another rookie.”

He still did not know why he had ended up in such a situation, but as always, Geom Mugeuk did his best in the circumstances he faced.

“I am Geom Yeon. Fortunately, I have very good luck. I’m sure I’ll be of help.”

At that, the man who had scowled and called him a rookie rose from his cot, picked up a leather pouch from the corner, and thrust it at Geom Mugeuk.

“I am Jeong Dae, your superior and the leader of White Tiger Seventeenth Squad. Stop talking nonsense and just listen to me from now on! Understood?”

“Yes!”

“Your spot is over there.”

He was assigned an old, shabby cot roughly carved from wood.

Geom Mugeuk considered trying to learn the situation from Jeong Dae, but for now, he decided to inspect the outside first and left the tent.

‘By the way, where is this?’

He had traveled all over the Central Plains, to every sort of place, but this place was unfamiliar. If this was a world created by the secret coffer anyway, it could all be an illusory world.

But for that to be the case, far too many people were speaking and moving like real human beings.

‘Secret coffer, what do you think of this situation? Is this a test where I have to survive in this war?’

No, it could not be such an easy test. With his martial arts, even without internal energy, how could he fail to survive?

It was just then.

“It’s the Martial Alliance Leader!”

“The Alliance Leader has arrived!”

Geom Mugeuk was startled by the cheers around him. The martial artists began running in a rush.

‘Alliance Leader Jin came here?’

Geom Mugeuk ran toward the place where the martial artists were gathering.

A middle-aged man in long robes embroidered with a dragon and a tiger was walking toward them.

An absolutely overwhelming presence!

The moment he saw him, Geom Mugeuk was shocked. He was not the Martial Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon that Geom Mugeuk knew.

The surrounding martial artists all cheered toward him and shouted in unison.

“Martial Emperor Divine Sword! Martial Emperor Divine Sword!”

At the words Martial Emperor Divine Sword, Geom Mugeuk was startled. The Martial Emperor Divine Sword was someone he knew as well. No, to be precise, someone he had heard of.

After all, he had been one of the most famous alliance leaders in the history of the Martial Alliance.

Geom Mugeuk understood. The place where he had awakened was not his own era.

This was the martial world of three hundred years ago.

Chapter 593: The Newcomer Sure Has Guts
---------------------------------------
With the appearance of the Muhwang Divine Sword, Geom Mugeuk could tell what war this was.

‘So this war is the battle between the Murim Alliance and Cheonui Palace.’

The reason the Muhwang Divine Sword was still spoken of even after three hundred years was that, aside from his absolute martial prowess that earned him the name Divine Sword, he had one great achievement.

The destruction of Cheonui Palace.

At the time, a mysterious force called Cheonui Palace was attempting to topple the Murim Alliance and seize control of the orthodox murim.

Cheonui Palace was a sect that used all manner of bizarre illusion arts and formations, and it revealed truly formidable power. The masters of the murim were helplessly trapped in their formations and died miserable deaths. Because of that, the hatred and resentment of the orthodox martial artists, who desired fair and honorable duels, grew all the stronger; the more they feared them, the more they hated them.

The Blood Cult is behind them, the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult is behind them, the Sado Alliance is behind them—amid all sorts of rampant rumors, a full-scale war broke out between the Murim Alliance and Cheonui Palace.

When the war first began, everyone expected the battle to end quickly. No matter how powerful Cheonui Palace was, their opponent was the Murim Alliance. And not just any Murim Alliance, but one led by the absolute master of the murim, the Muhwang Divine Sword.

Contrary to expectations, however, the war did not end easily.

The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Sado Alliance did not step forward, but countless sects excluding them intervened in this fight. Some came forward for the sake of a righteous cause, others in pursuit of profit. Even merchants trying to sell weapons became involved. It was truly a great chaos of tangled interests.

After all, the murim did not consist only of those who supported the Murim Alliance.

Numerous sects that supported Cheonui Palace and disliked the Murim Alliance joined the war as well. Rumors even spread that the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Sado Alliance were secretly supporting them.

‘Why on earth?’

For some reason, the secret coffer had brought him into the middle of the battle between the Murim Alliance and Cheonui Palace from three hundred years ago.

‘What is it trying to show me?’

Meanwhile, the Muhwang Divine Sword stepped before the martial artists.

The cheers grew even louder.

The martial artists were all overwhelmed by the fact that they were seeing him in person. Most of the martial artists here were lower-ranked warriors, so they had never had the chance to see the Alliance Leader this closely with their own eyes.

The Muhwang Divine Sword looked calmly over the martial artists. With his gaze alone, he overwhelmed this vast crowd.

Moreover, he was younger than they had expected; he looked as though he had only just entered middle age, and the martial artists grew even more fervent.

Those whose eyes met his lowered their heads in salute, their faces filled with emotion.

At last, the Muhwang Divine Sword opened his mouth.

“Since the fighting began, there have been many sacrifices. We have lost countless comrades.”

His low voice, imbued with inner power, could be heard clearly even by those standing far at the back.

“Countless masters who sought to uphold justice have been sacrificed, trapped in their wicked illusion arts and formations.”

With each word he spoke, the martial artists emitted a surge of heated excitement. In this war, the Muhwang Divine Sword was their hero and their pillar of support.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk watched him with a cool mind. There had to be some reason he had been brought here on the very day the man arrived.

Power entered the Muhwang Divine Sword’s voice.

“Even so, we must continue to fight. Because we cannot let our friends’ deaths be in vain, because we cannot allow those treacherous villains to swallow this murim whole, we must move forward.”

After looking over everyone, the Muhwang Divine Sword spoke with eyes full of resolve.

“We will fight the final battle here.”

Though he still did not know why the secret coffer had brought him here, he could at least understand why it had chosen this time and this place.

This was the very site of the final decisive battle against Cheonui Palace.

The Muhwang Divine Sword’s resounding cry echoed across the field.

“Do not fear death! Your blood will protect this murim!”

Even though his words demanded sacrifice, the martial artists went wild with enthusiasm.

After finishing his speech, the Muhwang Divine Sword approached the martial artists in the front row. It was a situation where an assassin might be hiding among them, but he showed no fear.

Geom Mugeuk quietly observed him.

‘Masters of the past really do give off a different feeling.’

The presence he felt from him was different. The way he emitted his aura was different as well.

Rough, but even more powerful.

To several people standing in the front row, the Muhwang Divine Sword offered words of encouragement on behalf of all.

“The murim will remember your hard work and sacrifice.”

Every time their eyes met the Alliance Leader’s, the martial artists lowered their heads with moved expressions.

At last, it was Geom Mugeuk’s turn.

“You have endured much.”

Geom Mugeuk did not simply let it pass.

“Not me, sir.”

Everyone’s gazes focused on Geom Mugeuk, wondering what he dared to say to the Alliance Leader. The Muhwang Divine Sword also looked at him with an expression asking what he meant.

“Today is my first day.”

Only then did the Muhwang Divine Sword smile, and the martial artists around them laughed. The solemn atmosphere loosened in an instant.

“What is your name?”

“I am Geom Yeon.”

“I will do my utmost so that you can return safely to your hometown.”

As he was about to move aside, Geom Mugeuk added one more thing.

“Is there a way for us to win?”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk saw it. The man’s pupils trembled ever so slightly. That emotion was unmistakably discomfort.

Geom Mugeuk had asked deliberately. This battle would surely end in victory for the orthodox faction and the Muhwang Divine Sword. The result was already decided.

But he remembered hearing something once. That there were no detailed records left about the final battle that day.

‘How did you end that final battle?’

Among the several martial artists who followed while assisting the Alliance Leader, one middle-aged martial artist stepped forward with a hardened expression.

“How dare you speak out so recklessly before the Alliance Leader?”

At that, the Muhwang Divine Sword raised a hand to stop him, then spoke to Geom Mugeuk.

“I have a way to end this battle.”

It was not an answer to Geom Mugeuk, but words meant for all the martial artists. Sure enough, the martial artists were startled, then soon let out thunderous cheers.

“However, I cannot reveal such important details in an open place. So you must fulfill your own duty.”

“Yes! Understood.”

After encouraging a few more people, the Muhwang Divine Sword headed straight toward the tent at the center. The middle-aged martial artist who had stepped forward with a stern expression earlier glared at Geom Mugeuk with displeasure before following the Alliance Leader.

Then a low remark came from behind him.

“The rookie has guts.”

When he turned around, he saw the squad leader, Jeong Dae, standing there.

“Who was that man who glared at me so fiercely before leaving?”

“He is the person in charge here.”

He was Jo Ung, the man in charge of this battlefield that would become the final stage.

“What on earth were you thinking, stepping forward like that?”

“When else would I get to have a conversation with the Alliance Leader?”

Jeong Dae stared at Geom Mugeuk, then turned away after leaving behind a single remark: “Madman.” Even if he was called a madman, Geom Mugeuk’s instinctive choice had been that he could not let the Alliance Leader simply pass him by.

He judged that making an impression in whatever way he could might help him later.

Geom Mugeuk stared at the back of the Muhwang Divine Sword as he grew farther away.

‘Great secret coffer, why did you bring me here? What on earth for?’

* * *

After returning to the tent, Geom Mugeuk quietly closed his eyes and circulated his true qi through one heavenly circuit. Compared to before, his inner power was truly meager, but he tried to increase it even a little and make it even a little purer. He did not know what situation awaited him, but escaping this place would not be easy.

“Everyone, attention. A reconnaissance mission has come down.”

The squad leader, Jeong Dae, woke even those who had been sleeping.

“A night mission?”

At the startled question from one of the squad members, Jeong Dae nodded.

Geom Mugeuk left the tent together with the squad members.

Including Geom Mugeuk, White Tiger Seventeenth Squad had five members. Originally, there had been thirteen, but he was told they had all died in two major battles.

“This is all your fault.”

Jeong Dae pinned responsibility for the night reconnaissance on Geom Mugeuk.

“What do you mean?”

“I’m saying this night mission came down because you said unnecessary things to the Alliance Leader. After our numbers dropped to four, our squad had never been assigned a night mission.”

Even so, he did not curse or blame him for it. From what Geom Mugeuk could see, Jeong Dae was a kind and dignified martial artist befitting the words “orthodox faction.” Though their connection would soon end, he was the sort of person Geom Mugeuk wanted to treat well, at least while they were together.

“I came, didn’t I?”

“Four or five, what difference does it make!”

Yes, he was right.

There was no way this had happened by chance. Jo Ung, who had wanted to look good before the Alliance Leader, must have been punishing him.

Geom Mugeuk hoped that even this matter was not coincidence, but inevitability. He had to find something within this chain of inevitabilities. He had to get out of this place as soon as possible.

“To give us such a disadvantage just because I asked that question? Alliance Leader, this is too much!”

“As if the Alliance Leader would have done that.”

Jeong Dae ultimately held back from saying that Jo Ung was probably punishing them for what had happened earlier. He knew better than anyone that if one hesitated over whether to say something, it was wiser not to say it. All the more so on this battlefield where dozens died every day. If one hesitated over whether to kill, one had to kill.

And so, White Tiger Seventeenth Squad, to which Geom Mugeuk belonged, entered the mountains.

The two sides were facing each other with a single ridge between them, and he was told that only localized skirmishes were currently taking place.

Like someone who had survived here for a long time, Jeong Dae skillfully made his way along the dark mountain path.

“If you cross that ridge over there, you’ll be in the enemy camp. You absolutely must not go any farther than that. Understood? There are ambushes everywhere.”

“Understood.”

Of course, contrary to his obedient answer, Geom Mugeuk wanted to examine the enemy camp first. In truth, he was more curious about that side than this one.

What kind of person the palace lord of Cheonui Palace was, what he was thinking, and who was around him.

It would have been better if he had been allowed to start in that camp instead. Why had it been this side?

Surely the reason the secret coffer had brought him here was not merely to let him meet the Muhwang Divine Sword.

“All right, we return for today.”

They were just about to finish their reconnaissance mission and go back.

“Careful!”

Swish!

Geom Mugeuk threw himself forward and shoved Jeong Dae aside.

A hidden weapon passed through the spot where Jeong Dae had been standing.

From the forest ahead, martial artists charged in while swinging their swords.

There were four opponents in all.

They launched a coordinated attack at Geom Mugeuk, who was closest, and thrust their swords. They were well-trained men with swift movements, but unfortunately for them, their opponent was Geom Mugeuk.

Swish.

Geom Mugeuk dodged the attack from the front as though lying down on the ground.

It was a posture from which he should have fallen, but Geom Mugeuk’s waist was not that weak.

Springing up like a coil, Geom Mugeuk struck upward with his sword.

Slash!

As the man collapsed with his chest deeply cut open, Geom Mugeuk stepped on his stomach and rushed toward the enemy on the left. His opponent was flustered by the unexpected movement.

Two swords brushed through the air, but only one reached its intended target.

Thud!

After stabbing his opponent through the heart, Geom Mugeuk spun quickly and swung his sword.

Swish.

The enemy charging from behind widened his eyes.

Even if he had eyes on the back of his head, he could not have imagined that Geom Mugeuk would strike into his opening so quickly and accurately.

Slash.

When the third enemy fell, the remaining one fled into the darkness. Geom Mugeuk was about to throw his sword into the man’s back and catch him, but for some reason he stopped.

It had truly happened in an instant. It happened in the brief moment when the other squad members hesitated, unsure how to rush in, and by the time Jeong Dae, who had been shoved to the ground by Geom Mugeuk, sprang to his feet, the situation was already over.

Jeong Dae asked with a startled, bewildered expression.

“You! What are you? You’re good at martial arts, aren’t you?”

“I never said I wasn’t.”

“Then why did you come in as a rookie?”

“That way, I can live longer. If I fight well, wouldn’t I have to face masters?”

Jeong Dae wore an incredulous expression but did not question him any further. He conveyed his gratitude with sincere eyes.

“Thank you. I lived thanks to you.”

“Then will you move me to a better bed?”

“I’ll switch mine with yours.”

He was truly moved. How had he managed to survive until now? On top of that, the war was nearing its end. If he had died here today to a dagger thrown by someone he did not even know, he would have been so wronged he would not have been able to close his eyes.

Geom Mugeuk examined the fallen corpses.

“Are these men a reconnaissance squad too?”

“No. Reconnaissance squads have an unspoken rule that even if they encounter each other, they avoid one another.”

It was a mutual survival strategy born in the midst of war.

“Then why were these men out here at night?”

Jeong Dae shook his head with an expression that said he did not know.

“But they didn’t use illusion arts or sorcery.”

“Only the masters of Cheonui Palace use formations and illusion arts. It’s not as if all our enemies are from Cheonui Palace, and even in Cheonui Palace, the lower-ranked martial artists use ordinary martial arts. Now, let’s hurry back.”

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk said something unexpected.

“You go on ahead. I’ll catch the one who escaped earlier and bring him back.”

“It’s dangerous!”

“The Alliance Leader is here and everything. We can’t just overlook such strange movements.”

“You said you wanted to live a long time, didn’t you? That’s why you hid your martial arts too, didn’t you?”

“Who knows? If I make a great contribution, maybe they’ll send me away early. Now, go on ahead.”

Geom Mugeuk dashed into the darkness.

Because Jeong Dae had learned through this fight that Geom Mugeuk’s skills were far from ordinary, he no longer tried to stop him.

“Make sure you come back alive!”

That was why Geom Mugeuk had not killed the last man earlier. He intended to use chasing him as a pretext to examine the enemy camp.

Following the path Jeong Dae had told him about, he arrived at their encampment.

Dozens of tents had been set up around a large tent in the center.

He did not enter recklessly. If the palace lord on that side was here, then formidable masters would be with him.

Geom Mugeuk activated the Divine Eye Art. Though his inner power had diminished, his immunity to all poisons remained, and he could also use the Divine Eye Art.

As Geom Mugeuk examined their tents, his gaze stopped on one place.

“!”

In that moment, Geom Mugeuk was shocked.

Only now did he understand why the secret coffer had brought him specifically to this place three hundred years ago.

At the entrance of the large tent Geom Mugeuk was looking at, a certain symbol was drawn.

It was a symbol Geom Mugeuk knew, but it was not the symbol of the secret coffer.

It was the very symbol that had been carved throughout the passages of the underground gate. The symbol that had become the key to breaking through the first gate, the one he had wondered might belong to the family that created this gate.

Now, the first clue was being unraveled.

‘So the ones who built the underground treasury beneath Hwangryong Martial Hall were the destroyed Cheonui Palace.’

Chapter 594: You've Never Even Raised a Child
---------------------------------------------
All of you, leave through the gate we open.

The Fist King and his subordinates understood. The gate the Fist Demon spoke of was the gate of hell.

The Fist King, Ganghu, did not grow angry. Instead, an inscrutable smile formed at the corners of his mouth.

“If it is a gate you open for us, I would gladly go.”

The moment those words ended, the Fist King’s subordinates, who had been standing on all sides, launched themselves forward and gathered together.

Their momentum, honed as killing weapons, was killing intent itself, and they numbered roughly more than thirty.

The Fist Demon silently stared at that sinister momentum, then looked at Ian. His eyes seemed to say:

Do you want to try?

Ian did not answer recklessly that she would.

Her gaze turned toward the enemies. Then she asked herself.

‘Are you prepared to leap into that killing intent right now?’

At the important moments in life, or in moments of crisis.

One must not entrust decisions to impulsive emotion. There were others who would answer that question.

The first to respond was the Flying Heaven Sword Art, which she had mastered.

—Have you forgotten what martial art you learned?

The next to respond was the Sun-Moon Sword in her hand.

Wuuung.

The Sun-Moon Sword hummed softly in her grasp. Through steady communion since receiving it, it had now acknowledged her as its true master.

The last to answer was her effort.

—Even if everything in the world betrays you, effort never will.

The days when she had cut down on sleep to train flashed past.

At last, Ian answered her father’s question.

“I will try.”

It was not an emotional, impulsive answer, but the answer of the life she had lived.

At Ian’s cautious reply, the Fist Demon silently nodded.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable imagined how this fight would unfold.

Could Ian truly win?

The answer did not come immediately. If she saw this fight as a razor-close match, the Fist Demon must see it the same way.

That was why she could tell. At this moment, the one trembling and afraid more was not Ian, but the Fist Demon. Within that blunt, fearsome face, how great a storm of emotion must be raging?

‘So win, Ian. If only for your father’s sake.’

And savor the moment when a martial artist becomes truly strong. Not by defeating a hundred people weaker than oneself, but in the very moment of defeating an opponent one cannot defeat!

Ian walked forward, her back straight.

The taut tension swelled as if it would explode. It was a fight against a beauty who could well be called the most beautiful woman under heaven, but they had no leisure to enjoy such an unusual experience.

Because the aura Ian exuded was anything but ordinary, they drew their momentum up even more fiercely.

Chaang.

Ian drew her sword with a clear ring. This was her first time facing fist artists, and many of them at once.

The moment the straight Sun-Moon Sword flashed in the sunlight—

She charged toward the enemies. When fighting many opponents, it was only natural tactics to avoid being surrounded. But Ian struck at that very blind spot and threw herself into their center.

Shiiiiik.

The instant she landed, blood sprayed from the tip of her sword as it spun in a wide arc. Everyone moved so quickly that it was impossible to tell whose blood it was.

Faster than the fists flying in from every direction, Ian thrust her sword at the enemy before her and rushed in.

Shiiik!

Just as the opponent twisted his body and threw a punch, her body rotated in the opposite direction. In that instant, her target changed.

Puuk!

Ian stabbed the enemy charging in from behind and drove forward without stopping. Even as he died, he grabbed the sword with his gauntleted hand.

Chwaaaak!

Ian’s sword split his torso sideways and cut the throat of the enemy rushing in from the side.

Seogeok!

The sharpness of the Sun-Moon Sword cut not only through human bone but even through the gauntlet he wore.

As Ian stirred up the very heart of the enemy ranks, both advantages and disadvantages arose at once.

The disadvantage was that she had to deal with enemies charging from every side, and the advantage was that her opponents could not recklessly release their astral force.

What if more than thirty people all unleashed their astral force at once to attack her?

Not only would it be difficult to evade, but the moment one strike landed, it would give all of their astral forces the chance to pour down upon her.

That was also why she had not poured out the forms of the Flying Heaven Sword Art from the very first move. She had judged that if this became a battle of astral force and internal energy, she would be at a disadvantage.

But if she was in the midst of the enemies?

Swaaaek!

Sword qi surged from her blade. The enemies could not easily fire off their astral force for fear of hitting their own allies, but she was different.

Without looking at the results of the sweeping sword qi, she slid toward the opposite side. She did not check how many were wounded or killed by her sword qi.

As if she were moving according to a predetermined sequence, there was no hesitation in any of her actions.

Papapapapak!

Through the spraying blood, Ian’s sword-light flashed with terrifying brilliance.

An arm that had aimed for her head was severed, and the fist of another who flew in to avenge it cut only through empty air. Ian was already surging toward yet another enemy.

Paaang! Paang!

Fists flew in from all directions as if lumps of iron had been fired.
