Her form wove through the fists. She fought with the resolve not to be hit even once. The moment she took a proper blow, it could all simply end.

One of the enemies rushed in, trying to burrow into her body and knock her down.

Puk, puk, puk!

Stabbing his chest in succession, she twisted her body as though throwing him backward.

The attack that had been flying at her from behind struck the corpse.

This time, they rushed in from both sides. Their thinking was that if they could somehow grab her and bring her down, it would be over. It was an absurd tactic against an expert like Ian, but they too were masters capable of unleashing fist astral force.

As if playing tag, she employed her footwork and slipped through between them.

Each time her sword embroidered the air, flesh was cut and blood sprayed.

Ian was truly fast and strong. Because she was fighting in a state of selfless immersion, she did not realize just how well she was fighting right now.

Rather, what tormented her most was not the fists, but the blood. Blood splattering in every direction. Her greatest fear was that the blood splashed onto her face might get into her eyes.

It was a worry she had never once had before experiencing this kind of fight.

As the number of enemies decreased, her battle began to grow more difficult. The enemies, who had been pressing her with numbers, now formed a proper formation and fought.

Kwang! Kwaaang!

And now the enemies too began to hurl fist astral force. The astral force released from their fists poured toward her. They no longer cared even if their own allies were behind her.

Kwang! Kwaang!

The astral forces they fired collided with one another. They intended to crush Ian between astral force and astral force.

Peoeok!

After allowing a single blow, her form slid backward. If her protective astral qi had been even a little late, it would have had enough power to shatter her five viscera and six bowels. Those who had survived were the stronger ones. They had survived because they were strong.

Her internal energy was also gradually running out. With this much internal energy, she had been confident she could face anyone. But it was not enough to kill all thirty-some subordinates of the Fist King, who were even wearing gauntlets.

Puaaaak!

Ian fought frantically, drenched in blood. Dodging the incoming fists, she rolled across the ground.

Like a tenacious fighter who would rise clutching even a stone if she fell, she rolled along the floor and cut an ankle.

Paaaaa!

She drove her sword into the chest of the fallen enemy, and when she pulled it free, blood surged up like a fountain.

She had become a complete blood-soaked figure. There was no elegance in this fight. It was a true fight, filled only with killing intent as each side tried to kill the other.

Neither the Fist Demon’s side nor the Fist King’s side interfered in this battle. As Ian grew more and more exhausted, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable became even tenser than the Fist Demon.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at the Fist Demon.

‘Remarkable.’

He must surely be worrying several times more than she was. And yet he was not stepping in, wishing for Ian to overcome this fight on her own.

She thought that if it were her, she would not have been able to just stand by and watch like this.

‘You have never even raised a child, so how can you endure so well?’

The young man with red bandages wrapped around him looked at the Fist King. His gaze seemed to ask whether he should not step in when their subordinates were all dying, but the Fist King showed no reaction.

The Fist King did not regret the deaths of his subordinates at all. His eyes seemed to say that if they could not even kill one girl like that, they might as well all die.

Among the two experts who had come with Red Bandages, the man holding a sword to his chest furrowed his brow.

It was a style of fighting he would absolutely never choose. Watching as one’s subordinates were killed off? He could not understand it.

Was fighting a game? Was life a game? They should all have joined in and created a melee. That way, they should have secured their numerical advantage.

Now, only Ian’s rough breathing echoed through the place.

“Haa, haa, haa.”

Only then did Ian’s eyes take in the scene around her. It was truly a complete sea of blood, and all the other enemies had collapsed as corpses. She could not even remember how she had killed them.

Now only four enemies remained.

Those four were certain of victory.

“You vicious bitch. Your internal energy has finally run out.”

Their faces were appalled. When this fight began, they had truly never imagined it would become a battle with only the four of them left.

“Don’t tell me you’re hoping for help at this point.”

Ian’s rough breathing subsided.

“Yes, my internal energy is gone.”

A meaningful sentence was added.

“Except for just enough to execute one form.”

The next instant, the Sun-Moon Sword rose before her chest.

Chwarrrrk.

In an instant, the Sun-Moon Sword began to split.

Four blades of sword qi in the shape of the Sun-Moon Sword.

Flying Heaven Sword Art, Seventh Form: Flowing Heaven Form.

Swaaaaaaaaa!

The sword qi shot out like light in four directions, piercing through their hearts.

Peok! Peok! Peoeok! Peoeok!

The joy of having won lasted only a moment.

The man who had held a sword against his chest suddenly launched a surprise attack. His target was Ian.

‘If this young bitch is already this formidable…’

He could not even guess how difficult the fight against the Demon Venerables would become. That was why he intended to eliminate even this woman before his eyes before proceeding to the next battle.

Shiiiiing.

The sword that had been held to his chest was now in the man’s hand, and the blade was flying toward Ian’s neck.

There was no internal energy left in Ian’s body. And the man’s attack was too fast for her to block without internal energy.

At that very moment.

Huk!

A short sound of wind rang out, as if a candle had been blown out.

A single streak of sword-light flashed and split the air.

Seogeok.

Ian saw it. The sword that had been about to cut her had stopped in midair.

A red diagonal line began to form on the man’s face, then passed his jaw and continued down his body.

Jjiiik.

The man’s body began to shift along that red line.

Seureureuk.

Along the bloodline cut diagonally across him, the man’s body began to slide apart.

Was it because he had been cut too quickly, or too sharply? Not even blood flowed from the severed cross-section.

Only when the body split in half and fell to the ground did blood finally surge out.

Beyond the spraying blood, the back of one person could be seen.

The one who had charged in as swiftly as light and cut him down was none other than the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

From the tip of the Ilhwa Sword in her hand—

Ttok.

Only a single drop of blood fell.

Her sword felt as though it were alive. The keen edge flowing along the blade was like the cold breath exhaled by the sword itself.

When she extended her hand, the Sun-Moon Sword embedded far away in the wall flew toward her through void-seizing retrieval.

Receiving the sword, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable handed the Sun-Moon Sword to Ian.

“It was a splendid fight.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable could tell. This fight had become an opportunity for Ian to grow once again. Even if one wandered the Central Plains for a lifetime searching for villains, chances to have a fight like this would not come easily.

Ian, soaked in blood from head to toe, respectfully received the sword. At the Sword Venerable’s words that it had been a splendid fight, her eyes reddened for no reason. After fighting so well. Don’t cry! There are still enemies left!

“I will never forget the grace of saving my life. Thank you.”

She would have been better off not looking back.

The moment her eyes met the Fist Demon standing behind her, the tears she had been holding back flowed down. The moment she saw her father’s face looking at her, she could no longer endure it. Whether enemies remained or not, whether she looked weak or not, she could not stop the tears from falling.

They were tears of joy that she had not betrayed her father’s faith.

The Fist Demon silently nodded to her. He said nothing, but to Ian, that was enough.

Because she could feel, with her whole body, the deep affection contained in that nod of her father’s head.

For a moment, silence flowed through the place.

The Fist King, his face expressionless, was staring their way, impossible to read.

The next person to step forward was Ghost Blade. As he walked out, he looked at the fallen man’s corpse and said,

“He was not someone who should have died so pointlessly.”

Ghost Blade stared at the Ilhwa Sword Venerable and asked,

“Do you know who it is you killed?”

At his question, the Ilhwa Sword Venerable slowly turned toward him.

A cold yet elegant aura flowed from her body.

If the aura of the Sword King she had shown Geom Mugeuk earlier had been filled with hundreds and thousands of blades in every direction, the aura of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable was the opposite.

A single sword.

It felt as though that solitary sword was aimed directly before one’s eyes.

That was the aura of the Ilhwa Sword Venerable.

“I have no interest in who he was.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable gazed at her opponent with those lonely, profound eyes and spoke calmly.

“So do not bother telling me at length who you are, either.”

Chapter 595: What Did You Do to Your Fist?
------------------------------------------
Admiration swelled to fill both of Ian's eyes as he gazed upon the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

Yes, that was exactly the future he wished for himself.

He wanted to live without watching anyone's mood, without a single restraint. That was what it meant to roam the Central Plains with but a single sword. A life where you went if you wished to go, and came if you wished to come.

Freedom was but an illusion for the weak; only the truly strong could possess it.

It was the same for Yeon Baek-in, the Hall Master of the Yellow Dragon Martial Hall, who watched from the side. If one were to reduce the words "don't ramble on" to a single sentence, it would be this. If it were him, this was how he would have said it.

"Shut up!"

That desire—to utter that single line—dominated him. That desire was what made him send everyone out of the martial hall and stand alone in this sea of blood.

Then, his chest suddenly felt suffocated at the sight spread throughout the hall.

The corpses strewn everywhere.

Even those considered experts had died in a single move.

And there he was, stuck to this side, acting arrogant without even knowing his opponent was a Demon.

Yeon Baek-in's gaze turned toward Gang Hu, the Fist King.

Sitting in the spectator seats, he was truly behaving like a mere onlooker.

"Even as everyone is dying, are you just going to sit there?"

Because he did not know how profound his skill was or what kind of person he was, Yeon Baek-in naturally thought of one person.

"What are you doing down there?"

He desperately wished that the man underground would come up here.

How wonderful would it be if that chair slid down and returned with that man seated upon it? Because he believed that man could kill every last one of those terrifying Demons.

A faintly displeased expression flashed across Gwido's face before vanishing. Despite being thoroughly ignored by the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, he was not angry.

"That would be difficult."

He leisurely smoothed his clothes with a hand.

"I am someone who wishes to make my name known far and wide. Can you not tell by looking at my clothes?"

The ghostly figure drawn in white upon red martial robes—the very symbol of Gwido.

"Especially since there are so many these days who fail to recognize me."

He had coincidentally consumed a spiritual herb. Ah, no—it was no coincidence. It was a treasure that the person closest to him had kept for a long time, which he had killed and plundered.

The Evil Spirit Deathless Grass.

Thanks to that spiritual herb, he had gained immense internal energy, and ever since, his skin had grown taut and his black hair had begun to regrow.

The reason his close friend had not consumed so precious a thing was because of its side effects. There was a side effect whereby evil energy gradually burrowed into the mind and eventually drove one mad.

No one knew when that side effect would manifest. It could appear the very next day, or ten years later.

Gwido had boldly consumed it. The reason was simple.

"I'm an evil, crazy bastard anyway."

Even in his old age, Gwido had been a renowned expert of the Demonic Path, and with youth and internal energy now added, he had truly become strong. He was confident he could defeat anyone.

This was why he overflowed with composure even after witnessing the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign's formidable display moments ago.

"Ilhwa Sword Sovereign, you shall become the proudest name among those I have killed."

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign responded as coldly as ever.

"Not interested."

However, there was one thing she was curious about.

"What I wish to know is what is happening down below."

Gwonma was not one to ask such questions, so if there was something worth finding out, she had to discover it herself. It was a matter of the Young Cult Leader's life and death, after all.

The enemies knew what was happening underground, but her side did not. If it had been anyone other than the Young Cult Leader, she would have descended to check long ago.

Gwido could not answer her question. Having realized that he did not know the situation below either, the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign dismissed him once more.

"You talk a great deal, but you do not know what you actually should."

She did not acknowledge her opponent. It was an enemy she had to kill in any case, and Gwido was no martial artist worthy of respect.

Gwido's expression hardened at her mockery.

At least on this point, he was inwardly furious. Gang Hu had absolutely refused to tell him anything about it. As if he would not deign to speak of it to a mere underling like him.

Truly, Gwido had met all manner of villains, but he had never met anyone so disagreeable and unpleasant as that man.

Then, Gang Hu opened his mouth.

"Do you know? You have come to a place where you should not be."

He was certain of Geom Mugung's death.

"The place where you must bear responsibility for the Young Cult Leader's death. Even if not by our hands, you will die by your Cult Leader."

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign smiled faintly.

"You know nothing either."

Of the Young Cult Leader, and of the Cult Leader.

She held a firm belief that the Young Cult Leader would not die, and even if so, the Cult Leader would not place the blame upon them. Rather, that would make it even more torturous.

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign's gaze turned back to Gwido.

"Then I have nothing more to say to you."

Cold contempt laced her every word, so Gwido also ceased speaking and quietly drew his blade.

His unique blade technique was the Black Shadow Ghost Hand Blade Technique.

A blade technique consisting of seven tyrannical moves, countless people had lost their lives to it. Now, this powerful martial art had been given wings.

Ssshhhaaaaa.

A powerful aura burst forth from Gwido's body. He already possessed an evil disposition, and combined with the evil energy of the Evil Spirit Deathless Grass, his aura contained a truly vicious malice.

Huaaaaaah.

The surging evil energy made the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign's pristine white robes flutter.

Even within that vicious energy, she gazed at Gwido with haughty eyes. She was no longer at the level she had been upon exchanging moves with Geom Mugung in the past.

She had stepped into a new realm beyond Grand Achievement. Her swordsmanship was advancing beyond the Eleventh Star toward the Grand Achievement of the Twelfth Star.

Her unique martial art was the Ilhwa Sword Technique.

Both her title and her sword had been named after her unique sword technique. She had once possessed such fierce pride in her unique martial art, but now she had changed.

She was pioneering her own realm without being bound by forms. The eight moves of the Ilhwa Sword Technique were gradually decreasing.

When two moves became one, and even that one move disappeared, only then would she reach the pinnacle of swordsmanship.

She had realized that only when the sword attained freedom could one reach perfect Martial Intent.

Amidst taut tension, it was Gwido who launched the first attack.

Swaaaeeng!

Powerful blade energy flew toward her.

As the Ilhwa Sword was drawn, it split the air.

Shwaaaaaak!

Like silk being torn, the swirling blade energy was split into two sides.

Kwaaang! Kwaaang!

The divided blade energy flew backward, smashing and collapsing the walls.

Gwido's expression hardened. He had known she would block it, but he had not thought she would do so with such ease.

"As expected! A Demon is a Demon, after all!"

In a flash, the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign was already cutting at him.

Shhk!

A short, sharp whistling of wind.

Kaaang!

Blade and sword clashed. An instant's delay would have meant his head flying off his shoulders.

Seeing the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign who had withdrawn before he knew it, Gwido's eyes blazed in an instant.

"Sword Sovereign! Today you die by my hand—"

Chaeng!

Sparks flew again, faster than before.

Just as he thought she was retreating once more after sending her sword at his neck, with a hwuk! of wind she was already swinging at him from before his eyes.

Kaaang!

Gwido blocked the move, but his wrist ached as if it would snap. He could feel it. Had he been his former self, he would have already been gravely wounded.

"She's strong!"

From the start, he was being pushed back in both speed and momentum. Never in his dreams had he imagined that he, who had grown incomparably stronger than before, would be pushed back like this. He had not underestimated the Demon, yet he had never considered that she might be stronger than his rejuvenated self.

Of course, he was not taking the beating lying down either.

Emitting even stronger evil energy, Gwido's blade continuously tore through the air.

Papapapapak!

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign blocked five consecutive attacks with two sword strikes, and evaded the remaining three with her footwork.

Her movements were concise yet elegant. Elegance would normally suggest broad, slow motions, yet she demonstrated how fast and concise movements could possess grace.

Just when it seemed she was moving slowly, a gust of wind accompanied her form already cutting at Gwido.

Light in movement, yet absolutely not light in blade; unhurried in stance even amid urgency—every one of her strikes felt not merely elegant, but noble.

Pauht!

Blood spurted from Gwido's shoulder.

He immediately launched a counterattack, but the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign had already withdrawn beyond the reach of his blade.

Freed from the shackles of forms, her movements were like the wind. Blowing lightly, then strongly; soft as a spring breeze, yet fierce as a tempest.

Ian, watching from the side, did not miss a single motion. The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign was showing him.

How one who held a sword ought to move.

Knowing that the longer this fight dragged on, the more disadvantaged he would become, Gwido unleashed his ultimate move without reservation.

Dozens of strands of blade energy poured down like rain—Ghost Rain.

The blade energy poured down upon the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign like rain. Could a human truly avoid rain?

Papapapapapapapapak!

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign ran through the rain. Running through rain ought to mean getting wet, but she was beyond common sense.

Avoiding where it fell first, slipping between strands of blade energy, parrying with her sword to evade. Truly, the instant one perceived her, she was already elsewhere.

And Gwido saw. Even amidst it all, she was gazing at him beyond the pouring rain.

The moment he saw those calm eyes, Gwido poured all his internal energy into his next move.

Huaaaaaang!

A demonic wind that blew everything away—his final move, Ghost Wind.

A massive blade storm rushed at her. The ground overturned under the blade storm laden with immense internal energy, and rocks and wooden walls were all swept away.

Gwonma turned and embraced Ian, who possessed no internal energy, raising his protective astral energy to its limit.

Hweeeeeeee.

Ian felt as though he were watching a typhoon sweep past his window from inside an indestructible building.

She worried for the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

With the surroundings in such a state, she had been caught in the very center of the blade storm.

Hweeeeee.

Eventually, the wind that had raged like a luminous ghost ceased.

A smile formed on Gwido's face.

This move had struck true. The arrogant Ilhwa Sword Sovereign had faced his Ghost Wind head-on without dodging.

"Arrogance is always the problem."

As the dust settled and the scene within was revealed, the smile vanished from Gwido's lips.

He widened his eyes, brimming with disbelief.

The Ilhwa Sword Sovereign stood in that same spot, having not moved a single step. Not a hem was torn; not even a strand of hair was disheveled.

She asked calmly.

"Is there more you wish to show?"

There should have been. Yet all that remained for him was disbelief and terror.

He had never thought he would utter such words to his opponent. They were the very clichés that dying enemies had always spoken to him.

"How on earth?"

The hwuk of wind heard this time was different from before.

It was the clearest, most refreshing wind he had heard yet.

Gwido stood blankly before her, and then...

Sssssshk.

Blood lines were drawn diagonally across his face and body.

"My life was only just beginning again..."

He collapsed, cleaved in two. The flowing blood quickly soaked Gwido's clothes. The ghost drawn in white was dyed red.

No matter how much younger he had grown, no matter how much his evil energy had strengthened and his internal energy had increased, he could not match the Ilhwa Sword Sovereign.

For she was silently walking toward the pinnacle of swordsmanship these days—no, sprinting at full speed.

It was at that very moment.

Jjeo-eong.

From the main building far behind Gwido's corpse, a sound was heard, and then...

A diagonal line began to form upon the building.

Urrrrr! Kwang kwang!

The main building that had housed Yeon Baek-in's office collapsed diagonally.

It was a moment that revealed just how profound the essence of the sword contained in the final move she had unleashed at Ghost Blade truly was.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable turned toward the Fist Demon and Ian.

As the Ilhwa Sword Venerable turned and came back, the Fist Demon bowed his head lightly. It was courtesy toward swordsmanship, and courtesy toward her.

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable likewise bowed her head lightly to him and accepted the greeting.

Ian’s face was filled with astonishment and emotion.

What had truly moved her was not that Ghost Blade had been killed, nor that the building had come crashing down.

It was the Ilhwa Sword Venerable’s clothes.

She had returned after finishing this battle in the midst of a sea of blood strewn with corpses, yet not a single drop of blood had splashed onto her white robes. She had traversed that downpour of saber qi, yet not even a drop of blood from the ground had stained her.

Ian bowed her head respectfully and said,

“Sword Venerable, you have just shown me the future I must advance toward.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable gave Ian a radiant smile.

In the meantime, the Fist Demon stepped forward.

Now, the only people left alive there were Ganghu, the young man with the red bandages, and Yeon Baegin.

Yeon Baegin, who had desperately fled beyond the saber wind and barely saved his life, stared at the collapsed building in utter despair. He felt an ominous sensation, as though he were looking at his own future.

All his subordinates were dead, and even the two experts he had brought with him had died, but even in such a situation, Ganghu remained calm.

“I’ve wanted to fight you someday.”

Ganghu slowly walked forward. The young man with the red bandages walked forward with him.

The two of them unwrapped their bandages at the same time.

Beneath them, their fists were densely engraved with tiny characters that could not be deciphered. The characters carved into the two men differed in color and content, and a bizarre energy was flowing from their fists.

Unlike the Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who watched the situation in silence, Ian’s expression hardened.

Even when they had not removed the bandages, their aura had been far from ordinary, and now some bizarre sorcery had been added to their fists. That aura of death flowing from their fists was no ordinary evil.

And there was not just one enemy. There was also that man with the red bandages, who appeared to be his disciple. He must have stepped forward with him because he had some role to play.

If only she had any internal energy left, she would have wanted to step out with him. She wanted to take on that younger one herself.

But unlike her complicated feelings, her father’s back, as imposing as Mount Tai, did not waver in the slightest.

Just like that back she had watched every night as he practiced fist arts beneath the cliff.

“What did you do to your fists?”

The Fist Demon clenched his massive fist with force and walked forward.

“If you have no confidence in your fists, you’d be better off picking up a weapon.”

Chapter 596: Now Death Is in the Stands
---------------------------------------
“Terrifying.”

The man wrapped in red bandages was Jindan, the head disciple of Fist King Ganghu. Since he had first begun learning the fist arts from his master, this was the first time he had ever looked at someone and thought them terrifying.

That horrifying appearance, the sort one should only ever see in hell, was one thing. The fearless momentum of him striding forward without hesitation, even though he must clearly feel the ominous aura flowing from their fists, was another.

But what felt most frightening of all was his master’s reaction.

In the fifteen years since learning the fist arts from Ganghu, this was the first time he had ever seen someone speak such words of contempt to his master, and the first time he had seen his master endure it in silence.

“As expected of the Demon Venerable.”

The strength that Ilhwa Geomjon had shown earlier was truly astounding. No, there was no need to even go that far—that young woman had cut down all the subordinates by herself. Though there were only three, he had felt the pressure of facing thousands.

“But the outcome of this fight is already decided.”

Because he could not even imagine his master losing to someone. He believed in his master.

“What matters is how I fight.”

If this became a closely fought battle against Kwonma, then victory or defeat would be determined by how he fought.

But that thought was his great delusion. There was no room in this fight for his will or judgment to intervene.

The figure of Kwonma, which had been striding forward, split the air and flew in.

Without dodging, Ganghu threw a fist.

The first punch signaling the start of the battle.

Boom.

When the fists, both imbued with internal energy, collided, an explosive sound erupted.

Having taken a step back, Kwonma couldn’t help but frown.

It was because of the vicious aura spreading from Ganghu’s fist. This bizarre and turbid energy, a mixture of killing intent and demonic aura, was the sort that would have already sent anyone with weak mental strength or insufficient internal energy into a stupor.

That energy pervaded the surroundings like the breath of death, consuming and crushing the battlefield.

Ganghu smiled coldly, putting on airs.

“Because I’ve yet to meet an opponent who could make me pick up a weapon against this fist.”

It had been a retort to Kwonma’s earlier mockery, but now Kwonma answered with his fist rather than words.

And with a powerful fist at that.

Thunder rumbled! Crash!

With the sound of thunder, a fist of tremendous power flew toward his face.

KABOOM!

The two fists clashed once more.

Though it was fist against fist, it left a shockwave more powerful than any clashing of weapons.

Whoosh.

The aftershock spread in all directions and fierce winds whipped around.

A shockwave surging from fist against fist? It was truly unbelievable power.

Ian shielded her eyes with her hand against the blowing whirlwind. Just as her father had protected her in the earlier fight, this time Ilhwa Geomjon protected her, for her internal energy was depleted. If not for Ilhwa Geomjon, she would have been swept away by that whirlwind.

In fact, Hall Master Yeon Baek-in of the Yellow Dragon Hall, who had been standing over there, was sent rolling across the ground far away.

Crack!

Trees standing around the distant wall bent greatly, and leaves flew through the air.

Sssshaaaa.

In that place where leaves fluttered like falling snow, Kwonma and the Fist King stood facing each other.

That punch from moments ago had been no ordinary fist.

The third fist among the six fists of Kwonma’s unique martial art, the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

Third Fist: Heavenly Thunder Asura.

That heavy fist had flown out and struck true.

Ian sensed a change in her father’s martial art.

“Father’s thunder has grown softer.”

It was the thunder she had heard many times during his late-night training. Yet even if it had quieted, it was far too quiet.

“Could there be something wrong with his body?”

She still did not know. That when this thunder dwindled and vanished completely, that was the moment he was finally ready to shatter the cliff. That Kwonma’s martial art was endlessly advancing toward that very moment.

Ganghu looked down at his fist. From his trembling fist, even more powerful killing intent and demonic energy were flowing. His fist was reacting on its own after that single move just now.

“So you want to get right to it?”

To Ganghu’s question, Kwonma said in a low voice,

“The stench of your fists is too foul.”

Ganghu’s expression hardened once more. Kwonma was thoroughly mocking him. If it were the mockery of someone ignorant of the heavens and earth, he could simply make them pay for it, but his opponent was someone qualified to say such things.

Ganghu forcibly suppressed the surging killing intent. Though that killing intent was his greatest strength.

“Not yet.”

Ganghu massaged his arm and stepped forward.

“Your fist hurts more than I expected.”

To that calm reaction, Kwonma nodded once. It was not a nod acknowledging his opponent.

Why would he acknowledge an opponent he must kill?

It was a signal to himself—a gesture meaning that since his opponent was someone worth acknowledging, he must not become complacent. Because that opponent had walked out completely unscathed even after the Heavenly Thunder Asura.

“Your temper is as short as mine.”

This time, Ganghu charged and threw a fist.

Even when fist met fist, it sounded like chunks of iron colliding.

Jindan watched the two fight while taking a step back. And amazingly, he saw openings.

“There! Now!”

Why wasn’t Master launching a counterattack there?

He saw a gap where he would have struck, yet his master did nothing.

The opposite was also true.

“It’s dangerous, Master!”

It was an opening in his master that he had never once seen before.

But Kwonma did not attack that opening.

Only then did he understand.

“Ah, it’s not an opening.”

That opening was not a flaw, but a trap. The moment a fist flew there, he would be counterattacked—a death trap. Ultimate techniques were exchanged in an instant, ones that even the eyes of a figure like the Fist King’s head disciple could not discern.

At a glance, it looked like a crude fight, but their battle was by no means such a simple one.

Fist artists are those who must face armed opponents with bare hands.

That is why these fist artists who savagely exchanged punches had to be even smarter. Otherwise, how could they defeat those wielding sharp weapons? It was a fight between two who had approached the pinnacle of the fist arts.

That was why this was a fight Jindan could not intervene in. Because the fight continued with no way of knowing what this move intended or what the next move would be. All he could do was not miss the moment when his master called for him.

Amidst the probing back-and-forth, this time Ganghu unleashed a hidden ace.

Ganghu’s unique martial art was the Nether Spirit Annihilation Fist.

Among them, the move unleashed now was the Second Fist: Soul-Severing Strike.

Sssshaaaek!

The small energy flying from the fist suddenly grew enormous like a great blade the moment it reached the opponent. An enormous energy impossible to evade by leaping aside.

Sssshaaaek!

Kwonma’s fist struck the incoming Soul-Severing Strike. Without taking a single step back from that massive energy, he threw a punch.

First Fist: Black Cloud Asura.

Though it was the first of the six techniques, that did not mean its power was the lowest. The Black Cloud Asura—you only understand once struck—became an energy blade and tore through the air.

The moment the two energies clashed!

WHOOOOOSH!

The surroundings were swept away with a tremendous explosion.

Crack! Rumble!

A tree that had merely lost its leaves earlier was uprooted entirely, and the surrounding walls collapsed all at once from the shock. The building that Ilhwa Geomjon had half-destroyed earlier now completely crumbled down.

Swept away by this shock, the one who rolled even farther across the ground than before and stood up was Yellow Dragon Martial Hall Master Yeon Baek-in. Clutching his chest from internal injuries, he lamented inwardly.

“My martial hall… my martial hall is all collapsing.”

Of course, a martial hall could be rebuilt, but after the main hall had collapsed from a single sword strike of Ilhwa Geomjon, all of this felt like his fate.

“I can survive if I run away now.”

That was the cry of instinct.

“This is not a fight I can intervene in.”

An ominous premonition that whoever won, they would show him no mercy.

But when that person underground brought out the peerless secret manual, what if he wasn’t there? What if that person came looking for him? He had waited all this time, so what if he wasn’t there at this final moment?

That regret, terrifying even to imagine, gripped Yeon Baek-in by the ankles. For sometimes, lingering attachment and regret can be more frightening than greed.

And this was another reason he ran toward the cliff even after seeing its edge.

“I even killed my younger brother!”

That fact made it impossible for him to turn back.

That is why one must never, in life, cross a bridge one cannot turn back from; only after standing at the cliff’s edge did he feel this keenly.

“He will come out. I just have to survive until then.”

Though he was traveling back and forth between the world of the living and the dead alone, no one was paying him any mind.

Ilhwa Geomjon, who watched the fight while protecting Ian, sensed that Kwonma, too, was no longer at his previous level of strength. After gaining a connection with the Geommu-geuk, she had grown stronger as well.

Seeing Ganghu fighting such a Kwonma, she realized anew how powerful the ones behind this were. It was not an organization prepared in just a year or two.

“Just when was this organization created?”

Her gaze turned to Ganghu. His expression had grown grave.

Having blocked the Soul-Severing Strike head-on, Ganghu’s momentum changed. He had always emanated the aura of death toward his opponents, but now he felt that death might turn toward himself.

Sssshaaaek!

Now sensing that Death itself sat in the spectator’s seat, the two men’s speed began to grow faster and faster.

Their figures vanished before one’s eyes and reappeared. Even those watching had trouble keeping up with their forms.

Big guys are slow?

Kwonma shattered that prejudice to pieces. He was fast. The muscles of that large frame seemed to exist not for strength, but for speed.

At the end of that feast of speed—

Fwoosh!

A different sound of wind than any before, created by a different attempt.

The wind sound was light, but the impact sound was heavy.

Thwack!

Ganghu’s head was snapped back. As if he were looking up at the sky itself. With vacant eyes gazing upward, Kwonma’s fist was thrust right before his chin.

Puaack!

Looking at the sky, Ganghu spewed blood. Truly, blood surged like a fountain and scattered in all directions.

The Second Fist, Thunderbolt Asura, had struck his face.

The Thunderbolt Asura was a technique that threw punches so fast they were invisible to the eye. If compared to the Bicheon Sword Technique, it was the same as the Changcheon Form of the Rapid Sword Style—that very technique had struck his face.

If he hadn’t tilted his head back at the very last moment, the force would have blown his entire face clean off.

Right then, Ganghu suddenly spewed curses.

“Kwonma, you bastard I’ll chew up and kill! I’ll completely pulverize that filthy face of yours.”

Curses completely unbefitting his skill spilled out. Now he revealed another—no, his true—self.

His killing intent was not elegant killing intent. He was a figure who utilized raw, primitive killing intent—the most violent and vulgar kind found at the very bottom of humanity. He was drawing up every shred of killing intent he possessed.

“I’ll beat you until your whole body turns to dust. I won’t leave a single bone in your body. I’ll chew you up and swallow you whole.”

Endless killing intent flowed from the body of a man whom no one had ever liked even once in his entire life. Curses and killing intent born of pure loathing.

Kwonma did not take that killing intent lightly. He mustn’t fall for that vulgarity. The moment he thought, “So this bastard is showing his true, pathetic self,” he would die.

Ganghu’s killing intent was precisely that kind of killing intent.

Unless his opponent were a mere ruffian, from someone with such skill, this was killing intent that screamed at the heavens beside the fires of hell. True, primal killing intent hidden within vulgarity.

Sure enough, wiping the blood from his face, Ganghu sneered coldly. For a moment, his eyes seemed to glow white with an intense killing intent, the likes of which he had never experienced in his life,

Sss, sss, sss.

Then the characters engraved on both his hands began to glow blue like will-o’-wisps. That, too, was a light not of this world.

Kwonma intuitively realized his opponent was preparing his final move, and sensed that this fight would not be an easy one.

Yes, if this was a fight that had to roll around in the mud to the very bottom, if it was a fight over who would open that hell gate and come out—

Just as Ganghu had said it had been a long time since he’d had to unravel his bandages.

Kwonma roughly tore off his top. He revealed a truly perfect, muscular upper body.

He had often stripped during training, but it had truly been a long time since he’d met an opponent in actual combat who made him take off his shirt.

Kwonma clenched his fist with vigor. The muscles of his arm were taut to the point of bursting, and his veins stood out as distinctly as flowing rivers. The true energy flowing through those vessels burned as hot as lava.

Of course, Kwonma did not lose his composure.

Because it was a fight before his daughter’s eyes.

“If it gets too painful to bear while trading blows, tell me. I’ll face you with a sword.”

Chapter 597: So You Were Quite Something
----------------------------------------
Everyone was exhausted.

The martial artists of Cheonuigung sitting around the campfire did not even engage in the usual small talk, gazing at the blazing flames with weary eyes.

Then, a man approached them shouldering a box. It was Geom Mugeuk, dressed in the garb of a Cheonuigung martial artist.

“That’s a face I haven’t seen before?”

At the question from the martial artist across the campfire, Geom Mugeuk set down his box and squeezed himself into their circle.

“Well, that’s because you’re seeing it for the first time. Hey, move your butt over a bit.”

The other martial artists burst into snickers. There were so many people encamped that it was impossible to know everyone. He must have just arrived to fill a dead man’s spot.

Geom Mugeuk pulled a bottle of liquor from the box he had brought, took a long swig, and then tossed the bottle to the man who had first spoken to him.

“Where did you get the booze?”

“Want to know?”

“No.”

The man drank. The others waited their turn and passed it around, each taking a sip. These days, with the fighting dragging on, getting liquor was like plucking stars from the sky.

Geom Mugeuk’s behavior was so natural, and since he had even shared liquor, no one suspected this new face. Surely, if he were an enemy, he wouldn’t have deliberately come and squeezed himself in among them.

Geom Mugeuk had infiltrated deep into the central area of the Cheonuigung encampment. His target was the central command tent visible up ahead.

‘This won’t be easy.’

A strict perimeter was spread around the area, but they were not the problem.

He did not possess enough internal energy to unleash the Guhwamagong, but he could execute a variation of the Pungsin Sobo footwork. Avoiding their eyes was not difficult.

The problem was the Cheonuigungju and the true experts guarding him at his side. It would be impossible to deceive them as well and infiltrate that place. Yet having come this far, it was too much of a waste to simply turn back, so he needed to find some way.

Then, the martial artist from earlier asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Any more liquor?”

“I have some. But could you handle it if I did?”

Geom Mugeuk gestured with his chin toward the large central command tent. It meant the liquor would be taken there. He might be able to siphon off a bottle or so, but if he diverted more and caused a problem, he would pay a truly steep price for the liquor.

The man did not dare ask for more liquor and instead poured out complaints.

“Damn it. When is this blasted war going to end?”

Then the others sitting around chimed in one by one.

“It’ll end soon. Haven’t you heard the rumors that the Muhwang Singeom has arrived on the other side? He’ll cross blades with our Palace Lord before long.”

“What if our Palace Lord loses?”

“What else? Then we’re all dead too.”

At the mention of death, everyone rather laughed.

Listening to their conversation, these men were no different from the Martial Alliance’s martial artists. At first, they had likely entered the war endowing it with all manner of meaning, but as time went on, everyone had only one wish: for the war to end soon.

Then a martial artist came running from the other side and shouted,

“The Palace Lord has returned!”

At that, everyone sprang to their feet and the surroundings grew hectic.

Geom Mugeuk’s eyes sparkled.

‘Then that means the Cheonuigungju is not in that tent right now?’

Carrying the box, Geom Mugeuk quickly moved toward the large command tent. He set the box down nearby and displayed his skill.

It was not the kind of spectacular footwork that could shave one’s nose off before one’s very eyes, but even so, infiltrating the interior of the tent guarded by ordinary martial artists was not difficult.

As expected, the interior of the tent was empty. Everything inside was luxurious. The fur covering the chair, the small table, even the liquor bottles and cups set upon it.

He had entered this place knowing full well that the Cheonuigungju would come, for there was one thing he believed in.

Just then, sounds of movement came from outside, and two men entered.

When they entered, Geom Mugeuk’s figure was nowhere to be seen.

He had unleashed the Space-Time Transference Technique. The space Geom Mugeuk created was just large enough for one person to stand—a space of darkness where not even a single ray of light could exist.

It was the only space he could create with his current internal energy. This too was possible because, thanks to ceaselessly training in the Space-Time Transference Technique and the Sicheon Bisul, his mastery had deepened, allowing him to open a space with very little internal energy.

From within the Space-Time Transference Technique, Geom Mugeuk watched the two who had entered: an old man and a middle-aged man.

The old man’s face bore deep furrows carved by time, yet there was also something about him that made one hesitate to call him old. His back was straight, his eyes fierce. And more than anything, he radiated not the setting sun of a life nearing its end, but the blazing heat of a sun still high in the sky.

This old man was none other than Wi Mucheon, the Cheonuigungju.

He was the very one who had started a war against the Martial Alliance, the symbol of the orthodox faction.

Wi Mucheon paused and looked around the interior, seemingly sensing a subtle disharmony. It was a moment that revealed just how extraordinary his martial prowess was.

“Why do you pause, sir?”

The middle-aged martial artist who had entered with Wi Mucheon asked.

He had a strong impression, looking like a man who would never back down from any situation. This was Baek Yanggi, the Palace Lord of Suhogung, one of the Three Great Divine Palaces of Cheonuigung.

“It is nothing.”

Wi Mucheon failed to detect the Space-Time Transference Technique that Geom Mugeuk had deployed.

He sat in his chair and poured liquor from the bottle on the side table.

“Then please rest.”

As the Palace Lord of Suhogung turned to leave, Wi Mucheon asked,

“Do you know why the Heavenly Demon Cult and the Heretical Alliance are not intervening in this fight with the Martial Alliance?”

The Palace Lord of Suhogung turned back to Wi Mucheon and answered.

“They must be hoping that the orthodox martial world’s vitality will be greatly damaged by this war.”

Wi Mucheon nodded at his calm reply.

“Exactly. The strategy of the borrowed knife. They strike the Martial Alliance with the blade that is Cheonuigung. That is their scheme.”

Wi Mucheon had started the war knowing that scheme better than anyone.

It was a strategy that exploited the longstanding enmity between the orthodox and demonic. Because of that ancient hatred that could never stand to see the other thriving, he was confident that even if one side was attacked, the other would never come to their aid.

As expected, the Heavenly Demon Cult and the Heretical Alliance did not help the Martial Alliance. Instead, they supported the various factions aiding Cheonuigung.

“They must think that we will mutually exhaust ourselves fighting the orthodox martial world.”

The smile formed by the wrinkles at the corners of Wi Mucheon’s mouth was full of confidence.

“Because of their arrogance, we will be victorious.”

The eyes of the Palace Lord of Suhogung watching him deepened. Who was truly the arrogant one? Did the Cheonuigungju truly believe he could achieve the unification of the martial world?

“Have a drink.”

Wi Mucheon held out a cup to the Palace Lord of Suhogung, who received it respectfully.

Glug-glug.

“You opposed this war.”

With the liquor falling into the cup between them, the two men’s gazes intertwined.

The Palace Lord of Suhogung knew. He knew of the Palace Lord’s fervent dream of unification under Heaven. He knew how long he had prepared for that dream. Even so.

“The Muhwang Singeom is a strong man.”

The Palace Lord of Suhogung knew all too well that these words could wound Wi Mucheon’s pride if he erred. But it was something that had to be said.

Even if they killed the Muhwang Singeom, if the Martial Alliance fell, countless hidden experts of the orthodox faction would reveal themselves.

And that was not all.

The Heretical Alliance remained, and the Heavenly Demon Cult remained.

Unification under Heaven.

The only thing that could fill those four characters was blood.

And the moment those four characters came together, they would become a monster that could drink blood without ever being sated.

It was unimaginable how much blood would have to be poured to complete those characters.

Wi Mucheon silently poured liquor into his own cup.

Of course, the Palace Lord of Suhogung knew. He was hardly a madman crazed for blood, was he? He merely possessed a fierce determination toward the unification of the martial world.

He understood his ambition as well. A martial prodigy, he possessed the strongest martial arts among all past Cheonuigungju.

“I apologize.”

To the Palace Lord of Suhogung’s apology, Wi Mucheon uttered something unexpected.

“This war is not mine.”

Wi Mucheon emptied his cup. The Palace Lord of Suhogung waited until he finished drinking, then poured him more and asked,

“Then whose war is it?”

“Heaven’s will.”

A brief silence passed.

The will of Heaven.

Cheonuigung was a place that existed to preserve that heavenly will. No one knew when Cheonuigung had descended through history, but the fact that it was an organization that protected the will of Heaven had never changed.

“I do not believe Heaven desires blood.”

The Palace Lord of Suhogung conveyed his honest feelings. He knew these words would greatly displease the Cheonuigungju, but they too had to be spoken. For Suhogung existed to protect Cheonuigung.

Wi Mucheon fixed his gaze upon the Palace Lord of Suhogung. The tension heightened to a breaking point, but the Palace Lord showed no fear.

As if he had expected such a reaction, the Cheonuigungju did not grow angry. Instead, he conveyed an astonishing fact.

“The Bigwe has awakened.”

“!”

The Palace Lord of Suhogung was startled. Not only him, but Geom Mugeuk who was listening was also startled. He had known the Bigwe was related to Cheonuigung, but he had not expected to hear of it in their conversation.

What could it mean that the Bigwe had awakened?

“As you know, the Bigwe is the sacred object of the main palace, said to contain the will of Heaven. No one knows who made it or for what purpose, but after the first Cheonuigungju obtained the Bigwe, Cheonuigung became a place that existed generation after generation to protect the Bigwe.”

From his words, Geom Mugeuk was able to understand the relationship between the Bigwe and Cheonuigung.

‘So the Bigwe was the sacred object of Cheonuigung.’

He could now understand more clearly why that Bigwe had brought him here.

The Palace Lord of Suhogung looked at Wi Mucheon with a surprised face.

He found it hard to believe. Over the years, the Bigwe had existed as merely a symbol. Like some bell somewhere, some incense burner somewhere, some sword somewhere.

‘But that Bigwe has awakened?’

Wi Mucheon showed him the proof directly. He took something from within his robe.

“The Bigwe produced this.”

What Wi Mucheon took out was a small Bigwe.

The moment he saw that Bigwe, the Palace Lord of Suhogung knelt down on the spot, paying his respects. It was shaped exactly like the great Bigwe.

“In accordance with the will of Heaven, this body shall follow the will of the sacred object.”

The Bigwe, sacred object of Cheonuigung, was not something one could dare to meet eyes with or gaze upon. In truth, the Bigwe was stored in the treasure vault of Cheonuigung, and only the Cheonuigungju could enter that place.

Wi Mucheon’s eyes as he gazed upon the Bigwe were also filled with awe and reverence.

“I can feel the power contained within. It is a fearsome power. Not something the likes of the Muhwang Singeom could stop.”

Only now did the Palace Lord of Suhogung understand why the Cheonuigungju had started such an unreasonable war.

And it was at that very moment.

At this instant, remarkably, the Bigwe cried out.

*Oooooong.*

Even Wi Mucheon was startled. It was the first time the small Bigwe had shown a reaction since he obtained it.

Wi Mucheon lifted the Bigwe high, and the Palace Lord of Suhogung bowed his head to the ground.

“I started this fight in accordance with the will of Heaven.”

“I shall assist Your Highness in achieving the unification of the world under Heaven’s will without fail.”

The resolve to no longer harbor other thoughts about the meaning of this war surfaced on the Palace Lord of Suhogung’s face.

Watching that scene, Geom Mugeuk realized one fact.

‘The Bigwe did not cry because of the Cheonuigungju.’

Because right now, the Bigwe in his own embrace was vibrating as well. Geom Mugeuk could feel it instinctively.

‘It is the same Bigwe.’

That across three hundred years, two Bigwes—one and the same—were resonating with each other.

No doubt it was because that Bigwe existed here that it had been possible to bring him across that long span of years.

‘You… you were this incredible, huh.’

Sorry. Since I drew a smiling face on you without knowing you were such an amazing sacred object.

Then, a subordinate’s voice was heard from outside.

“The Sinnyeo of Sinnyeogung has arrived.”

The Sinnyeo was the palace lord who led Sinnyeogung, another of the Three Great Divine Palaces.

The two men were startled to hear that she had come. For the Sinnyeo to come personally to this battlefield meant that something equally important had occurred.

“Bring her in.”

Shortly after, women dressed in white entered first.

As they lined up left and right to clear a path, the Sinnyeo walked in between them.

The Palace Lord of Sinnyeogung, Ye Seol.

She had her two eyes covered with a white cloth. Though her eyes were covered, she was known to see everything in the world.

A sacred aura flowed from her body, and with every step she took, mystery seemed to ripple up like heat haze.

Wi Mucheon greeted her with courtesy. Though the Sinnyeo was indeed his subordinate, she was a special existence.

“To what do we owe the presence of the Sinnyeo in such a perilous place?”

Then the Sinnyeo spoke astonishing words. Her voice possessed a composed dignity. She spoke in a voice that made all ears lean in.

“A prophecy has descended.”

At those words, both Wi Mucheon and the Palace Lord of Suhogung were startled.

The reason the people of Cheonuigung revered and valued the Sinnyeo was precisely because she could read the will of Heaven and make prophecies. Among all past Sinnyeos, she was the one who had made the most accurate prophecies.

Wi Mucheon and the Palace Lord of Suhogung cupped their fists simultaneously and paid their respects.

“We listen to the will of Heaven on behalf of the main palace.”

Anticipation filled Wi Mucheon’s eyes. A prophecy delivered before an important battle.

He prayed it would be a positive prophecy regarding his unification of the world.

“It was said that the unification of the world will be achieved.”

Joy filled Wi Mucheon’s face. Yes, there was a reason the Bigwe had awakened. He could now understand why the Bigwe had cried out.

Then, astonishing words flowed from the Sinnyeo.

“However, the will of Heaven is not with the Palace Lord.”

“!”

Puzzlement bloomed on Wi Mucheon’s face.

Had she not clearly said the unification would be achieved? Yet the will of Heaven was not with him?

A shocking prophecy flowed from the Sinnyeo’s mouth.

“The time when Cheonuigung’s heavenly will shall be fulfilled is three hundred years hence.”

Chapter 598: A Gift from My Disciple
------------------------------------
Wi Mucheon, lord of Heavenly Will Palace, was deeply shaken.

‘Heaven’s Will will be fulfilled three hundred years from now?’

Right beside him, the Guardian Palace Lord saw with his own eyes the corners of Wi Mucheon’s eyes trembling. It was the first time he had ever seen the palace lord so astonished and flustered.

Geom Mugeuk, within the Space-Time Transposition Art, was just as shocked.

‘They had already prophesied that three hundred years ago?’

Truly, it was hard to believe even after seeing it with his own eyes.

Silence settled over the hall.

Wi Mucheon, who had been staring at the Divine Maiden, slowly walked back and sat down. Then he poured wine into his cup.

Having the Divine Maiden stand before him while he sat and poured himself wine was discourteous, but at that moment, no one paid it any mind.

Trickle, trickle.

In that place where not even the sound of breathing could be heard, only the sound of wine being poured rang out.

The Guardian Palace Lord could feel it—just how furious Wi Mucheon was right now. That clear sound of falling wine was the sound of him suppressing his rage.

Without a word, Wi Mucheon looked down at the cup, filled to the brim and nearly overflowing.

So much blood had already been shed. They had already come to a point from which there was no turning back. And now Heaven had delivered such a prophecy? Then why had Bigwe awakened? Why had it wept like that in his hands?

After fiddling with the wine cup for a moment, Wi Mucheon asked the Divine Maiden in a calm tone.

“Would you care for a cup?”

The expressions of the women in white lined up on either side stiffened.

As if sitting alone and pouring wine were not enough, now he was offering her a drink. No matter if he was the lord of Heavenly Will Palace, it was something he must not do.

Because the Divine Maiden’s eyes were covered, it was impossible to tell what emotions she felt.

“I am fine.”

At the Divine Maiden’s calm reply, Wi Mucheon drank alone.

The very moment he set down his cup, astonishing words flowed from the Divine Maiden’s mouth.

“Though I cannot drink, I will pour you a cup.”

At the Divine Maiden’s words, the women in white all looked at her at once, then lowered their heads again.

They were startled and tense. The fact that the Divine Maiden was doing something she would never ordinarily do could not be simply because they were standing in the middle of a war.

It must mean this prophecy was that important.

The Divine Maiden slowly moved her feet. Though her eyes were covered, she walked precisely to Wi Mucheon.

As if she could see, she lifted the wine bottle and accurately poured wine into Wi Mucheon’s cup.

Trickle, trickle.

The clear sound of wine filling the cup made the hearts of the women in white even more fearful. They felt a taut tension, as if something terrible might happen at any moment. Everyone knew full well what the prophecy the Divine Maiden had delivered would mean to the lord of Heavenly Will Palace.

Without drinking the wine she had poured, Wi Mucheon asked,

“What will happen if I do not withdraw from this war?”

Then the Divine Maiden calmly delivered the prophecy.

“In this battle, Heavenly Will Palace will suffer a crushing defeat. And you, Palace Lord, will die at their hands.”

It was something no one would dare say. If she had not been the Divine Maiden, it would not have been the least bit strange for her head to be smashed apart by a single palm strike from the lord of Heavenly Will Palace.

But Wi Mucheon did not do so. His cold fury instead made him calm.

He drained the wine the Divine Maiden had poured.

“Regrettably, this prophecy is wrong.”

It was not “I think it is wrong,” but a declaration that it was wrong. It was an even greater discourtesy than offering her wine earlier.

The women lined up there all raised their heads and looked at the lord of Heavenly Will Palace. Their gazes were those of people staking their lives. To deny the Divine Maiden’s prophecy was to deny their very existence.

Wi Mucheon paid them no attention. His gaze was fixed solely on the Divine Maiden.

Amid the cold tension, the Divine Maiden opened her mouth. As if she had naturally expected the palace lord to deny it, she was utterly composed.

“There may be a Divine Maiden whose entire life passes without a single prophecy descending, but a prophecy that has descended is never wrong.”

From deep within Wi Mucheon’s heart, a desire surged upward.

It was killing intent—the desire to kill the woman before him and make her a sacrifice for this battle. But he could not do that. If he did, the morale of Heavenly Will Palace would fall to the ground, and the hearts of the people would leave him. The martial artists of Heavenly Will Palace believed in the Divine Maiden’s prophecies.

“Even so, I will not stop this war.”

“May I ask the reason?”

“This time, Heaven personally conveyed its will to me.”

“How did it do so?”

“Should you not know everything?”

Wi Mucheon’s emotion was closer to anger than mockery.

If Bigwe had not awakened, he would have believed the Divine Maiden’s prophecy. Even if he had not believed it, he would have ended the war. Because of this absurd prophecy.

Wi Mucheon rose from his seat and slowly approached the Divine Maiden. Standing close to her, he whispered low enough that only she could hear.

“After I unite all under heaven, I will kill you and destroy the Divine Maiden Palace.”

Even at those shocking words, the Divine Maiden showed no agitation. Wi Mucheon’s whisper continued.

“It should not matter anyway, should it? If your prophecy is correct, then I will die regardless. Conversely, if the prophecy is wrong, then you will have deceived me with a false prophecy, and you will have committed an act worthy of death.”

Wi Mucheon smiled and spoke loudly enough for everyone to hear.

“In that sense, I suppose this prophecy may be considered a special one.”

The Divine Maiden turned her head and looked at Wi Mucheon.

It was a gaze that seemed to see Wi Mucheon’s face through the cloth covering her eyes, look into his heart, and even gaze beyond into the future.

Was that why?

Wi Mucheon felt the desire to tear away the cloth covering the Divine Maiden’s eyes. What kind of eyes was she using to look at him from beneath that cloth?

‘I have treated you all so preciously until now, and yet before this crucial battle, this is all you can do for me?’

The Divine Maiden spoke to Wi Mucheon as calmly as she had at the beginning.

“I merely deliver the prophecy. The judgment and the decision are yours to make, Palace Lord.”

After bowing respectfully to Wi Mucheon, she turned away. Before leaving, she stopped in her tracks.

Her gaze, still covered, turned for a moment toward one wall.

Everyone thought she was looking at the map of the Central Plains hanging there, but in front of that map stood Geom Mugeuk within the Space-Time Transposition Art.

Geom Mugeuk could feel it.

‘She is looking at me.’

The Divine Maiden turned back to Wi Mucheon and said,

“I will not return either. I will remain here.”

Not only the women in white, but Wi Mucheon as well was greatly startled. None of them had expected her to say she would remain on the battlefield.

Without waiting for Wi Mucheon’s answer, she went outside.

Geom Mugeuk knew why she had said that.

‘She wants to meet me.’

* * *

The aura of the Fist King, Gang Hu, had changed completely.

His killing intent shone pure white and took form, while the characters glowing like will-o’-the-wisps from both his hands dazzled his opponent’s vision.

Behind Gang Hu, the gates of hell opened. Those who had committed evil deeds and fallen into the fires of hell screamed in agony. Amid the sea of flames, evil spirits rampaged in every direction.

Gang Hu’s aura was hell itself.

It was the kind of aura that filled one with terror merely by feeling it, that robbed one of the will to fight.

The Fist Demon was not frightened in the slightest. Having stripped off his upper garment and entered the fight in earnest, the Fist Demon’s presence was terrifying.

He strode forward without hesitation, straight toward hell. Evil spirits clung to him from all directions, and everything around him boiled like lava.

The Fist Demon flung open the gates of that hell and stepped inside.

Whoooooosh!

The Fist Demon’s aura was wind.

No longer a wind that tore something to shreds, but a wind that blew within a person’s heart—the changed wind of the Fist Demon.

That wind began to blow in hell.

In the Fist Demon’s wind, the evil spirits were blown away. The masses of fire that had been boiling like lava were swept up by the wind and hurled in every direction. The evil spirits burned in the flames and screamed. Hell became a true hell.

At the center of that wind-swept hell, the two men clashed.

Kwaaaang!

Enormous inner force was loaded into the fists flying toward each other. As if they had met an irreconcilable enemy, the two men hurled their fists at one another.

Crunch.

Each time they thrust out their fists, the ground split and cracked beneath their stamping feet.

Neither of them took even a single step back. From the fists that collided came the wailing of ghosts, and the roar of thunder burst forth.

Bang! Kwaang! Bang!

Killing intent and demonic energy that could never coexist pressed down upon each other.

Ian’s heart, as she watched, pounded like mad.

Even from where she stood far away, she could feel it—the dreadful energy bursting from Gang Hu’s fists.

If the Ilhwa Sword Venerable had not blocked that energy for her, she, with her inner force depleted, would not have been able to stand there and watch like this.

Even Yeon Baeginjo, whose inner force was perfectly intact, had retreated far away and was vomiting up everything inside him, unable to endure it.

‘These things are all monsters.’

Even if he obtained the peerless secret manual, would he be able to face those men? He had no confidence. If so, what meaning was there in obtaining that manual?

Secret manual, secret manual, secret manual.

With only that filling his head, he had forgotten. He had forgotten that all of this had begun out of something unavoidable in the first place.

The one who had made him forget that was the man who had gone underground.

Because of that man’s refined manner, he had believed that perhaps he really would give him a peerless divine art. In the end, that man too must be a monster like them. No, perhaps an even more terrifying monster than these men.

Even as that happened, the exchange between the Fist Demon and Gang Hu continued.

As the ghostly flames emitted by the characters engraved on his fists mingled with Gang Hu’s killing intent, the surrounding air became so oppressive that it was difficult even to breathe.

And yet within it, the Fist Demon did not retreat a single step.

As if to say, this was the Fist Demon’s fist.

But that was not it.

The Fist Demon did not have only his fists.

Shwaaaaaak!

Crack!

The move that shattered the tense deadlock in an instant was the Fist Demon’s kick, thrust straight out as it split the space between fist and fist.

Thunderbolt Asura Fist, Fourth Fist: Iron Fist Asura.

With that single blow, Gang Hu’s jaw snapped aside and he was sent flying backward.

Because he had never imagined the Fist Demon would use a kick, he had properly taken that swift and powerful strike.

The Fist Demon did not let this opportunity slip.

Thud!

The Fist Demon’s fist, following like a shadow, slammed mercilessly into Gang Hu’s stomach. His protective qi activated, but the pain was as if his intestines were about to burst.

“Ugh!”

It was the first time a groan burst from Gang Hu’s mouth.

Having seized the advantage, the Fist Demon’s fists became even faster and stronger.

Thud! Thud! Thuuud!

The Fist Demon’s fists slammed into Gang Hu’s body in succession.

“You fucking—enough!”

After taking repeated punches, Gang Hu spat out a curse and sprang away to the spectator seats in the distance. The moment he sat side by side with Death in the stands—

Shwaaaaaak!

The Fist Demon’s fist, rushing in after him, flew in to completely crush his face.

At that instant, the light in Gang Hu’s eyes changed. It was a look that said he, too, had been waiting precisely for this moment.

Instead of blocking with his fist, Gang Hu spread his palm wide.

Hissssss.

A pitch-black wall blocked the space before his palm. The Fist Demon’s fist flew in with enough force to smash anything and everything apart.

The moment the fist struck the wall, a different sound was heard instead of the impact.

Sluuurp.

The wall undulated. The wall, which felt like liquid, absorbed the Fist Demon’s fist. He tried to pull his fist out, but that bizarre liquid gripped it tightly.

Shwaaaaaak.

At the same time, Gang Hu’s other fist cut through the air.

This time, too, the sound was different.

It was not a booming impact.

Slice! Slice!

The sound of flesh being torn rang out in succession.

Paaak.

After barely pulling his fist out of the mass of liquid, the Fist Demon was flung backward.

Drip, drip.

Blood was flowing down from the Fist Demon’s arm, the one that had not been caught.

That thick, massive forearm and fist had been slashed lengthwise, and the wound was deep. Even this was fortunate. The places Gang Hu had aimed for were his neck and heart.

At the tips of Gang Hu’s fists, blades shimmering like light could be seen. They were no ordinary blades. They were blades of murderous qi formed by Gang Hu’s killing intent.

His hidden trump card had been unleashed.

Watching that sight, Ian’s heart sank. It was the first time she had ever seen blood flowing from her father’s fist.

It was then.

As if asking for a brief rest, the Fist Demon raised his hand.

As the Fist Demon pressed the acupoints on his arm and hand to stop the bleeding, Gang Hu also tended to the internal injuries he had received from the earlier beating.

Blegh.

While tending to his internal injuries, Gang Hu vomited blood. After spitting blood, his face instead became more at ease.

In the meantime, the Fist Demon strode over and picked up the upper garment he had thrown onto the floor.

Was he perhaps going to apply Golden Wound Medicine? Impossible. Gang Hu was watching him with that thought in mind.

The Fist Demon searched through his clothes and took something out.

It was a pair of gloves.

They stretched elastically, then fit snugly onto the Fist Demon’s enormous hands.

“Don’t tell me?”

Gang Hu realized what they were. It was something no one who had trained in fist arts could possibly fail to know.

“Fighting God!”

It was Fighting God, the gauntlets Geom Mugeuk had once given him. If the greatest of swords was the Heavenly Demon Sword, then the greatest of gauntlets was Fighting God.

Gang Hu’s expression hardened. He had never imagined the Fist Demon would possess Fighting God. No, the problem was that it was now fitted onto the Fist Demon’s hands.

“The Fist Demon under heaven is wearing gauntlets? The world would laugh at you.”

It was a provocation meant to make him take off Fighting God by any means.

But the Fist Demon did not take Gang Hu’s bait. The Fist Demon did not put his pride forward. In the past, even if it killed him, he would have tried to face him bare-fisted.

But the Fist Demon now had changed. Instead of showing shame, he boasted to Gang Hu.

“My disciple gave them to me as a gift.”

“What?”

To the startled and flustered Gang Hu, the Fist Demon added,

“Our Young Cult Leader gave them to me.”

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who was watching, could tell that the Fist Demon’s change was not limited merely to the level of his martial arts.

‘The Fist Demon has become flexible.’

The Fist Demon had changed. He, who had been so hard it seemed he would never break; he, who had stronger pride than anyone.

‘He became stronger because he changed.’

The Ilhwa Sword Venerable knew because of whom this change had come about. It must be the same as how she no longer wore makeup and lived with her white hair as it was.

“You fucking bastard!”

Gang Hu once again spewed vulgar curses that did not suit him. He drew up all the killing intent he possessed. It was not intentional; curses truly burst from his mouth. He truly hated the Fist Demon enough to kill him.

Saaaaak.

From the tips of Gang Hu’s fists, the blades of killing intent stretched out even longer and sharper. They had an edge so keen it seemed they could cut even ten-thousand-year cold iron.

“You damned, sickening Demon Venerable! Come on already. It’ll take time to slice up that huge body of yours.”

At that, the Fist Demon struck his Fighting God-clad fist against his palm, making a sound as he walked forward.

Paang! Paang!

“Consider it an honor. You’ll be their first use!”

Chapter 599: Might as Well Ask to Borrow a Sword
------------------------------------------------
The Fist Demon did not let his guard down just because he wore the Fighting God.

His opponent was strong. Even fighting with all he had, he still had not managed to kill him. He had even forced him to put on the gauntlets.

Moreover, Ganghu had now drawn up every last shred of venom within him.

If he let his guard down, he would die.

It was still true for both of them.

They surged toward each other.

Because of the blade jutting from Ganghu’s fist, his arm was, in effect, longer than the Fist Demon’s.

Shwaaaaaak.

The blade, sharp enough to cut iron as if it were paper, grazed past the Fist Demon’s face. Since he still did not know whether the Fighting God could block that blade of killing intent, the Fist Demon avoided attacks as much as possible and responded cautiously.

But one could not keep dodging forever.

In an instant, a blade of killing intent flew in at an angle he could not avoid.

Paaak!

The blade that had torn through the air came to a halt, blocked by the Fist Demon’s palm.

‘Damn it! He blocked it.’

Ganghu saw it. There was not a scratch on the gauntlet. This was the majesty of the greatest gauntlets under heaven.

Shwaaaaaak.

At the Fist Demon’s counterattack, Ganghu twisted his body and immediately leaped back.

“Must be nice, hiding behind those gauntlets. Why not carry a shield while you’re at it?”

It was a persistent attempt to scrape at his pride and make him take off the Fighting God.

Pang, pang!

The Fist Demon struck his palm with his fist again and said,

“This is enough.”

It was not the reaction Ganghu had wanted. His provocations and mockery did not work.

There was one thing Ganghu did not know. From his youth, the Fist Demon had wandered the Central Plains with the Cult Leader, and among the Demon Venerables, he had more practical combat experience than anyone.

He was someone who had experienced every manner of provocation from every type of enemy.

Shwaaaaaak.

Ganghu’s fist swiftly aimed for the Fist Demon’s vital points. If it landed properly just once, it would be over.

If the blade pierced his body, then the fist would smash into the wounded spot.

Avoiding the incoming blade, the Fist Demon threw a punch.

Whoooong.

As if a massive lump of iron had swept past, the cheek brushed by the Fist Demon’s fist grew hot as though burned.

Shiiik!

Ganghu, having avoided the attack, sent his blade toward the abdomen. It was such a large body that it seemed he should be able to stab anywhere and hit something.

Yet the attacks only grazed him, and grazed him again.

It was not easy to leave even a single scratch on the Fist Demon’s body.

Of course, Ganghu did not easily allow the Fist Demon’s fists to land either. This was a matter of survival.

One hit, and he would die.

That sense of crisis kept both men’s bodies in a state of extreme excitement.

With their level of skill, one might naturally assume they were calmly grasping the situation and fighting, but that was not the case. Right now, both of them were extremely aroused.

It was a state of such extreme excitement that even if bones broke and flesh burst apart, they would feel no pain, and even if a wrist were severed, it seemed they would throw a punch with the severed stump.

That was why they could block. The attacks they exchanged came with such extreme speed and power that they could never have been blocked without this state of excitement. Without that excitement, the mind could not endure it.

Astral qi suddenly surged into their fists, and their figures vanished in an instant.

The air surrounding them burst again and again as if it would rupture one’s eardrums.

Boom! Booom! Booooom!

It was power that would have shattered anything in that place. Had there been a boulder, it would have been ground to powder; had there been a lump of iron, it would have been crushed like rice cake.

Both of them had surpassed the realm of being bound by forms, and their battle was truly displaying the very limits of fist arts.

Ilhwa Sword Venerable, who had been watching their fight, now sensed that this battle was heading toward its end.

They had been fighting without pause while pouring in such tremendous internal energy. There was no way they had much left. Both of them would try to decide the match soon.

In her heart, she cheered for the Fist Demon.

If, by any chance, the Fist Demon died, that death would not be his alone. Ian, Geom Mugeuk, and even herself, who had watched it happen—the aftermath would reach them all.

‘You must win, for our sake as well.’

Ilhwa Sword Venerable looked at Ian, who stood beside her.

She sent Ian a voice transmission.

—Do not watch it as your father’s fight. Watch it as the Fist Demon’s fight.

How could the clever Ian not understand what that meant?

—Yes, Sword Venerable.

It meant to exclude personal feelings and engrave every one of their movements in her mind without missing a single one.

Where else would she ever get the chance to see such a fight in person?

Papapapang!

Just then, as they exchanged blows with extreme concentration, Ganghu lost his balance.

The Fist Demon did not miss that instant. His shoulder slammed into Ganghu’s chest.

Kwaaang!

The moment he was struck, Ganghu swung the blade of killing intent, but it was blocked by the Fist Demon’s hand.

Shwaaaaaak.

Bang!

Struck in the face by the Fist Demon’s fist, Ganghu slid backward.

Blood streamed down from his nose.

A nosebleed in a fight between experts of this caliber?

It was truly something that should not happen, and yet, precisely because it was their fight, it could happen.

Ganghu pressed the acupoint on the bridge of his nose to stop the bleeding and asked,

“That was your chance. Why didn’t you follow up?”

Even with his head tilted back as he looked up at the sky, the Fist Demon had not attacked.

Massaging his wrist and fist, the Fist Demon said,

“You like setting traps, don’t you?”

He was referring to the blade of killing intent Ganghu had used earlier when driven into danger.

It struck the mark. In truth, if the Fist Demon had followed up, Ganghu had intended to launch a surprise attack.

Ganghu felt a tightness in his chest. In all his life, this was the first time he had faced an enemy this difficult to deal with.

Naturally, his gaze turned toward Ian.

‘If only I could take the Fist Demon’s daughter hostage.’

The reason he still could not put it into action was Ilhwa Sword Venerable, standing in front of Ian. If he needlessly provoked her and she, too, intervened in this fight, then the gates of hell would truly open.

‘I didn’t want to use this method.’

Ganghu brought out the move he had kept in reserve.

It was a move that required him to use even his innate true qi, and one he could only use by taking the life of his foremost disciple, Jindan.

But this was the only way to defeat those huge fists that were stronger than expected.

“Good. Let’s see the end now.”

Ganghu’s gaze turned toward his foremost disciple, Jindan, who had only been watching the fight until now.

At his master’s gaze, Jindan finally felt that it was time for him to step forward.

‘I will offer my life to help you.’

The characters engraved on Jindan’s fist began to emit a red glow. Then, as if waiting for it, Ganghu’s hand also emitted a blue glow like ghost fire.

Saaaaaaaaaa.

Blue and red light began moving toward each other.

Originally, Ganghu had to contain both of these energies in his fists.
